## Gap 41 — Dragging a Tile Silently Rounded Its Duration to the Nearest Quarter-Hour *(closed, 2026-09-01)*

**The report:** found while testing Open Gap N's redemption-creation flow (`ARCHITECT_GOVERNANCE.md`) — a block created with an unusual duration (e.g. 20:23–21:01, 38 minutes) that was then dragged to a new start time came out a different length than it went in (dropped at 20:45, it became 20:45–21:30, a 45-minute block). The tile had silently gained 7 minutes nobody asked for. Flagged as especially dangerous for a redemption block specifically, where duration directly determines how much deficit gets covered — an unintended lengthening during an ordinary reposition would over-credit a redemption without the user ever touching a resize handle.

**Root cause:** `OnCanvasPointerReleased`'s tile move/resize commit (`PlannerPageView.axaml.cs`) always derived *both* the new Start and new End independently, by converting each edge's raw pixel position through `PlannerDragMath.YToSnappedTime` — which rounds to the nearest 15-minute mark. For a plain MOVE, the ghost's height (and therefore pixel duration) is preserved exactly during the drag, but re-deriving both edges from pixels at release re-introduces two *independent* roundings — the tile's real duration was never actually consulted at all. The same bug existed for RESIZE: dragging just the bottom handle still re-snapped the *top* edge from its pixel position too, capable of nudging an anchor the user never touched away from its exact original (non-quarter-hour) value.

**The fix, not merely "snap less aggressively":** a MOVE must never resize a tile, and a RESIZE must only ever move the edge actually being dragged — the other edge is a fixed anchor. Three new pure functions in `PlannerDragMath` (`ClampMoveStart`, `ClampResizeTopStart`, `ClampResizeBottomEnd`) replace the blanket `YToSnappedTime` + `ClampRange` pairing at commit time:
- **Move:** only the new Start snaps to the grid (`YToSnappedTime` on the top pixel); `ClampMoveStart` then shifts it earlier only if needed to keep the tile's *exact* original duration (`tile.End - tile.Start`, read straight off the dragged `PlannedBlockTileViewModel`, never re-derived) from crossing midnight. End is computed as `newStart + duration` — arithmetic, never independently snapped.
- **Resize top:** End is pinned to the tile's exact original end-of-day offset (`tile.End - dayStart`, so a block ending exactly at 24:00 stays 24:00, not the `00:00` `.TimeOfDay` would wrongly give); only the dragged Start snaps, then `ClampResizeTopStart` keeps it from crossing the fixed End (15-minute floor).
- **Resize bottom:** mirror image — Start is pinned exactly, only the dragged End snaps and is clamped against the fixed Start.

`UpdateTileGhostLabel` (the live floating time label shown *while* dragging, before release) was carrying the identical bug and got the identical fix — otherwise the label would keep promising a rounded time the release handler no longer actually commits, a live/final mismatch worse than the original bug.

**Scope: fixed once, in the shared drag math, not specially for real blocks.** `OnCanvasPointerReleased`/`MoveOrResizeBlockAsync` are the same code path for real persisted blocks *and* Nen Contract Wizard draft blocks (`_draftBlocks`) — confirmed with the architect this should apply everywhere rather than leaving draft-block dragging with the old behavior, since a second, differently-scoped code path for "the same drag gesture, but for drafts" would just be the exact kind of drift this codebase's own precedent (Gap 6, Gap 11) already exists to avoid.

**Verified:** 12 new `PlannerDragMathTests` (`NenFocus.Tests/ViewModels/Components/`) cover the exact regression (a 38-minute move stays 38 minutes), boundary clamping (a move or resize that would cross midnight shifts rather than truncates), the 15-minute floor on both resize directions, and — as an explicit contract, not just an assertion on the return value — that the anchor edge passed in is never itself mutated. Full solution builds with 0 warnings; `NenFocus.Tests` at 1163 passing (up from 1151 before this fix — 12 new, zero regressions).

**Principle demonstrated:** a snapping/rounding helper that's correct for *deriving a fresh value from a pixel position* (which is exactly what `YToSnappedTime` is for) becomes wrong the moment it's reused to *re-derive a value that was supposed to stay fixed* — the bug wasn't in the snap function itself, it was in applying it uniformly to both edges of every drag without asking which edge, if either, the user actually meant to move.

---

## Gap 42 — Open Gap N Closed: Redemption Creation Restricted to Cover Deficit *(closed, 2026-09-01)*

**The task:** Open Gap N (`ARCHITECT_GOVERNANCE.md`), interrogated and built the same session — narrowing Gap 26/Gap 39's redemption-attachment behavior so that **Cover Deficit is the only way a redemption-linked block can be created.** A manually-created Planner tile can never become a redemption block, even when its preset+time happens to match an open deficit exactly.

**First design considered and rejected in the same session: a bespoke calendar-drag draft-placement flow** — a new unpersisted draft tile dragged/resized/split via the tile-drag pipeline, with its own toolbar (`_redemptionDraftBlocks`, `IsCoverDeficitDraftMode`, a new banner). Built, then discarded before commit in favor of reusing the existing block-creation form — less new surface area, no new tile-rendering concept, no new domain state.

**What shipped:**
- **Manual creation always declines redemption.** `SaveAsync`'s plain "new block" path now always dispatches `CreatePlannedBlockCommand` with `DeclineRedemption = true`; the old passive preview notice and confirm-gate modal (`IsConfirmCreateRedemptionVisible`, `ConfirmCreateRedemptionCommand`, `CreateAsBonusBlockCommand` — Gap 39's own additions) are deleted outright. The type-changed delete-then-recreate path sets `DeclineRedemption = originalDto.ContractId is null`, so an already-redemption-linked block's attribution still recomputes on a type flip, but an ordinary block is never newly linked by one.
- **Editing an existing ordinary block already couldn't link it — confirmed, not changed.** `UpdatePlannedBlockCommandHandler` only recomputes attribution when `existingFocusBlock.ContractId is { }`, and `ContractId` is non-null on a Focus block if and only if it already carries `RedemptionEntries` (traced through `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQueryHandler`: an unmatched attribution always echoes back the caller's own `ContractId`, `null` for a fresh manual create). No code change needed here — the existing behavior already satisfied the new invariant.
- **Cover Deficit opens the ordinary block-creation form**, not an instant-create and not a bespoke draft: pre-filled at the suggested date/time, `IsFocusBlock` forced true, `SelectedPreset` locked to the deficit's own preset via a new `CanChangePreset => !IsBlockLocked && !_isCreatingRedemption` gate on the Preset ComboBox. Date stays freely editable but is checked against the covering contract's `StartDate`/`EndDate` (captured once when the form opens, not re-read at Save) — outside that range, `FormError` rejects it, the same reject-after-the-fact pattern every other form validation here already uses. Finer StrictnessRule-specific eligibility (e.g. same-day-only redemption under Locked/DailyFlexibility) is left to the server's existing `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQuery`/`CheckRedemptionEligibility`, surfaced the same way.
- **Split Block, before the placement is ever saved:** `CanSplitBlock` gained a `_isCreatingRedemption ||` branch, opening the existing Split dialog's two time-range fields against whatever the form currently holds. Confirm Split dispatches two `CreatePlannedBlockCommand`s (not `SplitPlannedBlockCommand` — there's no `OriginalBlockId` yet), not atomic across the two calls (no multi-command transaction exists anywhere in this codebase), so a first-succeeds/second-fails outcome is surfaced explicitly rather than silently.
- **The old "won't fully cover — create anyway?" warning is gone, not migrated** — it only existed because the previous flow instant-created with no chance to reconsider; an editable form makes it redundant.
- **Folded in, a separate smaller request from the same session:** the Focus/Background type toggle is now hidden once editing an already-placed block (`ShowTypeToggle => (!IsEditing || _editingIsDraft) && !_isCreatingRedemption`) — a committed block's type can't change after the fact anyway; still shown for a brand-new block and for an in-progress Contract Wizard draft, which stays freely re-typeable until Seal.

**No domain or enforcement changes, no new persisted state.** `PlannedBlockTileViewModel`, the drag pipeline, and `SplitPlannedBlockCommand`'s `DailyFlexibility`-only scope are all untouched. A Saved redemption block is governed from that moment by the existing, unmodified `NenContract.AllowsBlockTimeChange` per-`StrictnessRule` rules, exactly like any other contract block.

**Found and fixed in passing while manually testing this gap, filed separately as Gap 41 above:** dragging a tile with an unusual (non-quarter-hour) duration silently rounded it to the nearest 15 minutes — flagged as particularly dangerous for a redemption block, where duration directly determines deficit coverage.

**Verified:** builds clean; `NenFocus.Tests` at 1163 passing (1151 at this gap's own scope, plus Gap 41's 12 new drag-math tests found in the same pass) — this gap touched only the Planner ViewModel and its form's XAML, no Application/Domain/Infrastructure test target affected. Manually tested end-to-end in the running app by the architect, confirmed working.

**Principle demonstrated:** the first design that satisfies every interrogated requirement isn't necessarily the one to ship — building the calendar-drag version far enough to see its real shape (a new draft-tile system, a new toolbar, new tile-rendering state) made the case for the simpler reuse-the-form design obvious in a way the interrogation alone hadn't. Discarding already-working code in the same session it was built, once a strictly simpler alternative surfaced, cost nothing because nothing had shipped yet.

## Gap 43 — Covering a Sub-Minute Residual Deficit Crashed FocusBlock.Create *(closed, 2026-09-01)*

**The report:** after Cover Deficit created a redemption block, the original block's deficit still showed a small remainder; clicking Cover Deficit again to close it crashed with `System.ArgumentException: End time must be after start time.` from `FocusBlock.Create`.

**Root cause.** `FocusBlock.CapEndToRedemptionCoverage` (added alongside the redemption-duration cap the day before) shortened an over-long redemption block by flooring its entries' summed `MinutesCovered` to a whole minute (`start.AddMinutes(Math.Floor(coveredMinutes))`). A real-world deficit is essentially never an exact whole number of minutes, so this floor silently discarded up to 59 seconds of genuine coverage on *every* redemption — and once the residual deficit itself dropped under a minute (as it does once nearly closed), flooring rounded it to zero, making the capped End equal Start.

**Why the fix isn't a rounding-direction flip, and doesn't collide with the Gap 33 rule it looks like it should.** `MODULE_STANDARDS.md`'s "no baking a computed fraction into a persisted timestamp" rule (from Gap 33) protects a value that gets *read back out* of a timestamp later and compared against an "at least X" threshold — that's exactly what `GetSuggestedRedemptionPlacementsQueryHandler`'s own `Math.Ceiling` protects, and it was untouched here. `CapEndToRedemptionCoverage`'s `entries` are the opposite shape: each `RedemptionEntry.MinutesCovered` is already a precise `double`, stored as-is by `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQueryHandler` and never re-derived from *this* block's own End — nothing downstream re-reads this particular timestamp to recover a threshold-checked number. So capping to the exact fractional sum (`start.AddMinutes(coveredMinutes)`, no floor) is safe: it can never equal `start` (a `RedemptionEntry` can never be zero or non-positive — its own constructor throws), and it never grants a block more scheduled — and therefore more Day-Met-credited — duration than the deficit it actually covers, which the architect specifically flagged as unwanted during interrogation (rejected both "round up" and "round to nearest" for exactly this reason).

**A second symptom, fixed alongside it, not separately reported but present in the same flow.** Because the old floor also under-stated real coverage, `redemptionCapped` (`cappedEnd != endTime`) was true on nearly every ordinary Cover Deficit save — the ceiling-padded suggestion is essentially always a hair longer than the real deficit — surfacing "A redemption block can't be longer than the deficit it covers — shortened to match." as if it were an anomaly when it was routine. New `FocusBlock.WasRedemptionCoverageMeaningfullyCapped(requestedEnd, cappedEnd)` (>= 1 minute of trim) replaces the raw inequality check at both call sites (`CreatePlannedBlockCommandHandler`, `UpdatePlannedBlockCommandHandler`/drag-resize) — the built-in whole-minute suggestion margin can never trigger it, so it now only fires for a genuine overreach (e.g. a manually stretched redemption block).

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1166 passing, up from 1163 at Gap 42's close):** the prior floor-specific test rewritten to assert exact (non-floored) capping; a new regression test reproducing the reported crash shape directly (a ~4-second residual deficit no longer collapses `End` to `Start`); two new tests for the meaningfully-capped threshold. Full solution builds with 0 warnings.

**Principle demonstrated:** two rules can look identical in shape ("round a fraction before it touches a timestamp") and still point in opposite directions depending on whether anything downstream re-derives a number from that specific timestamp — applying Gap 33's rule here by pattern-matching the shape, rather than checking what actually reads the value back, would have reintroduced the exact bug being fixed.

---

## Gap 44 — Sub-Minute Deficits Now Write Themselves Off Rather Than Requiring a Redemption Block *(closed, 2026-09-01)*

**The task:** a follow-on from Gap 43, interrogated in conversation before any code was written. Even with Gap 43's exact-precision fix, a genuinely tiny deficit (a few seconds, from ending a block just barely early, or the residual left after an earlier redemption) still technically required going through the Ritual and creating a redemption tile to close out — correct, but disproportionate to what it was actually redeeming.

**Design considered and rejected: reuse `DeficitToleranceMinutes`.** That existing 0.01-minute constant already absorbed float/tick-quantization noise on the same comparison. Bumping its value up to a human-meaningful size (rather than adding a second, distinct constant) was rejected — it would have conflated two different claims ("this is numerically zero" vs. "this is small enough nobody should be bothered by it") under one name, meaning a future reader couldn't tell which reason the number encoded. Renamed and repurposed instead: `MinimumMeaningfulDeficitMinutes = 1`, whose comment explains it's a deliberate product write-off that happens to also subsume the original noise-floor concern (1 minute is many orders of magnitude above the ~1.7e-9-minute noise Gap 33 was protecting against), rather than adding a second parallel `if` for the same comparison.

**Design considered and rejected: per-contract configurability.** Letting a stricter contract set its own write-off (e.g. zero, for a high-stakes use case like `ARCHITECT_GOVERNANCE.md` Open Gap B's exam-blocking scenario) was raised and declined — no concrete use case exists yet, and building it now would be the same "empty box, narrow lid" abstraction-with-no-second-caller smell Gap 33 and Open Gap D already rejected in this exact codebase. Settled on a flat domain constant instead, mirroring `FocusBlockOutcomeClassifier.GracePeriodMinutes` — the one existing concept in this domain that's already this same shape (a flat, hardcoded forgiveness cliff, not a per-contract setting).

**Design considered and rejected: persisting a "this was written off" fact for future Insights use.** Declined for the same reason Open Gap G itself gives for staying deferred — nothing consumes it yet. Chosen instead: the write-off is purely a read-time exclusion inside `GetNenContractDeficitsQueryHandler` (one more `if remainingDeficit <= threshold, continue` — extending the exact line that already existed for the noise-floor case). Nothing is lost: the underlying `FocusBlock`/`Session` records still hold the real numbers, so a future Insights page could recompute "total forgiven" retroactively by rerunning the same query with the threshold removed. Mirrors `PlannedBlockContractBadge`'s existing "never persisted, always computed at read time" precedent (Gap 39) — a write-off is the same kind of read-time judgment over already-real data, not a new fact needing its own row.

**What a sub-minute deficit does now:** it's excluded from `GetNenContractDeficitsQueryHandler`'s results entirely (same threshold `FocusBlock.WasRedemptionCoverageMeaningfullyCapped` from Gap 43 already uses, for the same underlying judgment on the opposite side of a redemption) — no Cover Deficit prompt, no banner, no redemption block ever required. The block's own tile is untouched — it still honestly shows `EndedEarly`/`StartedLate` if that's what happened; only whether the deficit ledger treats it as an open item changes, not the historical record.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1168 passing, up from 1166 at Gap 43's close):** boundary tests for a deficit exactly at the 1-minute cutoff (excluded) and just over it (still surfaces, with the exact remaining minutes). `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md`'s `GetNenContractDeficitsQuery` entry updated to describe the renamed constant and its dual purpose. Full solution builds with 0 warnings.

**Principle demonstrated:** a threshold whose name only describes *how* a number is bounded (a "tolerance") stops being honest the moment its real job becomes *why* it's bounded (a forgiveness policy) — renaming it rather than just changing its value is what keeps the code and the constant's own comment telling the truth about what it's actually for.

---

## Gap 45 — Sub-Minute Write-Off Reached the Deficit Ledger but Not the Real Hours Total *(closed, 2026-09-01)*

**The report, surfaced in conversation while explaining Gap 44 to the architect, not from a bug report:** walking through what Gap 44's write-off actually does end-to-end raised a direct question — if a 1-hour block ends 40 seconds early, Gap 44 removes it from the deficit ledger (no Cover Deficit prompt), but does the *completed-hours total* (feeding `IsDayMet`/`IsPeriodMet`, and eventually Insights) still show 59m20s forever? Tracing it confirmed yes: Gap 44 only touched `GetNenContractDeficitsQueryHandler`'s ledger-exclusion check. `GetNenContractCompletedFocusHoursQueryHandler` (via `IFocusBlockOutcomeClassifier.CreditedWindow`) had no forgiveness at all for an early end — it credited actual elapsed minutes regardless of how small the shortfall was. A Contract-Floor-mode contract could therefore end with zero deficits shown anywhere, yet still fail `IsPeriodMet` by the sum of every such sub-minute shortfall — a real, permanent, unfixable-through-the-UI scar, since Gap 44 had removed the one mechanism (a redemption block) that could add the minutes back.

**Options considered for actually closing it, not just documenting it:**
- **Block ending a session in its final minute, forcing the user to wait it out.** Rejected: fights the app's own recent direction. Gap 44 exists specifically to reduce friction for trivial shortfalls; forcing a wait to avoid a 40-second scar adds friction back in, and echoes the "nudge, don't block" posture `ARCHITECT_GOVERNANCE.md` Open Gap J already settled on for a harder version of the same instinct (clock tampering).
- **A full symmetric grace period — forgive any early end within `GracePeriodMinutes` (5 minutes) of `scheduledEnd`, mirroring the late-start grace exactly.** Rejected: this would reverse a documented, deliberate decision from Gap 22 — `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md` stated outright that the grace period "forgives a late start, not an early end — never both," specifically so that someone who quits meaningfully early doesn't get full credit just because they started on time. A 5-minute forgiveness window is a real, human-perceptible amount of free time; reversing that call wasn't the ask.
- **Chosen: extend the forgiveness, but scoped to the exact grain Gap 44 already drew the line at (`MinimumMeaningfulDeficitMinutes`, 1 minute), not the full 5-minute start-side grace.** A shortfall this small is "not worth a redemption block" (Gap 44's own claim) and therefore should also not be "worth a permanent scar in the real total" — the two claims were inconsistent before this fix, consistent after. A shortfall bigger than a minute still costs real credited time, same as before Gap 22 through Gap 44.

**Implementation, in `FocusBlockOutcomeClassifier.CreditedWindow` (Domain):** the early-end branch now only fires when the shortfall from `scheduledEnd` exceeds `MinimumMeaningfulDeficitMinutes`; a shortfall at or below it falls through into the same on-time/late-start resolution a block that never ended early gets — no duplicated branch logic. For the late-start sub-case specifically, the fall-through alone wasn't sufficient (the existing `Math.Min(actualElapsedMinutes, scheduledEnd − completedAt)` cap would still have picked the fractionally-shorter real elapsed time), so the credited-minutes input is swapped to `scheduledEnd − completedAt` exactly whenever the forgiveness applies, making the cap a no-op and yielding the full late-start-to-scheduled-end span. `Classify` is completely untouched — a block's `EndedEarly` verdict stays accurate regardless of whether the shortfall was forgiven; only how much of it counts toward Daily Floor/Contract Floor changes.

**Constant consolidation, done alongside the fix rather than left to drift:** `MinimumMeaningfulDeficitMinutes` was about to exist as three independent hardcoded `1`s — `GetNenContractDeficitsQueryHandler`'s own constant (Gap 44), `FocusBlock.WasRedemptionCoverageMeaningfullyCapped`'s literal `1` (Gap 43), and this new check. Moved to a single `public const double` on `FocusBlockOutcomeClassifier` (alongside `GracePeriodMinutes`, the natural home for "what counts as meaningful" in this domain) — both other sites now reference it instead of carrying their own copy. Same drift risk the Recurring Blocks gap in `ARCHITECT_GOVERNANCE.md` already flags for triplicated "does this fire on date X" logic, caught here before it shipped rather than after.

**A visible consequence, not just a silent number fix — added per the architect's explicit request:** the Block Snapshot (Gap 28's read-only overlay) now shows a small italic note under the date/time/duration line whenever a block's early end was forgiven — "Ended 40s early — within grace, full credit given." The block's `EndedEarly` badge is deliberately left alone (confirmed with the architect before implementing — the tile stays an honest record of what happened; the note explains why it didn't cost anything, rather than replacing the badge with a `CompletedOnTime`-style relabel that would erase the fact entirely). New `PlannedBlockDto.EndedEarlyGraceSeconds (double?)` carries the raw fact from `GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandler`; a new `EndedEarlyGraceSecondsToNoteConverter` does the string formatting in the View layer, not the ViewModel, per the ViewModel Forbidden Zone's "no string formatting" rule — the same discipline `FocusBlockOutcomeToLabelConverter` already follows for the badge text itself.

**Explicitly left uncapped, confirmed with the architect:** the forgiveness applies per-block with no memory across blocks or any daily/contract-wide ceiling on how often it fires. Raised as an open question (could someone habitually end every block sub-a-minute-early and quietly bank free time?) and deliberately left as-is — worst case for any realistic contract is a handful of minutes total, judged not worth the complexity of a cap for a personal focus tool.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1175 passing, up from 1168 at Gap 44's close):** new `FocusBlockOutcomeClassifierTests` covering both start-timing sub-cases (ritual on time, ritual late) of the sub-minute forgiveness plus the exact-boundary and just-past-boundary cases; new `GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandlerTests` confirming `EndedEarlyGraceSeconds` populates only for a forgiven shortfall and that the tile's display window stays trimmed to the real early end regardless. Full solution builds with 0 warnings.

**Principle demonstrated:** two numbers that describe the same underlying judgment ("is this shortfall worth caring about") but live in different layers will eventually disagree if only one of them is fixed — the deficit ledger and the completed-hours total looked like separate concerns until tracing the end-to-end story showed they were actually the same claim, made twice, only one of which had been corrected.

---

## Gap 46 — Already-Open Tabs Weren't Blocked Until Reload *(closed, 2026-09-02)*

**The gap, reported by the architect:** activating a Focus or Background block did not block a site (e.g. `dba.dk`) that was already open in a browser tab — only a fresh navigation or reload triggered the block. Traced to `NenFocusExtension/background.js`: the extension's only blocking mechanism is `chrome.webNavigation.onBeforeNavigate`, which fires exclusively on new navigations. `WindowsEnforcementService`'s hosts-file rewrite and in-memory policy update on `ApplyPolicyAsync` have no notification path to the extension at all — an already-loaded page has no reason to re-check.

**Options interrogated:**
- **Native messaging push** (app pushes "reload now" to the extension the instant enforcement changes) — rejected. Requires a new native-messaging manifest + registry entry per Chromium browser (Chrome/Edge/Brave each register separately), a new stdio IPC protocol, and a second executable to bridge it to the existing HTTP API. Connections are extension-initiated, so a dead/asleep service worker still has no live channel regardless. Disproportionate infrastructure for the latency it buys.
- **SSE/WebSocket long-lived connection from the background service worker to `WindowsEnforcementService`'s existing `HttpListener`** — rejected. Manifest V3 suspends the background service worker after ~30s of inactivity, and an open socket is not reliably treated as an "alive" signal across Chrome versions; would likely need content-script polling as a fallback anyway, making this additive complexity rather than a replacement.
- **Centralized background-script polling** (single poll of the full policy, then `chrome.tabs.query` + reload matching tabs) — rejected for the same MV3 reason: a bare `setInterval` in the service worker doesn't count as activity that keeps it alive, and `chrome.alarms` (the platform-sanctioned way to survive suspension) has a 1-minute minimum period in production — far worse than the ~2s target.
- **Chosen: per-tab content-script self-polling.** A new content script (`poll.js`), matched against `http://*/*` and `https://*/*`, runs inside each page itself rather than the service worker — immune to MV3 suspension because it lives as long as the tab does. Every 2 seconds (and once immediately on injection) it calls the exact same `/check?domain=` and `/report?domain=` endpoints `background.js` already uses, with the same 500ms abort-timeout and fail-open-on-error posture, and redirects via `location.replace` to `blocked.html` if blocked. No backend/.NET change of any kind — the fix is entirely inside `NenFocusExtension/`. `background.js`'s `onBeforeNavigate` handler is untouched and remains the first line of defense for fresh navigations.

**Why per-tab instead of the more "centralized" version the architect first proposed (reload every open tab matching the preset in one shot):** functionally identical outcome — every open tab on a newly-blocked site gets redirected within ~2 seconds either way — but a centralized dispatcher would have to live in the service worker, which Chrome can silently suspend. Per-tab self-checking sidesteps the platform constraint structurally rather than working around it.

**Correction found during architect testing:** the first version of this fix only covered *future* page loads — Chrome's declarative `content_scripts` never retroactively injects into tabs that were already open when the extension installs, updates, or the browser starts. A tab open before the fix shipped (or before any later extension reload) kept working exactly like before: no block until manual reload. Closed by adding a `scripting`-permission backfill in `background.js` — on `chrome.runtime.onInstalled`/`onStartup`, it queries all currently-open `http`/`https` tabs (`chrome.tabs.query`) and explicitly injects `poll.js` into each via `chrome.scripting.executeScript`. `poll.js` gained a `window.__nenFocusPollActive` guard so a tab that receives it both declaratively and via backfill (a tab finishing its own load right as the backfill runs) doesn't end up with two redundant polling loops.

**Explicitly out of scope, confirmed with the architect:** no reverse-redirect when a site un-blocks (matches Cold Turkey — no auto-navigate-back on unblock); no change to the extension's fail-open posture when `localhost:5005` is unreachable.

**Surfaced but deliberately not folded into this fix:** verifying whether blocking survives the app process being killed (Cold Turkey's GUI-independent service model) revealed it does not — `WindowsEnforcementService`'s local HTTP API dies with the process, and the extension fails open on that failure. Recorded as `ARCHITECT_GOVERNANCE.md` Open Gap K rather than addressed here; the architect explicitly chose to scope this session to the already-open-tab fix and revisit the crash-resilience question later.

**Third correction found during architect testing (2026-09-07):** the fix above still didn't actually block an already-open tab in practice, confirmed via DevTools console — `poll.js`'s own `fetch()` to `localhost:5005/check` was failing with "Access to fetch... has been blocked by CORS policy: Permission was denied for this request to access the `loopback` address space." A first attempt at a fix (adding an `Access-Control-Allow-Private-Network: true` response header in `WindowsEnforcementService.HandleRequestAsync`) did not resolve it and was reverted — the denial happens before the browser even looks at response headers. Root cause: a content script's `fetch()` is attributed to the hosting page's own origin (`youtube.com`, `dba.dk`), and current Chromium enforces Local Network Access as a permission check with no grant path available to a content script — there is no user-facing prompt it can trigger, so the request is denied outright regardless of what the server sends back. `background.js`'s `onBeforeNavigate` check was never affected because it runs in the extension's own privileged context, not the page's — which is exactly why navigation-triggered blocking kept working throughout.

**Actual fix:** `poll.js` no longer calls `localhost:5005` directly. It keeps its own per-tab 2-second timer (the distributed architecture above is unchanged) but relays each check through `chrome.runtime.sendMessage({ type: "nenFocusCheck", domain })`. `background.js` gained a `checkAndReport(domain)` helper (extracted from `onBeforeNavigate`'s inline logic, now shared) and a `chrome.runtime.onMessage` listener that runs it and replies with `{ blocked }` — this fetch succeeds because it executes in the background service worker's own privileged context, the same reason `onBeforeNavigate` was never affected. This is a relocation of *where* the network call happens, not a change to the per-tab polling design itself.

**Fourth correction, a real behavior gap rather than a bug fix (2026-09-07):** once the third correction above made ambient blocking actually work, the architect noticed it was now also recording a Blocked Attempt for every site/app that merely happened to be open when a block activated — inflating "you tried to access X" counts for something the user never actively did. `BlockedAttempt`'s own definition (`DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md`) is "each time the user **attempted to access** a blocked app or website" — a resource that was already sitting open when enforcement turned on was never attempted, it was just swept. **Interrogated and confirmed with the architect:** (1) a later real navigation/relaunch back to the same resource, made after the block is already active, should still count — this only suppresses the one-time ambient catch, not the resource going forward; (2) the identical distinction applies to app-blocking's `KillBlockedProcessesImmediate`, which had the same bug (the immediate kill `ApplyPolicyAsync` runs when a block first activates was unconditionally reported); (3) a narrow, accepted gap: a same-page in-app navigation (e.g. clicking a video within an already-open YouTube tab, which doesn't fire a real browser navigation) occurring inside `poll.js`'s ≤2-second polling window right as a block activates is indistinguishable from the ambient sweep and will not be counted — accepted as a rare, low-stakes undercount (it never affects whether the site actually gets blocked, only the attempt tally) rather than building click/keydown-event detection to close it.

**Split, mirroring the check/report split this created:** `background.js`'s `checkDomain(domain)` (renamed from `checkAndReport`) now only checks; a new `reportBlocked(domain)` fires `/report` and is called solely from `onBeforeNavigate` (a real navigation, checked against an already-active block — a genuine attempt) — never from the `onMessage` relay `poll.js` uses (the ambient sweep). `WindowsEnforcementService.KillBlockedProcessesImmediate` gained a `reportAttempts` bool: `ApplyPolicyAsync`'s one-time sweep on block activation passes `false`; the ongoing 2-second process-kill timer (`OnProcessKillTimerElapsed`) — which only ever catches a relaunch attempted while the block is already active — passes `true`.

**Principle demonstrated:** a platform lifecycle constraint (MV3 service-worker suspension) can make the more "obviously correct-looking" architecture (one central check dispatching to many tabs) silently unreliable, while a structurally simpler, more distributed version (many small independent checks) is the one that actually holds up — worth verifying platform guarantees before preferring the tidier-looking design.

---

## Gap 47 — Planner Time-Picker Crash: Three AutoCompleteBox Patches Failed, Full Replacement Succeeded *(closed, 2026-09-02)*

**The report, from a crash dump with no usable stack trace:** `System.ArgumentOutOfRangeException` when picking a time for a block on the Planner page — `Source`/`StackTrace` both unevaluable from the debugger. No repro steps, no location. This gap covers the entire investigation-to-resolution arc across all six of the Planner form's time fields (Start, End, and the Split dialog's four fields), which all shared one `AutoCompleteBox`-based implementation and, it turned out, one underlying platform bug.

**Investigation, escalating only as each cheaper theory failed a real repro:**
- First hypothesis (code-reading only, no instrumentation): `BuildVisibleTimeOptions` swaps `VisibleStartTimeOptions`/etc. to a brand-new `ObservableCollection` while `AutoCompleteBox`'s internal `SelectingItemsControl` still holds a `SelectedIndex` from the previous, differently-sized list — plausible given this exact class of bug had hit this control before (Gap 37). Confirmed accurate via temporary diagnostic logging (`LogDiag`, a plain temp-file logger mirroring Gap 38's own "instrument the real thing" approach) plus a real Visual Studio Call Stack capture: `SelectingItemsControlSelectionAdapter.SelectedIndexIncrement()` → `SelectingItemsControl.SelectedIndex.set` → `OnSelectionModelSelectionChanged` → throw.
- **Fix attempt 1** — reset `list.SelectedIndex = -1` every time the popup repositions. Confirmed via a second real repro: did **not** prevent the crash. The adapter keeps its own private navigation state that this reset can't reach.
- **Fix attempt 2** — clear `AutoCompleteBox.SelectedItem` on open. Also confirmed ineffective: wrong target entirely — a separate, unbound outer property, not the object the Call Stack showed throwing.
- **Root cause, once properly diagnosed:** this is a known Avalonia issue class (`AvaloniaUI/Avalonia#6128` — `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` in `AutoCompleteBox` when `ItemsSource` changes dynamically while a selection is active), not something patchable from application code. `SelectingItemsControlSelectionAdapter`'s internal keyboard-navigation state is private and cannot be reset externally.

**Decision: stop patching, replace `AutoCompleteBox` entirely** for all six fields with a hand-built `TextBox` + `Popup` + `ScrollViewer`/`ItemsControl`, fully owned end to end — no `SelectingItemsControlSelectionAdapter` in the loop at all, so the crash class has no foothold. Confirmed and refined through a live design conversation with the architect (not a unilateral redesign):
- **The list is always shown in full, never sliced by typing** — a deliberate reversal of the *original* AutoCompleteBox-era design (which showed a filtered subset while typing). The architect's own description of Google Calendar's real behavior settled this: typing only changes where the popup is scrolled/highlighted, never what's in it. This incidentally also fully closes the crash's root mechanism, since the list backing the control never gets swapped out from under an active selection anymore.
- **Partial-typing centering**: a new pure helper, `PlannerTimeOptionLookup` (`ViewModels/Components/`, unit-tested — `TryGuessHour`/`NearestIndex`/`IndexAfter`/`IndexBefore`), resolves a best-guess time from partial digits (`"14"` → hour 14, a third digit doesn't refine the guess further) or an exact parse, and finds the nearest/next/previous slot. `IndexAfter`/`IndexBefore` compose "snap to nearest" and "step to next" into one rule with no special case for an off-grid starting value (confirmed against the architect's own example: `14:07` + Down → `14:15`, not the nearer-but-backward `14:00`).
- **Up/Down/Enter/Escape are handled entirely by application code**, never delegated to any list control's built-in keyboard navigation — the actual structural fix for the crash class, not just this specific bug. Enter resolves whichever slot is currently highlighted (hovered, or the centered default) and commits it — a real repro during manual testing showed the first version just closed without committing, leaving whatever partial text (e.g. `"17"`) had been typed, which then showed as invalid.
- **Hover highlighting is real tracked state (`PointerMoved` + a `SetHighlightedContainer` helper toggling a `.highlighted` CSS class), not a live `:pointerover` pseudo-class.** A pseudo-class-based first attempt was tried and rejected after a real repro: it's inherently transient, snapping back to the centered slot the instant the pointer left the list, instead of staying on whatever was last hovered — exactly what the architect flagged as wrong. Hovering only ever *moves* the highlight; nothing resets it back to centered on its own.
- **Two further Avalonia specifics discovered and fixed via real repros, not guessed:** (1) hovering only worked over a row's painted text, never its padding — a `Border`/`Panel` with no `Background` at all isn't hit-test-visible over its empty area in Avalonia (same rule as WPF); fixed by giving each row `Background="Transparent"`. (2) That background **had to be set via a style selector, not a local XAML attribute** — local values always outrank style setters in Avalonia, so a local `Background="Transparent"` would have permanently blocked the `.highlighted` class's own background override from ever showing color. Both confirmed against Avalonia's documented style-precedence rules before landing, not assumed.
- **A `[ObservableProperty]`-specific gotcha, also only found via a real repro:** opening the dropdown right after `SetFormTimes` had already computed the same scroll index silently failed to scroll at all — CommunityToolkit.Mvvm's generated setter skips raising `PropertyChanged` when the new value equals the old one. Fixed by having the View's own "open" handler call the scroll method directly, rather than relying on the property's own change notification.
- **Popup width, row count, and duration-label sizing** — three smaller, architect-reported polish items fixed in the same pass: popup width bound to `#Box.Bounds.Width` (previously auto-sized to content); the 5-row window height derived at runtime from the real measured row height (`Extent.Height / count`) instead of a hardcoded guess — this exact control has already gotten a hardcoded pixel constant wrong twice before, see Gap 37/38 — so this was made self-correcting from the start rather than repeating that mistake a third time; End's duration-suffix `FontSize="11"` override removed so it matches the time text's own size.
- **Click-away now genuinely defocuses the field.** Light-dismissing the popup didn't move keyboard focus off the `TextBox` on its own — confirmed via a real repro, the text stayed selected/blue and still accepted keystrokes after clicking away. Fixed by focusing the page root (`Focusable="True"`) whenever a popup closes, by any means (light-dismiss, item-click, Enter, Escape).
- **The pre-existing silent-revert-on-blur behavior was removed, per explicit architect decision.** An invalid value typed and then blurred used to snap back to the last valid value without any visible signal. `ShowStartTimeInvalid`/`ShowEndTimeInvalid` (deliberately separate from the always-live `IsFormStartTimeInvalid`/`IsFormEndTimeInvalid`, which other logic still needs immediately) are now forced false while a field has focus and only evaluated on `LostFocus` — a value that's actually invalid now stays exactly as typed and shows red, rather than being silently rewritten.

**Rollout order, each slice verified by the architect against the real running app before the next began:** Start → End → the four Split fields, in that order — deliberately not all six at once, given this exact code's own history (Gap 36→38) of fixes that looked correct and still failed live. End needed its own duration-space lookup (`ResolveApproxEndDuration`/`EndTimeOptions`, comparing by `Duration` rather than wall-clock time-of-day, since a long duration can roll End past midnight where the resulting clock time is numerically *smaller* than an earlier, shorter option). The four Split fields reused the exact same shared machinery (`OpenTimeDropdown`/`MoveTimeNext`/`MoveTimePrevious` in the ViewModel, `WireFlatTimePicker`/`WireDurationTimePicker` in the View) rather than hand-duplicating the pattern four more times, since by that point it was a fifth and sixth near-identical instance.

**Cleanup once the last `AutoCompleteBox` was gone:** removed `WireTimeDropdown`/`ScrollDropdownToWindow`/`RescrollIfOpen` (the old machinery, now fully dead) and the temporary `LogDiag` instrumentation (both in `PlannerPageViewModel.cs` and the `Program.cs` unhandled-exception hook added specifically to investigate this crash) — the crash class it existed to diagnose no longer has any code path left to trigger it.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests`:** 15 new `PlannerTimeOptionLookupTests` covering the guess/nearest/next/previous math. No ViewModel-level tests were added for the wiring itself, consistent with this codebase's existing convention (no `PlannerPageViewModelTests` file exists) — verification here was manual, against the real running app, at every slice.

**Principle demonstrated:** three consecutive fixes reasoned correctly about the *shape* of the bug (a stale index surviving a collection swap) but each still failed, because the actual state was private and unreachable — no amount of correct reasoning about a black box substitutes for confirming what's actually reachable. The fix that worked didn't out-think the framework; it stopped depending on the specific internal behavior that kept breaking.

---

## Gap 48 — Split Dialog: Duration Display, Same-Length Auto-Adjust Considered and Rejected, Overlap + Work Hours Validation *(closed, 2026-09-02)*

**Built directly on Gap 47's replacement** — the Split dialog's four fields already needed the crash fix; this gap is the follow-on feature work the architect asked for once that foundation was in place, interrogated and revised several times as the real intent came into focus.

**Duration display, mirroring the main form's own End field exactly:** `SplitFirstEndTimeOptions`/`SplitSecondEndTimeOptions` (new `ObservableCollection<PlannerEndTimeOptionDto>`, regenerated via `RegenerateSplitEndTimeOptions` — Split's own version of `RegenerateEndTimeOptions`, differing only in that the same-day cutoff always applies, not gated on `IsDraftingContract`, since Split's own rule is that both pieces stay on the original's day unconditionally) show each candidate time with its resulting duration (`"13:00 (1 hr)"`), reusing the existing `DurationLabelConverter` — no new formatting logic.

**A same-length "auto-adjust" mechanism was designed, then explicitly rejected after real examples.** The architect's original ask ("if I move the split point, the second block should auto-adjust to stay in sync") led through several interrogated designs — locking the original block's outer edges with a single shared, two-way-synced midpoint; then keeping all four fields editable with the midpoint fields specifically two-way-synced — each surfacing a real ambiguity once walked through with a concrete example (a 2-hour block, moving one edge: does the whole block's placement shift, or does the total silently drop below the original duration?). **Final, simplest design, confirmed directly by the architect: no cross-block coupling of any kind.** All four fields stay fully independent, exactly as they were structurally before this gap. The existing per-block duration-preserving shift (editing a piece's own Start shifts that same piece's own End to preserve its own duration — unrelated to the other piece) was already correct and untouched.

**A contiguity check ("Second Block should start at X") was built, then removed after the architect flagged it as wrong.** The first version of `SplitWarningMessage` required `SplitFirstEndTime == SplitSecondStartTime`. The architect's correction: "the whole point of split is that you can place the split wherever you want on that day" — the two pieces were never meant to have to touch. Removed entirely; the only remaining live checks are overlap and Work Hours (below), plus a separately-displayed total-duration comparison.

**Overlap checking — reused already-loaded state, no new query needed, per the architect's own suggestion ("can't it just reuse code that already exists").** `_blocksInWeek` (populated synchronously by `LoadWeekAsync` before the Split dialog can ever open) already has every other block's `Id`/`Start`/`End` for the week. `FindSplitOverlap` checks First against Second directly, then each piece against every other block in `_blocksInWeek` (excluding the original block being split via `_editingBlockId`), naming the conflicting block's time in the message (e.g. *"First Block overlaps an existing block from 13:00 to 14:00."*).

**Work Hours checking surfaced a genuine, separate, pre-existing gap: `SplitPlannedBlockValidator` had no Work Hours rule at all**, unlike `CreatePlannedBlockValidator`/`UpdatePlannedBlockValidator`, which both call `NenContract.AllowsBlockPlacement`. Confirmed by reading the real validator, not assumed. Fixed on both fronts, per explicit architect decision to close the whole gap rather than just add client-side-only checking:
- **Server-side:** `SplitPlannedBlockValidator` gained two new rules calling `AllowsBlockPlacement` for `FirstBlock`/`SecondBlock`, mirroring `CreatePlannedBlockValidator`'s existing pattern. Applies unconditionally to both pieces (no `isFocus` branch needed) since Split is already Focus-only and already requires a sealed contract, both enforced by existing rules in the same validator.
- **Client-side:** no existing DTO exposed a sealed contract's Work Hours at all (`ActiveNenContractDto` doesn't carry it; `NenContractDraftRules.WorkHoursStart/End` only exists for the Advanced wizard's drafting flow, not post-seal editing). New `GetContractWorkHoursForDateQuery`/`GetContractWorkHoursForDateQueryHandler` (`NenFocus.Application/Queries/GetContractWorkHoursForDate/`) resolves `WorkHoursPolicy.For(date)` — the exact same per-day resolution `AllowsBlockPlacement` already uses internally — and returns the raw window (or null for "no constraint that day") as a new `ContractWorkHoursDto`. Resolved once when the dialog opens (the contract and the split's date can't change while it's open) and cached, rather than re-queried on every keystroke; `FindSplitWorkHoursViolation` then applies the same `WorkHours.ContainsRange` value-object method the server validator relies on, locally, against the cached window.

**Total duration display — combined two design options into one, per explicit architect direction ("combine B and C"):** the dialog's intro text now states the actual original duration inline (via `<Run>` elements — a real crash on Planner startup was found and fixed here, see below); the "Total" figure itself turns amber with a "should be X" note when it doesn't match the original, normal color otherwise — no separate message for the total-mismatch case, only for overlap/Work Hours (above).

**A second real crash, found via architect testing:** `NotSupportedException` from `DurationLabelConverter.ConvertBack` on opening the Planner page at all — a regression introduced by this gap's own intro-text `<Run>` binding. This was not a new discovery: `TECHNICAL_SPEC_INFRA.md` already documents the identical pitfall from Gap 28 (2026-08-21) — `Run.Text` bindings attempt a two-way round-trip in a way plain `TextBlock.Text` bindings never do, calling `ConvertBack` on a converter that only ever implements one-way `Convert`. That prior gap's own fix (add explicit `Mode=OneWay` to every `Run.Text` binding) is exactly why every *other* `<Run>` in `PlannerPageView.axaml` already carries it; this one simply should have followed the same already-documented convention from the start rather than needing a live crash to rediscover it. Fixed by adding the missing `Mode=OneWay`.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1198 passing):** 3 new `SplitPlannedBlockValidatorTests` for the Work Hours rule (first-block violation, second-block violation, both-within-bounds), 5 new `GetContractWorkHoursForDateQueryHandlerTests` (not found, no policy, default window, per-day exception override, explicit per-day no-constraint). Manual verification against the real app for the ViewModel/View wiring, consistent with this codebase's established convention for this file.

**Principle demonstrated:** the architect's own mental model of a feature ("split wherever you want, doesn't need to touch") only became fully clear after two rejected designs and a concrete "what happens to a 2-hour block if you move this edge" example — interrogating with real examples surfaced ambiguity that abstract questions ("should this be locked?") hadn't. Separately: a proposed client-side check ("can we reuse existing code for this") is worth taking literally — it led directly to finding a genuine, real server-side gap (Split's missing Work Hours rule) instead of building a client-side-only approximation of a rule the server didn't actually enforce.

---

## Gap 49 — Cover Deficit Stopped Working the Day After a Miss; Redemption Eligibility Made a Real, Per-Contract Policy Instead of Hardcoded Per Strictness *(closed, 2026-09-04)*

**The report:** a Daily Flex contract missed a Wednesday block; by Thursday, the active-contract banner correctly showed a 0.5h deficit, but the Cover Deficit button had vanished. Traced to `NenContract.CheckRedemptionEligibility(missedBlockDate, proposedDate)` (`Locked`/`DailyFlexibility`: `proposedDate == missedBlockDate` only) — every layer downstream (`GetSuggestedRedemptionPlacementsQueryHandler`'s button-visibility filter, `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQueryHandler`'s actual attachment) was working exactly as designed; the design itself no longer matched what was wanted. Confirmed this was deliberate, documented behavior at the time (Gap 26), not a regression.

**Widened during interrogation** from "fix Daily Flex" to "apply uniformly to Locked, Daily Flexibility, and Period Time Budget" — the architect was explicit the per-strictness hardcoding itself was the thing to remove, replaced by an independent, per-contract-preset setting. Two options: place a redemption **anywhere in the contract's date range**, or **current day only** (redefined to mean "today," never "the day it was missed" — the missed date is no longer even a parameter). Manual redemption creation was confirmed already fully removed (Gap 26) before any of this — `PlannerPageViewModel.SaveAsync`'s own comment already states Cover Deficit is the only path in; nothing to delete there.

**The move-without-losing-coverage problem, and the option chosen to solve it (Option D).** Once eligibility could allow "any day," a new question opened: a strictness rule that permits moving an already-placed block (Period Time Budget) could move a redemption tile to a day that would fail eligibility as a *fresh* claim, silently stripping its `RedemptionEntries` and reopening a deficit the user had already closed. Four options interrogated:
- **A — accept it.** Simplest, but a real silent-data-loss bug: dragging a redemption tile within its own strictness rule's normal freedom could un-cover a deficit with no warning.
- **B — skip the eligibility gate whenever a block already has any entries.** Too blunt — also hides genuinely new claims from validation.
- **C — pin a redemption block's date forever once placed**, overriding whatever its strictness rule would otherwise allow. Closes the hole completely (an editable block's pinned date and the live "today" can never drift apart, since a block that's ended is already immutable via Gap 28's snapshot), but costs real strictness-granted mobility.
- **D — chosen.** Separate "can a new claim originate here" (day-gated, `CheckRedemptionEligibility`) from "does an existing claim still hold" (never re-gated, only its *amount* rescales with current reality — the same principle Rule 13 already applies to a covering block's own outcome, applied one level up). `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQueryHandler` now runs a carry-forward pass over the block's existing entries (by `MissedBlockId`, rescaled against the deficit's current remaining amount, never re-checked against eligibility) before a second pass considers genuinely new claims against leftover capacity, which *is* day-gated. Full strictness-granted mobility preserved, no silent loss, no coarse skip-everything hack.

**The closure:**

| Layer | Artifact | Role |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | `RedemptionPlacementPolicy` enum (new) | `AnywhereInContract` (default) / `CurrentDayOnly`. Carried on both `ContractPreset` and `NenContract` (mirrors `StrictnessRule`'s own copy-at-Seal pattern exactly), both as optional trailing params defaulting to `AnywhereInContract` to minimize test churn. |
| Domain | `NenContract.CheckRedemptionEligibility` (rewritten) | `(DateOnly proposedDate, DateOnly today) -> RedemptionEligibility` — `missedBlockDate` dropped entirely (confirmed unused for eligibility; a deficit stays claimable regardless of how long it's been outstanding). Switches purely on `RedemptionPlacementPolicy`, never on `StrictnessRule`. |
| Application | `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQuery`/`Handler` | Gained `ExistingEntries` (default empty). Two-pass: carry-forward (never day-gated, amount rescales against the missed block's current remaining deficit) then new-claims (day-gated, spillover oldest-first as before, skipping any `MissedBlockId` already carried forward). `CreatePlannedBlockCommandHandler` passes no existing entries (brand-new block, unchanged path); `UpdatePlannedBlockCommandHandler` now passes the block's own current entries. |
| Application | `GetSuggestedRedemptionPlacementsQueryHandler` | Eligibility no longer varies per deficit (missed date isn't a parameter anymore) — checked once per contract; if today qualifies, every deficit is offered, directly fixing the reported symptom. |
| Application | `SealNenContractCommand`/`EditNenContractCommand`, `ActiveNenContractDto`, `NenContractEditDto`, Contract Preset CRUD commands/DTO | All carry the new field through, same shape every other rule field already takes. |
| Infrastructure | Migration `AddRedemptionPlacementPolicy` | New string column on both `NenContracts` and `ContractPresets` (`HasConversion<string>()`, matching `StrictnessRule`'s own mapping). |
| UI | `ContractPresetPageViewModel`/View | New "Redemption Placement" picker on the preset form; shown on preset cards. |
| UI | `ContractRulesStepViewModel`/`ContractWizardViewModel`/`ContractReviewStepViewModel` | Advanced pulls from the picked preset (confirmation label + Review summary row); Simple hardcodes `AnywhereInContract` — no preset step exists for Simple to pick anything else. |
| UI | `PlannerPageViewModel` | Cover Deficit's date-range guard (`_redemptionContractStartDate`/`EndDate`) collapses to today-only under `CurrentDayOnly` — reuses the existing bound-check verbatim, no new branching. Its `FormError` wording now says "current day" instead of a degenerate one-day "range" when that policy is active. |

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1204 passing):** rewritten `NenContractTests` for the new eligibility semantics (Anywhere-in-range, Current-day-match, and the "ignores when the deficit was originally due" regression case matching the reported bug directly); new `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQueryHandlerTests` coverage for carry-forward (rescale down when the underlying deficit shrinks, drop when it's gone, leaves room for a fresh eligible claim) and the redefined day-vs-today semantics (including the existing local-calendar-day UTC-boundary test, now driven by `_clock.UtcNow` instead of the missed block's date); `GetSuggestedRedemptionPlacementsQueryHandlerTests` updated to assert the fix directly (a deficit missed yesterday is now offered today, where it previously asserted empty).

**Principle demonstrated:** the same lesson Gap 26 already taught, hit from the opposite direction — a rule that was correct and deliberate when it shipped can still be the wrong rule once the product's own requirements move past it; "this is documented, intentional behavior" and "this needs to change" are not in conflict. Separately, Option D shows the general fix for "should re-validating an operation ever undo an already-settled decision": split the check into "originating a new commitment" (gated) versus "an existing commitment's magnitude" (never re-gated, only rescaled) — the same shape Rule 13 already used for a covering block's own outcome, recognized and reapplied one level up rather than re-derived from scratch.

---

## Gap 50 — Debug-Only Seed Fixtures for Testing the Redemption Placement Policy *(closed, 2026-09-04)*

**The ask:** manually building test contracts for each `StrictnessRule` × `RedemptionPlacementPolicy` combination after every database wipe was slow and error-prone during Gap 49's manual verification. Requested: seed data that's ready immediately after a fresh database, for as long as testing continues.

**Why not `HasData()` migration seed data** (the mechanism the seeded `BlockPreset`s already use): those are permanent, shipped, static-value fixtures every real install gets — appropriate for generically-useful starter presets, wrong for test-only `NenContract`s with dates that would go stale the moment they weren't seeded "today," and wrong to ship into a real user's data at all.

**The closure:** `HostExtensions.EnsureDatabase()` gained `SeedDebugTestContracts`, called right after `Database.Migrate()`, wrapped in `#if DEBUG` (compiled out of Release entirely) and skipped whenever `NenContracts` already has any row (never touches real, later-created data). Four contracts, one `RedemptionPlacementPolicy`/`StrictnessRule` combination each (including Lockdown, to confirm a redemption block gets no special exemption from Lockdown's immovability), with **sequential, non-overlapping date ranges** — contract date ranges never overlapping is a system invariant relied on elsewhere (Gap G's Bonus resolution), and only one contract can ever be "the" active one the Planner banner/Cover Deficit button work against at a time, so four simultaneously-active-today contracts was never actually a reachable state to seed toward. Contract 1 is active from first launch with no setup; reaching 2–4 means jumping the debug clock (`Ctrl+Shift+T`) forward into their windows — each one's own "missed" block is dated on its own first day, only becoming a real deficit once the clock has advanced at least a day past it. Every date is computed from `DateTime.UtcNow` at seed time, never a baked-in calendar date, so wiping and relaunching always produces a genuinely fresh, immediately-testable deficit regardless of when that happens.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1179 passing, unaffected — pure Infrastructure startup glue, no existing test calls `EnsureDatabase` directly) and manual verification:** Debug and Release builds of `NenFocus.Infrastructure` both confirmed clean (Release build compiles with the seeding method entirely absent, not merely skipped at runtime).

**Principle demonstrated:** test scaffolding that needs realistic, non-stale dates belongs in code computed against the live clock at the moment it runs, not in migration-time static data — the same distinction that already separates "generically useful, shipped defaults" (the seeded `BlockPreset`s) from "this session's test fixtures."

---

## Gap 51 — `NenContract.Extend` Removed Entirely *(closed, 2026-09-04)*

**Not a bug fix — a deliberate scope decision**, raised while interrogating what should freeze a contract's total-hours completion target (Open Gap L). Mid-contract extension (`Extend(DateOnly newEndDate, ...)`, pushing `EndDate` forward on an Active contract) went against the product's own model, stated directly by the architect: a sealed contract is the commitment made at seal time — wanting more later means sealing a new contract, not reopening the one already made.

**Confirmed before deleting anything:** a full-codebase search found zero UI references anywhere — `ExtendNenContractCommand` was fully built (Domain method, Application command/handler/validator/result, `NenContractExtendedEvent`) but had never been wired to any ViewModel or View. Backend-only removal, zero UI impact by construction.

**Deleted:** `NenContract.Extend(...)` and its doc comment; `NenContractExtendedEvent`; the entire `NenFocus.Application/UseCases/NenContracts/Extend/` folder (`Command`/`CommandHandler`/`Result`/`Validator`); `ExtendNenContractValidatorTests`/`ExtendNenContractCommandHandlerTests`; the `Extend`-specific block of `NenContractTests` (the `PlannedForNewDays` helper plus nine `Extend_*` cases). `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md`'s lifecycle diagram and transition list, and `TECHNICAL_SPEC_INFRA.md`'s "Not Yet Built" table, updated to match — the removed feature isn't described as pending anywhere anymore.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1179 passing, down from 1204 — the 25 deleted `Extend` tests, nothing else moved).**

**Principle demonstrated:** removing a fully-built, tested, but never-UI-wired feature is a legitimate way to close a design question (here, "does the completion target need to account for extension") — deleting the capability is sometimes the correct resolution, not just a fallback when nothing else works.

---

## Gap 52 — Daily-Floor Badges Made Live Instead of a Seal-Time Snapshot; Bonus Split Out for Real *(closed, 2026-09-04)*

**The report:** completing a redemption block on a day that had no committed floor at Seal time still showed "Rest" on the Planner's badge — no visual feedback the block had happened at all beyond the tile itself. Traced to `DailyFloorPolicy` being computed once, at Seal, and never revisited: nothing a block does afterward (a redemption, or — under Period Time Budget — a committed block moving to a different day) had ever updated a day's floor status anywhere in the app. Not specific to redemption; the same staleness applied to any post-Seal block placement.

**An interim step shipped first, then had to be partially reverted.** `GetActiveNenContractQueryHandler` stopped zeroing `CurrentHours` for a rest day (that half was correct and stayed), but the badge label/color were changed to read "Bonus: Xh" — wrong, because the hours in question came from `completedHoursByDay`, which is scoped to `ContractId == contract.Id` (committed or redemption blocks only) and structurally can never include a genuine Bonus block (`ContractId == null`). The architect caught this directly: "bonus is only for blocks that are not a part of the daily floor... all redemption and contract planned blocks should show the normal badge." The label/color were reverted back to plain "Rest" pending the real fix below, rather than shipping a mislabeled state.

**The settled model, confirmed with a concrete example before building anything** (Advanced, 1h/day fixed floor; a non-rest Tuesday gets 3 committed/redemption hours scheduled and completed — does the badge read "3h/1h" or "3h/3h"?): **"3h/1h."** A day with a real committed floor never lets that floor become "whatever got scheduled" — completed floor-counting hours accumulate against the pinned target and can exceed it as visible surplus. A day with **no** committed floor that later receives a floor-counting block anyway gets an **ad-hoc target** equal to that block's own currently-scheduled duration instead, and stops reading as "Rest." A day with neither stays "Rest." Bonus is never folded into any of this — it's a wholly separate number, correct on its own.

**The closure:**

| Layer | Artifact | Role |
|---|---|---|
| Application | `GetNenContractBonusFocusHoursQuery`/`Handler` (new) | Mirrors `GetNenContractCompletedFocusHoursQueryHandler`, inverted: sums completed hours from Focus blocks with `ContractId == null` whose `Start` falls within the contract's own `[StartDate, EndDate]` — the exact Bonus-resolution rule `GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandler`'s tile-level badge already uses, so "is this block Bonus for this contract" is answered identically everywhere it's asked. No spontaneous-session handling (unlike the completed-hours query) — a spontaneous session has no `PlannedBlock` at all, so it can't be a Bonus *block* by definition. |
| Application | `GetActiveNenContractQueryHandler` (rewritten) | Per day: `committedFloor = contract.FloorHoursFor(date)` (untouched, still Seal-time). `hasAdHocFloor` when there's no committed floor but `plannedForDay > 0` (floor-counting hours, live from the already-scoped `ComputePlannedFocusHoursQuery`) — `FloorHours`/`IsMet` resolve to the ad-hoc target in that case; `IsRestDay` is true only when neither applies. `BonusHours` populated from the new query, entirely separate from `CurrentHours`. `TotalFloorHours`/`TotalCompletedHours` needed no changes — both already derive correctly from the per-day numbers. New `TotalBonusHours` added the same way. `DailyFloorPolicy`, `IsDayMet`, `DaysBehind`, and redemption eligibility are all untouched — this is purely a read-side projection change, the same "computed at read time, never touches scoring" precedent Bonus blocks already established at the tile level. |
| UI | `DayFloorPreviewViewModel` | New `BonusHours`/`HasBonus`/`BonusLabel` — genuinely separate from `Label`, which needed no special-casing at all once the Application layer resolved `IsRestDay`/`RequiredFloorHours` correctly (an ad-hoc-floor day already renders as a normal "X/Y" badge through the existing ternary). |
| UI | `PlannerPageView.axaml` | A second small pill under the day header, visible only when `HasBonus`, alongside the existing floor badge — never merged into it. `ActiveContractBannerText` appends `"— Xh bonus"` when `TotalBonusHours > 0`. |

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1189 passing):** 5 new `GetNenContractBonusFocusHoursQueryHandlerTests` (contract not found, unlinked block within range credited, already-linked block excluded, unlinked block outside the contract's own date range excluded, Background block excluded). `GetActiveNenContractQueryHandlerTests` gained 6 replacing the one written for the reverted interim step: true rest day stays rest, an originally-rest day getting a redemption gets an ad-hoc floor and stops being a rest day, the same case not-yet-completed reads unmet, a committed floor's surplus stays pinned rather than being replaced (the concrete Tuesday example, worked exactly), Bonus hours never enter the floor badge's numerator or un-rest a day on their own, and `TotalBonusHours` sums correctly across the contract.

**Principle demonstrated:** shipping the data-layer half of a fix before the display-layer half agreed on wording is a fine way to make incremental progress, but committing a display state everyone already knows is mislabeled is not — reverting the wrong label back to something merely incomplete (plain "Rest") until the real fix was ready kept the tree in a state honestly worse only in *feedback*, never in *correctness*. Separately: working through one concrete number ("3h/1h" or "3h/3h"?) settled an ambiguity that several rounds of describing the rule in the abstract hadn't — the same lesson Gap 48 already taught, re-earned here with a different kind of number.

---

## Gap 53 — Gap 52 Testing Surfaced Three More Bugs: a Null-Binding Badge, Unformatted Banner Totals, and a Live-Floor Double-Count *(closed, 2026-09-04)*

**Found entirely through architect testing of Gap 52, not through further design work.** Three separate bugs, fixed in the same pass:

**1 — Blank blue badge on every day with no contract.** The new Bonus pill's `IsVisible="{Binding DayFloorStatus.HasBonus}"` bound through a sub-property; when `DayFloorStatus` itself is `null` (no contract covers that day), Avalonia can't resolve `.HasBonus` and the control falls back to its default `IsVisible` (`true`) instead of `false` — a blank blue box on every non-contract day. The floor badge avoids exactly this by gating on the *object itself* (`DayFloorStatus, Converter=IsNotNull}`), not a sub-property. Fixed with a new `DayFloorStatusHasBonusConverter`, mirroring `DayFloorStatusToBrushConverter`/`ForegroundConverter`'s existing null-safe pattern — bound to the whole `DayFloorStatus` object, `false` when null.

**2 — Banner always showed "0h deficit"/"Xh bonus" even at zero, and hour-only formatting.** `ActiveContractBannerText` unconditionally appended the deficit segment and used `{0:0.#}h` regardless of magnitude. Fixed: both the deficit and bonus segments are now omitted entirely when their value is `0`, and both use the existing "1h 30m"/"30m" format (`MinutesToStringConverter`) instead of decimal hours — a new public `MinutesToStringConverter.FormatMinutes(double minutes)` static entry point lets the ViewModel reuse the exact same formatting rule Avalonia's `IValueConverter.Convert` already uses, rather than duplicating it inline.

**3 — Completing a redemption made the banner's total *grow*, not shrink toward 100%.** The deepest of the three. A contract committed 1h at Seal; that hour was missed, then redeemed on a day that had no committed floor of its own (an ad-hoc-floor day, per Gap 52). `TotalFloorHours = dayStatuses.Sum(d => d.FloorHours)` summed *both* the missed day's still-pinned 1h floor *and* the redemption day's ad-hoc 1h floor — two numbers for what was really the same hour, relocated. Banner read "1h / 2h completed" instead of "1h / 1h." Re-deriving the total from only "real" `DailyFloorPolicy` days (tried first) fixed that case but broke another: a contract using `DailyFloorPolicy.None` throughout (exactly what the debug seed fixtures use) has *no* real `DailyFloorPolicy` day anywhere, even for the block that was genuinely planned at Seal — so the total collapsed to 0 the moment nothing had been redeemed yet.

**The actual fix — pulling forward part of Open Gap L piece 2 rather than patching the live-floor approach a third time.** The architect had already specified the correct concept during that gap's interrogation: a frozen `CommittedFocusHours` total, captured once at Seal from what was *actually planned* (`plannedFocusHoursByDay.Values.Sum()`), never recomputed afterward — deliberately not derived from `DailyFloorPolicy` at all, since a day can be planned above its own floor. `NenContract` gains this as a new field, computed inside `Seal()` itself from data it already receives (no new parameter, no caller changes needed). For a `ContractFloor`-mode contract this is numerically identical to the existing `ContractFloorHours` (both already computed the same way at Seal) — `CommittedFocusHours` works uniformly across every strictness rule without needing that special case. `GetActiveNenContractQueryHandler`'s `TotalFloorHours` now reads `contract.CommittedFocusHours` directly; the flawed `dayStatuses.Sum(...)`/interim `totalCommittedFloorHours` accumulator approaches are both gone. New migration `AddNenContractCommittedFocusHours`.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1194 passing):** 3 new Domain-level `CommittedFocusHours` tests (sums the plan regardless of the floor, works with `DailyFloorPolicy.None`, matches `ContractFloorHours` for a Period-Budget contract) plus 2 new `GetActiveNenContractQueryHandlerTests` reproducing both reported scenarios directly (the double-count, and the `DailyFloorPolicy.None` under-count). One pre-existing test's helper (`ContractFloorContract`) had to stop faking a `ContractFloor` value disconnected from its `plannedFocusHoursByDay` — a mismatch only that test's own convenience shortcut could produce, never a real sealed contract.

**Principle demonstrated:** the second fix attempt (real-floor-only summation) was still wrong, and both wrong attempts shared a root mistake — deriving a value that's supposed to be a frozen commitment from data that's designed to be live. The moment the *right* concept was already sitting in an open design doc from the very same session (`CommittedFocusHours`, specified for a different reason — completion grading), building it for real closed both bugs at once, cleaner than either patch. Worth checking whether a fix already has a name before inventing a third patch.

---

## Gap 54 — Defense-in-Depth: a Preset Reference That Doesn't Exist Could Still Reach a Command *(closed, 2026-09-04)*

**Raised directly by the architect, generalizing from the Contract Preset seed-data discussion**: should "you need a preset to do X" ever be a UI-only rule, or does it also belong at the Application/Domain layer? Checked against the real code rather than assumed for both `NenContract` and `Session`.

**`NenContract`:** `ContractRulesStepViewModel.Continue()` already refuses to proceed in Advanced mode without a preset picked (Gap 27) — a real user cannot reach Seal without one. But neither `SealNenContractValidator` nor `EditNenContractValidator` ever checked that a *given* `ContractPresetId` actually resolves to a real `ContractPreset` — both already had the identical check for every individual block's `BlockPresetId`, just not for the contract-level preset reference itself. Fixed: both validators now fail with "The selected contract preset no longer exists — please pick another." whenever `ContractPresetId` is non-null and doesn't resolve; `null` stays valid, since a Simple contract legitimately has no preset — the check can't require one for every contract, only verify a given one is real.

**`Session` — a real, live gap, not just a hypothetical one.** `StartSessionValidator`'s only existence-adjacent rule for a spontaneous session's `PresetId` was "must be a Focus preset" — written as `preset is null || preset.PresetType == Focus`, which explicitly treats "doesn't exist" as passing. Traced into `StartSessionCommandHandler`: nothing there checks existence either — `Session.Create(..., blockPresetId: command.PresetId ?? Guid.Empty, ...)` builds and starts the session with whatever ID was given, and the one place the preset is actually looked up afterward is purely for the session's *display name*, `presetName = preset?.Name ?? string.Empty` — silently blank if missing, session already running. A stale or deleted `PresetId` would start a session with **no real blocking behind it at all** (`BlockedApps`/`BlockedSites` resolve to nothing for a preset that doesn't exist) — the app's whole purpose quietly defeated for that session. Fixed with a new existence rule ahead of the type check. Confirmed the Planned-session path (`OriginatingBlockId` set) doesn't share this gap: `DeleteBlockPresetValidator` already refuses to delete a preset still assigned to a future planned block, so that reference can't go dangling later the way a live spontaneous pick could.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1200 passing):** 3 new `SealNenContractValidatorTests`/1 new `EditNenContractValidatorTests` case for the preset-existence rule (null passes, missing fails, real one passes), 2 new `StartSessionValidatorTests` cases (spontaneous with a missing preset fails; planned with a missing preset still passes, confirming the rule is correctly scoped to spontaneous only).

**Principle demonstrated:** a UI-layer guard and an Application-layer guard answer different questions even when they look redundant — the wizard/Ritual protect a real user going through the one path they have; the validator protects the command itself against anything that reaches it another way (a future bug, test scaffolding, a later caller nobody's designed yet). The Session gap was real and already reachable, not a defensive hypothetical — worth checking the analogous case for real before assuming the same protection existed there too.

## Gap 55 — A Week Straddling Two Contracts Silently Dropped One's Badges; Planner Banner Gains a Switcher, Popover Decoupled From the Week View *(closed, 2026-09-06)*

**Reported by the architect as a bug, not requested as a feature:** a block tile on a Saturday looked like it belonged to a contract but sat outside that contract's own date range, was undeletable and unmovable. Investigating the live database (copied read-only, queried directly — not assumed) showed the tile was in fact correctly inside its own contract's range; it was actually a `#if DEBUG`-only test fixture (`HostExtensions.SeedDebugTestContracts`, Gap 50) seeded into the real `%AppData%` database because the app was running in Debug against the normal DB rather than a throwaway one — a red herring, not the real bug. The undeletable/unmovable behavior was fully correct by design (`ContractId`-linked blocks are permanently protected; `DailyFlexibility` only allows same-day moves).
