**The real bug, found once the architect re-framed the report around the Planner's day badges specifically:** two adjacent debug contracts (Sept 12–22 and Sept 23–Oct 3) each legitimately owned their own days, but the days belonging to whichever contract *lost* `GetActiveNenContractQueryHandler`'s tiebreak (Active preferred, then earliest `StartDate`) got no badge at all whenever both contracts' ranges touched the same displayed week. The handler was built around "resolve the one relevant contract for this week" and had no path for two non-overlapping contracts (they never overlap each other) sharing one calendar week, since a week is a fixed 7-day grid independent of contract boundaries.

**Fix — Application layer, interrogated before writing code (Design Gap Protocol):** `GetActiveNenContractQuery`/`ActiveNenContractDto` renamed and broadened to `GetNenContractsForDateRangeQuery`/`NenContractRangeViewDto`, returning `IReadOnlyList<NenContractRangeViewDto>` — every in-effect contract overlapping the range, each with its own full `DayStatuses`, pre-sorted with the same tiebreak as before (Active preferred, then earliest `StartDate`) so a single-contract range is unaffected. Renamed to `RangeStart`/`RangeEnd` rather than `WeekStart`/`WeekEnd` on the architect's own instruction, since the query does nothing week-specific and the architect named a concrete future plan (day/month/schedule views) that would otherwise force the same rename again. One query, one computation of "which contracts touch this span" — avoids the alternative (a second, separate "which contracts overlap" query) that would have duplicated the exact kind of resolution logic Gap 11/Gap 6 already caught drifting out of sync elsewhere in this codebase.

**Planner banner switcher (new feature, fully interrogated, then revised twice against real usage):**
- `PlannerPageViewModel.SelectedContract` (renamed from `ActiveContract`) now names one contract from `_contractsThisWeek` for the banner text and Cover Deficit only; a small "⇄" button (styled like the existing week-nav `‹`/`›` buttons, `navButton` class) appears only when more than one contract touches the week and cycles through them, tooltip naming the target.
- **First design, then reversed on direct instruction:** originally, switching also swapped which contract's badges the calendar showed (the non-selected contract's days went fully blank) — matching the architect's own initial answer during interrogation. After using it, the architect asked for the opposite: "all badges from all contracts visible at all times... switching... should not change the badges viewed in the calendar, it only changes what you see in the banner." `RebuildMergedDayFloorByDate` now merges every contract's `DayStatuses` unconditionally (safe because contract ranges never overlap — no date can ever come from two contracts), completely independent of `SelectedContract`; only the banner text and Cover Deficit's target contract respond to the switch.
- **A second bug, found by the architect after the first fix shipped:** switching silently reverted within seconds. Root cause: the pre-existing 3-second live poll (`OnFloorStatusPollTick`, added for Gap 18 to keep the banner's elapsed-hours live) called the same refresh method used for week navigation, which unconditionally reset the selection index to 0 every tick. Fixed by splitting the refresh into `RefreshSelectedContractAsync` (hard reset to default — used only by `NavigateWeekAsync` and app init, the only two call sites that are genuine week navigation) and `RefreshContractsPreservingSelectionAsync` (re-locates the previously selected contract by `Id` in the freshly fetched list, falling back to the default only if it's no longer present — e.g. just Broken or Completed by the very refresh that triggered this call). Every other refresh site (the poll, save/drag/split, session-change, seal, cancel-contract, cancel-draft) switched to the preserving variant, since all of them are exactly the same class of "something else changed, refresh the numbers" event the poll already was.
- Cancel was removed from the week banner entirely (a "✕" button that used to sit next to Cover Deficit) on direct instruction — it now lives only in the Nen Contract popover.

**Nen Contract popover decoupled from the week view (found by the architect, not requested as its own task):** after the switcher shipped, the popover's own "current contract" card was still reading `SelectedContract` — meaning the popover would show whichever contract the Planner's week/switch state happened to be pointing at, contradicting its own purpose as a global "manage your contracts" surface. Fixed with a new `GetInEffectNenContractsQuery` → `NenContractSummaryDto` (renamed/broadened from `GetScheduledNenContractsQuery`/`ScheduledNenContractDto`, same rename motivation as the first query above), returning every in-effect contract app-wide — Active first, then Scheduled by `StartDate` — completely independent of whatever week/range the Planner grid has on screen. The popover's Active card now binds to `PopoverActiveContract` (always the one true `NenContractStatus.Active` contract, if any) and simplified back to a single "Cancel" button, since it can no longer show a Scheduled contract the way `SelectedContract` sometimes could.

**Day-badge palette, iterated three times against direct visual feedback, not designed up front:**
1. First cut: a Scheduled (Upcoming) contract's days render as a uniform grey regardless of met/unmet/rest (freeing that meaning up from "rest" generically) — Active-contract rest days initially just reused the existing `MetBrush` green ("nothing left to do," same register as a completed day), on the architect's own explicit call.
2. The architect didn't like the flat green in practice and asked for suggestions differentiating rest days by Past/Today/Future, comparing against the palette already in use. Presented three concrete options (a new teal family, a muted sage/mint family, and an extended blue family) with hex values lined up against the existing swatches.
3. Chosen: a dedicated teal family — Future `#C5E4DE`, Today `#4FA89C`, Past `#2E7A70` — a hue nothing else in the converter uses, so a rest day never reads as "met" (green), "future on-track" (blue), or "Upcoming contract" (grey). `DayFloorPreviewViewModel` gained `ContractStatus (NenContractStatus?)`, null while drafting (same nullability convention as the pre-existing `ContractStrictnessRule`), consumed by both `DayFloorStatusToBrushConverter` and `DayFloorStatusToForegroundConverter`.

**Minor fixes bundled into the same session, each small enough not to warrant its own gap:**
- `MinutesToStringConverter.FormatMinutes` showed a pointless "1h 0m" for an exact-hour deficit; fixed to "1h", with a sub-hour amount now spelling out "30 min" and a mixed amount staying "1h 30m" — one shared formatter, so the deficit banner text, the Bonus label, and the redemption-covered display all changed consistently rather than drifting apart.
- The switch button's own size caused the banner's height to visibly jump depending on which of Cover Deficit/switch were showing (`Auto`-sized Grid row + `IsVisible` collapsing layout space entirely). Fixed with a `MinHeight` on the banner border sized to the tallest realistic combination, plus shrinking the switch button itself (smaller padding/font) so that combination is smaller to begin with.
- Debug-only seed contract/preset names (`HostExtensions.SeedDebugTestContracts`, Gap 50) shortened from "Redemption Test — X" to plain "X" — cosmetic, requested directly, reverted an earlier over-complication where the assistant also patched the architect's already-seeded live database to match rather than leaving the source-level rename to apply on the next fresh database, which the architect explicitly didn't want.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1240 passing)** at each checkpoint before expanding to the next slice, per the Design Gap Protocol's headlight-management discipline — the query rename/broadening, the switcher, the popover decoupling, and the palette change were each verified green before the next began.

**Principle demonstrated:** a query built as "one source of truth returning a list" (the `GetNenContractsForDateRangeQuery` shape, chosen explicitly over two competing designs during interrogation) absorbed three different, unanticipated consumption patterns without ever changing its own shape — merge-all for badges, pick-one-and-cycle for the banner, and (via its sibling `GetInEffectNenContractsQuery`) an entirely separate app-wide/week-independent read for the popover. None of those three uses needed a new query or a change to this one's contract, which is exactly the payoff "one source of truth over a list" was chosen for two designs earlier in the same session. Separately: several of this gap's real defects (the poll-reset bug, the popover's stale week-coupling) were found by the architect *using* the previous fix, not by further up-front interrogation — headlight management caught what design conversation alone did not, the same lesson in a UI feature that Gap 47/48 already taught in a dialog crash.

---

## Gap 56 — Same-Day Redemption Retry Double-Counted on the Day Badge; `RawDeficitMinutes` Promoted to `FocusBlock` *(closed, 2026-09-07)*

**Reported directly by the architect as a bug:** redeeming a 1h deficit on Monday via two attempts — a first redemption block ended early, a second placed the same day to cover what was left and completed — showed the day badge as "1h/2h" instead of "1h/1h." Gap 53 had already fixed the *banner's* version of this exact double-count (via the frozen `CommittedFocusHours` total), but that fix never touched the per-day `FloorHours` value each badge tile reads, which still summed every redemption block's raw scheduled span regardless of outcome. A redemption block's own `End` is never trimmed when its session ends early (`EndSessionCommandHandler` only sets `SessionEndedAt`) — so a first attempt's full original claim kept counting even after a second block picked up the leftover, double-booking one deficit across two blocks.

**Interrogated before writing anything — the architect specified the exact rule to build, not just "fix the number":** a day's floor may never exceed the deficit a redemption block is actually covering *unless a genuinely different missed block is also being redeemed that day* (in which case the badge should legitimately grow). Confirmed against a detailed walkthrough (redemption block placed → an ordinary committed block moved onto the same day under Period Time Budget → the redemption block ends early → the leftover redeemed via a *new* block on a *different* day): the original day's floor must stay pinned at its full historical total throughout — a retry on a different day must never retroactively shrink or inflate either day's badge. Same-day dedup, no cross-day dedup — each day is its own historical record.

**Where the "missed block's own settled deficit" number should live — interrogated as its own question.** Three options were weighed: (A) recompute the `scheduledMinutes - creditedMinutes` formula inline in the new badge logic — rejected as the exact "same rule reimplemented twice" drift pattern this project's own history (Gap 11/Gap 6, cited in Open Gap E) already flags as a repeat mistake; (B) widen `GetNenContractDeficitsQueryHandler` to also report fully-settled (zero-remaining) deficits it currently filters out — rejected as stretching an existing query's job rather than answering "who owns this rule"; (C) promote the formula to a pure method on `FocusBlock` itself, mirroring the already-established `CanContributeOwnDeficit`/`WasRedemptionCoverageMeaningfullyCapped` precedent ("the rule belongs with its data"). Option C was the one that actually matched how this codebase already treats this category of rule, and is what got built.

**The fix:**
- `FocusBlock.RawDeficitMinutes(double creditedMinutes)` — new pure Domain method, `Math.Max(0, GetDuration().TotalMinutes - creditedMinutes)`. `GetNenContractDeficitsQueryHandler`'s own inline copy of this formula was replaced with a call to it, so the two can never drift apart again.
- `GetNenContractsForDateRangeQueryHandler` gained three new dependencies (`IPlannedBlockRepository`, `ISessionRepository`, `IFocusBlockOutcomeClassifier`) and a new `ComputeRedemptionDedupAdjustmentByDayAsync` step: every redemption entry across the contract is grouped by `(local day the covering block sits on, missed-block-id)`. Each group's claimed minutes are capped at that missed block's own `RawDeficitMinutes` (resolved once per missed block, cached) — a per-day, per-hour correction that's added on top of the existing raw `ComputePlannedFocusHoursQueryHandler` sum rather than replacing it. `ComputePlannedFocusHoursQueryHandler` itself was deliberately left untouched — it's also used by `UpdatePlannedBlockValidator`/`SplitPlannedBlockValidator` for real capacity/floor-protection checks (Daily Flexibility / Period Time Budget) where "raw scheduled span" is the correct, unrelated meaning; changing it would have risked those validators for no reason.
- Grouping is keyed by the covering block's local `Start` date, not run through `IPlannedFocusHoursCalculator`'s day-splitting — a deliberate, noted simplification, since a redemption block spanning midnight would be mis-attributed by one day. Accepted given `FocusBlock.MaxDurationHours` and how these blocks are actually sized in practice.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1245 passing):** 3 new `FocusBlockTests` for `RawDeficitMinutes` (nothing credited, partially credited, over-credited never goes negative), plus 2 new `GetNenContractsForDateRangeQueryHandlerTests` reproducing both reported scenarios directly — the same-day double-attempt (asserts `1h/1h`, not `1h/2h`) and the cross-day retry (asserts neither day's floor moves).

**Principle demonstrated:** the architect's own clarifying example (four sequential states of one badge, worked through by hand) surfaced the actual rule — same-day dedup, no cross-day dedup — that a purely verbal description of "cap at 1h" would have gotten wrong in the cross-day case. Once the rule was pinned down precisely, "where does this formula live" turned out to be a separate, answerable-from-precedent question on its own (Option C matched two sibling methods already on `FocusBlock` for the identical reason) — worth interrogating as its own step rather than bundling it into "just fix the badge."

---

## Gap 57 — Whole-Minutes Crediting Redesign: Gap 44/45 Removed, Grace Reframed as "Enforcement Was Active," `DailyFloor` Made the Sole "Met" Authority *(closed, 2026-09-07)*

**Surfaced while testing Gap 56, not while looking for it.** After Gap 56 shipped, the architect noticed a day badge reading "1h/1h" but still rendering amber (unmet) — traced to `DayFloorPreviewViewModel.IsMet` (`CurrentHours >= RequiredFloorHours.Value`) comparing sub-second-precision doubles: a still-live redemption block's progressively-accruing credited time was genuinely a hair under the target (e.g. 0.983h), which the `{0:0.#}h` display format rounds to the same text as the target while the underlying exact comparison still — correctly — read unmet. Not a bug in the strict sense (the badge was honestly reporting "not finished yet"), but it exposed that four separate places in the app each independently compared raw, sub-second-derived doubles with no shared tolerance: `DailyFloor.IsSatisfiedBy`, this handler's own inline ad-hoc/period-floor comparisons, `DayFloorPreviewViewModel.IsMet`'s duplicate re-derivation, and `CompleteNenContractValidator`'s `totalCompleted < CommittedFocusHours` gate — the last of which meant a contract could, in principle, never reach exactly 100% and never become completable, over a residue of seconds nobody could act on.

**Interrogated in stages, with the architect steering each turn rather than a single up-front design:**
1. **First framing (rejected):** add a tolerance — reuse the existing `MinimumMeaningfulDeficitMinutes` (1 minute) write-off inside `DailyFloor.IsSatisfiedBy` so a shortfall that small is forgiven everywhere "met" is checked. The architect pushed back hard on the premise instead: ending a block 30 seconds before its scheduled end should still cost you the minute, specifically to discourage quitting the instant there's "barely any time left" — forgiveness was the wrong direction entirely.
2. **Second framing, converging on the real fix:** stop tracking sub-minute precision in the crediting math at all. Seconds only matter for the Main Page's own live countdown; crediting should work in whole minutes, floored (not rounded, not ceiling'd) — "if you complete 1 min that is what you're credited for... not credited for anything beyond that until you reach 2 min." A clean, whole-minute deficit, however small, must always be recoverable through Cover Deficit — nothing silently written off. This meant both Gap 44 (the ledger's 1-minute write-off) and Gap 45 (crediting the full scheduled duration when an early end fell within 1 minute of the scheduled end) were direct casualties: the architect confirmed removing both outright once the contradiction was pointed out — Gap 45 was specifically what would have credited a fabricated extra minute that never happened.
3. **The grace-period edge case, walked through together:** does the 5-minute Ritual grace period still forgive a late start once the block goes on to end early, or does an early end wipe out that forgiveness (today's shipped behavior, per Gap 45's own original comment: "the grace period's purpose is to not dock a few minutes of Ritual setup once the block has genuinely run its course")? Working the architect's own numeric example (Ritual 2:30 late, block 2:30 early) against both interpretations showed only "grace still applies, unconditionally" reproduces the "3 min deficit" the architect expected — the other reads as "5 min." Confirmed against real code before settling it: `PolicyEngine.ComputeSlice` keys blocking enforcement entirely off `PlannedBlock.Start`/`End`, never `RitualCompletedAt` — so the Ritual-setup window is genuinely enforcement-active time, and crediting it isn't inventing work, it's counting real blocking. Grace now forgives the late start unconditionally, regardless of how the session later ends; only a start *beyond* grace gets no allowance. A follow-up edge case (Ritual finished at 3 min, block ended 30 seconds later) resolved the same way: 3:30 raw, floored to 3 credited minutes — honest, not "free ritual credit," since blocking really was active for those 3.5 minutes. A more extreme case (Ritual at 20s, ended after another 20s — 40s total) floors to 0 credited minutes entirely; the tile still reads `EndedEarly` even at zero credit, which is correct, not a display bug.

**The fix, four pieces:**
1. **`Session.GetElapsedTime(DateTime asOf)`** (Domain) now floors its result to whole minutes — verified as the single true choke point first: every one of its ten call sites across the codebase (deficit ledger, completed/bonus hours, Post-Session duration, session history, missed-block reporting) is an accounting question, and grepping confirmed no live, second-by-second UI reads through this method — the Main Page's own countdown is built through an entirely separate mechanism. Flooring here alone, however, turned out to be necessary but not sufficient (see next point).
2. **`FocusBlockOutcomeClassifier.CreditedWindow`** rewritten around one unconditional rule instead of the old branching-by-outcome structure: grace forgives a late start regardless of what happens next; a genuine early end always counts exactly what was delivered, with zero forgiveness at any grain; a session that runs its course credits up to `scheduledEnd`, never beyond. Renamed the branch logic into a private `ComputeRawWindow`, with the public `CreditedWindow` flooring the *returned window's own End* to whole minutes as a final step — not just the minutes derived from it. This mattered: `GetNenContractCompletedFocusHoursQueryHandler` and `GetNenContractBonusFocusHoursQueryHandler` both read the window's `Start`/`End` directly (never going through `CreditedFocusMinutes` at all), so flooring only inside `CreditedFocusMinutes` would have left those two consumers exactly as sub-second-precision-fragile as before. `CreditedFocusMinutes` itself needed no further change — its `(w.End - w.Start).TotalMinutes` is already floored by construction once the window is.
3. **Gap 44's write-off removed** from `GetNenContractDeficitsQueryHandler` — the exclusion condition changed from `remainingDeficit <= MinimumMeaningfulDeficitMinutes` (1 minute, a product forgiveness) to `remainingDeficit <= 1e-6` (a pure floating-point safety guard, several orders of magnitude smaller, kept only because `RedemptionEntry.MinutesCovered` still round-trips through a `DateTime`'s tick quantization and can leave a microscopic residual behind for a redemption that visibly, functionally fully covers its target — a completely different concern from the removed product-level forgiveness, confirmed by a pre-existing test (`HandleAsync_CoverageWithinFloatingPointTolerance_TreatedAsFullyCovered`) that still needs to pass). `MinimumMeaningfulDeficitMinutes` itself survives only as `FocusBlock.WasRedemptionCoverageMeaningfullyCapped`'s UI-notice threshold ("is this shortening worth surfacing"), the one consumer of the constant it was never really about.
4. **`DailyFloor.IsSatisfiedBy` made the sole "met" authority, with zero exceptions** — closing a second loose thread the architect caught in direct follow-up, after being told "everything's single-source-of-truth now" wasn't quite true yet. `GetNenContractsForDateRangeQueryHandler`'s Period Time Budget and ad-hoc-floor branches previously compared `currentHours >= plannedForDay` inline, bypassing `DailyFloor` entirely (only the real-committed-floor branch routed through `contract.IsDayMet` → `DailyFloor.IsSatisfiedBy`). Both now construct a transient `new DailyFloor(plannedForDay).IsSatisfiedBy(currentHours)` — safe by construction, since the no-overlapping-blocks invariant `CreatePlannedBlockValidator`/`UpdatePlannedBlockValidator` already enforce keeps any single day's own block total within `DailyFloor`'s 0–24h range. `DailyFloor`'s own doc comment already documented this exact dual-purpose design ("used both at contract Seal... and during later day-met evaluation") — extending it to a third, ad-hoc caller wasn't a stretch of the type, just finishing what its own contract already promised. `DayFloorPreviewViewModel.IsMet` (the ViewModel-layer duplicate that started this whole gap) no longer re-derives anything — it's now a plain passed-in value, `NenContractDayStatusDto.IsMet` for the live/sealed path (verbatim, from the one place that decides it), locally computed only for the drafting path, which has no Application-layer answer to defer to.

**A live-data spot check, not just unit tests:** before diagnosing, the architect's own `%AppData%\NenFocus\NenFocus.db` was copied read-only and queried directly (a throwaway `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite` console project, cleaned up after) to confirm the actual redemption blocks behind the reported badge, rather than guessing at the scenario — found a still-*live*, in-progress redemption session (`SessionEndedAt` null) sitting behind the "1h/1h yet amber" report, which is what made "still accruing, correctly not yet met" the right diagnosis instead of a genuine bug.

**Dead code found and removed as a direct consequence, not a separate task:** `PlannedBlockDto.EndedEarlyGraceSeconds` (and its computation in `GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandler`, its `EndedEarlyGraceSecondsToNoteConverter`, and the Block Snapshot's "within grace, full credit given" note in `PlannerPageView.axaml`) existed solely to explain Gap 45's forgiveness to the user. With Gap 45 removed, no shortfall is ever forgiven any more — the field would have stayed permanently null (or worse, kept computing against a threshold that no longer means anything), silently lying about behavior that no longer exists. Deleted outright, including its three dedicated `GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandlerTests` cases, rather than left as unused scaffolding.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1242 passing after the dead-code removal, from 1245):** the classifier's own test file was substantially rewritten, not just patched — several tests that had encoded Gap 44/45's old forgiveness now assert the opposite (a 40-second, or 1-minute-1-second, early-end shortfall floors to real, non-zero credited minutes; a same-value grace-boundary test that used to stop at "credits actual elapsed" now confirms the forgiven setup time gets added on top). A new test reproduces the architect's own worked example verbatim (2:30 late, 2:30 early, 1-hour block → floors to 57 credited minutes). `GetNenContractDeficitsQueryHandlerTests`' one-minute-write-off test was inverted to assert the deficit now surfaces rather than gets excluded. `EndSessionCommandHandlerTests`' sub-minute-session test was inverted from "preserves fractional minutes" to "floors to zero." Full suite green at every stage before expanding to the next piece, per the Design Gap Protocol's headlight-management discipline — Domain method, then classifier rewrite, then window-level flooring (caught by re-running tests, not anticipated up front), then the `GetNenContractsForDateRangeQueryHandler` consolidation, each verified before the next began.

**Principle demonstrated:** the architect's own instinct ("does this really need second-level precision?") turned out to be the load-bearing insight the whole session converged on — not a tolerance patch layered over fragile precision, but removing the precision that was fragile in the first place, at its one true source (`Session.GetElapsedTime`). Two separate follow-up audits — "is everything really one source of truth now?" asked twice, a turn apart — each caught a real remaining gap (the window-level flooring miss, then the inline `DailyFloor` bypass) that a single design pass hadn't anticipated; both were exactly the kind of thing headlight management is supposed to catch by verifying rather than assuming completion.

---

## Gap 58 — ViewModel Forbidden Zone Audit: `HatsuPageViewModel`'s Live Countdown Math, Three Independently Hand-Rolled Label Formatters, and Five Inline-Formatted Validation Messages *(closed, 2026-09-07)*

**A third "is everything really one source of truth now?" follow-up, this time asked about the whole ViewModel layer rather than one query handler** — the architect asked directly whether any other ViewModel was computing something the Application layer should own, after Gap 57 had already caught two such gaps in the crediting redesign itself. Audited every ViewModel in the project against `MODULE_STANDARDS.md`'s own "ViewModel Forbidden Zone" rules (No Timers, No Math, No String Formatting) rather than guessing, using targeted greps for `Math.*`, `TimeSpan`/`DateTime` arithmetic, and `:`-format string interpolation, then judging each hit against the rule's actual intent — not every hit was a real violation, and the report distinguished them explicitly before any code changed.

**Found and fixed, three categories, all confirmed by the architect before touching code:**

1. **Real violations — `HatsuPageViewModel` (the Ritual's Hatsu step), the same shape as `DayFloorPreviewViewModel.IsMet`'s bug earlier in this session.** `TotalMinutes` computed `Math.Max(1, (int)(PlannedSchedule!.End - _clock.UtcNow).TotalMinutes)` inline — banned `TimeSpan` arithmetic plus a clamp, deriving "how much of this block is left right now" directly in the ViewModel. `PlannedDurationLabel` divided/modulo'd raw minutes and hand-built "1 h"/"30 min"/"1 h 30 min" strings — worse than a bare rule violation, since it was a **near-duplicate** of the already-existing `MinutesToStringConverter.FormatMinutes` (built in Gap 55 specifically so every "Xh Ym" display in the app formats identically), independently reinvented with slightly different output. Fixed: `FocusBlockSchedule` (Application) gained `RemainingMinutes(DateTime asOf)`, a pure method mirroring the same justification as `FocusBlock.RawDeficitMinutes`/`CanContributeOwnDeficit` — the ViewModel now asks, never derives. `PlannedDurationLabel`/`PlannedTimeRangeLabel` were deleted outright; `HatsuPageView.axaml` now binds `PlannedSchedule.TotalMinutes` through `MinutesToStringConverter` directly and two new raw `DateTime?` properties (`PlannedRangeStartLocal`/`PlannedRangeEndLocal` — `.ToLocalTime()` only, a data conversion not a format, mirroring `RitualShellViewModel`'s existing precedent for the same distinction) through `DateTimeToStringConverter`.

2. **Borderline, reported as "probably low-drift-risk formatting" — turned out to be a real triplicate.** `ContractPresetCardViewModel.FloorLabel`/`WorkHoursLabel`, `ContractRulesStepViewModel.SelectedPresetFloorLabel`/`SelectedPresetWorkHoursLabel`, and `ContractWizardViewModel`'s Review-step summary strings had each independently hand-rolled the identical "1.5h / day" / "No constraint" formatting — one of the three's own code comment even said "mirrors the exact formatting ContractWizardViewModel's own... strings already use," confirming it had been copied by eye rather than shared. Consolidated into two new static formatters, `DailyFloorToLabelConverter.FormatFloor`/`WorkHoursRangeToLabelConverter.FormatRange` — same "plain static entry point a ViewModel calls directly" idiom `MinutesToStringConverter.FormatMinutes` already established, chosen deliberately over introducing Avalonia `MultiBinding`/`IMultiValueConverter` (no precedent for it anywhere in this codebase, and a two-value XAML converter would have been a new mechanism for a low-stakes cleanup).

3. **Gray area, confirmed still worth fixing — `PlannerPageViewModel`'s live client-side validation messages.** Five spots (block-overlap, split-dialog Work-Hours, redemption date-range ×2, draft-placement date-range and Work-Hours) each inline-formatted a `DateTime`/`DateOnly`/`TimeOnly` value into a validation sentence. No drift risk in the strict sense (nothing here derives a *fact* that could disagree with the Application layer — it's describing already-known user input or an already-fetched Work Hours window), but still the letter of "no `ToString(format)`/`string.Format` for display" and still duplicated formatting code. Two more static formatters added (`DateRangeToLabelConverter`, `DateTimeRangeToLabelConverter`) to round out the set; all five call sites switched to them. One message's punctuation changed as a side effect — "overlaps an existing block from 09:00 – 10:00." read oddly once the shared formatter's en-dash replaced "to," so it became "...block (09:00 – 10:00)." instead, a deliberate small rewrite, not an oversight.

**Explicitly ruled *not* violations, reported as such rather than silently fixed or silently ignored:** `PlannerDragMath`, `PlannerLayout`, `PlannerEndTimeOptions`, `PlannerTimeOptionLookup`, `PlannerTimeShiftMath`, `PlannerDropdownWindow`, and the `FormEndTime = $"{...}:{...}"`-style textbox-state assignments scattered through `PlannerPageViewModel` and `ContractPresetPageViewModel`. All of these are pure UI-interaction mechanics — drag-to-time snapping, pixel-height layout, a time-picker dropdown's own option list, a text field's own edit-state serialization — deciding nothing business-meaningful and carrying no risk of disagreeing with the Application layer about a fact. `PlannerDragMath` in particular already has explicit sanctioned status in `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md`'s own technical mapping table ("Pure static helper").

**Follow-up, same day:** `DayFloorPreviewViewModel.Label`/`BonusLabel` — flagged above as spotted-but-deferred — were confirmed and fixed in a direct follow-up. Both had the same `$"{CurrentHours:0.#}h / {RequiredFloorHours.Value:0.#}h"`-style inline decimal-hours formatting this whole gap was about; `Label`'s branching logic (which of "Rest"/one-number/two-number shape to show) stayed in the ViewModel, since choosing *which already-known value to display* isn't deriving a new fact — only the actual number-to-string conversion moved out, into a new `HoursToStringConverter` (`Convert`/static `FormatHours(double)`, same idiom as every other formatter this gap introduced). No existing converter fit: `MinutesToStringConverter` produces the compound "Xh Ym" breakdown, not the plain "{0.#}h" this badge needed.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1242 passing, unchanged count throughout both passes — this touched only ViewModels, Views, and small Application/Converter additions, no test coverage gap opened or closed).**

**Principle demonstrated:** asking "is this now really the only place this is computed" a third time, about a different layer than the previous two asks, found a genuine, unrelated class of drift (three independently-typed copies of the same label logic) that neither of the first two follow-ups would have surfaced, since they were scoped to the crediting math specifically. The Forbidden Zone rule's own worked examples (`IsActive`/`EffectiveEnd`, `CanContributeOwnDeficit`, `IsAtLeastAsStrict`) were all Application-layer findings — this is the first pass that applied the identical audit discipline one layer up, to ViewModels, and found the same shape of problem waiting there too.

## Gap 59 — Open Gap L Closed: Contract Completion Grading, `GetNenContractCompletionReadinessQuery`, Work-Hours-Aware `StartsAt`/`EndsAt`, and the Post-Contract Recap Page *(closed, 2026-09-08)*

**Finished the remaining pieces of Open Gap L in one continuous slice run — readiness query, Work-Hours boundary, then the full Post-Contract UI — verifying the full suite between each.** Everything below was still listed as "not yet built" in `ARCHITECT_GOVERNANCE.md`'s Open Gap L write-up going into this session; the design itself had already been fully interrogated, so this was pure build-out against a settled plan, not new design work.

**`OriginalEndDate` added to `NenContract`, frozen at Seal exactly like `CommittedFocusHours`.** Needed because `Break()` now shrinks `EndDate` itself (below) — `OriginalEndDate` preserves "how big was the original commitment" for history/recap purposes regardless of what actually happened. Set once in `Seal()`, never touched again.

**`Break()` redesigned to shrink `EndDate` to the actual break date, clamped to never go below `StartDate`.** Settled directly with the architect as the fix for a real edge case surfaced while reasoning through this gap: a contract broken early used to keep its full original `EndDate`, meaning its date range could still block a brand-new contract from starting on a day that, in reality, was never going to be part of it. `CheckNenContractDateRangeOverlapQueryHandler` was switched from `GetAllInEffectAsync()` to `GetAllAsync()` at the same time — a Completed/Broken contract must still block a same-day start against its real (now-correct) range, which `GetAllInEffectAsync()`'s scope wouldn't have covered.

**`GetNenContractCompletionReadinessQuery`/Handler built** exactly as specified — composes `INenContractRepository`, `IPlannedBlockRepository`, and `GetNenContractCompletedFocusHoursQuery`, returning `NenContractCompletionReadinessDto { IsReady, RemainingHours, PendingBlockCount }`. `CompleteNenContractValidator` was refactored to a thin wrapper delegating to it — its old duplicated pending-block scan (a hand-rolled range query plus a `ContractId`/date-window match) is gone entirely. The Planner's `CanCompleteActiveContract`/`CanCompleteSelectedContract` bind to this same query directly, so the button's visibility rule and the command's gate can never drift apart, as designed.

**`StartsAt`/`EndsAt` added to `NenContract` exactly per the sketched implementation** — Work-Hours-aware instant boundaries, falling back to midnight/end-of-day when no policy applies. All four call sites that used to resolve Bonus-block eligibility via a date-truncated comparison (`GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandler`, `GetNenContractBonusFocusHoursQueryHandler`, `GetNenContractRecapQueryHandler`, `CompleteNenContractValidator`'s pending-block check) switched to the direct `DateTime` comparison against `StartsAt`/`EndsAt`, as designed — a real behavior change for a Bonus block placed outside Work Hours on the contract's first/last day, not a pure refactor.

**Post-Contract page built, mirroring Post-Session's structure throughout:** `PostContractViewModel`/`PostContractView.axaml` (header, date-range/hours/grade/streak summary, a day-strip block breakdown, reflection text box, Done command wired to `SaveNenContractReflectionCommand` → `AcknowledgePostContractCommand`). `ShellViewModel` gained `IsPostContractActive`/`PostContractViewModel`, a `PendingPostContract` case in the existing overlay queue (enqueued behind pending Post-Sessions), a live path (`PlannerPageViewModel.PostContractReady` firing directly off a Planner-triggered Complete/Break) and a passive path (`ISessionStateService.PostContractsReady`, raised by `NenContractHeartbeatService` after its sweep resolves recaps for any contract it completed unattended). `MainWindow.axaml`'s sidebar/content visibility switched from `!IsPostSessionActive` alone to a new `IsMainContentVisible` computed property covering both overlays, with a new full-width `ContentControl` for the Post-Contract overlay itself. The Planner gained a "Complete Contract" button both in the Nen Contract popover and, since the architect found the popover placement non-obvious during manual testing, next to Cover Deficit in the main banner — both bound to the same `CanCompleteActiveContract`/`CanCompleteSelectedContract` readiness state.

**Two small additions to `NenContractRecapDto` needed for the recap page to have anything to show:** `DailyBreakdown` (`IReadOnlyList<NenContractDailyRecapEntryDto>`, one entry per contract day with `IsRestDay`/`Percentage`) and `BestStreakDays`, both computed inside `GetNenContractRecapQueryHandler` using the already-built `NenContract.PercentOfFloorMet` (Open Gap C1).

**Unrelated feature bundled into the same pass at the architect's request, since it touched the same Planner popover code:** clicking a contract in the Nen Contract popover now navigates the week view to that contract's first scheduled day (`PlannerPageViewModel.GoToContractStartAsync`).

**Bugs found and fixed during the architect's own manual testing of this build, all in debug-only seed data (`HostExtensions.SeedDebugTestContracts`), none in application code:**
- Three seeded contracts shared the literal name "Focus Commitment" (their shared preset's name), making it impossible to tell which one a banner reading "0h/0h" referred to — each contract given a distinct name.
- Two future-dated contracts had blocks pre-seeded as already completed, with completion timestamps in the future relative to real `UtcNow` — stripped back to trivial/uncompleted, since a future-dated completion can't happen for real.
- "Hours Remaining" (a 7×4h-floor contract) declared `plannedFocusHoursByDay` without creating matching real `FocusBlock` rows for most of those days — every day's badge showed 0h despite the banner claiming 28h total. Traced to `DayFloorPreviewViewModel.ShowSimplifiedFuture` relying on a real app invariant (`SealNenContractCommandHandler` always derives a day's declared floor and its real blocks from the same `IContractBlockBatchPreparer.Prepare()` call, so a future day's real scheduled hours can never fall below its floor) that the seed code alone violated. Confirmed this needed no new *application* protection — the invariant already exists structurally in the real Seal path; only the seed code had to start honoring the same discipline. Fixed by adding real (uncompleted) blocks for all seven days, matching the declared plan exactly.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1284 passing, up from 1242 at Gap 58's close — new coverage for `OriginalEndDate`/`Break`'s `EndDate` shrink, `StartsAt`/`EndsAt`, `PercentOfFloorMet`, `DailyFloor.PercentComplete`, and `GetNenContractCompletionReadinessQueryHandler`). Full solution builds with 0 warnings. The remaining reported issue during manual testing — a block's End-time field becoming locked in the edit form after a *different* block's Ritual was completed — was root-caused to the architect's own use of the debug clock (Ctrl+Shift+T) to jump time forward mid-testing: `PolicyEngine.ComputeSlice`'s "which Focus block is currently due" resolution silently moved on to the next block the instant the manipulated clock crossed into its window, so a Ritual run believing it targeted the earlier block actually called `MarkRitualCompleted` on the later one. Confirmed a testing artifact of the debug clock, not an application defect — wall-clock time cannot retroactively jump across a block boundary the way a manual clock override can, and `PolicyEngineTests.cs` already covers real time-progression correctness for exactly this kind of stale/active-block resolution. No code change required; the affected row was left for a database reset rather than hand-patched.

## Gap 60 — Ending a Session Early Didn't Stop Enforcement: `PolicyEngine` Trusted an Over-Fetched Preset List, Then an Audit Found the Same Drift in Two More Places *(closed, 2026-09-10)*

**Reported as "I ended a Planned Block early and it's still blocking."** Live-DB inspection (`%AppData%\NenFocus\NenFocus.db`) showed the data was already correct — the session's `Status` was `Completed`, and `FocusBlock.RitualCompletedAt`/`SessionEndedAt` were both properly stamped by `EndSessionCommandHandler`, well before the block's original scheduled `End`. `SessionEndedEventHandler` was also confirmed to call `RemoveEnforcementAsync` synchronously, awaited inside `EfCoreUnitOfWork.CommitAsync` — enforcement removal isn't purely heartbeat-dependent, as first assumed. Yet the hosts file stayed blocked. A controlled repro against the real running app — a debug `FocusBlock` row inserted directly into the live DB, `ApplyPolicyAsync` allowed to fire naturally, then `RitualCompletedAt`/`SessionEndedAt` stamped exactly as an early end would — reproduced it deterministically: every single heartbeat tick kept calling `ApplyPolicyAsync` and reapplying the same blocked sites, for as long as "now" stayed inside the block's *original* `[Start, End)` window, regardless of `SessionEndedAt`.

**The gap:** `ComputeActivePolicySliceQueryHandler` resolves `presetIds` from its own `blocks` list — filtered only by `CancelledAt`, using the DB's raw `Start`/`End` range query — and hands the resolved `BlockPreset` entities to `PolicyEngine.ComputeSlice` as `presets`. Inside `ComputeSlice`, `activeBlocks` is correctly narrowed via `IsActive(at)` (which defers to `FocusBlock.EffectiveEnd`, accounting for `SessionEndedAt`) — but `activePresets` was just `presets.ToList()`, trusted unfiltered. So a block that had genuinely gone inactive (excluded from `activeBlocks`) still had its preset's blocked sites/apps folded into the computed policy, because nothing re-checked whether the preset actually belonged to a block or session still in `activeBlocks`/`activeSessions`. The code's own comment claimed `IsActive` was "the authoritative boundary check... independent of whatever window the caller's own block query used" — true for `ActiveFocusBlockId`, false for `BlockedSites`/`BlockedApps`. Two independent "is this active" computations were feeding one policy, and they disagreed.

**The closure:** `PolicyEngine.ComputeSlice` now derives `activePresetIds` from `activeBlocks`' and `activeSessions`' own `BlockPresetId`s, and filters the caller's `presets` down to that set before using them — the same "coarse fetch, precise in-layer narrow" discipline `PlannedBlockRepository.GetOverlappingAsync` already applies via `EffectiveEnd`, just not yet applied to presets. One authoritative "what's active" computation now governs everything the slice returns, not two.

**Audited for the same drift elsewhere, at the architect's request, before applying the fix.** `PlannedBlockRepository.GetOverlappingAsync` and `StartSessionCommandHandler` were both already correct (narrow via `EffectiveEnd`). But `GetNenContractCompletionReadinessQueryHandler`'s `PendingBlockCount` and `BreakNenContractCommandHandler`'s cancellation-eligibility loop both used raw `HasEnded` (`End < asOf`) to ask "has this block concluded" — the exact same staleness bug, one layer up: a `FocusBlock` that ended its session early would keep counting as "pending" (blocking contract-completion readiness) and remain eligible for `MarkCancelled` (mislabeling its real `EndedEarly`/`CompletedOnTime`/`StartedLate` outcome as "Cancelled" in Insights/recap) until its original scheduled `End` arrived.

**A fourth "is this ended" concept was considered and rejected.** The obvious fix — a new `PlannedBlock.HasEffectivelyEnded(asOf)`, mirroring `HasEnded` but via `EffectiveEnd` — was rejected on two grounds. First, it would have added a fourth parallel "has this concluded" concept to a codebase that already has three for genuinely different reasons: `IsActive`/`EffectiveEnd` (is this live right now — enforcement, scheduling overlap), `HasEnded` (has the calendar slot passed — Planner edit/delete permission, session-start eligibility), and `IFocusBlockOutcomeClassifier.Classify` (has this block's story been permanently resolved — already the established source for `GetNenContractRecapQueryHandler`, `GetNenContractDeficitsQueryHandler`, `GetNenContractCompletedFocusHoursQueryHandler`, and five other handlers). The two buggy call sites were really asking the third question, not inventing a new one. Second, redefining `HasEnded` itself to be `EffectiveEnd`-aware — the maximally "unified" option — was checked and found unsafe: for a `FocusBlock` whose ritual was never completed, `EffectiveEnd` collapses to `Start` exactly at raw `End` (freeing the slot for a genuinely Missed block), which would silently flip `HasEnded`'s exact-boundary answer and break `StartSessionCommandHandlerTests`'s deliberately-tested "block ends exactly now, still allowed" case — a real, separate edge case nobody asked to reopen.

**Chosen instead: `PlannedBlockResolutionExtensions.IsResolved(this PlannedBlock, IFocusBlockOutcomeClassifier, DateTime asOf)`**, a small extension reusing the *existing* single source of truth rather than adding a new one — for a `FocusBlock` it defers to `Classify(...) is not null` (a permanent fact once ritual/session data exists, not a "right now" check); for anything else (`BackgroundBlock`) it falls back to the unchanged `HasEnded`, at the architect's explicit direction — a `BackgroundBlock` has no ritual/session to account for and isn't getting outcome-classified for now. `IFocusBlockOutcomeClassifier` stays honestly named and strictly Focus-only; the polymorphic composition lives in its own file rather than being smuggled into that interface. Both handlers now call the one shared method instead of two independently-drifting reimplementations of the same question.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1287 passing, up from 1284 at Gap 59's close — three new regression tests for the exact scenarios found, plus fixture fixes to nine pre-existing `PolicyEngineTests` that had never actually wired `BlockPresetId` between their test blocks/sessions and presets, since the bug meant it never mattered before). Full solution builds with 0 warnings.**

**Principle demonstrated:** "one source of truth" doesn't mean collapsing every related concept into a single check — `IsActive`, `HasEnded`, and `Classify` are three legitimately different questions sharing the same underlying timestamps, and forcing them into one would have reopened a different, already-tested edge case. The actual violation was narrower and more specific: two call sites asking a question that already had a canonical answer elsewhere in the codebase, computing their own instead. The fix was to point them at the existing authority, not to build a new one.

## Gap 61 — BreakTimerPreset: Pomodoro-Style Timer Presets, Retiring Manual Break-Taking and Closing Open Gap I *(closed, 2026-09-12)*

**The feature:** Three built-in timer presets (Classic 25/5, Deep Work 50/10, Flow 90/20 — each also carrying a "cycles before long break" count and a long-break length), selectable at Hatsu for both Spontaneous and Planned sessions, replacing the old free-form break-interval/length picker entirely. A session's break cadence now cycles automatically — work, short break, work, short break, ..., long break, repeating — for as long as the session runs, with no manual "Take Break" click anywhere in the app.

**Modeled like BlockPreset/ContractPreset (own aggregate root); deliberately not like ContractPreset/NenContract's two-entity split.** `BreakTimerPreset` (Name + a `BreakTimer` value object: `WorkMinutes`/`ShortBreakMinutes`/`CyclesBeforeLongBreak`/`LongBreakMinutes`) is seeded with Classic/Deep Work/Flow as three ordinary rows — no protected/built-in status, freely editable and deletable, matching the "not a special hardcoded case" framing the feature was scoped under from the start. Unlike `ContractPreset` → `NenContract`, no second "instance" entity was needed: `Session` already *is* that instance — its own identity, its own lifecycle — so it simply snapshots `BreakTimer` by value at Start. Editing or deleting the source preset later never affects a session already running or already in history.

**The Deep Module: one pure calculation everything else reads.** `IBreakScheduleCalculator.ComputeSchedule(BreakTimer, TimeSpan totalDuration)` produces the complete ordered phase timeline (`Work`/`ShortBreak`/`LongBreak`, each with a `[Start, End)` offset) for the whole session up front, since total duration is fixed and known the moment the session starts. A break is only ever included if it can run to completion *and* leaves real Work time after it — one that would be cut short, or that lands exactly on the session's own end with nothing left to work on, is skipped, folding the remainder into the surrounding Work phase instead. That single rule, expressed once, answers three different questions for three different callers: which presets Hatsu even offers for the current duration (`GetAvailableBreakTimerPresetsQuery` — hidden unless the schedule contains at least one real break, re-queried live as the duration wheel changes), when `ActiveSessionMonitor` should auto-fire the next work→break or break→work transition, and where the Main Page's progress-bar tick marks land (long breaks rendered visually distinct from short ones).

**Manual break-taking retired app-wide — this is what closes Open Gap I.** `StartBreakCommand`'s `UserInitiated` flag, `MainPageViewModel.TakeBreak()`, and the "Take a Break" button are all gone; `ActiveSessionMonitor` is now the sole caller of `StartBreakCommand`, symmetric with its pre-existing auto-resume-from-break behavior. This closes Open Gap I's dead-code writeup via a third path neither of that gap's two proposed directions considered — not "wire up the abandoned reminder-toast design" and not "delete the dead code, keep manual-only" — breaks became fully automatic instead, and the concept of a break with no structure behind it was dropped entirely. `BreakSettings` (a single fixed interval/length pair, the old shape) is deleted outright, replaced by `BreakTimer`.

**Enforcement can now pause during a break — a new `PolicyEngine` merge rule, not a bolt-on.** `PolicyEngine.ComputeSlice` gained an optional `contracts` parameter, used solely to resolve whether a Focus Block committed to a sealed Nen Contract should keep enforcing through its own Session's breaks. Precedence: the not-yet-built Settings page's eventual global default is the strictness ceiling (hardcoded permissive for now, since Settings doesn't exist yet); a Contract Preset's own `PauseEnforcementDuringBreak` field can only ever narrow that default toward stricter, never loosen it — mirroring `BlockPreset.IsAtLeastAsStrict`'s existing tighten-only precedent. A Bonus Block (`ContractId == null`, even one whose `Start` falls inside a contract's date range) always follows the global default alone, never a contract's setting, since it was never actually committed to that contract — the same reasoning Open Gap G already established for why Bonus hours don't count toward the floor either. Only the specific block/session whose break is in progress loses enforcement; a merely co-occurring Background Block is untouched.

**Interrogated at every branch point before any code was written**, per the Design Gap Protocol: the total-duration/open-ended session-length question (settled on "fixed duration, same source as today, cadence runs inside it" — unifies Planned and Spontaneous with zero special-casing, since a Planned session's duration was already externally fixed); the "dead-air break" edge case (a break must leave real work on both sides, not just fit); where Timer Preset selection belongs relative to a Planned session's already-locked Block Preset (Hatsu, every time — the same bucket break reminders already occupied, not locked at scheduling time, since it never affects enforcement before the Ritual completes); and the pause-during-break precedence chain above, including the Bonus Block question.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1292 passing) plus manual verification in the running app** — automatic work→break and break→work transitions, duration-based preset filtering, enforcement actually lifting during a Spontaneous session's break, and the progress-bar tick markers, all verified using the Debug Clock's new arbitrary-minutes jump (`AdvanceCustomMinutes`, added specifically to make a 25+ minute work period testable without waiting it out in real time).

**Two real bugs found and fixed during the build itself, not just design review:** binding `DebugClockViewModel.CustomMinutes` as a non-nullable `int` against `NumericUpDown.Value` (actually `decimal?`) crashed with a `NullReferenceException` unboxing `null` — fixed by matching the property's type to the control's own type exactly rather than assuming a "reasonable" .NET type would bind cleanly. And the Hatsu Timer Preset chips never highlighted when selected, because comparing "this chip's own item" against "the ViewModel's selected item" across an `ItemsControl` boundary in XAML is inherently fragile — fixed by having the ViewModel pair each preset with its own `IsSelected` flag (`BreakTimerPresetChip`) recomputed on every selection change, so the chip's active-state binding is a plain single-value binding on its own `DataContext`, the same pattern the static Hours/Minutes chips already used.

**Principle demonstrated:** a Deep Module's value shows up hardest when a new UI need arrives *after* the domain design is settled — the request for progress-bar break markers didn't need a single new domain concept, because `IBreakScheduleCalculator`'s phase timeline was already the answer; it just needed a third reader (rendered via `MainPageView.axaml.cs` code-behind building proportional `Grid` columns, since Avalonia can't data-bind a `ColumnDefinitions` collection directly).

## Gap 62 — Enforcement Synchronizer: Breaks and Session End No Longer Drop All Blocking; the Ritual Question Gets Its Own Home *(closed, 2026-09-25)*

**The gap:** `BreakStartedEventHandler` called `IEnforcementService.RemoveEnforcementAsync()` — clearing *all* blocking the moment a break began. `PolicyEngine` (Gap 61) already knew exactly what a break should lift — only the on-break session's own preset, and only when its contract's `PauseEnforcementDuringBreak` allows — but the handler overrode that answer for up to one heartbeat tick (≤5s): a co-occurring Background Block and a contract that forbids pausing were both briefly unblocked. `SessionEndedEventHandler` had the identical bug (a Background Block still running was unblocked when a Focus session ended). And a break *ending* raised no event at all, so re-blocking waited for the next heartbeat. Root cause, found while designing the fix: four different places each decided what to block — the heartbeat, session start, session end, break start — at different moments, and three of them bypassed `PolicyEngine` entirely.

**Options interrogated, in order, each rejected for a more DDD one:**
- *Delete the `RemoveEnforcementAsync` call and let the heartbeat fix it* — correct but leaves the ≤5s delay; rejected because the architect wanted instant switching.
- *Each handler asks `PolicyEngine` and applies* (mirroring the old `SessionStartedEventHandler`) — DDD-correct, but a hidden race remained: `WindowsEnforcementService`'s lock serializes *apply*, not *compute + apply*, so a heartbeat that computed just before a break was saved could apply its stale slice on top of the break handler's fresh one.
- *A `ReconcileEnforcement` command* — satisfied the literal CQRS rule, but needed a `static` lock inside a per-call handler.
- **Chosen: one Application service, `IEnforcementSynchronizer.SyncAsync(DateTime at)`** — asks `PolicyEngine` (via `IQueryDispatcher`, a fresh scope per call, Gap 40) and applies or removes the result as one locked step; singleton so every caller shares the lock. Named "Synchronizer" rather than "Reconciler" because it doesn't diff actual vs desired state, only computes and applies. `ARCHITECT_GOVERNANCE.md`'s CQRS rule gained an explicit exception for this shape: an Application service that only coordinates a Domain decision into an outbound port, never serving UI reads.

**Wiring:** the heartbeat, `SessionStartedEventHandler`, `SessionEndedEventHandler`, `BreakStartedEventHandler`, and a new `BreakEndedEventHandler` all call `SyncAsync(now)`; none touch `IEnforcementService` directly any more. `Session.ResumeFromBreak(DateTime resumedAt)` now raises the new `BreakEndedEvent` (time passed in — the Domain never reads the clock); its handler also sends a "Break over — Back to focus." notification. The heartbeat's undocumented "slice is empty but a session exists, so don't remove" guard (present since the initial commit) was dropped — it was the reason a break had depended on the buggy remove-everything handler at all.

**Second half — the Ritual question moved out of the Policy Slice.** Deleting `ActivePolicySlice.FocusSessionCompleted` looked safe (after Gaps 32/60, `FocusBlock.EffectiveEnd` already drops a finished block from the slice), but tracing it showed the flag's real remaining job was the *Ritual prompt*: in a millisecond race at a block's end (heartbeat reads `now` just before `End`, the session completes at `End`), the heartbeat would see "Focus block active, no session" and re-prompt the Ritual for a block already done. Rather than keep the flag, `ActivePolicySlice` was narrowed to purely "what to block": `ActiveBlockPresetType`, `ActiveFocusBlockId`, and `FocusSessionCompleted` were all removed. The Ritual question now has its own home — `FocusBlock.IsAwaitingRitual(at)` (entity rule: active, not cancelled, Ritual not done) and `GetFocusBlockAwaitingRitualQuery(At)` (that block, only if no session is running — read from the database, not `ISessionStateService`'s in-memory copy, so both halves of the answer share one source). This moves where Gap 2's decision lives, not its principle: the answer is still a domain concept (which block), not a leaky boolean — and the deleted flag was itself exactly the kind of boolean Gap 2 warned against.

**Known and accepted:** a Focus Block whose preset no longer exists now prompts the Ritual (the old heartbeat skipped it because its slice was empty); the Ritual can't load the locked preset so it opens in unlocked mode. Unreachable through the app — `DeleteBlockPresetValidator` refuses deletion while any block using the preset hasn't ended — so left as-is rather than building UI for it. Also out of scope, flagged: `App.axaml.cs`'s shutdown path still removes enforcement unless a *Focus* block is active, ignoring Background Blocks — the same "decide outside `PolicyEngine`" smell, but shutdown behavior belongs to Open Gap K.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1308 passing):** 6 `EnforcementSynchronizerTests` (the concurrency test was verified to fail with the lock removed), 5 `FocusBlock.IsAwaitingRitual` tests, 9 `GetFocusBlockAwaitingRitualQueryHandlerTests` (two scenarios moved from `PolicyEngineTests`), a `PolicyEngine` guard test for a co-occurring Background Block surviving an early-ended Focus session, `BreakEndedEvent` tests on `Session` and `ResumeFromBreakCommandHandler`, and all four event handlers' tests rewritten against the synchronizer. Nine `PolicyEngineTests` that only asserted the deleted slice fields were removed.

**Principle demonstrated:** a bug that looks local ("this handler removes too much") was a symptom of a missing single owner — the same "one authority" principle Gap 2 and Gap 5 applied to *deciding* policy, now applied to *applying* it. And a value object that answers two unrelated questions accumulates workaround flags; splitting the questions deleted the flag instead of relocating it.


## Gap 63 — Time-Driven Breaks: a Session Owns Its `BreakSchedule`; a Break Is Never a Status *(closed, 2026-09-27)*

**The gap — four bugs from manual testing, one root cause.** (1) Skipping to 17:33 into a 17:30–17:35 break started a fresh 5-minute break. (2) Skipping during a break left the preset's sites unblocked. (3) At the end of a long break the timer sat at 00:00 until the session ended. (4) A 2h30m session recorded 2h31m. Root cause of 1–3: break timing had two clocks — `ActiveSessionMonitor` decided a break *starts* from the Gap 61 phase timeline, but *ends* at `BreakStartedAt` + length, and stored `OnBreak` status was a third copy. Resuming was one-shot: `_breakStartedAt` was cleared *before* `ResumeFromBreakCommand` succeeded, and a failed or `Fail`-returning resume was never retried, stranding the session `OnBreak` at 00:00. Root cause of 4: `EndSessionCommandHandler` stamped `EndedAt = now`, uncapped, so an end noticed late (sleep, restart, clock jump) ran past the plan. None depended on the debug clock — sleep and restarts reach all four in real use. (The missing break notifications reported alongside turned out to be Windows Do Not Disturb, not a bug; toast failures are now logged instead of swallowed.)

**Options interrogated, in order:**
- *Patch the monitor* (retry the resume, clamp the start) — keeps three sources of truth; rejected.
- *Monitor as reconciler over the schedule* (A) — rule lives in an Infrastructure timer class; rejected.
- *`Session.SyncPhase(at)` reconciling a stored `OnBreak` to the clock* (B2) — DDD-correct but keeps redundant state.
- *Application reconciler service* (C) — one aggregate's rule living outside it; rejected.
- **Chosen — the architect's own framing: a session is one continuous run punctuated by breaks, and once its parameters are set it "just knows" when they are.** No break status at all; `Session.IsOnBreakAt(at)` reads the schedule against the clock. An initial objection (edges need stored state) was withdrawn: the notification edge needs only the monitor's in-memory cursor, not domain state.

**Shape decisions, each interrogated:**
- *Store the timetable, not the rhythm.* First proposed: store nothing and recompute from the frozen `BreakTimer`. Revised when the architect asked about a future per-session custom schedule ("15 at 0:50, 5 at 1:35") — not expressible as a rhythm. So the session stores a `BreakSchedule` and never knows how it was produced; `BreakTimer.ScheduleFor(runLength)` is one producer. This also made a separate completion snapshot unnecessary: the stored list, trimmed by `Complete`, *is* the history — satisfying the architect's rule that Insights history is always a snapshot, never recomputed from rules that could change.
- *Offsets from `StartedAt`, not clock times* — a schedule exists before Start, stays reusable, and validates against the run length. A late planned start moves the breaks with it (architect: starting "inside" a break would make no sense).
- *`BreakTimer` / `BreakSchedule` stay value objects, `BreakTimerPreset` stays the entity*; names unchanged (`BreakTimer` for the preset would have given one word two meanings).
- *Calculation moved onto `BreakTimer`* rather than passing `IBreakScheduleCalculator` into the entity; the calculator, `SessionPhase` and their DI registration are deleted. The rule previously had **no tests at all**; it now has 8.
- *Notifications:* `Session.BreakTransitionsBetween(from, to)` reports crossed boundaries; the monitor (cursor only) passes them to `IBreakTransitionHandler`, which does one `SyncAsync` and announces only the latest boundary. A notification is not a consequence of unblocking (a no-pause contract or a Background Block would silence it), so both come from the schedule, not from each other. Windows scheduled toasts were rejected — a second copy of the timetable held by the OS.
- *Preset name snapshotted* as `BreakPresetName`; null when the schedule turned out empty (a preset that stopped fitting between Hatsu and Start — the session still starts).
- *Session end capped* in `Session.Complete` itself, so every caller gets it.

**Removed:** `SessionStatus.OnBreak`, `StartBreak`/`ResumeFromBreak` (entity methods and command slices), `BreakStartedEvent`/`BreakEndedEvent` and their handlers (the latter two only a day old, from Gap 62), `SyncState` and the shutdown hook that called it, `BreakTimerDto`, and the Main Page's "Resume Early" button with its styles — the architect chose to drop it rather than rebuild it now and parked it with skip-a-break in Open Gap P. **Migration** `TimeDrivenBreakSchedule`: adds `SessionBreaks` and `BreakPresetName`, drops the old break columns, turns `OnBreak` rows into `Active`; deletes no rows (old sessions simply show no breaks — the architect doesn't need that test data).

**Known trade-off, accepted:** changing breaks mid-session (end early, skip, budget, extend) now needs an explicit schedule revision instead of falling out of a state machine. Each was checked to fit, and all are parked in **Open Gap P**. Moving the system clock also moves breaks — Open Gap J's territory, like every other clock-driven rule.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1344 passing):** `BreakTimerTests` (8) and `BreakScheduleTests` (22) for the rule and the value object; `SessionTests` rewritten around the schedule, including the capped-end regression; `PolicyEngineTests` now prove pause and re-block with no state change; `BreakTransitionHandlerTests` (5); StartSession/EndSession handler tests for late planned starts, "No breaks", mid-break end and the capped end; two real-SQLite round-trip tests for the owned `SessionBreaks` collection, including replacement on trim.

**Principle demonstrated:** a value that can be derived must not also be stored as state — three copies of "is it a break" is where all four bugs lived. And separate *what* a thing is from *how it was decided*: the session knows its breaks, not the preset that proposed them, which is what leaves room for every Gap P feature.

## Gap 64 — "Keep Blocking During Breaks": a Settable Contract Rule, Copied onto the Session *(closed, 2026-09-27)*

**The gap:** Gap 61 gave `ContractPreset` and `NenContract` a `PauseEnforcementDuringBreak` flag that `PolicyEngine` honoured, but nothing could set it — and three code paths silently reset it even if something had: `UpdateContractPresetCommandHandler` never passed it (so `ContractPreset.Update`'s default of `true` reset it on every edit), and `SealNenContractCommandHandler` / `EditNenContractCommandHandler` never passed it to `NenContract.Seal` (every contract sealed, and every edited contract re-sealed, as "pause allowed"). `RedemptionPlacementPolicy` already travelled the exact path needed (Presets page → preset → DTO → wizard → Seal/Edit → contract → edit DTO), so the flag follows it.

**Decisions, each interrogated:**
- *Where it's set:* on the Contract Preset only, as a checkbox on the Presets page. The Advanced wizard applies the preset as-is and shows the rule read-only — keeping the 2026-08-21 rule that the wizard only picks, never edits. Rejected: a per-contract override in the wizard (it would be the first field to break that rule).
- *Simple contracts* (Lockdown / Daily Flex / Period Study) all get the permissive value, like every other non-strictness rule Simple sets. Rejected: making Lockdown keep blocking (the only Simple option with a baked-in extra rule, while it still allows cancellation).
- *Contract editing* needed no decision: only a Scheduled contract can be edited, so the flag changes freely before a contract starts and is fixed once it is Active.
- *Name and polarity:* renamed to `KeepBlockingDuringBreaks` (default `false`) everywhere, so the domain, database and UI use the user's own words. Rejected: keeping the domain name and inverting only in the UI (two names for one concept).
- *Tell the user:* yes — the break notification and the Main Page break panel both say "Blocking stays on (contract rule)". Otherwise a break that doesn't lift blocking looks like a bug.
- *Where the answer lives:* options were a shared Domain service (every consumer still fetches block + contract to ask it), putting the flag inside `BreakSchedule` (fuses a preset-derived fact with a contract-derived one), or a `BreakPlan` value object (only wraps two things today — noted in Open Gap P for when a break budget adds a third rule). **Chosen: copy it onto the session at Start**, the same "decide at Start, then the session just knows" shape as Gap 63's `BreakSchedule`. `StartSessionCommandHandler` resolves it once (the contract's rule for a planned block committed to one, `false` otherwise — Spontaneous, Bonus); `Session.LiftsBlockingAt(at)` answers everyone. Safe because a contract can't change while its sessions run. `PolicyEngine` lost its `contracts` parameter and `ComputeActivePolicySliceQueryHandler` its contract fetch.

**The trap, closed structurally:** `ContractPreset.Update` now takes every rule as a required parameter (including `RedemptionPlacementPolicy`, which had the same latent default), and `NenContractDraftRules.KeepBlockingDuringBreaks` is `required` — a caller that forgets the rule no longer compiles, instead of silently resetting it. **Migration `KeepBlockingDuringBreaks`:** adds the new columns (DB default `false`) plus `Sessions.KeepBlockingDuringBreaks`, copies values across inverted, then drops the old columns. A plain column rename was rejected: the old column's `DEFAULT 1` would have stayed, and EF omits a CLR-default `false` from INSERTs, so every new "don't keep blocking" row would have been stored as `true`.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1362 passing):** `Session.LiftsBlockingAt` (work, break, break with keep-blocking, completed); `PolicyEngine` keeps a keep-blocking session's preset blocked inside its break slot with no contract passed; regression tests for all three traps (preset update, Seal, Edit); `StartSessionCommandHandler` copies the contract's rule for a committed planned block and uses `false` for Bonus and Spontaneous without looking up a contract; `BreakTransitionHandler` wording; real-SQLite round trips for all three tables, and a migration test that runs the real migrations over old-style preset rows and checks both directions of the inversion.

**Principle demonstrated:** a rule that no code path can set, and three that silently reset it, is what a defaulted parameter on an update method produces — make every rule required where it's written. And Gap 63's pattern generalised: a fact fixed for a session's whole run belongs on the session, decided once at Start, so every consumer asks the session instead of re-walking the aggregates it came from.

---

## Gap 65 — Open Gap Q Closed: a Contract Is Its Days; the Lifecycle Runs on Exact Local Moments *(closed, 2026-09-28)*

**The bug that started it:** the lifecycle sweep compared `DateOnly.FromDateTime(UtcNow)` — the **UTC** date — against a contract's **local** `StartDate`/`EndDate`. At UTC+2 a contract turned Active ~2 hours late (still "Upcoming" at 00:28 local while its block ran) and completed ~2 hours late. `Seal` (Active vs Scheduled) and `Break` (the handler passed `UtcNow`) had the same mistake, and the repository restated `IsDueForActivation`/`IsDueForCompletion` in its own words — a duplicated rule.

**Why it grew:** fixing only the date left the real question open. A contract runs between two **moments** the user picks (2 Feb 09:00 → 15 Feb 17:00), not two dates, and answering that exposed two parallel "default + exceptions" policies (`WorkHoursPolicy`, `DailyFloorPolicy`) that re-resolved every day on every read.

**What was built:**
- **`ContractDay`** `{ Date, WindowStart, WindowEnd, Floor? }` — local instants, so a window can end at 24:00. It refuses a floor longer than its window.
- **`ContractCalendar`** — the days, one per date with no gaps; `StartsAt`/`EndsAt` are read off the first and last day, never stored. `Generate(...)` is the one place Work Hours, rest days and planned hours become days — used once at Seal/Edit, never stored. Every day has a window: Work Hours, or the whole day. A floor that doesn't fit its day's window is **refused, never shrunk** — see "Manual-testing addendum" below.
- **`DailyFloorRule`** (`Fixed` \| `FollowsPlan` \| `None`) stored on the contract — how days get their floor when (re)generated. Found mid-build: the old policy shape carried this implicitly (`Default` set = fixed floor), and the days alone can't tell a Simple contract that planned 2h/day from a fixed 2h floor, which Edit needs. Strictness can't stand in for it, since Advanced pairs any strictness with either.
- **`WorkHoursPolicy` and `DailyFloorPolicy` deleted.** The calendar is private to `NenContract`; callers keep asking named questions (`IsRestDay`, `FloorHoursFor`, `AllowsBlockPlacement`, …) plus new `WindowOn(date)` and `Covers(localMoment)`.
- **Lifecycle on exact local moments:** `IsDueForActivation(localNow) => localNow >= StartsAt`, `IsDueForCompletion(localNow) => localNow >= EndsAt`. The repository's due-for queries are gone; the sweep loads the in-effect contracts and asks the entity, passing `_clock.ToLocal(now)`.
- **Seal refuses a start in the past** (before the current minute — "right now" is allowed). The handlers also refuse a block outside its day's window, which covers a block in the past — a check that didn't exist before. A block starting on the start minute is at most 59 s late at Seal, well inside the 5-minute grace.
- **Break keeps the break day whole** and uses the local date.
- **`Covers` replaced four inline `StartsAt`/`EndsAt` comparisons** (Bonus classification in the day query, recap, readiness, Bonus hours). `EndsAt` is now exclusive, so their old `<= EndsAt` would have counted a block starting at 00:00 the next day as Bonus.
- **Bridge until Open Gap W:** the command still carries no start time, so the handlers start at `ContractCalendar.SuggestedStart(...)` — the day's Work Hours start (or midnight), or the current minute if that has passed today. **Edit** reads the days (`NenContractEditDto.Days`); a temporary UI adapter `ContractEditPrefill` fills today's stencil-shaped wizard fields from them.
- **Migration `ContractDays`** is schema-only (adds `Days`/`FloorRule`, drops the policy columns) — the architect wipes the local database before testing.

**Rejected:** activating at midnight whatever the start time (the old Gap 59 note); two separate per-day maps (duplicates the one-per-day invariant, can drift); keeping default + exceptions ("default" is how the days were filled in, not a fact); storing the whole stencil for Edit (Work Hours in two places); guessing the floor mode from the days; Break cutting the break day at the break moment (its floor could no longer fit — breaking would clean up a bad day).

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1400 passing, up from 1362):** `ContractDay`/`ContractCalendar` invariants, generation, the refused-not-shrunk floor and `SuggestedStart`; exact-moment activation/completion, `Covers`, the Seal start guard (09:01 refused, 09:00:40 accepted), Break keeping the day whole; UTC+2 regressions for activation and completion just after local midnight; Seal handler starting at the current minute with the full day-1 floor, refusing the reported 16:38 case, and refusing a block outside its window; real-SQLite round trips including a 24:00 end and a Break; the Edit prefill adapter. About 80 test fixtures had sealed a contract *after* its start and were moved to seal at their start — the new guard caught every one.

**Manual-testing addendum (same day) — the floor is refused, not capped.** The interrogation first agreed to cap a fixed floor that didn't fit a late day 1 (a 6h floor on 12:00–17:00 became 5h). Manual testing showed why that was wrong: sealing at 16:38 with a 2h floor and Work Hours until 18:00, the draft still demanded 2h (the Planner's draft badge re-derives the floor from the stencil — a known duplication), and even a working cap makes day 1 a moving target, since every minute spent drafting shrinks the window, the floor, and pushes placed blocks into the past. Now `Generate` never shrinks a floor: one that doesn't fit is refused ("Not enough time left on Mon 28 Sep for its 2h floor — start on a later day or lower the floor"). The day-1 badge already shows the full floor unmet while drafting; an early warning belongs in Open Gap W's When step. The one-click way out — editing a single day's floor or making it a rest day while drafting — is added to Open Gap X. Also found in manual testing: the contract heartbeat still ran once a minute (sized for day-boundary activation), so a contract could show "Upcoming" for up to a minute after its start while its block's Ritual had already fired — shortened to 5s, the enforcement heartbeat's interval. And an older Edit bug surfaced: Edit's drafting showed the contract's blocks twice — the saved ones (protected by the sealed contract, so Delete did nothing) on top of their editable draft copies. The saved blocks of the contract being edited are now left out while its draft is open; Edit is ordinary drafting, pre-filled (architect: everything on an Upcoming contract stays editable). Other blocks in the range becoming draft tiles is Open Gap S.

**Principle demonstrated:** a "default + exceptions" shape describes how data was filled in, not what it is — store the resolved fact per day and keep the filling-in as a factory input. And the same Handler Forbidden Zone lesson again: the repository's copy of the due-for rule, and four copies of "is this moment inside the contract", drifted the moment the rule changed.

---

## Gap 84 — Notifications Through One Door; Blocks Announce Their Own Boundaries *(closed, 2026-09-28)*

**The gap, found auditing what else should sit beside `IEnforcementSynchronizer`:** the "Block Starting Soon" warning never fired. `ScheduledEnforcementService` kept one `_lastWarningSentAt` for every block and checked `_lastWarningSentAt?.AddMinutes(10) < now` — `null < now` is `false` in C#, so the first warning was never sent and so neither was any other. Even fixed, one shared timestamp can't tell "warned about this block" from "warned about some block", its range fetch also returned already-running blocks (which sorted first and hid the real upcoming one), and the rule lived in Infrastructure, derived from raw `Start`. Around it, six classes each wrote their own toast text straight into `INotificationService`; Background blocks started and ended silently.
