**Decisions, each interrogated:**
- *Who detects a block boundary:* the block. `PlannedBlock.TransitionsBetween(from, to)` returns `StartingSoon` (5 min before `Start`) and `Started`/`Ended` — the moment `IsActive` flips, the same definition `PolicyEngine` enforces, so "Blocking is on/off" can never disagree with actual blocking, and an early-ended session moves the end for free (`EffectiveEnd`). Crossing detection (half-open `(from, to]`, a heartbeat cursor) replaces the cooldown: each block is announced once, with no memory of what was sent. Rejected: a domain event (nothing about the block changes — the Gap 63 reasoning for breaks).
- *Who owns a planned block's ending:* the session. A planned session always ends exactly when its block's effective end is, and the session's end is the real state change (`SessionEndedEvent`) while the block's is derived from it — so `FocusBlock.AnnouncesOwnEnd => RitualCompletedAt is null`. A missed Focus block or a Background block announces its own end, since nothing else will. No duplicate to suppress: one fact, one owner.
- *Session vs block as sole source:* rejected — a missed block's enforcement comes from the block with no running session (the Missed session is only written afterwards), and a spontaneous session has no block. `PolicyEngine` is the authority above both, but only for "what is blocked"; it merges presets and can't say which block started.
- *Block ↔ session timestamps:* the block keeps its own `RitualCompletedAt`/`SessionEndedAt` copies (reference by id + a copy of the one needed fact, written in the same transaction). Tightening each start/end pair into one Domain operation is deferred to the first second writer — noted on Gaps 70, 72 and 78.
- *Wording:* one `INotifier.NotifyAsync(NenNotification)`; sources report a typed fact carrying everything the text needs (`BlockStartingSoon`, `BlockStarted`, `BlockEnded`, `SessionStarted`, `SessionCompleted`, `BreakStarted`, `BreakEnded`, `ContractActivated`, `ContractCompleted`), and `Notifier` alone composes titles and bodies. Focus and Background are told apart, and blocks are named by `PlannedBlock.DisplayName(presetName)` — title if set, else the preset name, which is what the tile shows (nothing sets a title yet); a planned session's toasts name its block the same way. Muting per kind will plug in here (Gap 83).
- *Edge cases:* the heartbeat's first tick reports nothing (a boundary crossed before the app was watching is not announced late — an app opened mid-block gets the Ritual, not a stale "started"); a jump over a whole block reports nothing; back-to-back blocks announce the end before the start; boundaries are reported after `SyncAsync`, so the toast is already true when read.

**The door:** `INotificationService` is a bare toaster (`ShowAsync`); the never-called `ScheduleBreakReminderAsync`/`CancelReminderAsync`/`CancelAllRemindersAsync` were deleted, and `WindowsEnforcementService`'s unused `INotificationService` parameter removed. `NotificationDoorTests` fails if anything but `Notifier` takes the toaster. The stale `IEnforcementSynchronizer` return-value comment (still citing the pre-Gap 62 Ritual trigger) was corrected.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1445 passing, up from 1403):** `TransitionsBetween` (crossing, not yet, already crossed, jump past start, jump over the block, backwards, cancelled, Focus with/without Ritual, early-ended session); one wording test per fact; `BlockTransitionHandler` (each kind, time order, empty first-tick window, loose fetch); session handlers name the block for a planned session; the one-door guard. Manually tested live the same day: Background full cycle, Focus run to end, ended early, missed, spontaneous, mid-block launch, launch just before a block, back-to-back blocks, breaks, contracts — all as specified.

**Principle demonstrated:** a rule that remembers "what I already sent" is a sign the question was asked of the wrong thing — ask each block whether the clock just crossed its own moment and there is nothing to remember. And one fact has one owner: when two records seem to report the same moment, find which one actually changed and let the other stay quiet.

**Renamed the same day:** `BlockTransition`/`BlockTransitionKind`/`TransitionsBetween`/`IBlockTransitionHandler` became `BlockMarker`/`BlockMarkerKind`/`MarkersPassedBetween`/`IBlockMarkerNotifier` (`NotifyPassedAsync`). "Transition" implies a state change, and `StartingSoon` changes nothing — each is a marker on the block's timeline the clock passed. The Domain names stay neutral (the block knows nothing of notifications); the Application handler is named for its one job. `BreakTransition` keeps its name: both its kinds really are state changes.

---

## Gap 85 — A Cancelled Block's Own Window Says It Stopped: Cancellation Folded into `EffectiveEnd` *(closed, 2026-09-28)*

**The gap, found in the area-1 audit (Application code re-deriving block facts):** `PlannedBlock.IsActive` ignored `CancelledAt`, so a cancelled block still answered "active" inside its window. "A cancelled block is not live" was re-applied by callers instead — in `PolicyEngine` itself, `ComputeActivePolicySliceQueryHandler`, `FocusBlock.IsAwaitingRitual`, `PlannedBlock.TransitionsBetween`/`MarkersPassedBetween`, plus several queries and repository filters. Nothing was broken (enforcement had two guards, not one — the audit first overstated the risk as a single guard), but any new caller that forgot the check would enforce a broken contract's block.

**Options weighed:**
- *Add `!CancelledAt` to `IsActive`* — works, but keeps cancellation a separate flag the time questions must remember, and says a block cancelled mid-run was never active (rewrites history).
- *An enum state (`Upcoming / Active / Over / Cancelled`)* — rejected: the code deliberately has two "ended"s (`HasEnded` = scheduled end passed, for edit locks and history; `IsActive` = real end, via `EffectiveEnd`); one `Over` would fuse them and recreate the Gap 21 bug. It would also overlap `FocusBlockOutcome`.
- *A stored status column* — rejected: "active" depends on the clock, so it would go stale (a second source of truth).
- *An EF global query filter hiding cancelled blocks* — rejected: the recap needs them, and the rule would live invisibly in Infrastructure.
- **Chosen: cancellation is one more reason the reserved window shrinks.** `PlannedBlock.EffectiveEnd` (the existing one rule for "how much of the window this block really occupies") now ends the window at the cancel moment, or empties it if cancelled before `Start`; `FocusBlock`'s override adds its own reasons (session ended early, missed) and the earliest wins. `IsActive`, overlap checks, `IsAwaitingRitual` and the markers all read that window, so they follow without checking `CancelledAt` — and a block cancelled at 10:30 truthfully *was* active 10:00–10:30.

**Removed:** the cancelled checks in `PolicyEngine`, `ComputeActivePolicySliceQueryHandler`, `IsAwaitingRitual` and `MarkersPassedBetween` (whose "starting soon" now asks whether the block still reserves any time). **Kept on purpose:** repository filters (a query-speed optimisation, not the rule), the Planner hiding cancelled tiles (a display choice), and the recap/readiness counting cancelled blocks (reporting a fact).

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1450 passing, up from 1445):** cancelled before start is never active and reserves nothing; cancelled mid-run is active until the cancel moment only; a Focus block's earliest reason wins (cancelled vs ended early); a cancelled block cancelled mid-run passes an Ended marker at the cancel moment; `PolicyEngine` blocks nothing for a cancelled block handed straight to it (the regression guard); the query handler now passes blocks through for `PolicyEngine` to rule out.

**Principle demonstrated:** when a fact has to be re-checked by every caller, it isn't part of the concept it belongs to yet. Fold it into the one rule those callers already ask, and the copies stop being needed rather than needing to be kept in sync.

---

## Gap 86 — Outcome Classification Has One Source: the Block *(closed, 2026-09-28)*

**The gap, found in the area-1 audit:** `Classify(scheduledStart, scheduledEnd, ritualCompletedAt, sessionEndedAt, asOf)` was called from four places, each spelling the arguments out by hand — and they fed "when did the session end" from two different sources. The Planner tile and `IsResolved` passed the block's `SessionEndedAt` stamp; the deficit ledger and the contract recap loaded the `Session` and passed its `EndedAt`. No live bug: for classification the two agree (only an early end matters, and both are written together in `EndSessionCommandHandler`). But it was one fact read from two places — the Planner tile and the recap could disagree about the same block the day the two copies drift (the Gaps 70/72/78 note) — five loose dates per call can be swapped and still compile, and the recap loaded every session just for that one date.

**Found while fixing:** the two are *not* the same fact in general. `Session.EndedAt` means "ended"; `FocusBlock.SessionEndedAt` means "ended **early**" and stays null when the session ran to the end. That is enough for `Classify`, but not for `CreditedWindow`/`CreditedFocusMinutes`, which must know a session finished on time — so credit keeps reading the session. The stamp's doc comment now says so; the similar names are a trap.

**Options weighed:**
- *An overload taking the block, keeping the five-argument form public* — rejected: two ways in means callers can still feed the other source.
- *Move classification onto the block (`focusBlock.OutcomeAt(asOf)`)* — tempting, since every input is the block's own, but the grace-period rule it uses is shared with credited minutes, which needs the session and so can't live on the block. Moving `Classify` alone would split one rule across two homes.
- **Chosen:** `Classify(FocusBlock block, DateTime asOf)` is the only public form; the five-argument version is private. The classifier stays the single owner of the outcome and credit rules; the block is always the source of its own facts.

**Changed:** all four callers are `classifier.Classify(block, now)`; `GetNenContractRecapQueryHandler` no longer depends on `ISessionRepository`. The deficit ledger still loads the session — for elapsed minutes and whether it has truly ended, which only the session knows.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1450 passing):** the classifier's own rule tests run unchanged through a helper that builds a real block with the given stamps; handler tests mock `Classify(block, now)`.

**Principle demonstrated:** a fact reachable two ways will eventually be read two ways — close the second door rather than documenting which one to use. And check that two similarly named fields are really the same fact before unifying them: here they were only the same for one of their two consumers.

**Addendum (same day) — the stamp now means "ended", not "ended early".** Asking why the two facts differed traced it to Gap 22: `SessionEndedAt` replaced the destructive `TrimEnd` and inherited its "only if before `End`" condition by accident, not design. Every reader already compares the stamp to `End` itself (`Classify`, `EffectiveEnd`, the Planner tile), so an on-time stamp changes no answer. `EndSessionCommandHandler` now always calls `MarkSessionEnded`, making the block's copy a faithful copy of `Session.EndedAt` — one meaning for one name, and an empty stamp only ever means "no session has ended". **Migration `BackfillFocusBlockSessionEndedAt`** (data only, no schema change) fills the stamp for existing on-time blocks from their completed session; `Down` clears every stamp at or after `End`, restoring the old rule exactly. Verified against a copy of the real database seeded with an on-time, an early and a no-session block (forward and back); the real database currently holds no Focus blocks. The comments warning about "ended early only" were removed. Grouping `RitualCompletedAt`/`SessionEndedAt` into a `SessionRun` value object was considered and parked with the session ↔ block pairing note on Gaps 70/72/78.

---

## Gap 87 — Cover Deficit Suggestions Compared UTC "Now" with a Local Work Hours Close *(closed, 2026-09-28)*

**The gap, noticed while reading `GetSuggestedRedemptionPlacementsQueryHandler` for the area-1 audit:** the Planner passes `AsOf` in UTC. The handler took "today" straight from it (`DateOnly.FromDateTime(query.AsOf)`, no conversion), read today's boundary from `contract.WindowOn(today)` — **local** since Gap 65 — and ran the placement search with UTC "now" and UTC block times against that local boundary. For a user at UTC+2 with Work Hours 09–17 and a 2-hour deficit at 16:00 local, it saw "now = 14:00, close = 17:00", decided 2 hours fit, and suggested 16:00–18:00 local marked as fully covering — an hour past Work Hours, which the create form then refused. Between 00:00 and 02:00 local, "today" was the previous UTC day: wrong contract day, wrong eligibility, wrong day's blocks. Tests missed it because every fixture used a single frame.

**DDD framing:** the domain speaks the user's wall clock — Work Hours, "today", contract days and quarter-hour slots are all local concepts; UTC is only how times are stored. So the handler's job is to translate at its edge: convert the inputs into the domain's frame once, decide everything in that one frame, convert the answer back once. `ISystemClock` stays the only place conversions happen: it gained `ToUtc`, the reverse of `ToLocal`, so no handler calls `ToLocalTime()`/`ToUniversalTime()` (untestable, and the source of earlier silent bugs). `SystemClock.ToUtc` treats its input as local whatever its `Kind` (a local value built from a contract window can carry `Unspecified` or even `Utc`) and uses `ToUniversalTime`, which never throws on a DST-skipped local time.

**Changed:** the handler converts `AsOf` and today's blocks to local, computes "today", the boundary and the placement there, and returns the suggestion via `ToUtc`. `ResolveBoundary`'s outside-the-contract fallback is local midnight, not UTC midnight. A scan found no other UTC value turned into a calendar date in the Application or Domain layers.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1454 passing, up from 1450):** at UTC+2, 16:00 local with a 17:00 close suggests 16:00–17:00 local as partial coverage (failed before the fix); at 00:30 local, "today" is the local day (failed before); `ToUtc` round-trips `ToLocal` and ignores the input's `Kind`. All existing placement tests run unchanged on a zero-offset clock.

**Principle demonstrated:** a value's time frame is part of its meaning. When a handler touches both stored (UTC) and domain (local) times, pick the domain's frame, translate once at the edges, and never let a comparison span the two.

\---

## Gap 94 — The Recap Counted Background Blocks as Bonus Blocks *(closed, 2026-09-29)*

**Found by** the one-source investigation of "how many Focus hours does this count for" (raised as Gap 95). `GetNenContractRecapQueryHandler` counts the contract's Bonus blocks with a comment saying it mirrors `GetNenContractBonusFocusHoursQueryHandler`'s block-sourcing "exactly" — but the hours query takes only unlinked `FocusBlock`s the contract `Covers`, while the count took unlinked blocks of *any* type. An unlinked Background block inside the contract's range was reported as a Bonus block that had earned no Bonus hours.

**Changed:** the count takes `OfType<FocusBlock>()` first — the same set the hours come from. One line.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1461 passing, up from 1460):** `HandleAsync_BonusBlockCount_IgnoresBackgroundBlocks` — one Focus Bonus block plus one unlinked Background block counts 1 (counted 2 before the fix).

**Principle demonstrated:** a comment that says "mirrors X exactly" is a promise nothing checks. "Which blocks are Bonus for this contract" is still defined in three places (hours, this count, completion readiness) — the real fix is one definition; that is Gap 95.

\---

## Gap 96 — Spontaneous Sessions Matched on UTC Days; Background Tiles Badged "Bonus" *(closed, 2026-09-29)*

**Found by** the one-source investigation of "which contract governs this block" (raised as Gap 97). Two bugs:

1. **Spontaneous sessions on the wrong days.** `GetNenContractCompletedFocusHoursQueryHandler` asked `ISessionRepository.GetHistoryAsync(contract.StartDate, contract.EndDate)` for the spontaneous sessions that count toward the contract. The contract's dates are **local**; the repository turns them into **UTC** midnights (`SessionRepository.cs:35-36`) and compares with the UTC `StartedAt`. At UTC+2, a session at 01:00 local on the first day (23:00 UTC the day before) was left out, and one at 01:00 local the day after the last day (23:00 UTC on the last day) was counted — same local/UTC mix-up as Gap 87.
2. **"BONUS" on Background tiles.** `GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandler` gave any unlinked block the contract `Covers` a Bonus badge, with no Focus filter, and `PlannerPageView.axaml.cs` draws it — so a Background tile inside a contract read "BONUS" while earning no Bonus hours. Same slip as Gap 94.

**Changed:** the handler fetches a day either side and keeps a session only if its local start date (`ISystemClock.ToLocalDate`) is inside the contract's dates. The tile takes Bonus only for a `FocusBlock`.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1449 passing, up from 1447):** `HandleAsync_SpontaneousSessions_MatchedOnTheContractsLocalDays` (UTC+2 clock; the first-day session counts, the day-after session doesn't — failed before) and `HandleAsync_UnlinkedBackgroundBlockWithinAContractRange_HasNoBonusBadge` (failed before). Existing completed-hours tests re-stubbed for the wider fetch; behaviour unchanged.

**Left for Gap 97:** `GetHistoryAsync` still reads its dates as UTC days — a trap for its next caller (the Insights `GetSessionHistoryQuery`, not yet called by any UI); spontaneous sessions still use whole dates while Bonus uses `Covers` (exact moments); "is this block Bonus" is still written in four places.

**Principle demonstrated:** a `DateOnly` has no time zone, so every method that takes one silently picks one. A date that means "the user's day" must be turned into instants through the clock, never by `ToDateTime(..., DateTimeKind.Utc)`.

\---

## Gap 98 — Architecture Tests: Rules That Fail on Drift *(first pass closed, 2026-09-29)*

**Why it was a gap:** written rules alone didn't stop drift — the Design Review Rules in `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` exist because mistakes were made with the rules already loaded. What reliably stops drift is a test that fails at `dotnet test` time. One precedent exists: `CompositionRootTests` (ViewModel tree resolves under `ValidateScopes`, Gap 40).

**Three kinds of rule, three timings:**
1. **Already holds today → test now**, locking it in before the Architecture box's refactors move code.
2. **Broken today → test now with a known-exceptions list that may only shrink** (a new violation fails at once; each gap that fixes a file removes it; empty list = rule complete).
3. **Made true by a gap → that gap's last step** (the box rule in `ARCHITECT_GOVERNANCE.md`): e.g. after Gap 88, nothing but `BlockPlacementChecker` decides placement / no `ritualOnly`; after Gap 93, no ViewModel computes edit permissions; after Gap 90, no public `PlannedBlock.Start`/`End` comparisons.

**What a test can and can't check (agreed 2026-09-29):** tests catch *structural* drift; *judgment* stays a review job (Design Review Rules, "check the plan against the standards **and the code**"). Tests make the review smaller, not unnecessary.

| `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` section | Testable? | How |
|---|---|---|
| Layer rules (Domain references nothing; UI → Application ← Infrastructure) | ✅ | dependency rules on compiled assemblies |
| Domain forbidden (no async, no `DateTime.UtcNow`) | ✅ | type and source scan |
| ViewModel Forbidden Zone — no repositories, handlers, Domain entities, timers | ✅ mostly | dependency rules (`CompositionRootTests` already guards one) |
| ViewModel — no math, no formatting | ⚠️ partly | ban specific calls (`Math.Clamp`, `ToString("…")`, `ToLocalTime()`); can't spot all arithmetic |
| Vertical slices (four files, validator in its folder, `ICommand<T>`) | ✅ | naming/folder rules |
| Naming conventions (`…Query`, `…CommandHandler`, Domain services not `…Service`) | ✅ | name rules per folder/type |
| Interface-first, ≤ 5 members | ✅ as a number | count public members — flags big interfaces, can't judge whether the size is justified |
| Deep Module ("30 seconds", small interface hiding a lot) | ❌ | needs judgment |
| Where a Rule Lives (entity / value object / service) | ❌ | a design decision — tests only enforce its consequences afterwards |
| One source of truth | ⚠️ indirectly | (1) ban the known wrong way once a right way exists (e.g. no `ritualOnly`, no raw `.Start`/`.End` on blocks outside `TimeSlot` after Gap 88 — the compiler takes over after Gap 90); (2) parity tests where a copy must exist (SQL mirrors of `IsUnresolved`, `IsInEffect`); (3) per-gap behaviour tests pinning the same answer in two places (preview vs save) |
| Rounding rule | ⚠️ partly | a regression test per case (like Gap 33's), not a general check |

**Interrogated 2026-09-29 — decisions:**
- **Tooling: plain xUnit, no library.** Reflection for type rules (as `CompositionRootTests`, `NotificationDoorTests`), a comment-stripping source scan (`SourceCode`) for call bans. Rejected: NetArchTest (can't ban a call like `.UtcNow`, only "uses `DateTime`" — would split rules across two mechanisms), ArchUnitNET (heavier, call bans unproven), BannedApiAnalyzers (build-time, but exceptions become scattered `#pragma`s and type rules still need tests).
- **Exceptions: an array in the test, next to its rule, one reason comment per entry naming the gap that removes it.** `KnownExceptions.Check` fails both ways — a new violation, and a listed entry that no longer violates (stale) — so the list can only shrink. Rejected: central baseline file (rule and exceptions in two homes), attributes at the violation site (test concern in production code), fix-everything-first (pulls other gaps' refactors in), one-way check, count ratchet.
- **Generated code is out of scope, not an exception** — EF `Migrations/`, Avalonia's `CompiledAvaloniaXaml`/`XamlClosure_<n>`/`!`-named members. Matched narrowly, so a rename on their side fails red, never silently green.
- **Guard hierarchy** added to `MODULE_STANDARDS.md`: compiler → test → review; each gap's plan says which guards its rule. Rule 10 below is a test standing in for Gap 90's compiler step.
- **Depth itself stays a review job** (the interface-first sign-off). A member-count target would be gamed into worse design (one method with a mode flag); the tests catch the *signs* of shallow modules instead — logic leaking out (rule 10), second doors (rules 2, 3), silent growth (rule 9).

**First-pass rules** (`NenFocus.Tests/ArchitectureTests/`; each probed red with a deliberate violation before being trusted):

| # | Rule | Exceptions at close |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Layers: Domain → nothing, Application → Domain, Infrastructure → Application + Domain; only the composition root (`App`, `Program`) uses Infrastructure from the UI | 0 |
| 2 | Only `SystemClock` reads the machine clock (all four projects); Domain has no async | `WindowsNotificationService` toast expiry (permanent, Windows wall-clock) |
| 3 | UI holds no repositories, no handlers, no `IServiceProvider`/`IServiceScopeFactory` | 0 |
| 4 | Command slices (four files, `ICommand<T>` ⇄ `…Command`, validator in its command's folder); query slices (one Query + one QueryHandler; a result type in the folder isn't shared with another slice) | `RedemptionAttributionResult` (→ Gap 88 phase 3) |
| 5 | Naming: handler = request + `Handler`, result = command − `Command` + `Result`, events `…Event`, nothing in Domain `…Service`, observable UI types `…ViewModel`, queries Get/Compute/Check, every `…Dto` in `DTOs/`, `IValueConverter` ⇄ `…Converter`, Converter Catalogue ⇄ code | 3 formatters named like converters (→ Gap 89 area 2). The 10 converters missing from the catalogue got their rows the same day — that list is empty |
| 6 | No `ToLocalTime()`/`ToUniversalTime()` outside `SystemClock` (all four projects) | 12 files: `NenFocusDbContext` silent save-time conversion (→ Gap 99, first thought permanent, then judged a hidden second copy of the rule); Create/Update block handlers (→ Gap 88 phase 3); `PlannedBlockRepository` local-day window (→ Gap 97); `PlannerClockService.LocalNow` (→ Gap 89 area 3); 7 UI files (→ Gap 89). The earlier count of 9 missed the three Infrastructure files |
| 7 | No UI code uses Domain entities or Domain services (source scan — an entity's constant is copied into the caller at compile time, so reflection can't see it) | `PlannerEndTimeOptions` (→ Gap 89 area 2) |
| 8 | No UI code owns a timer — any timer type or a `Task.Delay` loop, source scan of the whole UI project except the composition root (Views included, so polling can't move there; visual effects use Avalonia animations) | `PlannerPageViewModel` badge poll (→ Gap 89 area 3, "the heartbeat announces what changed") |
| 9 | New interfaces ≤ 5 public members; existing larger ones may not grow — entries are "Name has N", so growing *and* shrinking both force an edit (a new member is a visible decision) | 8 capped: `ISessionStateService` 15 (→ Gap 92 A3), `IActiveSessionMonitor` 10 (→ Gap 92 A4), and six repositories — `IPlannedBlockRepository` 12, `INenContractRepository` 9, `ISessionRepository` 9, `IBlockPresetRepository` 7, `IBreakTimerPresetRepository` 6, `IContractPresetRepository` 6 — capped deliberately: an extra query method is usually a business rule moving into SQL (Gap 92 A6) |
| 10 | Only Domain combines raw time fields (`Start`, `End`, `StartedAt`, `RitualCompletedAt`, …) — operators *and* the method-call forms (FluentValidation `.GreaterThan(x => x.Start)`, `CompareTo`, `DateTime.Compare`, `Math.Min/Max`); entity and DTO fields alike (a ViewModel asking "has it ended?" of a DTO re-answers a Domain question too); entries "File has N", so growth inside a listed file fails. Bridge until Gap 90 — then deleted; repositories/`DbContext` excluded (parity tests guard SQL mirrors). Rejected: Roslyn semantic analysis (heavy, would tempt narrowing to entities, and Gap 90/99 hand the job to the compiler), compiled-IL inspection (can't tell a comparison from copying into a DTO) | 16 files: Gap 88 — day-view display end, Cover Deficit and drag overlap checks, Create/Update/Seal/Edit validators' copies of `FocusBlock`/`TimeSlot` rules; Gap 93 — "has it started" in the Split validator and `PlannerPageViewModel`; Gap 89 — "has it ended" in `HatsuPageViewModel`, `ShellViewModel`; Gap 90 — lengths and "how long until" (`ActiveSessionMonitor`, `StartSession`, Ritual context, `BlockMarkerNotifier`); allowed — `PlannerPageView.axaml.cs` drag geometry |

**Standards written down along the way (`MODULE_STANDARDS.md`):** Guard Hierarchy; no machine-clock reads in any layer; query slice shape; validators check form and ask, never own a rule; naming table (`Check…Query`, what a DTO is, yes/no converter names, catalogue must match code). **Code changed:** `PlannerEndTimeOptionDto` → `PlannerEndTimeOption` (a UI item, not a DTO); CS8629 in `CreateContractPresetValidator` silenced with `!` like its sibling (the duplication behind it → Gap 92 V9).

**Surfaced by the tests, each routed to its gap rather than fixed here:** `PlannerPageViewModel`'s 3 s badge poll timer and ViewModels formatting strings through static "converters" (→ Gap 89 area 2); `PlannerClockService` designed for 60 s but firing about every second, plus the agreed heartbeat direction — one clock, one background heartbeat, events instead of polling (→ Gap 89 area 3); duplicated contract-preset rules in two validators while `ContractPreset` enforces none (→ Gap 92 V9); a query result type saved by commands, i.e. a business rule living in a query (→ Gap 88 phase 3); EF's storage converter silently converting non-UTC values, which raised **Gap 99** (moments vs calendar times as types). Each of Gaps 88, 90, 93, 95, 97 and 99 now ends with a "last step" naming the narrow rule it must guard and the exception entries it deletes.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1483 passing, up from 1449):** 34 new tests in `ArchitectureTests/` — the `SourceCode` scanner and `KnownExceptions` two-way check test themselves (7), plus 27 rule tests. Every rule was probed red with a deliberate violation (a temporary `ZzProbe` file, or a line appended to a listed file and restored from a backup) before being trusted; two probes exposed gaps in the tests themselves — static classes skipped by the converter-name rule (.NET marks them abstract), and Avalonia's generated XAML code flagged by the service-locator rule.

**Principle demonstrated:** a rule on paper is a promise; a rule in a test is a guarantee — but only for what a test can see. Structure (layers, names, doors, sizes, raw comparisons) fails at `dotnet test`; judgment (is it deep, is this the right home) stays with the architect's sign-off. And a test that stands in for the compiler says so and names the gap that retires it.

**Addendum — pass 1, 2026-09-29 (same day, after the commit):** the written rules that were still untested. A scan of each came first, so every decision was made on real counts.

| # | Rule | Violations | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| 11 | Command handlers leave side effects (enforcement, notifications) to event handlers | 0 | built — `SideEffectRuleTests` |
| 12 | Queries only read: no unit of work, no command dispatch, no write call | 0 | built — `CqrsRuleTests` |
| 13 | Only `EnforcementSynchronizer` holds `IEnforcementService` (Gap 62) | 0 | built — `SideEffectRuleTests` |
| 14 | ViewModels don't format values (format specifiers, `ToString("…")`, `string.Format`, converters' static `Format…` helpers) | 7 ViewModels, 55 uses | built, "File has N" → Gap 89 — far more spread than the Planner alone |
| 15 | ViewModels don't do math (`Math.*`) | 3 Planner geometry helpers | built; the three listed as allowed pixel geometry |
| 16 | Application does no display formatting | 5 files, all validator messages | **not built** — a message must be text when it leaves a validator. Its finding went further: the floor message is written three times (→ Gap 93) |

Rule 16 led to a design rule instead: **a refusal is a typed reason, not a sentence** (`MODULE_STANDARDS.md`), with the wording built in one place, like `INotifier`. Its test arrives with Gap 88, which creates the mechanism; Gaps 93 and 100 empty its list. **Gap 100** (One Home for User-Facing Text) was raised first in box 4. It was reshaped when the iOS front end turned out to be SwiftUI: C# wording can't reach Swift, but typed reasons can be its contract. Plain UI copy built from names may stay in ViewModels until a second front end, translation or a repeated sentence gives it a reason to move.

Two probes showed what reflection can see. `App`'s shutdown path turned up because the compiler stores an async method's local variables as fields on a hidden class, so the composition root is excluded by name. `System.Math.Round` is caught by the same pattern as `Math.Round`. Shared reflection logic was moved into `TypeUse`. **1489 passing, up from 1483.**

**Addendum: Pass 2 begins (Gap 89 areas 3 and 4, 2026-09-29).** Reading `SessionStateService`, `ActiveSessionMonitor` and both heartbeats turned up two structural rules:
- **17: outside Application, only the dispatchers run a use case.** This widens rule 3. Four Infrastructure services fetch handlers by hand, and they're listed for Gap 89. The dispatchers, the DI setup, the handler wrappers and the composition root are exempt by role.
- **18: one builder per DTO.** The rule is "at most one production file", not "its own file", because about 20 DTOs are each built in exactly one query and are already one source. Four DTOs are listed, starting with `SessionDto`: its three builders had already drifted, and the restart path loses `OriginatingBlockId` (Gap 89 F1, which led to the suspected unlocked running tile, F2 → Gap 93). A named factory on the DTO is the fix; `private init` was weighed and rejected because tests could no longer build DTOs freely.

The findings (F1–F6) are recorded in their gaps (89, 93, 95, 100). **1491 passing.**

**Addendum: Pass 2 continued (repositories and the uncovered use cases, 2026-09-29).**
- **Repositories (R1–R4).** R1: the shutdown path re-decides enforcement with SQL that ignores `EffectiveEnd` and Background blocks. That raised **Gap 101 (Closing the Window Never Stops Enforcement)** — close to tray, Quit refused while enforcing, shutdown via `SyncAsync` failing closed — placed first as a live-behaviour fix, before Gap 76's service. Narrowed the same day to the shutdown fix alone (**Shutdown Asks the Synchronizer**); the tray and Quit refusal moved to Gap 76 as its first step, since their key question is Gap 76's open Master Locked Mode idea. R2 (`IsInEffect` copied into SQL with no parity test) rides along with it. R2 and R3 led to a standard: **repositories narrow with a superset filter, and the entity decides**; a SQL copy of a rule is allowed only when volume demands it, with a parity test.
- **Use cases (U1–U4).** U1: start and end update the session noticeboard in different layers. That led to **rule 19** (command handlers don't update the noticeboard; a bridge, with `StartSession` listed) and the agreed direction for Gap 89 area 4: the noticeboard **refreshes itself** from `GetActiveSessionQuery` instead of being handed a `SessionDto`. That one change fixes F1, F3 and U1; the read/write split was weighed and set aside for Gap 92 A3's job split. U2 (input checks in handlers) → Gap 100/88; U3 confirms R3; U4 → Gap 97.

Pass 2 is done apart from Gap 89 area 2 (`PlannerPageViewModel`), to be read after Gap 93 rewrites its placement and permission logic. **1492 passing.**

## Gap 101 — Shutdown Asks the Synchronizer *(closed, 2026-09-29)*

**Found by** Gap 89 Pass 2 (R1) while reading the repositories. When the app closed with no session running, `App.axaml.cs` decided for itself whether to keep blocking. It asked `IPlannedBlockRepository.HasActiveFocusBlockAsync`, which was its only caller. That SQL (`Start <= now < End`, not cancelled) **ignored Background blocks entirely**, so closing the app lifted blocking for a running Background block. It also ignored `EffectiveEnd`, so a Focus block whose session had ended early still counted. That made a third answer to "what should be blocked right now", beside `PolicyEngine` and the heartbeat. A repository test, `HasActiveFocusBlockAsync_BackgroundBlockActiveOnly_ReturnsFalse`, had pinned the bug as intended behaviour.

**Changed:**
- **Shutdown asks the one door.** The handler holds the first close request, calls `IEnforcementSynchronizer.SyncAsync(now)` with a **2-second limit**, then closes for real; an "already synced" flag lets the second request pass. On any error or timeout, blocking is left as it was (**fail closed**). Holding the request matters: the old `async` handler let the process exit mid-way, so its own removal could be cut off halfway.
- `HasActiveFocusBlockAsync` and its three tests are deleted, and the repository cap goes from 12 to 11 (rule 9 forced the edit).
- `App` no longer touches `IEnforcementService`, so Gap 98 rule 13 lost its only exemption. It was made red first, then green.
- **R2 rode along:** `NenContractRepository`'s two "mirrors `IsInEffect` exactly" SQL copies now fetch and let `NenContract.IsInEffect` decide. All the existing behaviour tests stayed green.

**Narrowed while designing:** it first also covered hiding to the tray on X and refusing Quit while enforcing (the architect's requirement: "always active like other blockers"). Those moved to Gap 76 as its first step. They're a feature rather than a fix, and their key question is Gap 76's still-open Master Locked Mode idea. Relaunching a closed window was rejected: a watchdog can itself be killed, and a window popping back reads as a bug.

**Rejected:** waiting synchronously on the UI thread, which risks freezing the app if anything inside needs the UI thread back; and fire-and-forget, which doesn't guarantee fail-closed.

**Confirmed via `NenFocus.Tests` (1489 passing; 3 obsolete repository tests removed) and by hand** (hosts file checked after each close with X):
- a running Background block: blocking kept (the bug)
- nothing active: blocking removed
- a running session: blocking kept

**Known limit (Gap 76):** once the app is closed, only the hosts-file website blocking survives. Blocked apps aren't killed, the extension loses its local server, and nothing lifts blocking at a block's end until the app reopens.

**Principle demonstrated:** a decision that has one owner (`PolicyEngine` decides, the synchronizer applies) must not grow a shortcut copy at the edges of the app. Shutdown was the one path that answered "what should be blocked" with its own small rule, and that rule quietly disagreed with the real one. And a test can pin a bug just as firmly as it pins a feature.

## Gap 102 — Avalonia 11.0.10 → 11.3.22 *(closed, 2026-09-29)*

**Why:** the dependency scan's last finding, `Tmds.DBus.Protocol` 0.15.0 (CVE-2026-39959), came in through Avalonia 11.0.10's Linux-only parts. It was never loaded on Windows, but a scan that's always red teaches everyone to ignore it. The architect chose to fix it at the source rather than pin it, and to do it before Gaps 88/93/89 so their Planner rewrites are built once, on the new version.

**Changed:**
- All five app packages and `Avalonia.Headless.XUnit` moved to 11.3.22. The build stayed at 0 warnings, 0 errors. The 11.1–11.3 release notes list no breaking changes for this app (binding rewrite in 11.1, TextBox keeps its selection on lost focus since 11.2).
- **Dependency scan fully clean, no pin needed.**
- **Before the bump:** 13 headless smoke tests (Zetsu, Ren spontaneous/planned/allowlist, Hatsu, Post-Session completed/missed/reflection, Block and Contract Presets, the Planner End dropdown), green on 11.0.10 first, plus a small kit (`HeadlessTestKit`, `FakeQueryDispatcher`).
- **Regression they caught:** since 11.1–11.3, a TextBox **with a selection handles Down itself** (collapsing the selection) before a bubbling `KeyDown` handler runs. The Planner's six time fields select all on focus, so Down stopped stepping through their options (Up still worked). Fixed in `PlannerPageView.WireTimePickerDropdown` by listening in the **tunnel** phase. The Planner test was red on 11.3 before the fix.
- **Manual click-through passed:** the Ritual, Planner create/drag/resize/Split/arrow keys, the Contract wizard date pickers, both preset pages, Post-Session, toasts, and close with X (a running Blocklist Background block kept its 5 sites in the hosts file — Gap 101 unaffected).

**Found on the way, not part of this gap:** Gap 103 (a broken contract's day blocks a new contract) and Gap 104 (first 09:00 block's empty End list, built directly).

**Guard (step 5):** yes — `PlannerEndDropdownViewTests` fails if the time fields' arrow keys stop reaching the Planner again, on any future Avalonia update. The headless tests as a whole are the before/after check for the next update.

**Principle demonstrated:** a "no breaking changes" release note isn't the same as "nothing changes for us". A behaviour change in one control (a TextBox handling a key) broke code that depended on the old event order. Tests written *before* the update turned "does it still work?" into a red/green answer.

## Gap 104 — First Planner Block at 09:00 Got an Empty End List *(closed, 2026-09-29)*

**Found by** Gap 102's headless Planner test. The End dropdown's options were rebuilt only when Start or the block type *changed*. The form's Start already defaults to "09:00", so the first block of an app run created at 09:00 changed nothing and its End dropdown opened empty (typing still worked). Any other hour, or 09:00 after another block, was fine — which is why it didn't show up when checked by hand.

**Changed:** `PlannerPageViewModel.SetFormTimes`, which every form-opening path goes through (new, drag-create, edit, edit draft, Cover Deficit), now always rebuilds the End options and re-centres their scroll. That also covers the same gap for drafting: its "no next-day End times" cutoff was only applied when the list happened to be rebuilt.

**Confirmed via** a new headless test (`FirstBlockAtTheFormsDefaultStartStillGetsEndOptions`), red before the fix, and the full suite (1504 passing).

**Guard (step 5):** yes — that test.

**Principle demonstrated:** a derived list rebuilt only "on change" silently depends on the starting value being different from the first real one. Rebuild it where its inputs are set, not only where they change.


## Gap 88 — One Source for Time: Moments, Time Slots and Block Placement *(closed, 2026-10-01; Gaps 90 and 99 folded in)*

**What was wrong:** three rules about time — what a block occupies, may Focus time go here, does it fit the contract's window — were each copied into up to four places, the UI included. One type, `DateTime`, meant both a moment and a calendar time, which had already cost three fixes (Gaps 65, 87, 96). Blocks and sessions carried loose `Start`/`End` that anyone could compare.

**Changed (phases 0–8, 2026-09-30):**
- **0** — started blocks are locked (`PlannedBlock.HasStarted`): no move, resize, split or edit; delete only once ended.
- **1** — `Moment` (always UTC, no calendar members), `UserTimeZone` (the one place a moment becomes a calendar time), `ISystemClock.Now`/`TimeZone`.
- **2–3** — `TimeSlot` (half-open, `Start`/`End` Domain-only); blocks and sessions hold one; `BlockPlacementChecker.CheckFree`.
- **4** — days split in real time, DST-correct; fixed Seal/Edit counting planned hours per UTC day.
- **5–6** — every stored timestamp and every DTO time is a `Moment`; `ISystemClock` shrank to `Now` + `TimeZone`.
- **7** — Cover Deficit suggestion simplified to "next quarter hour, needed length" (`TimeSlot.Covering`).
- **8** — `BlockPlacementChecker.Check` is the one placement answer behind Create/Update (via `GetBlockPlacementVerdictQuery`), Split, Seal and Edit, worded once in `BlockPlacementVerdictDto.From`; the UI's copies (`WouldCollide`, `FindSplitOverlap`, `FindSplitWorkHoursViolation`) and `GetContractWorkHoursForDate` deleted; attribution returns the capped slot.
- Phase 9 (drafting's placement check, `WorkHours.ContainsRange`) moved to Gap 68, which gives drafting a calendar to ask.

**Found on the way:** Split let a half land at an hour already gone (red test first, fixed by asking `Check` per half). The first phase-8 session left the test project not compiling and the verdict query untested — restored before continuing, placement tests moved rather than deleted. Two plan items dropped after checking the code (capping rounding; `ConflictStart/End` on the verdict DTO). Spun off: **Gap 106** (redemption attribution into the Domain; atomic Cover Deficit split), **Gap 89 area 4 F7** (session DTOs' `Moment` pairs → `TimeSlot`), **Gap 100 R1** (validator text into `Refusals.resx`).

**Confirmed via:** the full suite (1566 passing), each new guard probed red; the architect's manual click-through of the earlier phases. Phase 8's dialog checks: to be reported if anything comes up.

**Guard (step 5):** yes.
- New tests: `PlacementRuleTests` (no overlap question outside the Domain; no `ritualOnly`), `ValidatorWordingRuleTests` (no validator gains a sentence — shrink-only, 94 today), `ClockRuleTests.TestsMakeFakeClocksOnlyThroughTestClock` (a fake clock throws until set — it caught a Planner view test running at year 0001), `CqrsRuleTests.CommandsDoNotPromiseNonNullWithNullBang`; plus `MomentRuleTests`/`ClockRuleTests` from the earlier phases.
- `RawTimeFieldRuleTests` **kept, not deleted** as Gap 90 planned: the compiler guards entities now, not DTOs' public `Moment`s — it caught `ActiveSessionMonitor`'s own session length at close.
- Exception entries retired: `PlannerDragMath`, `ActiveSessionMonitor`, `RedemptionEntryDto` (built in 3 places); the ViewModel formatting count shrank 35 → 33. `RedemptionAttributionResult` re-tagged to Gap 106.
- The 15 close-out lessons: tests for 1+12 and 13; compiler already owns 2, 3, 11; `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` lines for 4 (prefer Domain-built SQL filters — the one raw repository comparison left is Gap 97 finding 5), 5+6 (migrate in steps with `[Obsolete]` bridges) and 13; `TESTING_GUIDE.md` notes for 8, 9, 10, 14, 15; 7 already guarded.

**Principle demonstrated:** a rule copied "just for a quick check" becomes a second rule. Giving time a type (`Moment`, `TimeSlot`) and placement one home (`BlockPlacementChecker`) turned most copies into code that no longer compiles; where the compiler can't reach (DTOs, test fakes, validator wording), a test that fails on drift holds the line.

### Write-up as planned and built (moved from `OPEN_GAPS.md` at close)


**Folded in 2026-09-29 (architect's call):** Gap 90 (blocks and sessions hold a `TimeSlot`) and Gap 99 (`Moment`) are the same question as this gap — *what is time in this app*: a point (`Moment`), a span and its rules (`TimeSlot`, placement), and who holds them. Designed separately, their order went circular (`TimeSlot` should be built on `Moment`; `Moment` first would retype every scattered `b.Start` comparison that this gap then moves into `TimeSlot`). **One design, one interface review — still built in phases, each compiled, tested and committed separately** (Rule 3, headlight management); `Moment` and `TimeSlot` stay two value objects (Design Review Rule 2). Their write-ups are kept below under their own headings. **Phase order approved 2026-09-29** (see Phases below). Gap 99's Q1–Q3 decisions stand. **Interface review completed 2026-09-29** (see "Interface review" below). **Phases 0–6 built 2026-09-30** (see their lines under Phases; 2–6 uncommitted — held until the gap closes, architect's call). **Next: phase 7.**

**Standards check of today's code (2026-09-30, read fresh from the code, not the plan):** every breach in this area is already tracked, with one exception.
- Tracked: 16 files compare raw `Start`/`End` outside the Domain (all on `RawTimeFieldRuleTests`, tagged by gap); 12 files convert UTC↔local themselves (all on `LocalTimeRuleTests`); the ViewModel's own overlap and work-hours checks (table #6, #7, #9, #10); validators copying `FocusBlock.Create`'s rules and "has it started" written two ways (Gaps 88/93); `PlannerPageViewModel`'s 3-second timer (Gap 89, not this gap).
- **Verified in the code — the three Domain answers disagree at the exact end instant:** `IsActive` (`at < EffectiveEnd`) says not active, `HasEnded` (`End < asOf`) says not ended, `Classify`'s Missed (`asOf >= End`) says missed. Fixed by phase 3's half-open rule + parity test.
- **New (added to phase 9):** `WorkHours.ContainsRange` duplicates `ContractDay.Contains` inside the Domain.

**Overview in plain terms (for resuming):** the app has no single source of truth for *time*. Three questions get re-answered in many places — what time a block occupies (planned, real, or displayed), may Focus time go here (~7 copies, disagreeing), does it fit the contract's hours — on top of one `DateTime` meaning both "a moment" and "a time on the user's clock" (Gaps 65/87/96). The fix gives time one home and makes the wrong code impossible to write: `Moment` (a point, UTC, no calendar methods), `UserTimeZone` (the only moment ↔ clock-time conversion), `TimeSlot` (a span that answers questions but hides its raw fields outside the Domain, and hands the database the same rules as filters), `BlockPlacementChecker` (the one placement rulebook). Phases 0–9 below, each its own tested commit.

**The problem:** three rules about time are each copied into up to four places, including the UI:
1. **What does a block occupy?** Scheduled time (`Start`–`End`), real time (`Start`–`EffectiveEnd`) or display time (trimmed at both ends) — different callers use different ones.
2. **May Focus time go here?** No Focus over Focus; Background may overlap anything; a running spontaneous session also occupies time.
3. **Does it fit the contract's window?** `NenContract.AllowsBlockPlacement` → `ContractDay.Contains` (the real rule).

**Where they live today:**

| # | Where | Rule | Problem |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `PlannedBlockRepository.GetOverlappingAsync` (create/move/split/seal) | 1, 2 | Focus-only is a `ritualOnly` flag each caller must pass to the repository |
| 2 | `CreatePlannedBlockCommandHandler` / `UpdatePlannedBlockCommandHandler` | 2 (running session) | Same overlap formula written twice; split and seal don't check the session at all |
| 3 | `StartSessionCommandHandler` | 1, 2 | Own inline check |
| 4 | `GetSuggestedRedemptionPlacementsQueryHandler` (Cover Deficit) | 1, 2 | Scheduled time, cancelled blocks counted as taken, running session ignored — suggestions disagree with create (the freed tail of an early end, cancelled blocks) and can land on a running session |
| 5 | `PlannedBlock.OverlapsWith` (seal: new blocks vs each other) | 1 | Correct (scheduled time for brand-new blocks); should share the formula |
| 6 | `PlannerDragMath.WouldCollide` (draft form save only — the "drag drop" caller was already gone) | 1, 2 | UI math on display times, ignores the running session, calls `ToUniversalTime()` directly — **deleted in phase 8** |
| 7 | `PlannerPageViewModel.FindSplitOverlap` (Split form warning) | 1, 2 | ViewModel math on display times, counts Background blocks as conflicts — **deleted in phase 8** |
| 8 | `NenContract.AllowsBlockPlacement` | 3 | ✅ the real rule |
| 9 | `PlannerPageViewModel.FindSplitWorkHoursViolation` | 3 | A ViewModel "mirror" of #8 — **deleted in phase 8**, with its `GetContractWorkHoursForDateQuery` |
| 10 | `PlannerPageViewModel.ValidateDraftPlacement` (drafting) | 3 + past + date range | Re-implements #8 — no contract exists yet while drafting |
| 11 | `SplitPlannedBlockValidator:120`, `UpdatePlannedBlockValidator:61` | "has it started?" | Written two ways; no `PlannedBlock.HasStarted` (area-1 finding D) |
| 12 | `CreditedWindow`, `WindowOn`, `PlannedFocusHoursCalculator`, `DayRangeSplitter`, several queries | — | `(DateTime Start, DateTime End)` tuples: values that travel together |

**Agreed design (interrogated 2026-09-29, by `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` → Where a Rule Lives):**
- **`TimeSlot`** (value object) — always real instants (UTC). `Overlaps`, `Contains`, `Length`, `HasStarted(asOf)`, `HasEnded(asOf)`, `StartsIn(asOf)`, `Covering(start, minutes)` (static; see below), *(Splitting into calendar days moved to `UserTimeZone.SplitByDay(slot)` in the interface review — calendar knowledge, not a span rule; see "Interface review" below.)* **Half-open `[Start, End)` is the one boundary convention**, written only here: at exactly `End` the slot has ended and the next may start. Callers never compare `Start`/`End` themselves again.
- **`PlannedBlock.Scheduled` / `ReservedSlot(asOf)`** (entity) — named as Gap 90 will need them (`Scheduled` computed from `Start`/`End` here, stored there; factories unchanged). `IsActive`, `HasEnded`, `OverlapsWith` and `MarkersPassedBetween` are rebuilt on these slots in phase 3's first commit, full suite green before anything else switches — so no Domain question about a block's time compares raw fields. Same commit: `FocusBlock.EffectiveEnd`'s missed check (`asOf >= End`, `FocusBlock.cs:120`) and `Classify`'s Missed (`asOf >= scheduledEnd`, `FocusBlockOutcomeClassifier.cs:49`) ask `Scheduled.HasEnded(asOf)`; `IsUnresolved` already asks `HasEnded`; its SQL mirror (`GetUnresolvedFocusBlocksAsync`) is replaced by a Domain-built filter (interface review, option C), so the half-open change reaches SQL by construction and that SQL parity test retires. (`EffectiveEnd`'s own cancellation arithmetic computes an end, it isn't a question — it stays.) The plan vs what it really occupies (`EffectiveEnd`: early end, missed, cancelled); `ReservedSlot` null when it occupies nothing. **`HasStarted(asOf)`** next to `HasEnded`; `HasEnded` moves from `End < asOf` to `End <= asOf`. **`Session`'s slot** (`StartedAt`–`EndAt`) for every active session — a running planned session duplicates its block's slot harmlessly, so no "which kind of session" case.
- **`BlockPlacementChecker`** (the one domain service — needs blocks, sessions and a contract, owned by none):
  - `Check(slot, type, contract?, blocks, sessions, asOf, timeZone) → PlacementVerdict` — not in the past → inside the contract's window (`NenContract.AllowsBlockPlacement` — same rule; takes `(slot, timeZone)` from phase 4) → free for Focus. `Allowed` or the **first** failure (`PlacementOutcome` + the conflicting slot when `TimeTaken`). Background skips only the "free" step; every other step applies to both types.
  - `CheckFree(slot, blocks, sessions, asOf) → PlacementVerdict` — just "is it free" (Focus blocks' `ReservedSlot`s + active sessions' slots). Replaces the draft's `FocusTimeRules`.
- **`GetBlockPlacementVerdictQuery`** (application) — fetches only: blocks around the slot, active sessions, and the contract that applies, resolved one way for every caller (including redemption attribution via `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQuery`); hands them to `BlockPlacementChecker.Check`. No rules. The repository only fetches; `ritualOnly` goes.
- **Callers:**
  - Create / Update / Split validators and the Planner drop ask `GetBlockPlacementVerdictQuery` (Split once per half) — preview and save run the same code. Split's own rules (same days as the original, halves add up, halves don't overlap each other) stay in its validator, but ask `TimeSlot` (`Length`, `Overlaps`) — today the halves-vs-each-other check is a raw formula (`SplitPlannedBlockValidator:143`), switched in phase 3. Split's halves now also get the "not in the past" check (today only the original is checked) — a small new rejection, pinned by a test.
  - Seal / Edit contract call `BlockPlacementChecker.Check` directly with the in-memory contract; `CheckFocusBlockBatchOverlapQuery` (the calendar check they use today, via `ritualOnly`) is replaced by `CheckFree` and deleted. New blocks vs each other (`ContractBlockBatchPreparer`) use `TimeSlot.Overlaps` on scheduled slots.
  - Start Session calls `BlockPlacementChecker.CheckFree`.
  - **Cover Deficit has no search (decided 2026-09-29).** The suggestion only pre-fills the block form (`PlannerPageViewModel.OpenRedemptionCreateFormAsync`), so it starts at the next quarter hour from now, length = the needed minutes rounded up (`TimeSlot.Covering`, keeping Gap 33's guarantee) — regardless of what is already there. The user places it; Save (Create → `BlockPlacementChecker.Check`) says if it overlaps or falls outside the contract. The search (shift earlier, skip conflicts, partial fallback) and `CanFullyCover` (unused by the UI) are deleted. `BlockPlacementChecker` stays pure — rules only (`Check`, `CheckFree`), no suggestion strategy; if a smarter suggestion is ever wanted, it is its own domain service that asks `CheckFree`, never a method on `BlockPlacementChecker` (it changes for UX reasons, not rule reasons). A ghost-tile placement for Cover Deficit was discussed and parked — not raised as a gap yet.
- **UI** — a query **per drop only** (as today), no cached slots, through `IQueryDispatcher` (slice folder `Queries/GetBlockPlacementVerdict/`). `WouldCollide`, `FindSplitOverlap`, `FindSplitWorkHoursViolation` deleted (ViewModel Forbidden Zone). **As built (2026-09-30):** no live query replaced them. The Split dialog keeps one live warning, "the halves overlap each other" via `TimeSlot.Overlaps` (the rule its validator asks, no database); clashes, past and contract hours are answered on Confirm. Draft tiles get no overlap check before Seal: they aren't blocks yet, so Seal's `Check` on the whole calendar is the one chokepoint (Gap 68 builds on that).
- **Parity test** — `IsActive`, `HasEnded` and `Classify`'s Missed agree at the exact `End` instant.
- **Started blocks are locked (rule change, decided 2026-09-29).** Once `PlannedBlock.HasStarted(asOf)`, a block (Focus or Background) cannot be edited (no end, no preset, no move/resize), and cannot be deleted **while it is running** (started, not ended). **Refined 2026-09-29:** an *ended* ordinary block can still be deleted — deleting history ends nothing early, and it keeps today's deliberate cleanup (`DeletePlannedBlockValidator`'s comment, the snapshot view's `CanDeleteSnapshotBlock`); contract blocks stay undeletable forever, as today. It ends early only two ways: the session's End Early button, or breaking its Nen Contract (a deliberate, recorded act, only possible with `AllowCancellation` — still cancels running blocks immediately via `MarkCancelled`/`EffectiveEnd`, unchanged). A started Background block otherwise runs to its end. Adding to the block's preset stays allowed — the block only holds `BlockPresetId` and `PolicyEngine` reads the preset's current contents each tick; the preset itself owns "only stricter while locked" (`BlockPreset.IsAtLeastAsStrict`, Gap 30). Replaces today's documented "bottom-resize allowed once started (GCal behaviour)" (`UI_UX_STANDARDS.md` → Planner Drag Interactions, `IsTileStartLocked`) and the started-block branch in `UpdatePlannedBlockValidator:54-65`; `DeletePlannedBlockValidator` gains the same guard. UI: a started tile is fully locked (no bottom-resize in `PlannerPageView.axaml.cs`, no edit form); `IsTileStartLocked` merges into the full lock; the ViewModel's own `dto.Start <= now` (`PlannerPageViewModel.cs:1488`) goes — the block DTO from `GetPlannedBlocksForDayQuery` carries `HasStarted`, computed by `PlannedBlock.HasStarted(now)`, and the ViewModel only reads it. Consequence for `Check`: its "not in the past" step needs no exception. **Checks that collapse into `HasStarted` (found 2026-09-29, see Gap 93):** `IsTileStartLocked`/`IsStartLocked` (`Start <= now` in the ViewModel), Split's "only a future block" (`Start > now`), every "is the active session's originating block" check (Delete validator, `IsBlockLocked`, `CanDeleteFromForm`, `CanSplitBlock`, `IsTileFullyLocked` — a planned session only starts once its block has), `IsCompleted` inside those locks, and `HasEnded` inside `IsTileFullyLocked`/the Update guard. So the drag lock and the form lock become the same flag (today they differ on ended blocks), and `UpdatePlannedBlockValidator:30-32`'s comment (claims to mirror a Delete "ended" guard that doesn't exist) is rewritten.
- **The candidate is just a slot and a type.** Callers pass only the blocks that will exist in the calendar: Update fetches without the block being moved (today's `excludeId`), Edit contract fetches without its own old blocks, which it is about to replace (today's `ExcludeContractId`). That describes the future calendar — not a placement rule — so `BlockPlacementChecker` needs no `replaces`/exclude parameter.
- **Module-standards check (2026-09-29):** `IBlockPlacementChecker` in `Domain/Services/` (like `IPolicyEngine`), approved before any implementation — 2 members. `GetBlockPlacementVerdictQuery` returns a DTO (`Outcome`, `Message?`), never a `TimeSlot`. *(`ConflictStart?`/`ConflictEnd?` dropped 2026-09-30: they were for the UI to name the block in the way, and the UI no longer asks the query itself. If the message should ever name its time, `BlockPlacementVerdictDto.From` can word it from `PlacementVerdict.Conflict`, given the time zone, with Gap 100.)* **The query owns the wording** — validators (which already ask it) fail with `dto.Message` via `CustomAsync`, and the Planner shows the same `dto.Message`, so preview and save agree on the text as well as the verdict; the ViewModel formats nothing. *(Changed 2026-09-30, phase 8: the wording lives in `BlockPlacementVerdictDto.From(verdict)`, which the query and Split's validator both call. Split asks the checker directly, once per half, so it doesn't run attribution per half (Gap 106), and prefixes "First block: " / "Second block: ". Gap 100 moves the sentences to the front end.)* Seal/Edit contract (which call the checker directly with an in-memory contract) keep their own batch sentences — a different situation, not a copy. `ISystemClock.TimeZone` supplies the user's `UserTimeZone` (the Domain cannot read the machine's zone). The Gap 33 rounding test must stay green through phase 7. Out of scope, noted for Gap 89: "is this preset locked?" (active session or `HasFutureBlocksForPresetAsync`) is computed inline in both the Update and Delete preset validators — `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md` calls that deliberate until a third caller, but the Forbidden Zone's hard rule says the second caller already requires a named method. Doc drift (Interface-First Rule said `Application/Interfaces/` only) fixed 2026-09-29.

**Interface (proposed 2026-09-29; approved in the interface review below, with `Moment`/`UserTimeZone` types):**
```csharp
// Domain/Services/IBlockPlacementChecker.cs
public interface IBlockPlacementChecker
{
    // In order, first failure wins: slot.HasStarted(asOf) → InThePast;
    // contract?.AllowsBlockPlacement(slot, timeZone) false → OutsideContractHours;
    // Focus only: CheckFree. blocks/sessions = the calendar as it will be.
    PlacementVerdict Check(TimeSlot slot, BlockPresetType type, NenContract? contract,
        IEnumerable<PlannedBlock> blocks, IEnumerable<Session> sessions, Moment asOf, UserTimeZone timeZone);

    // Allowed, or TimeTaken with the first Focus time in the way: a FocusBlock's
    // ReservedSlot(asOf) or an active Session's ReservedSlot. Background blocks never clash.
    PlacementVerdict CheckFree(TimeSlot slot,
        IEnumerable<PlannedBlock> blocks, IEnumerable<Session> sessions, Moment asOf);
}
```
- **`PlacementVerdict`** (`ValueObjects/`) — `Outcome` + `TimeSlot? Conflict`; constructor rule: `Conflict` is set exactly when the outcome is `TimeTaken`; `static Allowed`. One result for both methods — no separate conflict type.
- **`PlacementOutcome`** (`Enums/`, agreed 2026-09-29) — `Allowed`, `InThePast`, `OutsideContractHours`, `TimeTaken`. Block-vs-session was dropped as a distinction: both mean "pick another time", a running planned session and its own block occupy the same time (so which one got reported would be arbitrary), and the conflict slot's end still gives "plan after 15:00". Start Session's two messages ("starts in N minutes" vs "active now — complete the Ritual") come from `conflict.StartsIn(now)`, not the kind.
- **`TimeSlot.HasStarted(asOf)`** (`Start <= asOf`) — the one "past" rule: `Check`'s first step and `PlannedBlock.HasStarted` (phase 0's lock) both ask it. **`TimeSlot.StartsIn(asOf)`** → `TimeSpan`, for Start Session's "a block starts in N minutes"; replaces `PlannedBlock.MinutesUntilStart`.
- **`Session.ReservedSlot`** — `StartedAt`–`EndAt` while `Active`, else null; no `asOf` (depends only on state, unlike a block's `EffectiveEnd`).
- Parameters in the `IPolicyEngine.ComputeSlice` style (raw entities, `asOf` passed in). A parameter object (blocks + sessions + contract + clock) was considered and rejected: a type with no rule of its own. Registered `AddScoped` in the Application `DependencyInjection.cs` beside `IPolicyEngine`.

**Interface review (started 2026-09-29, after the fold):**
- **Finding 1 — plan corrected:** `DayRangeSplitter` splits *calendar* times (`.Date`; all three callers pass local values), so it can't move into a `Moment`-typed `TimeSlot`. It stays until phase 4 replaces it.
- **Approved — `UserTimeZone`, the one conversion rule** (named `UserCalendar` until 2026-09-30; renamed because "calendar" already means `ContractCalendar` and the Planner, and the type is a time zone's rules, not a calendar; `ISystemClock.Calendar` → `TimeZone`). Putting the rule on `Moment` with a zone passed in was rejected: a moment knows nothing about zones (the zone owns how instants map onto its calendar, DST included), and it left two doors (`moment.ToCalendarTime(zone)` plus shallow pass-throughs on `ISystemClock`). Instead:
  ```csharp
  // Domain/ValueObjects/UserTimeZone.cs
  public sealed record UserTimeZone
  {
      public UserTimeZone(TimeZoneInfo zone);
      public DateTime ToCalendarTime(Moment moment);
      public Moment ToMoment(DateTime calendarTime);            // DST: today's ToUtc behaviour, pinned by tests
      public DateOnly DateOf(Moment moment);                    // replaces the ToLocalDate extension
      public (DateTime Start, DateTime End) ToCalendarTimes(TimeSlot slot);   // display (added with option C)
      public IEnumerable<(DateOnly Date, TimeSlot Part)> SplitByDay(TimeSlot slot);   // phase 4
  }
  // Application/Interfaces/ISystemClock.cs (final shape)
  public interface ISystemClock
  {
      Moment Now { get; }
      UserTimeZone TimeZone { get; }   // only SystemClock builds it from TimeZoneInfo.Local (architecture test)
  }
  ```
  Where a Rule Lives: values with a real owner → value object; `ISystemClock` becomes pure coordination (2 members). Earned (Design Review Rule 6): it replaces `ISystemClock.ToLocal`/`ToUtc`, the `ToLocalDate` extension, `DayRangeSplitter` and a `TimeZoneInfo` parameter that would have been a second conversion copy. Splitting into days moved here from `TimeSlot` (calendar knowledge, Rule 2). **No `DaySegment` type** — it carried no rule; a segment is `(DateOnly Date, TimeSlot Part)`, real length `Part.Length`, local start/end via `ToCalendarTime(Part.Start)`. Tests use a fixed calendar (e.g. Copenhagen) for DST cases.
- **Approved — Finding 3, option C: nothing outside the Domain touches a slot's `Start`/`End`.** They're `internal` to the Domain. Application reads were checked (19 comparisons → `TimeSlot` questions; 14 local conversions → `UserTimeZone`; 13 DTO assignments → DTOs carry the `TimeSlot`; ordering and one subtraction → sortable + `Length`/`StartsIn`). The only raw need was EF's LINQ, so **the Domain hands the repositories ready-made filters** (as finally approved: `TimeSlot.Filters`, below) — the SQL filter *is* the Domain's expression, so the SQL copies of time rules and their parity tests go. EF maps the fields by name in `NenFocusDbContext` only. `UserTimeZone` gains `ToCalendarTimes(TimeSlot) → (DateTime Start, DateTime End)` for display. Rejected: public fields + a permanent test (A); `InternalsVisibleTo` into Infrastructure (B — a new cross-project exception, and repositories could still write formulas). **Spike (2026-09-29):** private slot fields mapped by name save and load; a filter and ordering built inside `TimeSlot` translate fully to SQL (`WHERE "b"."Start" < @end AND "b"."End" > @start … ORDER BY "b"."Start"`); the columns stay `Start`/`End` — no migration. Constraints it showed: `TimeSlot` is a `sealed record` **class** (EF stores owned types only as reference types), with a private parameterless constructor for EF. **To write into `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` when phase 3 lands:** "Repositories: narrow in SQL, decide in the entity" gains the Domain-built filter as the preferred form, ahead of a marked SQL copy with a parity test.
- **Approved — where the filters live: on `TimeSlot`, over `TimeSlot` only.** A `Domain/Filters/` folder with one class per entity was rejected: a fifth kind of home that Where a Rule Lives doesn't have, and each entity's filter would write `Start < End` again. Instead `TimeSlot.Filters` (nested) holds the SQL form of its own rules, and one generic Infrastructure helper (~10 lines, `OnSlot(b => b.Scheduled, rule)`, rewrites the expression's parameter — plumbing, no rules; Rule 2) applies them to whichever entity holds a slot. **Spike (2026-09-29):** the same `Filters.Overlaps` became correct SQL for blocks (`"b"."Start" < … AND "b"."End" > …`) and sessions with different column names (`"s"."StartedAt" < … AND "s"."EndAt" > …`); `HasEnded` → `"b"."End" <= @asOf`; `ByStart` → `ORDER BY "b"."Start"`; rows correct. Also rejected: loading everything and deciding in memory (no filters — but the 5-second heartbeat, Planner and history would load every block ever, growing forever, worse with recurring blocks), and `EF.Property(b, "Start")` in repositories (today's formula written with strings — unchecked and copied again).
- **Approved — final signatures:**
  ```csharp
  // Domain/ValueObjects/TimeSlot.cs
  public sealed record TimeSlot : IComparable<TimeSlot>
  {
      public TimeSlot(Moment start, Moment end);            // throws unless end > start
      internal Moment Start { get; }                        // Domain only (Finding 3)
      internal Moment End { get; }
      public TimeSpan Length { get; }
      public bool Overlaps(TimeSlot other);                 // half-open: touching isn't overlapping
      public bool Contains(Moment moment);                  // Start <= moment < End
      public bool HasStarted(Moment asOf);                  // Start <= asOf — the one "past" rule
      public bool HasEnded(Moment asOf);                    // End <= asOf
      public TimeSpan StartsIn(Moment asOf);
      public static TimeSlot Covering(Moment start, double minutes);   // whole minutes, rounded up (Gap 33)
      public int CompareTo(TimeSlot? other);                // by start, then end

      public static class Filters                           // the SQL form of the same rules
      {
          public static Expression<Func<TimeSlot, bool>> Overlaps(TimeSlot other);
          public static Expression<Func<TimeSlot, bool>> HasEnded(Moment asOf);
          public static Expression<Func<TimeSlot, Moment>> ByStart { get; }
      }
  }
  // Domain/ValueObjects/PlacementVerdict.cs
  public sealed record PlacementVerdict
  {
      public PlacementOutcome Outcome { get; }
      public TimeSlot? Conflict { get; }                    // set exactly when Outcome is TimeTaken
      public static PlacementVerdict Allowed { get; }
      public static PlacementVerdict Refused(PlacementOutcome outcome, TimeSlot? conflict = null);
  }
  ```
  8 public members on `TimeSlot` (the 5-member limit is for interfaces); each replaces a copied formula from the table above. Filters are written only as repositories need them. **Interface review complete.**

**Contract resolution rule the query must keep (checked 2026-09-29):** a *new* block (Create) may pick up a contract through a deficit (`ComputeRedemptionAttributionQuery` with `ContractId` null searches); an *existing* block (Update/Split) always keeps its own — attribution is scoped to its stored `ContractId`, which the query echoes back unchanged, and nothing re-parents a block on edit. So today's validators are consistent (a suspected Update mismatch was checked and ruled out); `GetBlockPlacementVerdictQuery` must resolve the same way: candidate is new → attribution search; candidate replaces an existing block → that block's `ContractId`. So the *query's* input is richer than the checker's candidate: slot, type, preset id (attribution needs it) and the id of the block being moved/split, if any — used only to fetch (leave it out of the calendar, read its contract), never passed to the checker. **The query checks the capped slot:** Create/Update shorten a redemption block to what it covers (`FocusBlock.CapEndToRedemptionCoverage`) *before* their overlap check today — deliberately, so a long drawn block whose capped length fits isn't rejected for a clash in the part that gets cut off (`UpdatePlannedBlockCommandHandler:53-55`). Moving the check into validators would check the uncapped slot and regress that. **Decided 2026-09-29: attribution returns the final slot.** `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQuery`'s result grows to `{ ContractId, Entries, Slot, WasCapped }` — it calls `FocusBlock.CapEndToRedemptionCoverage` once (the rule stays on `FocusBlock`); `GetBlockPlacementVerdictQuery` checks `attribution.Slot` for all three steps and knows nothing about capping (one job: fetch, ask, word); Create and Update handlers use `attribution.Slot`/`WasCapped` instead of each capping themselves (two copies deleted). Removes today's small inconsistency too (the contract-hours validator checks the uncapped end, the overlap check the capped one). Rounding rule to check when moving it: `CapEndToRedemptionCoverage` does `start.AddMinutes(entries.Sum(e => e.MinutesCovered))` — a `double` from `Sum(...)` into a persisted timestamp, the exact smell `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` names. Deficits have been whole minutes since Gap 44/45, so it is probably safe today, but nothing enforces it. **Decided 2026-09-29 (phase 8, when the call moves):** failing test first (fractional `MinutesCovered` in → whole-minute end out, never past the requested end), then the fix via `TimeSlot.Covering` (below). **Architecture test (Gap 98):** `RedemptionAttributionResult` is on `VerticalSliceRuleTests`' shrink-only list (a query result that commands save = a business rule in a query); when attribution moves into the Domain, delete that entry — the test goes red until you do.
- **`TimeSlot.Covering(start, minutes)`** — the shortest whole-minute slot from `start` that covers at least `minutes` (`Math.Ceiling` inside). Two callers need this exact fact: Cover Deficit's suggestion (phase 7; today `Math.Ceiling` + `AddMinutes` inline in the handler, the Gap 33 fix) and `CapEndToRedemptionCoverage` (phase 8) — so by the Forbidden Zone's second-caller rule it gets a name, in the Domain, instead of two inline copies. `CapEndToRedemptionCoverage` then returns `min(requested end, Covering(start, covered).End)` — the clamp matters: rounding up alone could push the end past what the user asked for (asked 30.5 min, covers 30.2 → 31 min). Rejected: capping inside `FocusBlock.Create` — changes when "your block was shortened" is told, and validators run before any block exists, so they'd still need the final slot from somewhere.

**Phases (approved 2026-09-29 after the fold; each compiled, tested and committed separately):**

*Transition technique:* 55 production reads of `ISystemClock.UtcNow` (+51 in tests) and ~260 raw `.Start`/`.End` reads can't flip in one step. So from phase 1 `ISystemClock` carries **both** the old members (`UtcNow`, `ToLocal`, `ToUtc`) and the new `Now`/`TimeZone` (5 members — temporary; the old three are deleted in phase 6). Where a converted part meets an unconverted one, the bridge is `ToStorage()`/`FromStorage()`, each on the storage-exit test's shrink-only list tagged with the phase that removes it.

0. **Started blocks are locked** (from Gap 88) — **built 2026-09-30**; the Delete validator's separate "active session's originating block" rule and the ViewModel's `IsEditingCompleted`/`IsEditingBlockEnded`/session checks folded into `HasStarted` as planned; `RawTimeFieldRuleTests` shrank (Split entry deleted, Update 3→2, ViewModel 4→2 — its overlap check, phase 8): `PlannedBlock.HasStarted` (plain `Start <= asOf` until phase 3 rebuilds it on the slot), Update validator (started → no edit), Delete validator (running → no delete; ended ordinary blocks still deletable), Split's "only a future block" check switched to it (#11), UI full lock, `UI_UX_STANDARDS.md` rewritten. A behaviour change on its own, first so later phases build on the simpler rule; also half of Gap 93.
1. **`Moment` foundation** (from Gap 99) — **built 2026-09-30.** Two deviations from the plan text, both small: the storage doors are `NenFocusDbContext` **and `SystemClock`** (where "now" enters from `DateTime.UtcNow` — the plan listed only the DbContext and the test helper); `UserTimeZone` reaches the raw value through `internal` members of `Moment` (Domain-only), so the public storage pair stays the only way out. `Moment` has no `ToString` override (formatting is deliberately absent), so tests register a FluentAssertions formatter in `At`. The EF converter is registered but unused until phase 3 — tested there. Parity test pins `UserTimeZone` over the machine zone to today's `ToLocal`/`ToUtc` at every quarter hour of 2026: the type + its tests, the `At.Utc(...)` test helper, the EF converter registered, `UserTimeZone` (`ToCalendarTime`, `ToMoment`, `DateOf`; DST behaviour of today's `ToUtc` pinned by tests) + its tests, `ISystemClock` gains `Now`/`TimeZone` beside the old members, the storage-exit and "only `SystemClock` reads `TimeZoneInfo.Local`" architecture tests. Nothing uses it yet — no behaviour change.
2. **`TimeSlot` on `Moment`** + its tests — **built 2026-09-30** (plus `UserTimeZone.ToCalendarTimes`). `DayRangeSplitter` stays where it is — it splits calendar times, which a `Moment` can't (corrected in the interface review).
3. **Blocks and sessions hold a `TimeSlot`** (from Gap 88 + Gap 90) — **built 2026-09-30.** Decisions made while building: block `Start`/`End` stay as `[Obsolete]` read-only views of `Scheduled` until phase 6 (option C, architect's call — each remaining use is a build warning, 63 today); factories still take `DateTime`s (UTC; a local one now throws instead of being silently shifted on save). `Session.ReservedSlot` is computed (not stored) until phase 5. The "now" chain (heartbeat → synchronizer → policy query → `PolicyEngine`, Ritual query, block markers, missed sweep) runs on one `Moment`; repository `GetForRangeAsync`/`HasFutureBlocksForPresetAsync`/`GetUnresolvedFocusBlocksAsync` take `TimeSlot`/`Moment`; contract queries' padded ranges now go through `UserTimeZone.ToMoment` (were local midnights read as UTC). Bridges: one `[Obsolete]` Application helper (`Bridges/MomentBridge`) plus `PlannedBlockRepository.AsMoment`, both on `MomentRuleTests`. `GetOverlappingAsync`/`ritualOnly` and `CheckFocusBlockBatchOverlapQuery` deleted; `MinutesUntilStart` deleted (`TimeSlot.StartsIn`). Create/Update ask `CheckFree` twice (blocks, then the running session) only to keep their two messages until phase 8. Start Session passes no sessions yet (one runs at a time; the message would be wrong — phase 8). Behaviour changes, each pinned by a test: half-open end (a block has ended at exactly its end; a session can no longer start at that instant); Seal/Edit and Split refuse Focus time over a running session. No migration (EF confirms). Test fakes: `SetNow` sets `UtcNow`+`Now`, `SetTimeZoneOffset` mirrors each `ToLocal` fake: `Scheduled` stored (`OwnsOne`, same columns, no migration) and `ReservedSlot(asOf)`; `IsActive`, half-open `HasEnded` + parity test, `HasStarted` rebuilt on `TimeSlot.HasStarted`, markers, `EffectiveEnd`, `Classify`, `PolicyEngine` take a `Moment`; `Session`'s slot; `BlockPlacementChecker.CheckFree`; create/move/split/seal/start-session switched over — split and seal now also respect a running session; callers use `clock.Now`; repositories use Domain-built filters and write no time formula of their own (option C); `ritualOnly` removed. DTOs still receive `DateTime` through tagged bridges. The largest phase — compiler-led: change a field's type, fix every red line.
4. **DST fix** (was 1b) — **built 2026-09-30.** Also fixed a bug found on the way: `ContractBlockBatchPreparer` (Seal/Edit) counted planned Focus hours per **UTC** day — every other caller converted to local first — so a block just after local midnight counted toward the previous day at Seal; the calculator now takes the time zone, so no caller can leave it out (test: `Prepare_BlockJustAfterLocalMidnight_CountsOnTheUsersDay`). `IPlannedFocusHoursCalculator.Calculate(slots, timeZone)`; `IContractBlockBatchPreparer.Prepare(…, timeZone)`; `NenContract.AllowsBlockPlacement(slot, timeZone)`; contract queries' credited windows go in as slots.
