An empty/inverted range from input (reported by another rule) counts nothing, as before, via `MomentBridge.TrySlotFromUtc`. Edit-contract tests had passed Work Hours vacuously (their fake `ToLocal` returned year 0001); they now run with a UTC zone: `UserTimeZone.SplitByDay(slot)` → `(DateOnly Date, TimeSlot Part)` on real time, replacing `DayRangeSplitter` (deleted); callers' `ToLocal` conversions before `AllowsBlockPlacement` removed; DST tests for both nights. A behaviour change (hours correct on DST nights), kept separate so any changed number traces to it.
5. **Remaining single timestamps** (from Gap 99) — **built 2026-09-30**, in three verified batches (block stamps + classifier; `Session`; attempts/contract/events/slice). Shapes decided while building: the classifier takes the block's `TimeSlot` + `Moment?` stamps and returns the credited window as a `TimeSlot?` (its "before the scheduled end" checks ask `!scheduled.HasEnded(x)`); `Session.Start(Moment, TimeSlot? plannedWindow, …)`/`RunLength(…)` take the block's slot, since a slot's end is Domain-only; `Session.Miss(TimeSlot)`; `NenContract.Seal(…, Moment sealedAt, UserTimeZone timeZone)` and `Break(Moment, UserTimeZone)` work out local time themselves — which **fixed a mix-up**: Seal and Break put a *local* time into their event's `OccurredAt` while Activate/Complete put UTC (nothing reads event times yet, so no user impact); `CancelledAt` also became a `Moment?`. `BlockPreset.Create/Duplicate` take a `Moment`. The contract's own times (`StartsAt`, `EndsAt`, `IsDueFor…(localNow)`) stay `DateTime` — they are calendar times. DTOs still get `DateTime`s through `MomentBridge.ToUtc` (phase 6). New temporary exits on `MomentRuleTests`: `SessionRepository` (UTC-days history window, Gap 97) and `SessionStateService` (DTO, phase 6). 47 obsolete-use warnings remain for phase 6. No migration (EF confirms): `RitualCompletedAt`, `SessionEndedAt`, `Session.EndedAt`, `PostSessionAcknowledgedAt`, `BlockedAttempt.OccurredAt`, `PostContractAcknowledgedAt`, domain events, `ActivePolicySlice`.
6. **DTOs carry `Moment`, the UI asks the clock** (from Gap 99) — **built 2026-09-30.** `ISystemClock` shrunk to `Now` + `TimeZone`; `SystemClock` matches; `MomentBridge` and `SystemClockExtensions.ToLocalDate` deleted; `IPlannerClockService.LocalNow` deleted (`Tick` stays — a Gap-89 concern); ViewModels (`MainPageViewModel`, `PlannerPageViewModel`, `DebugClockViewModel`) ask `clock.TimeZone`; the `DateTime` storage converter (`UtcDateTimeConverter`/`NullableUtcDateTimeConverter`) **throws** on a non-UTC save; the obsolete `PlannedBlock.Start`/`End` deleted (their ~40 test uses now read `block.Scheduled` through `At.Start`/`At.End`); the command `Slot`/`First`/`Second` fields became `required` (the compiler owns "set it", the `NotNull` validator rules dropped). Architecture-test exceptions retired: `LocalTimeRuleTests` (NenFocusDbContext, PlannerClockService) and `MomentRuleTests` (MomentBridge, SessionStateService); the two repository doors (`PlannedBlockRepository`, `SessionRepository`) stay, tagged Gap 97 (the UTC-days trap — a separate, not-interrogated gap). No migration (EF confirms).
7. **Cover Deficit simplified** (was 2) — **built 2026-09-30** (marked during phase 8, checked in code: search and `CanFullyCover` gone, the Gap 33 test kept, `TimeSlot.Covering` tested in `TimeSlotTests`): "next quarter hour, needed length" via `TimeSlot.Covering`; search and `CanFullyCover` deleted; tests pinning shift-earlier/partial/conflict-skip rewritten, the Gap 33 rounding test kept. `MODULE_STANDARDS.md`'s rounding rule names this handler's inline `Math.Ceiling` as its canonical example — repointed to `TimeSlot.Covering`.
8. **Placement checker** (was 3): `BlockPlacementChecker.Check` + `GetBlockPlacementVerdictQuery` (its DTO born with `Moment`); attribution returns the final slot (capping moved there, rounding test + `Covering` fix); Create/Update handlers' capping copies deleted; Create/Update/Split validators and the UI switched over; the handler-level `CheckFree` calls phase 3 put in Create/Update are removed (the validators now own the check — no second copy); Seal/Edit contract's inline `AllowsBlockPlacement` loops replaced by `Check`; UI checks deleted. **Built 2026-09-30** (backend in one session, UI and the rest in the next). Notes from building:
   - **The first session's backend was never run against the tests.** The test project didn't compile (21 errors in 6 files) and the verdict query had no tests. Restored before continuing: placement tests moved to a new `GetBlockPlacementVerdictQueryHandlerTests` (13) instead of being deleted, and capping tests moved to the attribution tests, where the cap now lives. It had also added a third `RedemptionEntryDto` builder, which `DtoBuilderRuleTests` caught; `RedemptionEntryDto.From(entry)` fixed it, and the exception entry was deleted.
   - **Split had no past check per half** (checked "the original hasn't started" only). A half could be moved to an hour already gone today. Found in code, pinned by a test that went red first; fixed by asking `Check` per half, which replaced four hand-picked rules with two.
   - **Seal/Edit** build the contract first, then ask `Check` per block (free, hours and past in one answer). The past used to be caught only indirectly, through the contract's start.
   - **Dropped: "rounding test + `Covering` fix" for `CapEndToRedemptionCoverage`.** The method documents a deliberate choice against rounding (`FocusBlock.cs`: coverage is stored exactly and never re-read from the block's end; an earlier flooring version lost coverage and crashed), and deficits are whole minutes since Gaps 44/45. So the plan's reason doesn't hold. The attribution test "45 min covered → a slot of exactly 45 min" pins today's behaviour. The `TimeSlot.Covering` rule above keeps its one caller.
   - **Dropped: `ConflictStart?`/`ConflictEnd?` on the verdict DTO.** Nothing would read them now that the UI checks are gone (see the standards-check line above).
   - **Guards:** `RawTimeFieldRuleTests` lost its `PlannerDragMath` entry; `ViewModelRuleTests`' formatting count for `PlannerPageViewModel` went 35 → 33.
9. **Drafting** (#10, was 4) — **moved to Gap 68 (2026-09-30)**, where drafting gains a real calendar to ask; with it the deletion of `WorkHours.ContainsRange`. Kept here would have held this gap (and its commits) open until Gap 68, several gaps later.

*Why this order:* 0 is a standalone behaviour change; 1–2 land the new types, tested, before anything depends on them; 3 before 5–6 so the comparisons move into `TimeSlot` once, already typed; 6 before 8 so the verdict DTO is never retyped; behaviour changes (0, 4, 7) each get their own commit. `ContractDay`'s local window stays `DateTime` — once moments are typed, a plain `DateTime` means calendar time.

**Lessons to settle at close-out (Step 5 — each becomes an architecture test, a `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` rule, or a one-line "why no test"; added as found, 2026-09-30):**
1. **A fake that isn't configured passes silently.** Edit-contract tests never configured `ToLocal`, got year 0001, and the Work-Hours check looked at an empty range — passing for months without checking anything. Candidate: every `ISystemClock` fake goes through one test helper (`SetNow`/`SetTimeZoneOffset`, or a `TestClock.Create()`), guarded by a test that bans a bare `Substitute.For<ISystemClock>()`.
2. **Grouping by calendar day without a time zone is a UTC-day bug waiting to happen.** `ContractBlockBatchPreparer` counted Seal/Edit hours per UTC day while every other caller converted first (phase 4). Fixed by making the calculator *require* `UserTimeZone` (compiler). Candidate test: no `DateOnly.FromDateTime(`/`.Date` on a stored time outside `UserTimeZone`.
3. **Three answers to one boundary question drift.** `IsActive`, `HasEnded` and `Classify` disagreed at the exact end instant. Rule: a boundary convention is written once (`TimeSlot`, half-open) and pinned by a parity test (done).
4. **A SQL copy of a rule needs a parity test forever; a Domain-built filter doesn't.** `TimeSlot.Filters` + `SlotQuery.OnSlot` replaced the `IsUnresolved` SQL mirror. Candidate: `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` → "narrow in SQL" prefers Domain-built filters; a test that repositories write no raw time comparison (today they're excluded from `RawTimeFieldRuleTests`).
5. **Put transition bridges in one `[Obsolete]` file.** `MomentBridge` makes every remaining use a build warning — the compiler keeps the to-do list, and the guard test lists one file instead of many. Process rule for future migrations (Guard Hierarchy note).
6. **`[Obsolete]` read-only pass-throughs let a type change land in verified steps** (option C). Same end state as a one-shot change; each leftover is a warning. Process rule.
7. **A validator's "skip if another rule reports it" guard shouldn't compare raw fields** — use a try-constructor (`TrySlotFromUtc`) so the check stays with the type. Candidate: covered by `RawTimeFieldRuleTests` already (it caught this).
8. **A test written after its fix proves nothing until probed.** Every post-hoc test in this gap was probed red by breaking the code on purpose. Already in Step 5; worth repeating in `TESTING_GUIDE.md` for bug-fix tests.
9. **A type change the assertion library can't see compiles and fails only at runtime.** `block.RitualCompletedAt.Should().Be(someDateTime)` still compiled after the field became `Moment?` (FluentAssertions' `Be(object)`), failing 9 tests with "expected 12:00:00, found 12:00:00Z". Harmless here because the tests ran, but the same shape could make an assertion quietly pass on a wrong type elsewhere. Candidate: a test-code rule that expected values match the subject's type (or prefer typed assertions); at minimum, note it in `TESTING_GUIDE.md`.
10. **Bulk text edits must match the file's line endings.** A deletion that matched `\n` instead of `\r\n` left an orphan `\r`, which the compiler counts as a line break — shifting every later line number and breaking position-based tooling. Not an architecture rule; a tooling note for `TESTING_GUIDE.md`/dev notes: normalise or match `\r?\n`, and check `file` output after scripted edits.
11. **A field named for a moment silently held a calendar time.** `NenContract.Seal`/`Break` raised events whose `OccurredAt` was a *local* time while every other event's was UTC — nothing could tell them apart until the type did (phase 5). Guarded now by the compiler (`DomainEvent.OccurredAt` is a `Moment`); lesson for close-out: when a value's meaning is "a moment", type it — a naming convention alone didn't hold.
12. **A test fake missing one member fails loudly only if the code reaches it.** Three test classes broke in phase 5 only because the handler newly asked `clock.TimeZone`; before, a `ToLocal` fake returning year 0001 passed silently (lesson 1). Same candidate guard as lesson 1.
13. **`required` beats a `null!` lie.** A non-nullable reference field defaulting to `= null!` is a compiler hole (the annotation says non-null, the value can be null), so the real enforcement was a validator `NotNull` rule. `required` makes the compiler own "must be set" (Guard Hierarchy rank 1), and the `NotNull` rule disappears. Candidate: `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` says a genuinely-required reference field on a command uses `required`, not `null!` + a validator rule (phase 6 applied it to `Slot`/`First`/`Second`).
14. **"Delete X.Y" deletes the member, not the type.** Phase 6e over-deleted `IPlannerClockService` when only `LocalNow` was in scope, and had to restore it — its `Tick` heartbeat is a separate Gap-89 concern the task left in place. Tooling note: read the exact member the plan names before deleting.
15. **A text-search replacement must not match the tail of an alias.** Replacing `At.PlannedWindowEnding(` matched the trailing `At` of `TestAt`, mangling `TestAt.PlannedWindowEnding(block.End)` into `Testblock.Scheduled`. Tooling note: anchor to a word boundary (`\bAt\.`) or account for the `Test`-prefixed alias.

**On close:** `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md` gains terse rows for `TimeSlot`, `BlockPlacementChecker`, `PlacementVerdict`/`PlacementOutcome` and the started-block lock (pointing back here); the story goes to `DESIGN_GAP_HISTORY.md`; `TECHNICAL_SPEC_INFRA.md` gets one "Done" line.

**Not now:** replacing every `(Start, End)` tuple in credit/planned-hours code — only as those files are touched. The Planner tile's "actual worked window" (late start / early end trim, computed inline in `GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandler`) becomes a `FocusBlock` method returning a `TimeSlot` once a second caller needs it (area-1 finding G).

**Last step — architecture tests (Gap 98):** add the narrow rules this gap makes true — *only `BlockPlacementChecker` decides placement* (no `OverlapsWith` call outside the Domain; no `ritualOnly` anywhere). Delete the exception entries it retires, each tagged "Gap 88" in the tests: `RawTimeFieldRuleTests` (day-view display end, Cover Deficit and drag overlap checks, Create/Update/Seal/Edit validators), `LocalTimeRuleTests` (Create/Update handlers' clash messages), `VerticalSliceRuleTests` (`RedemptionAttributionResult`). **Also:** this gap creates the typed-reason mechanism (a refusal is data, not a sentence — `MODULE_STANDARDS.md`, Gap 100), so it adds the test *"validators don't write sentences"* with every still-sentence-writing validator on a shrink-only list as "File has N" (~108 messages today); Gap 93 and Gap 100 empty it. Exact test form decided in the interface review. **Built 2026-09-30** (each new test probed red with a deliberate violation):
   - `PlacementRuleTests`: no `Overlaps`/`OverlapsWith` outside the Domain (wider than planned: `TimeSlot.Overlaps` too, which is how `FindSplitOverlap` drifted; `TimeSlot.Filters` excepted; Split's halves-vs-each-other listed in the validator and the dialog), and no `ritualOnly` anywhere.
   - `ValidatorWordingRuleTests`: 17 validator files, 94 sentences, shrink-only; the test form (count literal-writing `WithMessage`/`AddFailure` calls per file) decided with the architect that day, since the interview hadn't recorded one. Gap 93's three permission validators tagged to it, the rest to Gap 100 R1.
   - Exception entries: the `RawTimeFieldRuleTests`/`LocalTimeRuleTests` entries tagged Gap 88 were already gone. `VerticalSliceRuleTests`' `RedemptionAttributionResult` was **re-tagged to Gap 106**, not deleted: attribution is still a query whose answer commands save.
   - Folded Gap 90: `ActiveSessionMonitor`'s session length and break progress moved onto `TimeSlot` (`SinceStart`, `RemainingAt`, `ProgressAt`) and `SessionBreak` (`RemainingAt`, `ProgressAt`), test-first, and its entry was deleted. `RawTimeFieldRuleTests` itself was kept (see Gap 90's last step below; → Gap 89 area 4 F7).
   - Folded Gap 99: already done (storage refuses non-UTC; `NenFocusDbContext` is the `Moment` storage door).

#### Folded in 2026-09-29 — Gap 90: Blocks and Sessions Hold a `TimeSlot` (raised 2026-09-29 during Gap 88's interrogation; direction agreed, not interrogated; after Gap 88)

**Why it's a gap:** Gap 88 puts every rule about time into `TimeSlot` and makes `PlannedBlock`'s own methods (`IsActive`, `HasEnded`, `OverlapsWith`, markers) ask its slots — but `Start`/`End` stay public loose fields, so a new handler can still write `block.End > now` and bring back a second boundary convention; only review and the Forbidden Zone rule stop it. `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` → Where a Rule Lives, step 3: "the same primitives keep appearing together (`Start` + `End`) and the same formula keeps being written on them" → a value object the entity holds, like `Session` holds `BreakSchedule`. Fixes no known bug (Gap 88 does); makes the wrong comparison impossible to write.

**Direction:** `PlannedBlock.Scheduled` (a `TimeSlot`) becomes stored instead of computed from `Start`/`End`; `Reschedule(TimeSlot)` swaps the whole slot, so `End > Start` is checked once, in `TimeSlot`'s constructor (today `Reschedule` and `TrimEnd` each check it). EF: `OwnsOne` with the same column names (no migration), the UTC conversions (`NenFocusDbContext.cs:300-314`) moved onto it; repository queries switch from `b.Start` to Domain-built filters (option C, interface review), including the SQL that mirrors `IsUnresolved`. `Session` follows (`StartedAt`/`EndAt`). About 100 raw `.Start`/`.End` reads to convert (≈20 Domain, 60 Application, 20 Infrastructure, 2 UI). Folds in Gap 88's "Not now" note (credit/planned-hours tuples).

**Why after Gap 88, not first or together:** C-first would convert formulas Gap 88 deletes (running-session overlap, Cover Deficit conflict check, `WouldCollide`). Together mixes rules, the enforcement path and persistence in one change. Gap 88 names things as this gap needs (`block.Scheduled`, `ReservedSlot(asOf)`, factories unchanged), so the rework here is a handful of lines.

**Still to interrogate:** whether `Session` moves in the same gap or its own; whether `Start`/`End` survive as read-only pass-throughs during the transition or go in one step; factory signatures (`FocusBlock.Create` taking a slot vs two `DateTime`s).

**Last step (Gap 98):** delete `RawTimeFieldRuleTests` — it stands in for this gap's compiler guard (Guard Hierarchy). Before deleting, check its remaining entries: anything still listed for a later gap (93, 89) needs a narrower guard of its own first. *(Changed 2026-09-30, Gap 88's last step: **kept, not deleted.** The compiler now guards entities (`TimeSlot`'s `Start`/`End` are internal), but not DTOs: their public `Moment`s can still be subtracted, which is how `ActiveSessionMonitor` held its own session length until that day. The test's header now says it watches DTO time fields; Gap 89 area 4 F7 narrows it to single moments.)*

#### Folded in 2026-09-29 — Gap 99: Moments vs Calendar Times as Types (raised 2026-09-29 during Gap 98; direction preferred, form not decided; decided at the start of Gap 88's interface review)

**Why it's a gap:** one type, `DateTime`, means two different things — **a moment** (a point in time, stored UTC: when a session started) and **a calendar time** (what the user sees and types: 09:00 on their calendar). Nothing but discipline tells them apart, and it has cost three fixes already: Gap 65 (contract activation on UTC dates), Gap 87 (Cover Deficit local time), Gap 96 (spontaneous sessions on UTC days). Found while building Gap 98 rule 6: EF's storage converter (`NenFocusDbContext.UtcDateTimeConverter`) **silently converts** any non-UTC value on save, treating an unmarked one as local — a hidden second copy of the local→UTC rule that hides a mix-up instead of failing on it.

**Options weighed 2026-09-29:**
1. **Plain `DateTime`, rule by discipline** — `TimeSlot` UTC-only (Gap 88) plus tests. Cheap, but only start/end pairs are guarded; single timestamps (`StartedAt`, `RitualCompletedAt`, `SessionEndedAt`, `CancelledAt`, `PostSessionAcknowledgedAt`) stay unprotected.
2. **A moment gets its own always-UTC type** — the compiler refuses a local time where a moment is expected; only `ISystemClock` turns a calendar time into a moment. **Preferred:** one source of truth (no second conversion can be written), deep module (callers never handle "is this UTC?"), DDD (make the hidden concept explicit), Guard Hierarchy rank 1. Bigger: every stored timestamp, EF mappings, DTOs.

**To decide (first thing in Gap 88's interface review, so `TimeSlot` is built on it from day one):** which form of option 2 — our own `Instant` value object (simplest, rule in our Domain), `DateTimeOffset` (built in, but carries an offset rather than guaranteeing UTC), or NodaTime (most complete model of moments and calendar times; a new dependency in every layer). Also takes over Gap 92 V5: local windows (`ContractDay`, `WorkHours`, `ChangeWindow`) are the calendar-time side of the same question.

**Last step:** once stored times are guaranteed UTC by type, the storage converter stops converting and throws on a non-UTC value; delete `NenFocusDbContext` from Gap 98's local-time rule exceptions list.

**Form decided 2026-09-29 (survey + spike): our own value object, named `Moment`** (the ubiquitous-language word; "Instant" is NodaTime's).
- **Survey:** 13 stored moments on 5 entities (+ ~6 on events/`ActivePolicySlice`), ~63 Domain time parameters, ~54 `DateTime` members on commands/queries/DTOs, ~10 SQL range comparisons/orderings, test churn ~500 lines in 91 files. The calendar side is mostly typed already (`DateOnly`, `TimeOnly`); the exception is `ContractDay.WindowStart/End`, local times in a `DateTime`. Mix-ups found: `PlannerPageViewModel` reuses `PlannedBlockDto` (UTC) to carry local tile times; `Create/UpdatePlannedBlockCommandHandler` call `.ToUniversalTime()` on input "just in case", so the commands accept either meaning.
- **Why `Moment`:** Where a Rule Lives puts it in a value object (values, no identity; the same conversion keeps appearing). It carries a constructor rule and is used widely (Design Review Rule 6). It's Guard Hierarchy rank 1, and it has no `.Date`, which is the exact bug behind Gaps 65/87/96.
- **Rejected:** `DateTimeOffset` — SQLite supports only equality on it, so range comparisons and ordering would run in memory ([EF Core SQLite limitations](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/ef/core/providers/sqlite/limitations)), and it doesn't guarantee UTC. NodaTime — would put a dependency into the Domain ("no dependencies"), replace `DateOnly`/`TimeOnly` too, and the UI date pickers still need `DateTimeOffset`. Typing the calendar side instead — smaller, but `utcValue.Date` would still compile.
- **Spike:** a `Moment` stored through an EF value converter to `DateTime` keeps `<`/`>`, `IS NULL` and `ORDER BY` in SQL, and is stored in the same text format as today — **no migration, no data conversion**.
- ~~Split: Gap 99 converts single timestamps, Gap 90 `Start`/`End`~~ — withdrawn 2026-09-29: once `ISystemClock.Now` is a `Moment`, every rule comparing "now" with a `DateTime` `Start` stops compiling, so the split would need `ToStorage()` bridges everywhere (against Q1). Resolved by folding into Gap 88 (see the note at its top).

**Interrogation, Q1 decided 2026-09-29 — the exits:** `Moment` has no public `DateTime`. **DTOs carry `Moment` itself** (the UI may use Domain value objects; only entities and services are forbidden), switched field by field with the entity field behind them. So the only way to a calendar value, UI included, is `UserTimeZone.ToCalendarTime(moment)`, reached as `clock.TimeZone` (interface review) — compiler-guarded; ViewModels lose their own `ToLocalTime()` as a side effect (Gap 89's fix). The one raw exit is a storage pair (e.g. `Moment.FromStorage(DateTime)` / `ToStorage()`) for EF's converter, since the Domain can't reference EF — guarded by a test allowing only `NenFocusDbContext`. Rejected: a public `Utc` property (`moment.Utc.Date` would compile again); DTOs carrying raw `DateTime` (a test allow-list of every DTO builder where the compiler could own it).

**Q2 decided 2026-09-29 — `ISystemClock`, the only door** *(shape superseded in the interface review: the conversions moved onto `UserTimeZone`, `ISystemClock` is `Now` + `TimeZone`; the names `ToCalendarTime`/`ToMoment` and the `LocalNow` deletion below stand)*: `Moment Now { get; }` (was `DateTime UtcNow`), `DateTime ToCalendarTime(Moment moment)` (was `ToLocal`), `Moment ToMoment(DateTime calendarTime)` (was `ToUtc`) — names in the gap's own words; "local" had to be explained. `ToLocalDate` extension becomes `UserTimeZone.DateOf`. **`IPlannerClockService.LocalNow` is deleted in this gap** — a side door that converted with its own `.ToLocalTime()`; its ~15 callers in `MainPageViewModel`/`PlannerPageViewModel` ask `ISystemClock` (three of them did `LocalNow.ToUniversalTime()` just to get "now"), and `PlannerClockService.cs` leaves the local-time exceptions list. **Not merged:** `Tick` (a screen-refresh timer — changes for different reasons, Design Review Rule 2) and `IDebugClockController` (controls the clock, isn't one); their names are Gap 89's.

**Q3 decided 2026-09-29 — what a `Moment` offers:** `readonly record struct` in `Domain/ValueObjects/`. In: `Moment.FromStorage(DateTime utc)` (throws unless `Kind` is `Utc`). Out: `ToStorage()`. Comparison and ordering operators, `CompareTo`; `Moment ± TimeSpan → Moment`; `Moment − Moment → TimeSpan`. **Deliberately absent:** `.Date`, `.Hour`, `.TimeOfDay`, `.DayOfWeek`, formatting — calendar questions go through `UserTimeZone.ToCalendarTime`. **Tests** make moments through one helper in `NenFocus.Tests` (e.g. `At.Utc(2026, 9, 29, 13, 0)`), so the `FromStorage`/`ToStorage` allow-list is two files: `NenFocusDbContext` and that helper.

---

## Gap 97 — One Source for Contract Membership *(closed, 2026-10-02)*

**What was wrong:** "which contract governs this block, and how" had no single answer. Bonus was worked out as "no contract id, starts inside the period" in four places (Bonus hours, recap count, completion readiness, tile badge). "Inside the contract" meant two things (exact moments for Bonus, whole dates for sessions). A redemption attached to the oldest deficit across every contract in effect, not the contract the block sat in. A plain new block was checked against no contract's Work Hours, although `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md` said blocks can't be placed outside them. Two repositories read their day arguments as UTC days or through the machine's local time.

**Changed (phases 0–6, 2026-10-01):**
- **0** — probes: an optional `OwnsOne` membership keeping the `ContractId` column; a Domain-built "moment inside the slot" filter in SQL.
- **1** — `ContractRole` (`Committed`, `Redemption`, `Bonus`) and `ContractMembership` (id + role, made only by the Domain, role Domain-only) on `PlannedBlock.Membership`; migration `ContractMembershipRole` backfills existing rows.
- **2** — one meaning of "inside": the slot against the contract's planned period (`NenContract.Governs`, `AllowsBlockPlacement`). `BlockPlacementChecker.Check` takes the block's membership and every contract in effect and returns the governing one, so every block is checked.
- **3** — Bonus and Redemption blocks get their membership at creation and on a move (`MembershipForPlacedBlock`, `PlannedBlock.PlaceUnder`); the role is set once. The four Bonus sites read the role; the nine `GetForContractAsync` callers state which members they want; keep-blocking-during-breaks applies to every member's session. `CreatePlannedBlockCommand.ContractId` and `NenContract.Covers` deleted; attribution is asked of the governing contract only.
- **4** — Break asks the contract per block (`FateWhenBroken` → `BlockFateOnBreak`); Delete releases Bonus blocks to plain blocks.
- **5** — repositories take a `TimeSlot`: `UserTimeZone.SlotCovering`, `TimeSlot.Filters.Contains`, `ISessionRepository.GetHistoryAsync(TimeSlot, …)`; `IPlannedBlockRepository.GetForDayAsync` deleted.
- **6** — guards and dictionary rows.

**Found on the way:** the Create validator ran attribution even when the command declined redemption, so it checked a capped slot while the handler saved the full one (fixed, phase 3). One membership handed to several blocks was saved on one of them only (fixed; the block keeps its own copy). The approved `Check(contractsInEffect)` was wrong for blocks already in a contract (fixed by passing the membership). **Bridges left on purpose** (decision 17, least change): Bonus permissions stay "has a contract id" until Gap 93; editing a Scheduled contract deletes its Bonus blocks until Gap 68; spontaneous sessions still count by whole local date until Gap 67. **Spun off:** Gap 107 (what the Shell shows next), Gap 108 (the break prompt for Bonus blocks).

**Found in the app checks (2026-10-02):**
- Editing a block that existed before Seal inside the contract's period — a resize in place included — makes it a Bonus block. Accepted as known behaviour until Gap 68's adoption (decision 3).
- A spontaneous session can run across a contract's start, and one before the start on the first calendar day still counts toward it. Both belong to Gap 67's session half; recorded there as item 7.
- Built during the checks, on request: a drafted Focus block's tile shows a **COMMITTED** tag (`ContractMembership.IsCommitted`, `FocusBlock.IsCommitted`, `PlannedBlockContractBadge.Committed`), answering Gap 67's question 6. Separately, three contract presets are seeded, one per strictness rule (migration `SeedContractPresets`) — a convenience for testing, not part of this gap's design.

**Changed 2026-10-07 by Gap 106 (on purpose):**
- **Cover Deficit names its contract.** For a redemption block the client now says *which debt it pays* (`CoverDeficitCommand.ContractId`) — an exception to "which contract comes from where the slot sits, never from the client". The Domain still refuses any slot outside that contract, so the client never decides where a block may go (Gap 106, decision 12).
- **Every member is judged against its own contract.** `BlockPlacementChecker.Check` holds a Bonus block to its own contract too (`membership is not null`, was `{ IsBonus: false }`), (Gap 106, decision 17).
- **A drag never joins.** `PlannedBlock.PlaceUnder` is deleted: a plain block moved where a contract governs is refused (`ChangeOutcome.WouldJoinContract`). This replaces the accepted behaviour above ("editing a block that existed before Seal … makes it a Bonus block"): such a block can now be moved out or deleted, not moved within, until Gap 68's adoption; a preset change in place is still allowed (Gap 106, decision 18).

**Confirmed via:** the full suite (1629 passing). The architect's manual checks on 2026-10-02: 29 passed. Two could not be run and are covered by tests only — an existing contract surviving the migration (none existed), and Cover Deficit outside a contract (the form doesn't allow it). One was dropped — a just-after-midnight spontaneous session counting toward the first day — because its expectation is superseded by Gap 67.

**Guard (step 5):** yes.
- Compiler (rank 1): the role is Domain-only and a membership can only be made by the Domain (internal constructor), so nothing outside it can read or invent a role.
- New test: `MembershipRuleTests` — outside the Domain and persistence, nothing compares a block's contract id to decide membership. Probed red by writing the old "no contract id = Bonus" check back. Exceptions: the three permission validators and the Planner's copy of the Delete rule (→ Gap 93), Edit's overlap exclusion (→ Gap 68).
- New tests: `ContractMembershipPersistenceTests` (round trip, incl. one membership given to several blocks — probed red), the migration test ("no member without a role").
- Exception entries retired: the Gap 97 entries in `MomentRuleTests` and `LocalTimeRuleTests`; `IPlannedBlockRepository` 10 → 9 in `InterfaceSizeRuleTests`.
- The six close-out lessons: `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` Design Review Rule 16 (run every caller's hardest case through a signature before approving it) and a second example on Rule 12; `TESTING_GUIDE.md` — a round-trip test for every new stored value object, and "a text scan sees only the shapes it lists"; `TECHNICAL_SPEC_INFRA.md` → Database — an owned value object can't be shared between rows, and an optional one loads as null when any column is null. **Left open:** `TimeSlot` has the shared-instance trap with no guard (no production path shares one today); the Planner's draft-tile filter that `MembershipRuleTests` can't see (→ Gap 93 / Gap 89 area 2).

**Principle demonstrated:** a fact worked out from an absence ("no contract id means Bonus") gets worked out again, differently, by everyone who needs it. Stored once at the moment it is decided, with the role readable only inside the Domain, the copies stop compiling, and placement and membership come from the same comparison.

### Write-up as planned and built (moved from `OPEN_GAPS.md` at close)

**The question:** "which contract governs this block — and how?" (committed, redemption, Bonus, or none), and for spontaneous sessions "does this count toward the contract?". This is Gap 67's agreed `ContractRole` concept, currently answered piecemeal.

**Where it is answered today:**

| Relationship | Decided by | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Committed / redemption | the block's stored `ContractId` | validators, `StartSession` (`KeepBlockingDuringBreaks`), completed hours, deficits |
| Which contract a new redemption attaches to | oldest eligible deficit across **all in-effect contracts** | `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQueryHandler.cs:68-97` |
| Bonus (unlinked block inside the contract) | `contract.Covers(localStart)` — exact local moments | Bonus hours, recap count (Gap 94), completion readiness, Planner tile badge (Gap 96) — **four places** |
| Spontaneous sessions counting toward the contract | **whole local dates** (fixed from UTC days in Gap 96) | completed hours |
| Contract still in force | `NenContract.IsInEffect` — the only definition; the repository fetches and asks it (Gap 101) | Update validator, Break, tile DTO |

**Findings still open:**
1. **Two meanings of "inside the contract":** Bonus uses `Covers` (respects Work Hours on the first and last day); spontaneous sessions use whole dates. A session and a Bonus block at the same moment (e.g. before Work Hours open on day 1) are treated differently. Product question: should spontaneous sessions use `Covers`? (Gap 67 may stop counting them at all.)
2. **"Is this block Bonus" in four places** (above), each with its own type filter and pre-fetch range.
3. **A redemption can attach to "some" contract:** attribution searches every in-effect contract, not the one covering the block's date; whether `CheckRedemptionEligibility` already prevents a mismatch is not yet traced.
4. ~~**`IsInEffect` copied in SQL** without a parity test.~~ **Closed 2026-10-01 — already fixed by Gap 101:** `NenContractRepository` loads the contracts and asks `IsInEffect`; no SQL copy is left. Decided to keep that form (few rows; `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` → Repositories). Upgrade path if the row count ever matters: one expression declared on `NenContract`, used by both the database filter and `IsInEffect` (needs an EF Core probe first). Rejected: a date filter — an Active contract past its end must stay visible to the lifecycle sweep, which finds its work through `GetAllInEffectAsync`. **Still open, same shape:** "Completed or Broken" is written in SQL in `GetUnacknowledgedPostContractsAsync` and twice on the entity (`SetReflection`, `MarkPostContractAcknowledged`) with no named question — moved to Gap 103 (2026-10-01), built once on the ended moment that gap adds.

**Noted during interrogation 2026-10-01 (not decided):** `Covers` takes a local time, so each of the four Bonus sites converts the block's start itself. Candidate: the contract answers in `Moment`s from its planned period, worked out from `ContractCalendar` when asked (never stored — it would be a second source for `StartsAt`/`EndsAt`); other readers would be Gap 103's overlap check and Gap 105's start rule.
5. **`ISessionRepository.GetHistoryAsync(DateOnly, DateOnly)` reads its dates as UTC days** — the trap behind Gap 96; its next caller (Insights `GetSessionHistoryQuery`, no UI caller yet) would repeat it. Should take UTC instants, or local dates plus the clock. Its sibling `PlannedBlockRepository` (the day query) gets the local day right but with its own `ToUniversalTime()` copy of `ISystemClock.ToUtc` — on Gap 98's local-time rule exceptions list; fix both the same way.
6. `PlannerPageViewModel` chooses "the" contract itself (`_contractsThisWeek`, `SelectedContract` for Cover Deficit) — mostly UI selection; the rest of its contract logic is Gap 93.
7. **Found in Gap 89 Pass 2 (U4, 2026-09-29):** `StartSessionCommandHandler:127` decides which contract's keep-blocking rule applies with `originatingBlock.ContractId is {}` — the "Bonus = no `ContractId`" definition this gap replaces with a role. One more caller `ContractRole` must answer.

**Direction (for interrogation, not decided):** `NenContract` answers "what is this block to me" (`Committed | Redemption | Bonus | None` — Gap 67's `ContractRole`) and "does this moment count as inside me", with `Covers` as the one meaning; Bonus hours, completed hours, recap, readiness, the tile badge and Gap 95's per-day read model ask it.

**Decided 2026-10-01 (interrogation in progress):**
1. **Membership is stored, and built here for blocks.** Every block that belongs to a contract carries its `ContractId`, Bonus blocks included (today Bonus is worked out as "no id, starts inside the period"). Under the id, a stored role says how it belongs: Committed (drafted and sealed), Redemption, or Bonus. Only Committed and Redemption count toward the floor. Background blocks carry the id too and never count (the floor is Focus-only). A role can't be worked out later: a Committed and a Bonus block both have the id and no redemption entries. Supersedes the "Direction" above, which derived the role.
2. **Split with Gap 67:** this gap takes the block half of Gap 67's agreed design (stored id and role, one question on `NenContract`, the migration, the architecture test). Gap 67 keeps the session half (spontaneous sessions become Bonus and stop counting toward the floor) and its remaining open questions, so a number that moves traces to Gap 67.
3. **A block that existed before Seal and was not drafted gets no id**, even if it sits inside the period — "finished or made before Seal = outside" stays a clean rule. Gap 68's adoption later closes the hole for not-yet-finished blocks: entering drafting pulls them in, kept ones become Committed at Seal, removed ones are deleted. Until Gap 68 is built, such a block is a non-member inside the period (today it would show as Bonus) — accepted as temporary (single user, app in development). **Known behaviour, accepted 2026-10-02 (found in the app checks):** editing such a block in any way, a resize in place included, makes it the contract's Bonus block — every edit of a plain or Bonus block re-asks which contract governs where it sits (`PlaceUnder`, decision 8), and until Gap 93 it is then bound by the Bonus permissions bridge (decision 17). Rejected: a rule that only a real move in changes membership — code Gap 68's adoption would delete again.
4. **Background blocks use the same three role values:** drafted → Committed, created after Seal → Bonus. One enum, no branching on block type. "Counts toward the floor" and "earns Bonus hours" are answered for Focus blocks only, and the badge shows only on Focus tiles. Rejected: a separate value for Background (wording only — Design Review Rule 5); id without a role (Gap 93's Delete rule must tell drafted from added-later).
5. **The role comes from how the block came to be, not from the contract's status.** Any block placed on the range of a contract in effect (Scheduled or Active alike) gets its id. Committed only through drafting — the first Seal or an Edit's re-draft. Redemption when it covers a deficit. Everything else placed on the range is Bonus. Rejected: membership only while Active — a block added between Seal and start would be a permanent non-member that adoption can never pick up.
6. **A finished contract hands out no ids.** A block placed on the range of a Completed or Broken contract is a plain planned block with no `ContractId`. Only a contract in effect governs new blocks.
7. **One meaning of "placed on the contract's range": the block's slot against the contract's planned period** (first window's start to last window's end, worked out from `ContractCalendar` when asked, never stored; closes finding 1). Entirely before or after the period → not this contract's: a plain block, allowed, no id, even on the first or last day. Entirely inside and within each day's window → allowed, gets the id. Inside the period but outside a day's Work Hours → refused. Straddling the period's start or end → refused. So with Work Hours 9–17 Mon–Fri, Wednesday 19:00 is refused and Friday 19:00 is a plain block: Work Hours bind only while the contract runs. Membership and placement come from the same comparison. **Drift this fixes:** `GetBlockPlacementVerdictQueryHandler` only passes a contract to the checker for a block that already has an id or picks one up through a deficit, so a plain new block is checked against no contract's hours — `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md` says blocks cannot be placed outside Work Hours. No ended moment is needed: a finished contract governs nothing (decision 6) and a contract in effect has not ended.
8. **Moving follows Gap 67's agreed rules, unchanged:** a Bonus block's membership follows where it is placed (moved out → plain block; a plain block moved in → Bonus); Committed and Redemption blocks never leave their contract's period.
9. **Breaking a contract — the contract owns "what happens to a block with this role when I am broken".** Committed and Redemption, unfinished → cancelled (as today). Bonus, not yet started → by the user's choice: **kept** (released: loses the id, becomes a plain block) or **removed** (cancelled, exactly like the others — `MarkCancelled`, so it leaves the Planner, frees its time, and stays in the database as history for Insights). A Bonus block that has started or finished is left untouched with its id and role, so the recap's Bonus hours stay true. The handler asks the contract per block and does what it says; it never branches on roles. **In this gap the handler passes "keep"** — today's behaviour, nothing visible changes. The prompt that lets the user choose is Gap 108.
10. **Stored as one value object on the block — its membership in a contract:** contract id plus role, both required, so neither can exist alone (compiler-guarded, Guard Hierarchy rank 1); the block has one or none. It answers "counts toward the floor" (Committed or Redemption). The role is an enum (three fixed values, no rule of its own). Stored like `PlannedBlock.Scheduled` and `ContractPreset.WorkHours` already are (`OwnsOne`, two plain columns on the block's row): the `ContractId` column keeps its name and a role column is added; the mapping follows that existing pattern but has not been run. Rejected: two separate nullable fields (nothing stops one without the other); a role nested inside a contract-id value object (same two values with an extra layer, and an id does not have a role). Names to confirm at interface review.
11. **The role is set once, when the block is made, and never changes.** Committed stays Committed and never claims a deficit; Redemption stays Redemption even if its coverage drops to zero; Bonus stays Bonus when edited and never claims a deficit. Only a Redemption block has its coverage recomputed on edit. A Bonus block joining or leaving (moved in or out, released on break) is membership beginning or ending, not a role change. To use a planned Bonus block's time as a redemption, the user deletes it and creates a redemption block there; where the contract's rules forbid deleting it, the redemption goes elsewhere — accepted as the consequence of a strict contract. Rejected: Bonus turning into Redemption as a side effect of an edit (unpredictable, and Gap 93's permissions would shift). Possible later, as its own gap: an explicit "use this block to cover a deficit" action. **Why it needed deciding:** `UpdatePlannedBlockCommandHandler:48` recomputes attribution, new claims included, for any Focus block with a `ContractId` — so once Bonus blocks carry the id, an edit would have made them claim. 12. **What of the contract applies to a Bonus block.** Unchanged from today: its hours never count toward the floor (completed, planned, banner) and show as Bonus hours; a missed Bonus block creates no deficit; it is counted apart from the recap's on-time/late/missed outcomes; the contract can't complete early while one is unfinished. Changed: it must fit the contract's Work Hours (decision 7), and **its session keeps blocking during breaks when the contract says so — the rule applies to every member's session, Bonus included** (architect, 2026-10-01; today only blocks with an id get it, `StartSessionCommandHandler:129`, finding 7). Move, split and delete rules: Gap 93. Whether a spontaneous session inside a contract follows the keep-blocking rule is Gap 67's (the session half). **Guard for the nine `GetForContractAsync` callers** (Delete, Break, Edit, `GetNenContractsForDateRange`, `ComputePlannedFocusHours`, Recap, `GetNenContractForEdit`, completed hours, deficits): today "the contract's blocks" means Committed and Redemption only; each caller states whether it wants every member or only those that count toward the floor, with a test that adding a Bonus block doesn't change its number.
13. **Deleting or editing a Scheduled contract never destroys a Bonus block** (same principle as decision 9). Today both handlers delete every block carrying the id (`DeleteNenContractCommandHandler:26`, `EditNenContractCommandHandler:144`), and Edit's overlap check ignores every block carrying it (`:135`) — harmless only while Bonus blocks have no id. **Delete:** Committed blocks deleted (as today), Bonus blocks released to plain blocks. **Edit:** Committed blocks replaced by the re-draft (as today); Bonus blocks left in place and still seen by the overlap check; afterwards each is compared with the new period (decision 7) — still inside and within its day's window → stays Bonus; now entirely outside → released; now outside Work Hours or straddling the new start or end → the edit is refused until the user moves or deletes it. **Temporary by design:** Gap 68's adoption pulls Bonus blocks into the re-draft, which removes Edit's re-check loop and rewrites its overlap exclusion (about ten lines and their tests — mark them in the code); the contract's question, Delete's release and "outside the new period → released" stay. Rejected: letting Edit delete Bonus blocks until Gap 68 (silent data loss); refusing any edit while Bonus blocks exist; building adoption now.
14. **A redemption block belongs to the contract it sits in, and only that contract is asked about deficits (closes finding 3).** The handler asks each contract in effect "is this slot inside my planned period" (decision 7; contracts never share a day, so at most one says yes) — on `NenContract`, from its `ContractCalendar`, beside `AllowsBlockPlacement`; not in Gap 93's `ContractRules`, which a preset shares and which has no dates. Inside a contract with a matching deficit its placement policy allows → Redemption; inside with none → Bonus; inside no contract → a plain block that can redeem nothing. Replaces `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQueryHandler:69-98` searching every contract in effect and picking the oldest deficit across them — a "which contract" rule in a query handler, with "the block sits inside the contract it redeems" written nowhere (`CheckRedemptionEligibility`'s `CurrentDayOnly` only checks the day is today). How much of which deficit is covered stays in that query until Gap 106.
15. **Repositories take a `TimeSlot`; the caller converts through `UserTimeZone` (finding 5).** `SessionRepository.GetHistoryAsync` reads its dates as UTC days and `PlannedBlockRepository.GetForDayAsync` converts with the machine's local time, bypassing `UserTimeZone`. Fix: `UserTimeZone` gets one method, the mirror of `SplitByDay` — the slot covering a local day or range of days (local midnight to the next local midnight, so 23/25-hour days are right; half-open) — and the handlers pass that slot. `GetForDayAsync` is deleted (`GetForRangeAsync(TimeSlot)` exists; interface 10 → 9); `GetHistoryAsync` takes a slot and applies a Domain-built filter for "started inside the slot" (`TimeSlot.Start`/`End` are Domain-only, so the repository cannot write it; **probe against EF Core first**). Callers: `GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandler`; completed hours and `GetSessionHistoryQuery`. Delete the Gap 97 entries from `MomentRuleTests` and `LocalTimeRuleTests`. Left alone: completed hours still counts spontaneous sessions by whole local date (Gap 67 deletes that counting) — only its fetch-a-day-either-side workaround goes. Rejected: passing the time zone into the repository; two bare moments instead of a slot; a separate "local day" type (a wrapper with no rule).
16. **Migration.** Blocks that already carry a `ContractId` get their role in SQL inside the migration: redemption entries → Redemption, otherwise → Committed (Gap 67's mapping; permanent code, tested). **Existing Bonus blocks are not backfilled** — they have no id today and working one out needs the contract's calendar and the time zone, which SQL cannot do; they become plain blocks, and their hours drop out of their contracts' Bonus totals once, at the upgrade (accepted: single user, development database). Rejected: a one-time C# backfill at startup (throwaway code for one database); a dates-only SQL approximation (wrong near Work Hours and time-zone boundaries).
17. **Bridges take the least change (architect's rule, 2026-10-01): where behaviour only has to hold until a later gap builds the final design, nothing is built for it — temporary wrong behaviour and a disposable database are accepted.** Applied:
    - **Permissions until Gap 93:** the three permission validators keep deciding by "has a contract id" (only the mechanical change to read it from the membership). So until Gap 93, a Bonus block cannot be deleted, is bound by the contract's strictness when moved, and can be split. Gap 93, the next gap, rewrites all three. They go on this gap's architecture-test exception list, pointing at Gap 93.
    - **Editing a Scheduled contract until Gap 68 (revises decision 13's Edit part):** no re-check loop is built. Edit keeps deleting every block that carries the id, so it deletes the contract's Bonus blocks too. Gap 68's adoption replaces this. Decision 13's Delete part (Bonus blocks released) stands — it is final design, not a bridge.
**Suspected, not verified (→ Gap 106):** the same code runs for a Committed block, so moving one while a same-preset deficit exists may give it redemption entries, letting hours already owed also cover a missed block.

**Plan (proposed 2026-10-01, checked against the code and `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` the same day — four changes added; signed off by the architect 2026-10-01; not started — next step is phase 0, then the Domain interface for review before anything is built on it; names to confirm at that review):**

*Domain surface (the interface, Step 2):*
- `ContractRole` enum — `Committed`, `Redemption`, `Bonus`.
- `ContractMembership` value object — contract id + role; answers "counts toward the floor". **The role is Domain-only** (as `TimeSlot.Start`/`End` are): outside code gets named questions (counts toward the floor, is Bonus, is Redemption), so the compiler guards "nobody outside the Domain reads the role" (Guard Hierarchy rank 1). The contract id stays readable — handlers need it to load the contract — and the architecture test covers only that.
- `PlannedBlock.Membership` (one or none) replaces `PlannedBlock.ContractId`; the block can join a contract and leave it (leaving only as Bonus).
- **The role is the only answer to "is this a redemption block".** `FocusBlock.IsRedemption` today means "carries redemption entries" (`FocusBlock.cs:33`; read by the tile badge, the contract banner, the recap count and `FocusBlock.cs:195`) — with decision 11 the two would disagree when coverage drops to zero. It is answered from the role; entries only say what the block covers, and the block refuses entries unless its role is Redemption.
- `NenContract`: (a) "is this slot inside my planned period" — from `ContractCalendar`, in `Moment`s; replaces `Covers(DateTime)`; (b) `AllowsBlockPlacement` also refuses a slot that straddles the period's start or end; (c) "the membership a block placed on me gets" — Redemption if it claims a deficit, else Bonus; Committed only from the Seal/Edit batch; (d) "what happens to this block when I am broken" — cancel, release or leave, taking the keep-or-remove choice.
- **`BlockPlacementChecker.Check` takes the contracts in effect, not one pre-chosen contract, and returns the governing contract with the verdict** — so placement and membership come from one answer and no caller can pass the wrong contract (Guard Hierarchy; Design Review Rule 12). Callers changed: the placement query (Create/Update), Seal and Edit (which pass their own contract as a list of one). The contract-picking in `GetBlockPlacementVerdictQueryHandler:44-73` goes. **Bridge to Gap 106:** the checker also exposes "which contract governs this slot", which the query asks once to scope attribution — the order is forced (who governs → attribution may shorten the slot → placement on the shortened slot) and attribution stays in the Application layer until Gap 106, after which the three steps are one Domain answer. Rejected: leaving `Check` unchanged and only adding the question — a caller could skip it or pass a different contract.
- **Split:** both halves keep the original's membership; how coverage divides stays with Gap 106.
- `UserTimeZone`: the slot covering a local day or range of days.
- `TimeSlot.Filters`: "this moment is inside the slot", for the session query.

*Phases — each compiles and passes before the next:*
0. **Probes:** the optional `OwnsOne` membership mapping keeping the `ContractId` column; the session "started inside the slot" filter in SQL. **Done 2026-10-01, both work** (throwaway tests on in-memory SQLite, deleted afterwards):
   - *Membership mapping:* an optional `OwnsOne` with the id public and the role mapped by name (`Property<ContractRole>("Role")`, stored as text) gives two nullable columns on the block's row, `ContractId` (name kept) and `ContractRole` — so the migration is one added column plus the backfill. Round-trips none and every role on both block subtypes; `b.Membership.ContractId == id` becomes `WHERE ContractId = @id`; a tracked block can leave, join and switch contract through the repository's `Update` and saves correctly.
   - *Session filter:* a rule over a nullable moment (`moment => moment >= start && moment < end`), put onto `s.StartedAt` the way `SlotQuery.OnSlot` does, becomes `WHERE StartedAt >= @start AND StartedAt < @end`; half-open, and a session that never started is left out. `SlotQuery.OnSlot` only needs its slot type made general (plumbing).
   - *Found:* a row with a `ContractId` but no role loads as **no membership**, silently (`Membership == null` is "id is null or role is null"). So the migration's backfill must reach every row that has an id — pinned by the migration test in phase 1.
1. **Membership on the block, no behaviour change.** Enum, value object, `PlannedBlock.Membership`, migration (decision 16). Every `ContractId` reader moves to the membership (compiler-driven). Bonus blocks do not get ids yet. **Done 2026-10-01** (1581 tests green). Interface reviewed and approved the same day, with **only the Domain creating a membership** (internal constructor; the drafting batch makes Committed, `NenContract.MembershipForPlacedBlock(claimsDeficit)` makes Redemption or Bonus; tests go through `TestContracts.Committed/Redemption/Bonus`). Rejected: public factories on the value object — a bare id can't check that the contract is in effect or that a deficit exists, and it would need an architecture test where the compiler now guards. As built: `PlannedBlock.Join`/`Leave` replace `AssignContract`; migration `ContractMembershipRole` adds `ContractRole` and backfills it; `CreatePlannedBlockCommandHandler` loads the contract and asks it for the membership (it fails if the contract isn't found). Nothing reads the role yet — `FocusBlock.IsRedemption` still means "carries entries" until phase 3. One case stores Bonus where the old code implied Committed: the Planner's type-change path (delete and re-create under the same contract id, no deficit claimed) — invisible until phase 3, whether a Committed block may change type at all is Gap 93's.
2. **One meaning of "inside" (decision 7).** Planned period on `NenContract`; `BlockPlacementChecker.Check` takes the contracts in effect and returns the governing one, so every block is checked, not only linked ones; `Covers` deleted. **Done 2026-10-01** (1600 tests green), with one change to the reviewed interface, decided by the architect the same day: **`Check` also takes the block's current membership.** A Committed or Redemption block is held to its own contract (it must contain the slot and allow it — decision 8); a plain or Bonus block is governed by the contract whose period the slot touches (`NenContract.Governs`). Why: with only the contracts in effect, a drafted block on day 1 before the contract's start, or a Committed block moved past its period, touched no period and came back "not governed, allowed" (today the first is refused). Rejected: a second method for member blocks (the caller picks the rule); handlers comparing the governing contract with the block's own (a rule in four handlers). As built: `NenContract.Governs(slot, zone)`; `AllowsBlockPlacement` now requires the whole slot inside the planned period (a slot touching a date outside the contract used to be skipped); `PlacementVerdict.GoverningContractId` on an allowed verdict; `IBlockPlacementChecker.GoverningContract` (the Gap 106 bridge, used from phase 3); `TimeSlot.Contains(TimeSlot)`. Callers: the placement query passes the block's membership and every contract in effect; Seal and Edit pass the drafted block's membership and their own contract; Split passes the original's membership. **`Covers` is not deleted yet** — its last callers are the four Bonus sites, which phase 3 moves to the role. **Until phase 3**, which contract a new block *joins* still comes from attribution (phase 1), so it can differ from the contract that governed its placement.
3. **Bonus and Redemption get their membership at creation and on move (decisions 5, 8, 11, 14).** The four Bonus sites read the role; the nine `GetForContractAsync` callers state what they want, each with a "Bonus block changes nothing" test (decision 12); keep-blocking applies to every member. **Done 2026-10-01.** As built:
   - *Creation:* the Create handler and the placement query take the same three steps — `GoverningContract(slot)` → attribution asked of that one contract (Focus, not declined) → `MembershipForPlacedBlock(claims)`; inside no contract, a plain block and attribution is never asked. A new Redemption block is passed to `Check` with its membership, so one capped to before its contract's start is refused. `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQuery.ContractId` is required; the search across every contract in effect is deleted, and so is the result's `ContractId`.
   - *`CreatePlannedBlockCommand.ContractId` is deleted* (and the placement query's): which contract a block joins comes from where it sits, never from the client. Its only setter was the Planner's type-change path, which a contract's block never reaches (its delete is refused first).
   - *Move:* `PlannedBlock.PlaceUnder(governing)` — a plain block moved in becomes Bonus, a Bonus block moved out becomes plain, Committed and Redemption are untouched. Only a Redemption block's coverage is recomputed on an edit or a split (decision 11); `FocusBlock.IsRedemption` reads the role, and the block refuses entries unless its role is Redemption. `Covers` is deleted.
   - *The nine callers:* floor-counting only (`FocusBlock.CountsTowardFloor`) — completed hours, planned hours, deficits, the redemption dedup; Bonus only — Bonus hours, the recap's Bonus count (Focus, not cancelled); every member — readiness, For-Edit, Edit; Break and Delete in phase 4. The tile badge is the stored role.
   - **Drift found and fixed:** the Create validator's placement check ran attribution even when the command declined redemption — which every manual creation does — so it checked a capped slot under a deficit's contract while the handler saved the full slot unlinked (Design Review Rule 12). The query now takes `DeclineRedemption`.
   - **For-Edit seeds every member, Bonus blocks included** (least change): Edit deletes all of them, so one left out of the draft would vanish silently; a kept one is sealed as Committed — what Gap 68's adoption will do.
4. **Break and Delete (decisions 9, 13).** Break asks the contract per block, passing "keep"; Delete releases Bonus blocks. **Done 2026-10-01:** `NenContract.FateWhenBroken(block, isResolved, brokenAt, keepBonusBlocks)` → `BlockFateOnBreak` (`Cancel`/`Release`/`Leave`); the handler only does what it says. "Resolved" stays an input — it needs the block's session.
5. **Repository date trap (decision 15).** **Done 2026-10-01:** `UserTimeZone.SlotCovering(day[s])`, `TimeSlot.Filters.Contains(slot)`, `SlotQuery.OnMoment`; `ISessionRepository.GetHistoryAsync(TimeSlot, presetId)`; `IPlannedBlockRepository.GetForDayAsync` deleted (interface 10 → 9), the day query asks `GetForRangeAsync(SlotCovering(date))`. Completed hours lost its fetch-a-day-either-side workaround and its own date filter.
6. **Guards and close-out — guards built 2026-10-01, close-out waits for the app checks:** `MembershipRuleTests` (probed red by writing the old "no contract id = Bonus" check back; exceptions: the three permission validators and the Planner's copy of the Delete rule → Gap 93, Edit's overlap exclusion → Gap 68); the Gap 97 entries are gone from `MomentRuleTests` and `LocalTimeRuleTests`; `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md` and `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY_NEN_CONTRACT.md` updated. **Still to do at close:** the `DESIGN_GAP_HISTORY.md` entry, settle the lessons below, Gap 67's write-up. 1628 tests green. Original item: architecture test "outside the Domain and persistence, nothing reads a block's contract id to decide membership" (probed red; the three permission validators listed → Gap 93; the role needs no test — it is Domain-only); Gap 97 entries deleted from `MomentRuleTests` and `LocalTimeRuleTests`; `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md`, `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY_NEN_CONTRACT.md`, `DESIGN_GAP_HISTORY.md`, `THINGS_TO_TEST_IN_APP.md`; Gap 67 and Gap 93 write-ups updated for what this gap took.

*Lessons to settle at close-out:*
- **A value object stored as part of a row can't be shared between two rows.** The drafting batch and a split hand one `ContractMembership` to several blocks; EF then saves it on one of them only (the first test run failed with a missing `Start` when a test shared one `TimeSlot`). Fixed for the membership: the block keeps its own copy (`PlannedBlock.Membership` setter), pinned by `ContractMembershipPersistenceTests.OneMembershipGivenToSeveralBlocks_IsSavedOnEachOfThem` (probed red). **Open:** `TimeSlot` has the same trap and no such guard; no production path shares a slot between blocks today.
- **Handler tests on mocks don't see storage.** The shared-instance bug above passed every Seal and Split handler test; only a real round trip caught it. Candidate `TESTING_GUIDE.md` note: a new stored value object gets a round-trip test through a second context.
- **A row with a contract id and no role loads as no membership** (phase 0). Pinned by the migration test's "no member without a role" check.
- **A reviewed signature can still be wrong for a caller nobody walked through.** `Check(contractsInEffect)` was approved, then failed for blocks that already belong to a contract (Seal's day-1 block, a Committed block moved out) — found only when each caller was traced against the new rule. Candidate Design Review Rule: before approving a signature, run every existing caller's hardest case through it.
- **Removing a field found a mismatch the field was hiding.** Deleting the command's `ContractId` exposed that the validator and the handler disagreed about `DeclineRedemption` (phase 3). Already covered by Design Review Rule 12; no new rule — note it as an example there.
- **A text-scan guard has a blind spot.** `MembershipRuleTests` sees comparisons of the contract id; `PlannerPageViewModel` filters draft tiles with `b.ContractId is { } id && id == editingId`, which it does not match. Left for Gap 89 area 2 / Gap 93.

*Not in this gap:* spontaneous sessions' membership and counting (Gap 67); Bonus permissions (Gap 93); how much of which deficit is covered (Gap 106); the break prompt (Gap 108); adoption (Gap 68); `PlannerPageViewModel` choosing "the" contract (finding 6 → Gaps 89/93).

**Why before Gap 95 and 67:** Gap 95's per-day read model and Gap 67's membership change both build on "what counts as the contract's" — one definition first.

**Why before Gap 93 (moved ahead 2026-10-01):** Gap 93's Delete rule depends on the block's role — committed/redemption blocks never deletable, Bonus blocks never once past, future Bonus blocks only if the contract's rules allow — so Gap 93 is a consumer of `ContractRole`, alongside Bonus hours, recap, readiness, the tile badge and Gap 95.

**Last step — architecture tests (Gap 98):** add the narrow rule this gap makes true — *only `NenContract` decides membership*: no `ContractId` comparison outside the Domain and persistence to decide "is this block the contract's / Bonus", and a parity test if `Covers` gets a SQL mirror. Delete `PlannedBlockRepository` from `LocalTimeRuleTests` once the repositories take UTC instants or local dates plus the clock (finding 5). Exact test form decided when the gap is designed.

---

## Gap 93 — One Source for Block Change Permissions *(closed, 2026-10-02)*

**What was wrong:** "may this block be changed or deleted" was answered in three validators and eight Planner properties, several disagreeing. Update and Split each wrote out the strictness rules and floor protection, with different day coverage; Delete refused any block with a contract id; the Planner hid a Split that would have worked and showed a Delete that would be refused. After Gap 97 a Bonus block carried a contract id, so it could not be deleted and followed the strictness — a bridge. After a restart the running block's tile was unlocked, because the Planner read the lock from the in-memory session.

**Decided (interrogated 2026-10-01 and 2026-10-02, decisions 1–17 below):**
- Whether a contract's strictness governs its Bonus blocks is a setting the user chooses, **off by default**: a Bonus block is free (move inside the period, re-preset, delete) unless the contract says it is bound like a Committed one.
- **No member ever moves outside its contract's planned period** — a fixed rule on the contract, not a setting. It reverses half of Gap 97's decision 8 (a Bonus block moved out no longer becomes plain).
- A bound block keeps its preset and is never deleted; Split is offered only for a bound block, never for a plain or free Bonus one.
- A finished contract's blocks are history; the contract answers that itself.
- Floor protection is one rule on the contract, checking every day.

**Changed (phases 0–8, 2026-10-02):**
- **0–1** — `ContractRules`: the six settings without dates, shared by `ContractPreset` and `NenContract`, stored on the existing columns. The floor rule is worked out from the fixed daily floor and the strictness instead of stored (migration `ContractRules`). The checkbox on the Contract Presets form.
- **2** — the contract's answers about one of its blocks (`Binds`, `CheckBlockMove`, `CheckBlockMayMove`, `CheckBlockPresetChange`, `CheckBlockSplit`, `CheckBlockDelete`, `CheckFloor`) and the block's own lock (`CheckOwnChange`, `CheckOwnSplit`, `CheckOwnDelete`); `ContractRules.MovementRange` is the one place strictness is asked.
- **3–5** — `IBlockChangeChecker` combines them, first refusal wins, worded once in `BlockChangeVerdictDto.From`. The Update, Split and Delete validators ask it, then placement. `NenContract.AllowsBlockTimeChange` deleted.
- **6** — `PlannedBlockDto.Allowances`; the Planner's own lock properties deleted.
- **7** — the split dialog asks for a day per piece only where a piece may change day.
- **8** — guards; the temporary `Seal` bridge deleted.

**Found on the way:** the floor rule was a second stored copy of something the strictness and the fixed floor already decide (phase 0). The approved interface had two flags for move and resize and one reason for "started"; the tests gave one flag and two reasons. Lockdown refused saving an untouched form — only what changes is asked about now. Under Daily Flex a block across midnight could not be resized at all; "own day" is now every day the block sits on. Split's "a session already ran against it" was dropped as unreachable, after checking. `AllowCancellation` is stored and shown but never enforced, against what the dictionary said — corrected there, left for Gap 83.

**Left on purpose:** enforcing "Allow cancellation" and Strict Mode (Gap 83); contract lifecycle rules repeated in validators, and "no daily floors → the whole-period target is the sum drafted" in two handlers (Gap 92); clicking the running session's own tile is still decided from the in-memory session (Gap 89 area 4); the validators' remaining input-shape sentences and the wizard's second copy of the strictness names (Gap 100); a refusal shown while dragging; a "no splitting" contract setting.

**Confirmed via:** the full suite (1717 passing; each new group of cases seen red first). The architect's manual checks on 2026-10-02 — the migration on his database and the preset form (phase 1), six on-screen checks of the Planner's buttons and fields (phases 3–6), the split dialog's day fields (phase 7). Rule cases were left to the tests on purpose.

**Guard (step 5):** yes.
- Compiler (rank 1): the block DTO no longer carries the fields a ViewModel computed locks from (`HasStarted`, `IsUnderInEffectContract`, `ContractStrictnessRule`), only `Allowances`. `ContractRules.MovementRange` is a `switch` with no discard arm, so a new strictness level is a compiler warning.
- New test: `ChangePermissionRuleTests.NothingOutsideTheDomainComparesStrictnessValues` — probed red with a deliberate comparison in the Application project. Two exceptions, both display (the label converter; the wizard's second copy of the names → Gap 100).
- New tests: `ContractRulesPersistenceTests` (round trip, shared instance — probed red, migration), and the Domain cases for every decision (`NenContractBlockChangeTests`, `PlannedBlockOwnLockTests`, `BlockChangeCheckerTests`).
- Exception entries retired: this gap's four in `MembershipRuleTests`; `ValidatorWordingRuleTests` Update 9 → 4, Split 15 → 7, Delete 3 → 1.
- The six close-out lessons: `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` — a warning sign under Where a Rule Lives ("two stored fields where one follows from the other"), and examples on Design Review Rules 5 (settle the count of flags and reasons by writing the cases) and 9 (show a state is unreachable before deleting the rule that guards it); `TESTING_GUIDE.md` — manual checks only for what tests can't prove, and how a scripted edit half-fails; "a no-op counted as a change" is pinned by tests on the checker and the Update validator, no rule needed.

**Principle demonstrated:** a permission written where it is used gets written once per use, and the copies drift. Giving each owner its own answer — the block its lock, the contract its rules — and one service that only asks them, lets the screen and the validator read the same answer, so a button can no longer be shown and then always refused.

### Write-up as planned and built (moved from `OPEN_GAPS.md` at close)

**From Gap 97 (built 2026-10-01):** the role this gap was waiting for exists — `block.Membership` with `IsBonus` / `IsRedemption` / `CountsTowardFloor`. Bonus blocks now carry the contract's id, and the three permission validators still decide by "has a contract id", so **until this gap** a Bonus block can't be deleted, is bound by its contract's strictness when moved, and can be split. Those validators, plus `PlannerPageViewModel.CanDeleteSnapshotBlock`, are the listed exceptions in `MembershipRuleTests` — this gap empties them. Also open here: whether a Committed block may change type (the Planner's delete-and-recreate path is refused for any member today).

**The question:** "may this block be changed (moved, resized, preset changed, split) or deleted?" — the sibling of Gap 88's "may a block go here". Asked by every edit path and by the Planner before it shows a button or starts a drag.

**Where it is answered today:**

| Where | Decides |
|---|---|
| `UpdatePlannedBlockValidator` | not ended; preset fixed under an in-effect contract; `NenContract.AllowsBlockTimeChange` (the one Domain home); floor protection, branching on `StrictnessRule` |
| `SplitPlannedBlockValidator` | Focus only; contract blocks only; not Lockdown; not cancelled; future only; no session yet; floor protection, branching on `StrictnessRule` again |
| `DeletePlannedBlockValidator` | never a contract block (any status); not the active session's block |
| `PlannerPageViewModel` | `CanDeleteFromForm`, `IsBlockLocked`/`CanSaveBlock`, `CanChangePreset`, `CanSplitBlock`, `CanDeleteSnapshotBlock`, `IsTileFullyLocked`, `IsTileStartLocked` |

**Disagreements found (checked in code 2026-09-29):**
1. ~~**Live bug:** `CanSplitBlock` requires a Daily Flex contract; `SplitPlannedBlockValidator` only rejects Lockdown, so it also accepts Period Study — the Split button is hidden for a split that would succeed.~~ *(Stopgap fixed 2026-10-01: the ViewModel copy now says "not Lockdown", `PlannerSplitButtonViewTests` pins it. The copy itself still goes in this gap — repoint that test to the DTO's `CanSplit` flag.)*
2. `CanDeleteFromForm` (`:673`, also claims "exactly"): hides Delete for an ended ordinary block the validator allows (the snapshot view's `CanDeleteSnapshotBlock` allows it); checks "in-effect contract" where the validator refuses *any* contract block — the button can show for a delete that will be rejected.
3. Drag lock (`IsTileFullyLocked`, includes ended) vs form lock (`IsBlockLocked`, doesn't) — an ended missed block can't be dragged but its form shows Save, which the validator then rejects. *(Fixed by Gap 88 phase 0 — both become `HasStarted`.)*
4. `UpdatePlannedBlockValidator:30-32` claims to mirror a Delete "ended" guard that doesn't exist. *(Rewritten in Gap 88 phase 0.)*
5. Update applies contract rules only while `IsInEffect`; Split checks `StrictnessRule` regardless of status — consistent today only because Split also requires a future block.
6. Floor protection — *which strictness needs which protection, over which days* — is a contract rule decided in two Application validators (Forbidden Zone), with different day coverage: Update (Period Study, no period floor) checks the original and new start day; Split checks every touched day. Update may miss a cross-midnight move's end day — suspected, not yet confirmed by a test.

**What Gap 88 phase 0 already removes:** every "has it started / is it running / did it complete / has it ended" variant collapses into `PlannedBlock.HasStarted` (see Gap 88). Left for this gap: findings 1, 2 (contract-status part), 5, 6.

**Direction (for interrogation, not decided):** same shape as Gap 88 — what the block itself allows → `PlannedBlock` methods; what the contract allows (strictness, in effect, which kinds of change, which days need floor protection) → `NenContract` methods, so no validator branches on `StrictnessRule`; one Domain answer to "may this change happen" combining both, asked by validators for a concrete change; ready-made flags on the block DTO (`CanMove`, `CanResize`, `CanChangePreset`, `CanSplit`, `CanDelete`) from the same rules, so the ViewModel's seven properties go. The floor check depends on the concrete change, so it is checked per change (like a placement verdict), not precomputed.

**Decided 2026-09-29:** Split is allowed in a Period Study contract — the validator is right, `CanSplitBlock`'s Daily-Flex-only condition is the bug (finding 1).

**Decided 2026-10-01 (interrogation, paused — waits for Gap 97):**
1. **Permissions are Domain rules, owned by their owners.** What the block itself allows → entity methods on `PlannedBlock`, built on `Scheduled` (`TimeSlot`). `PlannedBlock.HasStarted` is a bare pass-through whose only callers are the three permission validators and the DTO — it is deleted once they ask the permission methods (today the lock rule lives only in its doc comment, and `DeletePlannedBlockValidator:37` re-derives it as `!HasStarted || HasEnded`). `PlannedBlock.HasEnded` has callers outside permissions (Start Session, outcome classifier, `IsUnresolved`) — left for Gap 92, not widened here.
2. **One Domain service combines them** (agent-noun name, typed verdict, `BlockPlacementChecker`-style): asks the block, then the contract, then (later) user settings; first refusal wins. It holds no rule of its own — no `StrictnessRule` branching inside it. Built so a new *source* of permission is one input + one line: **strict mode** (a future Settings toggle — no placed block may be moved, edited, split or deleted; noted for Gap 83, not built here, no placeholder parameter). The contract is asked one question ("may this change happen to a block that is *X* to you, at this time"), so **new contract rules change only `NenContract`**, never the service or its callers.
3. **Separate from `BlockPlacementChecker`**, split by one test: *does the rule need the original block?* Yes → change permission (e.g. `AllowsBlockTimeChange(originalDate, …)`, floor protection); no → placement. Checked in code: all three placement rules (`InThePast`, `OutsideContractHours`, `TimeTaken`) look only at the target slot and are asked by Create too — **nothing moves out of placement**. Update and Split ask permission first, then placement, so a dragged running block reports "running", not "in the past".
4. **Delete:** committed and redemption blocks — never, past or future (a fixed contract invariant, not a configurable rule: allowing it would defeat the contract). Bonus blocks — never once in the past; in the future, if the contract's rules allow it. Blocks in no contract — today's rule (not while running). Needs "what is this block to the contract" → **Gap 97's `ContractRole`**; hence Gap 97 moved ahead of this gap (not merged: 97 answers one contract-owned question with many consumers, this gap combines several owners — different reasons to change).
5. **Split:** the block allows it only as a future Focus block; the contract decides the rest. Splitting is moving two halves, so it follows the **movement range** (decision 7) — Lockdown never; Daily Flex both halves on the block's own day; Period Study both halves anywhere inside the contract (replaces today's "halves land on the original's days" for every strictness, `SplitPlannedBlockValidator:138–154`; the split dialog needs a date picker for it). A future contract setting "no splitting" overrides every level (not built — one more toggle on `ContractRules`, decision 6). The validator asks the service; the button reads a `CanSplit` DTO flag from the same answer.
6. **`ContractRules` — one value object for all of a contract's rules (proposed 2026-10-01; confirm its name and exact contents when the interrogation resumes).** Holds strictness plus every toggle (`AllowCancellation`, `KeepBlockingDuringBreaks`, `RedemptionPlacementPolicy`, later "no splitting") and answers the rule questions (may a block move / split / be deleted, may the contract be cancelled). `NenContract` holds one and keeps what is date-bound (days, floors, status, floor protection — needs the calendar); `ContractPreset` holds the same type, so Seal copies one value instead of five duplicated fields and the snapshot stays intact (a preset is a template, never a live source — like `BreakTimerPreset`).
