A new contract rule is one field + one check inside it; preset, wizard and Seal carry it without changes. Today strictness is branched on 20 times in 10 files outside it — the copies this gap removes.
7. **Strictness stays an enum, asked in one place.** It answers one question — *how far may a block move*: Lockdown nowhere, Daily Flex within its own day, Period Study anywhere inside the contract — so the one `switch` lives in one `ContractRules` method (a `switch` expression, so an unhandled new level is a compiler warning). Per-level types only if levels grow behaviour across several questions; then they become types inside `ContractRules`, invisible outside it.
8. **Finding (2026-10-01):** a past Bonus block can be deleted today — it has no `ContractId`, so `DeletePlannedBlockValidator:25` lets it through, and the snapshot view's Delete offers it. Bonus hours count in the contract recap, so this rewrites a finished contract's history. Fixed by decision 4.

**Decided 2026-10-02 (interrogation resumed after Gap 97):**
9. **Whether a contract's strictness governs its Bonus blocks is a contract rule the user sets** — one toggle on `ContractRules` (decision 6), set on the Contract Presets form beside "Allow cancellation" and "Keep blocking during breaks", copied to the contract at Seal. **Default off — Bonus blocks are free**, also for existing contracts and the seeded presets. Off: a future Bonus block can be moved, resized, split, re-preset and deleted like a plain block (Work Hours still bind it inside the period). On: a future Bonus block moves only as far as a Committed one may. Either way: a started or past Bonus block is locked and never deleted (decision 4), and floor protection never runs on a Bonus block (it never counts toward the floor — today's check on it is meaningless). Answers Gap 67's question 5 and replaces Gap 97's bridge (decision 17 there). Rejected: always free with no toggle (a second migration and form pass later); always bound (adding extra work becomes a trap — under Lockdown a Bonus block could never be moved or deleted).
10. **With the toggle on, a future Bonus block is never deletable** — "binding" means one thing: once placed it is treated as a Committed block is, except for the floor. It runs or is missed (a missed one creates no deficit), and while it hasn't run it still blocks completing the contract early. Strictness keeps answering only "how far may a block move" (decision 7). Rejected: deletable where the strictness allows any movement (strictness would answer two questions); always deletable (delete-and-recreate defeats the movement limit).
11. **A block that belongs to a contract never moves outside its planned period — a fixed rule on `NenContract`, not a setting** (architect, 2026-10-02). Every member, whatever its role and whatever the toggle: Committed, Redemption and Bonus alike. Two layers: `NenContract` (owns the calendar) answers "still inside my planned period", in moments; `ContractRules` (no dates) narrows how far inside it a block may move (decision 7). Membership then ends only through the contract itself (Break or Delete releasing Bonus blocks), never as a side effect of a drag. **Reverses half of Gap 97's decision 8:** "a Bonus block moved out becomes a plain block" goes — `PlannedBlock.PlaceUnder` keeps only "a plain block moved in becomes Bonus", and the moved-out half and its tests are deleted. A free Bonus block wanted outside the period is deleted and re-created there; the refusal's wording says so. **Drift this fixes:** `AllowsBlockTimeChange` compares whole dates while "inside the contract" is measured in moments, so under Period Study a block dragged to the first day before the start time passes the date rule (for a Committed block only the placement check catches it; a Bonus block would come out plain and deletable). Rejected: letting a free Bonus block leave (the rule would need a branch on role and toggle, so it could no longer be a plain invariant).
12. **Preset and type follow the same two meanings.** Committed, Redemption and binding Bonus blocks: preset and type fixed while the contract is in effect (deficits and redemptions are matched per preset — swapping a committed block's preset would change what a redemption can cover). A free Bonus block (toggle off): both may change while it is in the future, like a plain block. The contract answers "may this block's preset change" from the role and the toggle, in one place. A free Bonus block's type change stays the Planner's delete-and-re-create (two steps; making it atomic is not this gap). Answers Gap 97's open point "whether a Committed block may change type": no. Rejected: by strictness level (strictness would answer a second question, and it breaks per-preset matching); fixed for every member (pointless for a block that may be deleted and re-created).
13. **"In effect" is the contract's own business (closes finding 5).** Asked "may this change happen to this block", a finished contract (Completed or Broken) answers from one fixed rule: its blocks are history — nothing changes, nothing is deleted. No validator asks `IsInEffect` any more (today Update checks it and Split doesn't). Checked in code: a finished contract has no upcoming blocks (it can't complete while one is pending; Break cancels unfinished Committed blocks and releases upcoming Bonus ones), so strictness, the toggle and the preset lock only ever matter while Scheduled or Active. Why this home (`MODULE_STANDARDS.md` → Where a Rule Lives): the answer needs only the contract's own status, so it is an entity question; a validator combining `IsInEffect` with `StrictnessRule` is the Application Handler Forbidden Zone. Rejected: letting a finished contract's past blocks be deleted to tidy the calendar (rewrites its recap).
14. **Floor protection is one rule owned by `NenContract` (closes finding 6).** "After this change, does the plan still meet my floor?" — the contract is handed the planned hours per day as they would be after the change and checks **every** one of its days (or its whole-period target when it has one); it answers yes, or which day (or the period) falls short and by which floor. No strictness branch — a day's floor is a day's floor; strictness has already decided whether the block may move there. No list of touched days, so the suspected cross-midnight miss (Update under Period Study checks the original and new start day only) can't happen. Runs only for blocks that count toward the floor (decision 9). Who does what: the contract owns the rule; the existing planned-hours calculator (Domain) works out the hours after the change; the Application only loads the blocks and words the typed reason once (replaces the three copies of the floor sentence, Gap 98 finding). Accepted: a day already below its floor before the change (Seal guarantees it isn't) would refuse every change in that contract. Rejected: "only the days the change touches", computed in one place — the same answers when done right, but working out the touched days is what went wrong.
15. **Split is offered for exactly the blocks a contract binds** (narrows decision 5). Committed, Redemption, and Bonus when the toggle is on: Lockdown never; Daily Flex both halves on the block's own day; Period Study both halves anywhere inside the contract. A plain block and a free Bonus block can't be split — "free = treated as plain" holds everywhere (move, resize, re-preset, delete yes; Split no); either can be shrunk and a second block added. Architect's reason for plain blocks: the form is already crowded (Split, Delete, Cancel, Save). **The rule is the Domain's, not the view's:** "a block no contract binds can't be split" and "Lockdown allows no split" are answered by the permission service (the Lockdown part from `ContractRules`, decision 7); the view only shows or hides the button from the DTO's `CanSplit`, and the validator refuses from the same answer. Rejected: any future Focus block splittable (clutters the form); a view-only rule hiding the button under Lockdown (not enforced, and it is the copy that caused this gap's first live bug); a free Bonus block splittable in any contract (the only block with Split under Lockdown).
16. **The block's own lock, and ready-made flags for the Planner (fixes F2 and finding 2).** Every block, in a contract or not, answers from its own schedule and the clock: not started → change and delete allowed (then the contract is asked, if it has one); running → no change, no delete; ended → no change, delete only if it is in no contract. The block DTO carries `CanMove`, `CanResize`, `CanChangePreset`, `CanSplit`, `CanDelete`, computed from the same Domain answer the validators ask; `PlannerPageViewModel`'s own properties (`IsBlockLocked`, `CanSaveBlock`, `CanDeleteFromForm`, `CanSplitBlock`, `CanChangePreset`'s lock part, `CanDeleteSnapshotBlock`, `IsTileFullyLocked`, `IsTileStartLocked`) go. Nothing reads the in-memory session to decide a lock, so after a restart the running tile is locked, and a refused change on it says "this block is running", not the overlap message. **Two moments, one source:** the flags remove what is never allowed for this block (stopped at the beginning — no drag, no button, no message); whether one concrete change is allowed (stays on its day, inside Work Hours, floor still met) is checked on drop or Save and refused with its reason, as placement is today. **Possible follow-up, not this gap:** asking the same rule during a drag so the tile shows a refusal before the drop.
17. **`ContractRules` confirmed (decision 6): six settings, no dates** — strictness; the daily floor rule (`DailyFloorRule`: fixed, follows the plan, none — today the preset stores "hours or empty" and the contract the rule, so the migration gives the preset the contract's shape: hours → fixed, empty → follows the plan); allow cancellation; keep blocking during breaks; redemption placement; Bonus blocks follow the strictness (decision 9). It also takes **"which floor rule does a contract get when no fixed floor is set"** (Lockdown and Daily Flex follow the plan; Period Study has no daily floor, only a whole-period target) — written twice today, `SealNenContractCommandHandler:55-67` and `EditNenContractCommandHandler:77`. **Stays on `NenContract`** (needs status or calendar): the days and their windows, each day's actual floor and the whole-period target, status, "still inside my period" (11), "floor still met" (14). **Stays on the preset as a drafting template:** default Work Hours (a sealed contract keeps no Work Hours setting, only each day's window), name. Name `ContractRules` kept over `ContractTerms` ("the contract's rules" is the wording the docs already use). **Not in this gap, checked 2026-10-02:** lifecycle rules repeated in the contract validators (only Scheduled edited or deleted, only Active completed, only in effect broken) → noted under Gap 92; app-wide constants (5-minute grace, 12-hour Focus block, 1-minute minimum deficit) are not contract rules; **"Allow cancellation" is stored and shown but not enforced** (`BreakNenContractValidator:31`, deliberate interim since 2026-08-18, waiting for Strict Mode) → `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md` corrected, noted under Gap 83; the Change Window is parked (Gap 92) — it would be one more check inside the contract's answer.

**Plan (proposed 2026-10-02, not signed off; names to confirm at the interface review):**

*Domain surface (the interface, Step 2):*
- `ContractRules` value object (decision 17), held by `NenContract` and `ContractPreset`. The one `switch` on strictness is its movement range: none, the block's own day, the whole period. Each holder keeps its own copy (Gap 97's lesson: a stored value object can't be shared between rows).
- `NenContract` answers, for one of its blocks: does it bind this block (Committed, Redemption, or Bonus with the toggle on; nothing while finished — then everything is refused as history, decision 13); may it move to this slot (inside the planned period in moments — decision 11 — then the movement range); may its preset change; may it be split; may it be deleted; is the floor still met for these planned hours per day (decision 14). `AllowsBlockTimeChange` is deleted.
- `PlannedBlock` answers its own lock from its schedule and the clock (decision 16); `HasStarted` loses its outside callers.
- `IBlockChangeChecker` in `Domain/Services/` (agent noun, beside `BlockPlacementChecker`), four members:
  - `AllowancesFor(block, contract, asOf)` → `BlockChangeAllowances` (`CanReschedule`, `CanChangePreset`, `CanSplit`, `CanDelete`) — what is never allowed for this block; the DTO carries it.
  - `CheckReschedule(block, newSlot, presetChanges, contract, contractBlocks, asOf, timeZone)` → `ChangeVerdict`.
  - `CheckSplit(block, first, second, contract, contractBlocks, asOf, timeZone)` → `ChangeVerdict`.
  - `CheckDelete(block, contract, asOf)` → `ChangeVerdict`.
  It holds no rule: it asks the block, then the contract, first refusal wins. `contract` is the block's own contract or none — it throws if handed another's (as `FateWhenBroken` does). `contractBlocks` are the contract's members; it works out planned hours after the change with `IPlannedFocusHoursCalculator` and hands them to the contract.
- `ChangeVerdict` value object: allowed, or the first reason not (`ChangeOutcome`: block has started; the contract is over; a contract block is never deleted; strictness allows no movement; outside the block's own day; outside the contract's period; preset fixed; split not offered for this block; below the daily floor — with the day and floor; below the period target — with the target). Worded once in `BlockChangeVerdictDto.From` (the typed-reasons rule), which replaces the three floor sentences and the strictness `switch` in the Update validator.
- **One flag, not two, for move and resize** (changes decision 16's list): since Gap 88 phase 0 a started block is fully locked, so no rule separates moving from resizing — `CanReschedule` covers both (Design Review Rule 5).
- Order in Update and Split: permission first, then placement (decision 3).

*Checked against every caller's hardest case (Design Review Rule 16):* a plain block (no contract → only its own lock); a plain block moved into a contract (permission has nothing to say, placement makes it Bonus); a free Bonus block moved out of the period (refused by decision 11 — needs the contract and the time zone, hence both on `CheckReschedule`); a block of a finished contract (history); the block DTO for a whole week (`AllowancesFor` needs no blocks or time zone — the day query already has every contract loaded); the Planner's type change (delete then create — goes through `CheckDelete`). **To trace during the build:** a Redemption block's slot is capped by attribution in the handler after the validator ran — the floor check must see the capped slot (Rule 12; the capping itself is Gap 106's). **Stays in the ViewModel (UI flow state, not permissions):** the Cover Deficit split path (`_isCreatingRedemption`), draft tiles while drafting, `ShowTypeToggle`.

*Phases — each compiles and passes before the next:*
0. **Probes:** `ContractRules` as a required owned value on both tables keeping today's column names; the daily floor rule inside it (the contract stores it as JSON today, the preset as "hours or empty"). **Done 2026-10-02, all work** (throwaway tests on in-memory SQLite, deleted afterwards):
   - *Mapping:* a required `OwnsOne` with each property given today's column name maps onto the existing flat columns (no `Rules_` prefix); rows written before the new column load with the toggle off; a filter on a rule still runs in SQL; replacing the rules on a tracked owner is saved.
   - *Sharing:* one rules object given to a preset and a contract is saved on both (different owner types). Two owners of the same type would hit Gap 97's trap, but no path does that — Seal and Edit build the rules from the command's values, never from a tracked preset.
   - **Found — the floor rule is not a stored fact of its own.** Both handlers work it out the same way: fixed hours given → fixed; else Period Study → none; else follows the plan. So `ContractRules` stores only **the fixed daily floor, or none**, and answers the `DailyFloorRule` from that and the strictness — the "which floor rule" choice (decision 17) *is* that answer, and a combination like "Period Study, follows the plan" can no longer be stored. **Changes the migration in decision 17:** the preset keeps its `DailyFloorHours` column as it is; the contract's `FloorRule` JSON column becomes `DailyFloorHours` (`json_extract` gives the hours for a fixed rule, null otherwise — probed) and is dropped. `DailyFloorRule` stays as the type `ContractCalendar.Generate` takes.
1. **`ContractRules`, no behaviour change.** The value object; preset and contract hold one; Seal and Edit pass it whole; the "which floor rule" choice moves in; migration (preset floor shape, the Bonus toggle column, default off); the checkbox on the Contract Presets form. **Done 2026-10-02** (1644 tests green; interface approved by the architect the same day). As built:
   - `ContractRules` (`Domain/ValueObjects/`): the six settings and `FloorRule`, worked out from the fixed daily floor and the strictness. `NenContract.Rules` and `ContractPreset.Rules`; each owner keeps its own copy (`rules with { }`), pinned by `ContractRulesPersistenceTests.OneRulesObjectGivenToSeveralOwners_IsSavedOnEachOfThem` (probed red by removing the copy). The old properties (`StrictnessRule`, `FloorRule`, `AllowCancellation`, `KeepBlockingDuringBreaks`, `RedemptionPlacementPolicy`, the preset's `DailyFloor`) stay as read-only pass-throughs for the DTO builders.
   - `NenContract.Seal` takes the rules as one value; `ContractPreset.Create`/`Update` take `(name, rules, workHours)`. Seal's and Edit's handlers build the rules from the command and ask them for the floor rule — their two copies of the strictness branch are gone. **Left in both handlers, two lines each:** "no daily floors → the whole-period target is the sum of what was drafted"; it could move into `Seal`, which already has the planned hours — decide in phase 2.
   - **Bridge:** the old `Seal` signature is kept as an `[Obsolete]` pass-through (`MODULE_STANDARDS.md` → migrating in steps). Only tests call it (about 60 call sites, each a build warning); delete it when they are converted, at the latest in phase 8. It no longer takes the floor rule as given: one test sealed "Locked, no daily floors", a combination that can't exist any more, and was moved to Period Time Budget.
   - Migration `ContractRules`: adds `BonusBlocksFollowStrictness` to both tables and `DailyFloorHours` to contracts, converts the stored floor rule, drops `FloorRule`; `Down` rebuilds it. Mapping shared by both owners (`MapContractRules`). Pinned by `ContractRulesPersistenceTests` (round trip, no fixed floor, update, migration).
   - UI: the checkbox and a card line on the Contract Presets page; the wizard carries the setting from the picked preset (Simple contracts: off) through `NenContractDraftRules` into the Seal and Edit commands; the edit prefill reads it back.
   - **Nothing reads the toggle yet** — phases 2–5 do.
2. **The contract's and the block's answers** (Domain, test first): binds, movement in moments, preset, split, delete, floor still met; the block's own lock. **Done 2026-10-02** (1691 tests green; the 47 new cases seen red against empty methods first). As built:
   - `ContractRules.MovementRange` (`MovementRange`: `None`, `OwnDay`, `WholePeriod`) — a `switch` expression with no discard arm, so a new strictness level is a compiler warning.
   - `NenContract`: `Binds(block)`, `CheckBlockMove(block, newSlot, timeZone)`, `CheckBlockPresetChange(block)`, `CheckBlockSplit(block)`, `CheckBlockDelete(block, asOf)`, `CheckFloor(plannedFocusHoursByDay)`. Each throws for a block of another contract; a finished contract answers `ContractIsOver` (delete: `ContractBlockNeverDeleted`).
   - `PlannedBlock`: `CheckOwnChange`, `CheckOwnSplit`, `CheckOwnDelete`.
   - `ChangeVerdict` (`ValueObjects/`) and `ChangeOutcome` (`Enums/`). **One outcome more than reviewed:** `BlockIsRunning` (a running block can't be deleted) beside `BlockHasStarted` (a started block can't be changed) — two different facts, not two wordings: an ended block has started but isn't running, and may be deleted.
   - **"Own day" under Daily Flex is every day the block already sits on** — the same for a normal block; a block across midnight may stay across its own two days (today's rule pinned it to its start day, so it could not even be resized). Matches Split's "the original's days".
   - **Not moved:** "no daily floors → the whole-period target is the sum drafted" stays as two lines in the Seal and Edit handlers. Moving it into `Seal` would give every test-sealed Period contract a zero target and switch which floor check they exercise — not worth it here; noted under Gap 92.
   - Nothing calls the new answers yet, and `AllowsBlockTimeChange` is still there — phase 3.
3. **`IBlockChangeChecker`, `ChangeVerdict`, wording; Update repointed.** `AllowsBlockTimeChange` deleted; `PlaceUnder` loses "moved out becomes plain" (decision 11). **Done 2026-10-02** (1705 tests green; the checker's 25 cases seen red first). As built:
   - `IBlockChangeChecker` / `BlockChangeChecker` (`Domain/Services/`, registered in Application's DI) with the four reviewed members; `BlockChangeAllowances`; `NenContract.CheckBlockMayMove` (the general "may it move at all" the flags need — a concrete move goes through it too). A member whose contract is gone is treated as history instead of throwing.
   - `BlockChangeVerdictDto.From` — the one wording home (`Application/DTOs/`).
   - `UpdatePlannedBlockValidator`: one rule asks the checker, then placement — placement is not asked when permission refuses. Its nine sentences are four; its `MembershipRuleTests` entry is deleted. It no longer depends on `ComputePlannedFocusHoursQuery`.
   - `UpdatePlannedBlockCommandHandler`: when attribution shortens a redemption block's slot, the checker is asked again for the shortened slot before anything is changed (the item marked "to trace during the build" — pinned by `HandleAsync_RedemptionBlock_CappedBelowItsDaysFloor_FailsAndSavesNothing`).
   - `PlannedBlock.PlaceUnder` only turns a plain block into a Bonus block.
   - **Behaviour that changed for moving, resizing and re-presetting:** a free Bonus block moves anywhere inside the period and may change preset, under any strictness; no member leaves the period; the floor is checked on every day and never for a Bonus block; **saving a form with nothing changed is allowed even under Lockdown** (before, Lockdown refused any save — only what changes is asked about now).
   - Validator tests: the eight floor cases that mocked the planned-hours query are replaced by three that run the real checker over real blocks; the rules themselves are tested in the Domain.
   - **Until phase 6 the Planner still decides its own locks**, so it may hide or refuse a drag the backend would now allow (a free Bonus block under Lockdown, for instance).
4. **Split repointed** — both halves follow the movement range (decision 5 as narrowed by 15); "must belong to a contract" becomes "must be bound by one". **Done 2026-10-02.** `SplitPlannedBlockValidator` keeps only the shape of a split (the pieces don't overlap, add up to the original, each on one day) and asks `CheckSplit`, then placement per piece — fifteen sentences are seven. Gone from it: Focus only, contract only, not Lockdown, cancelled, a session exists, future only, "on the original's days", floor protection. **Two rules changed owner:** a cancelled block is refused by its own lock (`PlannedBlock.CheckOwnChange` — it is part of a broken contract's record); "a session has already run against it" is dropped — a session only exists once its block has started, which the lock covers. Under Period Study the rule now lets a piece land on another contract day; the dialog can't enter one until phase 7.
5. **Delete repointed** (decisions 4, 9, 10). **Done 2026-10-02.** `DeletePlannedBlockValidator` is one question to `CheckDelete`. A future free Bonus block can be deleted; a past one, a binding one and every Committed or Redemption block can't.
6. **Flags on the block DTO; the Planner's own permission properties deleted** (decision 16); F2 fixed; `PlannerSplitButtonViewTests` repointed to `CanSplit`. **Done 2026-10-02** (1710 tests green). `PlannedBlockDto.Allowances` (required) replaces `HasStarted`, `IsUnderInEffectContract` and `ContractStrictnessRule`; `GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandler` asks `AllowancesFor`. `PlannerPageViewModel`: one `EditingAllowances` replaces three state flags; `CanRescheduleBlock` (new, the date and time fields), `CanChangePreset`, `CanSaveBlock`, `CanSplitBlock`, `CanDeleteFromForm`, `CanDeleteSnapshotBlock` and `IsTileFullyLocked` read it; `IsBlockLocked` is gone. The session-changed handler no longer re-announces locks (F2: nothing decides a lock from the in-memory session). `PlannerSplitButtonViewTests` is folded into `PlannerBlockLockViewTests`. `MembershipRuleTests` has no Gap 93 entries left. **Checked after phase 6:** nothing outside the Domain compares `StrictnessRule` values (the seed data aside) or calls `PlannedBlock.HasStarted`. **Left:** clicking the running session's own tile still does nothing because of an in-memory check (`EditBlockAsync`), which after a restart opens a read-only form instead — not a lock, noted for Gap 89 area 4 (F1).
7. **Split dialog date picker** so a Period Study half can land on another day (decision 5) — UI only; the rule already allows it after phase 4. **Done 2026-10-02** (architect's choice: a date field per piece, shown only when another day is possible). `BlockChangeAllowances.SplitPiecesMayChangeDay`, from `NenContract.AllowsBlockOnAnotherDay` — one more ready-made answer, so the Planner still doesn't know what a strictness means. `PlannerPageViewModel`: `SplitFirstDate`, `SplitSecondDate`, `ShowSplitDates`; the dialog shows a `DatePicker` above each piece's times when the flag is set, and looks as before otherwise. Pinned by two headless tests in `PlannerBlockLockViewTests`. Rejected: always showing the dates (offered, then always refused under Daily Flex); leaving the dialog as it was.
8. **Guards and close-out:** architecture test "nothing outside the Domain compares `StrictnessRule` values (the label converter excepted) and no ViewModel computes a permission from fields"; delete this gap's entries in `MembershipRuleTests`, `RawTimeFieldRuleTests` and `ValidatorWordingRuleTests`; dictionary rows; history entry; manual checks. **Guards built 2026-10-02** (1717 tests green), close-out still to do:
   - `ChangePermissionRuleTests.NothingOutsideTheDomainComparesStrictnessValues` — probed red with a deliberate `== StrictnessRule.Locked` in the Application project. Two listed exceptions, both display: the label converter, and the wizard's second copy of the three names (→ Gap 100).
   - **"No ViewModel computes a permission from fields" is the compiler's** (Guard Hierarchy rank 1): the block DTO no longer carries the fields a ViewModel would compute from (`HasStarted`, `IsUnderInEffectContract`, `ContractStrictnessRule`) — only `Allowances`.
   - `MembershipRuleTests`: this gap's four entries are deleted (one left, Gap 68's). `ValidatorWordingRuleTests`: Update 9 → 4, Split 15 → 7, Delete 3 → 1 — what is left is input shape, for Gap 100. `RawTimeFieldRuleTests` had no entry of this gap left to delete.
   - The `[Obsolete]` `Seal` bridge is deleted from the Domain; the 54 test call sites that describe a contract by strictness and floor rule go through `TestContracts.Seal`, which builds the rules.
   - **Still to do at close:** `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md`, `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY_NEN_CONTRACT.md` and `UI_UX_STANDARDS.md` rows that name the deleted members; the `DESIGN_GAP_HISTORY.md` entry; settle the lessons below; the index line.

*Lessons to settle at close-out:*
- **The interrogation's "one flag or two" and "how many reasons" were only settled by writing the tests.** Move and resize turned out to be one answer; "has started" and "is running" turned out to be two. Reviewing the interface on paper got both the other way round.
- **A fact stored twice is found by asking "can these two disagree?"** The floor rule was stored beside the strictness it follows from; the phase 0 probe found it only because the mapping had to be written out.
- **A no-op counted as a change.** Lockdown refused saving an untouched form because the rule was "any update", not "any change". Asking only about what changes removed the special case.
- **Rules that guard an impossible state can be deleted, once the state is shown impossible.** "A session already ran against it" on Split — a session only exists once its block has started. Checked in the code before deleting, not assumed.
- **A script with one parse error runs none of it; a script that throws midway has run the first half.** Both happened while patching tests. Check what was written before re-running.
- **Manual checks: only what tests can't prove** (architect, 2026-10-02) — on-screen wiring, a migration on the real database. A twenty-item list repeated what about 110 new tests already covered.

*Not in this gap:* Strict Mode and enforcing "Allow cancellation" (Gap 83); the Change Window (parked, Gap 92); lifecycle rules in the contract validators (Gap 92); redemption capping (Gap 106); a refusal shown while dragging (possible follow-up); a "no splitting" contract toggle (decision 5 — not built, no placeholder).

**From Gap 97 (2026-10-02):** `MembershipRuleTests` lists this gap's files as exceptions (the three permission validators and the Planner's copy of the Delete rule) — delete those entries as each is rewritten. One use the test can't see: `PlannerPageViewModel` filters draft tiles with `b.ContractId is { } id && id == editingId`; decide here (or in Gap 89 area 2) whether that is a membership decision. Until this gap, a Bonus block can't be deleted, follows the contract's strictness when moved, and can be split (Gap 97, decision 17; confirmed in the app 2026-10-02).

**Still to interrogate (after Gap 97):** where "in effect" belongs (finding 5: Split checks strictness regardless of status); the service's name, method and verdict shape (per `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` → Design Review Rules); how floor protection is asked per concrete change (finding 6, incl. the suspected cross-midnight miss); the DTO flags and F2's "may the running block change" answer.

**Last step — architecture tests (Gap 98):** add the narrow rule this gap makes true — *no validator or ViewModel decides change permissions itself*: nothing outside the Domain branches on `StrictnessRule` values (the label converter excepted) and no ViewModel computes `Can…`/`…Locked` from fields — it reads the block DTO's flags. Delete the "Gap 93" entries in `RawTimeFieldRuleTests` (Split validator, `PlannerPageViewModel`'s "has it started"). The permission validators return typed reasons as they're rewritten (Gap 100 direction) — delete their entries from Gap 88's typed-reasons test. Exact test form decided when the gap is designed.

**Finding (Gap 98, 2026-09-29):** the floor-protection messages are written three times — "This would drop {date}'s total below its {floor}h daily floor." twice in `UpdatePlannedBlockValidator` and once in `SplitPlannedBlockValidator`, and "…the contract's total planned hours below its {floor}h…" in both. Same guard, same wording, three copies; a typed reason (`BelowDailyFloor { Day, FloorHours }`) with wording built once removes them.

**Finding F2 (Gap 89 Pass 2, 2026-09-29) — confirmed, UI only:** after an app restart during a planned session, the Planner's "is this the running block" checks read `ActiveSession.OriginatingBlockId`, which the restart path never fills (Gap 89 area 4, F1). So the running block's tile is unlocked: Save, Delete, Split and drag are all offered. The backend refuses each of them, but by coincidence rather than by a rule about the running block: Delete's validator asks the database, Split refuses a started block, and Update hits the active-session overlap check with a misleading "please plan your session after HH:mm". **This gap should give "may the running block change" one explicit answer**, from the database or the block DTO, never from the in-memory session copy, with a clear typed reason instead of the overlap message.

---

## Gap 95 — One Source for Focus Hours *(closed, 2026-10-06)*

**What was wrong:** "how many Focus hours does this count for" had one rule (`FocusBlockOutcomeClassifier.CreditedWindow`) fed by the same three steps in six handlers, each loading the block's session; "was this day met" had two answers in one query (the badge's four-way branch with an ad-hoc target, the banner's and recap's `IsDayMet` on stored floors); a missed block's deficit was computed twice; `PostSessionDto` was built in three places; a stored `ContractFloor` sat beside the rules it followed from; and a contract could be completed while a session was still adding hours.

**Built (2026-10-06, eight slices, each committed green):**
1. `FocusBlock` answers for itself from its own stamps — `IsLateAt`, `StartedLate`, `EndedEarly`, `Outcome`, `WorkSlot`, `CreditedSlot` — pinned by parity tests against the classifier across 23 cases; `MarkSessionEnded` refuses an end without, or before, the Ritual.
2. Every reader repointed to the block (no session lookups for credit); `OwnDeficit` replaced `CanContributeOwnDeficit` + `RawDeficitMinutes`; `FocusBlockOutcomeClassifier` deleted. One accepted number change: a session still running, started within grace, can read up to a minute higher live (one rounding instead of two — decision 2).
3. One "done": `PlannedBlock.IsFinal`; `PlannedBlockResolutionExtensions` deleted. No contract ends while a session runs — `NenContract.CheckComplete` / `CheckBreak` → `ContractEndVerdict` (decision 18), asked by the readiness query, both validators and both handlers; Break's force-complete path removed.
4. `Session.CountedTime(originatingBlock)` and one `PostSessionDto.From` builder.
5. The block's stamps are Domain-only (EF maps them by name, no model change); the tile reads `WorkSlot`, `StartedLate`, `EndedEarly`, `Outcome`; `IsCompleted` deleted; the missed validator asks `IsUnresolved`; the missed-block SQL uses `FocusBlock.Filters`.
6. Floors are planning minimums of what was signed: `CheckFloor` counts Committed blocks only and Seal asks it; a Redemption block's length is fixed (`NenContract.CheckBlockLength`); `ContractFloor` deleted (migration `DropContractFloor`), the period target read through `HasPeriodTarget`.
7. One day rule: `NenContract.DayStatuses` → `ContractDayStatus`, read by the badges, days behind, banner, recap and completion through `GetNenContractDayStatusesQuery`; the completed/Bonus hours queries, the four-way branch, the ad-hoc target, the dedup, `IsDayMet`, `PercentOfFloorMet`, `IsRestDay`, `DailyFloor.PercentComplete` deleted; `ComputePlannedFocusHoursQuery` drafts-only. Decision 5's moved numbers pinned by tests written first.
8. Close-out: no reader works out outcome or credit around the block (checked in code); stale comments and doc rows fixed; `Session.HasEnded`, left without callers by slice 2, deleted.

**Where the code differed from the signed-off plan (each recorded in its decision or slice line below):** the Redemption length rule is `CheckBlockLength`, not part of `CheckBlockMove` — Split asks `CheckBlockMove` for each shorter half, so the planned home would have refused every Redemption split; editing a Redemption block no longer caps it, so the Update handler's re-check and `RedemptionCapped` went; the drafts-only step moved from slice 6 to 7, because the badges still read saved blocks through the query until `DayStatuses` replaced them; `FloorHoursFor` stays (the edit form needs the stored minimum); a cancelled Committed block still counts toward its day's target; `AllowCancellation` stays unenforced with no unreachable enum value (Gap 83).

**Guard (step 5):** yes, by the compiler first (Guard Hierarchy rank 1) — the Ritual and session-end stamps are `internal`, so code outside the Domain can't compute lateness, outcome or credit; making them so turned the build red at exactly the readers decision 14 predicted, which were then moved onto the block. The classifier, `IsResolved`, `ContractFloor`, `IsDayMet`, `PercentOfFloorMet`, `IsRestDay` and both hours queries are deleted, so nothing is left to call. Tests: `DtoBuilderRuleTests`' "PostSessionDto built in 3 files" entry deleted (seen failing with "no longer violates" first); `ValidatorWordingRuleTests` Complete 4 → 2; the badge-vs-banner same-verdict test; pinning tests for every number decision 5 moved and for decision 11 (not ready, and Complete and Break refused, while a session runs). No architecture test added: what remains is the Domain's public answers themselves.

**Lessons (settled):**
- **A signed-off *home* needs the hardest-caller check too, not only a signature** — `CheckBlockMove` read right for the length rule until Split's halves went through it. Design Review Rule 16 already says this for signatures; the same check applies when a rule is placed in an existing method. *No test — a review judgment; added to Design Review Rule 16 in `MODULE_STANDARDS.md`.*
- **A plan step that deletes a caller's source needs that caller listed first** — the drafts-only query and `FloorHoursFor` both still had a caller the plan expected to be gone (Design Review Rule 11). *No test — Rule 11 covers it.*
- **Parity tests before repointing paid off:** comparing the new block answers with the classifier across 23 cases found the live double-rounding before any number moved. *Kept as the slice pattern; no new rule.*
- **A stub that throws turns every test red, including ones that would pass for the wrong reason** — so new tests were also probed by breaking the real code on purpose. *Already the working habit; no rule.*

**Manual checks (2026-10-06):** tile labels, live badge, the mid-session refusal, the Redemption resize drop and day badges passed. The `DropContractFloor` upgrade of a database holding contracts couldn't be checked — the architect had reset the database, so it ran on a fresh one with every other migration. Follow-up from the checks: a refused cancel showed as a line inside the Nen Contract popover; it is now a modal ("Can't Cancel Contract", one OK button) and the popover closes behind it (`PlannerPageViewModel.DismissCancelContractError`). Then, at the architect's request, the Cancel button is hidden whenever a cancel would be refused, and **the contract's own "Allow cancellation" is now enforced** — ending the 2026-08-18 interim that decision 18 had kept: `CheckBreak` refuses `CancellationNotAllowed` first, `CheckNenContractCancellableQuery` gives the Planner the same answer (`CanCancelActiveContract`), and the Break validator and handler refuse it too. The modal stays only for a session started after the popover opened. The follow-up checks — Cancel hidden when a session runs and when sealed without cancellation, and the modal fallback — passed in the app. Gap 83 keeps only "does Strict Mode add to this".

*The write-up as it stood in `OPEN_GAPS.md` at close:*


**The question:** "how many Focus hours does this count for?" — credited minutes per block, completed and Bonus hours per day, deficits, "was this day met". The *rule* has one home (`FocusBlockOutcomeClassifier.CreditedWindow`); how it is fed, what counts, and what "met" means do not.

**Findings (checked in code 2026-09-29):**
1. **The same three steps in six handlers** — load the block's session → elapsed → five loose values into `CreditedFocusMinutes`/`CreditedWindow`: `GetNenContractCompletedFocusHours`, `GetNenContractBonusFocusHours`, `GetNenContractDeficits`, `GetNenContractsForDateRange` (`ResolveMissedBlockRawDeficitMinutesAsync`), `EndSessionCommandHandler`, `GetUnacknowledgedPostSessions`; one database lookup per block for the session. The block already holds what is needed: `RitualCompletedAt` is set with the same `now` as `Session.StartedAt` (`StartSessionCommandHandler.cs:130,134`) and `SessionEndedAt` is a faithful copy of `Session.EndedAt` (Gap 86) — so credit can be a `FocusBlock` method returning a `TimeSlot` (e.g. `CreditedSlot(asOf)`), no session lookup. Same smell Gap 86 fixed for `Classify` (callers pass `session?.EndedAt`, not the block's stamp).
2. **"Which blocks are Bonus for this contract" defined three times** — Bonus hours (unlinked Focus blocks the contract `Covers`), the recap's count (had no Focus filter — fixed as Gap 94), completion readiness's pending count (unlinked blocks of any type — the contract can wait on a Background block that earns nothing; deliberate? not written down). The "a day either side" range pre-fetch is copied three times.
3. **"Was this day met" has two answers in one query** — `GetNenContractsForDateRangeQueryHandler`'s day badge uses a four-way decision built in the Application layer (period-floor mode / committed floor / **ad-hoc floor** = a floorless day with blocks gets its planned hours as target / rest), while the banner's "days behind" a few lines earlier, and the recap's streak and percentage, use `IsRestDay`/`IsDayMet`, where the same day is rest. The badge can say unmet where the banner and recap say rest. The ad-hoc floor exists only in this read query. **Product question** (may be decided in Gap 67).
4. **Planned hours per day, two versions** — the same query adds a redemption dedup adjustment before comparing to the floor; `ComputePlannedFocusHoursQuery`, used by the Update/Split floor-protection guards, doesn't. Only differs on days with repeat redemption claims — suspected, not confirmed by a test.
5. **A missed block's deficit computed twice** — `GetNenContractsForDateRange` recomputes it without the deficits query's "outcome is final only once the session has ended" rule (Redemption Rule 18).
6. **Service shape** — `FocusBlockOutcomeClassifier` has four jobs (late start, outcome, credited minutes, credited window); `Classify(block, asOf)` is a single-entity question (the "Where a Rule Lives" warning sign), yet `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` names this class as its example domain service. For Gap 92.
7. **For Gap 67** — completed hours also count spontaneous sessions on the contract's Focus presets; Bonus hours don't. Gap 67 changes exactly this, decided today inside one handler's loop.
8. **Seen again in Gap 89 Pass 2 (F6, 2026-09-29):** `PostSessionDto` is built in three handlers (`EndSession`, `MarkFocusBlockMissed`, `GetUnacknowledgedPostSessions`); all ask `FocusBlockOutcomeClassifier.CreditedFocusMinutes`, but each assembles its inputs. On Gap 98's `DtoBuilderRuleTests` list ("PostSessionDto built in 3 files") — this gap's one source for credited minutes should give it one builder.

**Direction (for interrogation, not decided):** the block answers "how much did I earn" from its own stamps; the contract answers "is this block mine, Bonus, or neither" and "what does this day owe" (including whether an ad-hoc target is real); one per-day read model on top, used by the badge, banner, recap, readiness and the floor guards alike.

**Interrogation decisions (2026-10-03 to 2026-10-06; findings re-checked in code, still hold after Gaps 97/93; read through for consistency 2026-10-06 — later decisions win, and each earlier one they change carries a pointer):**
1. **Credit, outcome, late start and resolved move onto `FocusBlock`; `FocusBlockOutcomeClassifier` and its interface are deleted** (Where a Rule Lives step 3: one block's question from its own data; a separate value object for the stamps was weighed and rejected). New members: `GracePeriodMinutes`, `IsLateAt(moment)` (asked with the Ritual stamp and with "now"), `Outcome(asOf)`, `CreditedSlot(asOf)` (no `CreditedFocusMinutes` — callers read `?.Length`), `IsResolved(asOf)` as a virtual on `PlannedBlock` (Background → `HasEnded`; `PlannedBlockResolutionExtensions` deleted — closes Gap 92 S3) — *superseded by decision 11: the virtual is `IsFinal`, `IsResolved` goes.* *Members extended by decision 15: `WorkSlot()`, `StartedLate`, `EndedEarly`; `Outcome` and `CreditedSlot` built from them.* `MinimumMeaningfulDeficitMinutes` moves to `FocusBlock`. Handlers stop loading sessions for credit. `MODULE_STANDARDS.md`'s example domain service becomes `PolicyEngine` (closes Gap 92 S2).
2. **Credit rule kept, end side collapsed:** start = scheduled start if the Ritual completed within grace, else the Ritual moment (grace stays — dropping it would leave every block a few minutes short; a question for Gap 67 if ever revisited); end = `SessionEndedAt`, or `asOf` capped at the scheduled end while running. Early-vs-on-time stays only in `Outcome`. No number changes, with one accepted exception (found in slice 1, 2026-10-06): a session still running, started within grace, can read up to 1 minute *higher* live — today's callers pass an elapsed time already floored to whole minutes and `CreditedWindow` floors again, while `CreditedSlot` floors once. Seen only in the Planner's live badge, banner and Bonus pill (tenths of an hour, so rarely visible); identical once the session ends. Pinned by `CreditedSlot_RunningWithinGraceWithSeconds_FloorsOnceWhereTodayFloorsTwice`. *Shape refined by decision 15: these rules live in `WorkSlot()`; `CreditedSlot` cuts and rounds it.*
3. **`MarkSessionEnded` gains two guards:** throws if `RitualCompletedAt` is null, or if the end is before it. The block is now the only source credit reads, so it refuses stamps its rules can't interpret.
4. ~~**`FocusBlock.IsCommitted` deleted** (no callers, not even tests).~~ *Corrected by decision 15: the COMMITTED tile tag uses it; it stays.* `IsRedemption`/`IsBonus`/`CountsTowardFloor` settled with finding 2, once their callers move.
5. **One day-target rule for every contract type (product change, decided here):** a day owes its committed blocks' planned hours (live; a missed or ended-early block still counts in full), plus, for each missed block it redeems, the deficit minutes it covers — counted once per missed block per day and never more than that block's deficit. Target 0 = rest day; met = completed ≥ target. Replaces the badge's four-way branch and ad-hoc floor (finding 3) and the dedup adjustment, and the floor guards read the same planned-hours number (finding 4) — the plan side only, and Committed blocks only (decision 16); they compare it against the stored minimum (decision 7), never the target, or they'd always pass. A fixed daily floor becomes a planning minimum only (Seal and the move guards), not the day's target. **Numbers that move, each pinned by a test:** fixed-floor days planned above the floor; Period Study's banner/recap (now count days behind); banner/recap now count days carrying a redemption.
6. **Home: `NenContract.DayStatuses(blocks, sessions, asOf, timeZone)` → one `ContractDayStatus { Date, TargetHours, CompletedHours, BonusHours, IsRest, IsMet }` per day** (step 4: the contract owns day questions with blocks passed in, as `CheckBlockMove`; a Domain service was weighed and rejected — it would need the private calendar opened or copied). The contract filters to its own blocks by role (finding 2) and decides which spontaneous sessions count (finding 7, today's behaviour, until Gap 67). Arithmetic in a Domain-internal helper so the entity doesn't bloat. One Application query wraps it for the badge, banner, recap and readiness; `GetNenContractCompletedFocusHours` and `GetNenContractBonusFocusHours` fold into it. *(Read-through: the floor guards and `ComputePlannedFocusHours` were listed here too — superseded by decisions 7 and 9: the guards stay in the Domain (`BlockChangeChecker` → `CheckFloor`) and compare the plan against the stored minimum, and the query stays, drafts-only.)*
7. **Every stored floor is a planning minimum only (2026-10-06):** the per-day floor (Fixed, or Follows Plan's sealed plan) and the whole-period total are read by Seal and `NenContract.CheckFloor` — "the plan may not drop below what was signed" — and never decide "met" (decision 5's target does). Confirmed with the case: a Fixed 2h day planned at 3h shows 0/3h. Still needed after Seal: without it a Daily Flex block could shrink to 5 min and the live target shrink with it. `CheckFloor` stays one checker; each contract has per-day minimums or a total, never both. *Its plan side is Committed blocks only — decision 16.* `IsDayMet`, `PercentOfFloorMet` and `FloorHoursFor` are deleted once `DayStatuses` replaces their callers. **Weighed and rejected:** storing only one total per contract (daily floor × working days) — it loses per-day protection under Period Study with a fixed floor (allowed: `ContractRules.FloorRule` puts Fixed first) and can't hold Gap 82's different floor per day.
8. **`ContractFloor` deleted (field, column, migration) (2026-10-06):** Seal/Edit set it to the planned-hours sum exactly when the floor rule is None — the same number as `CommittedFocusHours`, its existence following from the rules (two stored fields where one follows from the other). `CheckFloor` asks the rules: no daily floors → the minimum is `CommittedFocusHours`. "Is period mode" is read from `FloorRule is None` and carried on the DTO under its own name, replacing `ContractFloorHours.HasValue`. Seal's "both a daily and a whole-contract floor" check goes — the state can't be stored. No numbers change.
9. **Drafting's floor preview goes to Gap 68; this gap only trims (2026-10-06):** the guards are already Domain (`BlockChangeChecker` → `CheckFloor`, Gap 93), so the "draft variant" is only the wizard's day badges, whose rest/required/met logic is a ViewModel copy (`PlannerPageViewModel.RefreshDayFloorPreviewsAsync`). It moves when drafting gets a real `ContractCalendar` (Gap 68 with 82) — moving it now would need a draft-only Domain API that Gap 68 then replaces. Here: `ComputePlannedFocusHoursQuery` becomes drafts-only (`ContractId` branch and the caller-less `ExcludeBlockId` deleted; the badge's saved-blocks use folds into `DayStatuses`), and Seal's per-day "planned ≥ floor" loop (`NenContract.Seal`) calls the same check as `CheckFloor` — one rule, asked twice.
10. **`FocusBlock.OwnDeficit(asOf) → TimeSpan` replaces `CanContributeOwnDeficit(outcome)` + `RawDeficitMinutes(credited)` (2026-10-06, finding 5):** zero unless the outcome is final (session ended, or missed — Rule 18 moves onto the block, a private helper until a second caller needs it), zero for a redemption block, else scheduled length minus `CreditedSlot`. The deficit ledger and `DayStatuses`' redemption cap both call it. Finding 5's divergence (the badge skipped Rule 18) was verified unreachable — a redemption entry can only point at a block already final — so no number changes. Walked "Decide in this order": one block's data → entity method; a Domain service (anemic) and a `Deficit` value object (wrapper, no rule) rejected. How much a covering block delivered against its claim stays with the ledger — Gap 106's attribution.
11. **One "done": `IsFinal(asOf)`; a contract can't be ended while a session runs (2026-10-06, product decision):** `IsResolved` counted a block done once its Ritual completed, session still running — right for Break (cancelling it would cut the session's blocking), wrong for Complete's readiness (a contract could complete mid-session, freezing the recap while hours still grow — traced in code, a test proves it first). Instead of two definitions: **Complete and Break are refused while a session is running** (extends Gap 105's "no contract changes while Focus time is running"; built here, since Break's switch depends on it), so the running case can't occur when a contract ends. `IsFinal(asOf)` — session ended, or missed; Background → `HasEnded` — is a virtual on `PlannedBlock`, asked by readiness, `FateWhenBroken` (asks the block itself, its `isResolved` parameter deleted) and `OwnDeficit` (decision 10's private helper becomes this). `IsResolved` and `PlannedBlockResolutionExtensions` deleted; replaces decision 1's `IsResolved`. **Tests:** Complete and Break refused while a session runs; readiness not ready with a running block.

12. **Finding 2 already closed by Gap 97 (verified 2026-10-06):** Bonus is a stored role; Bonus hours, the recap and `DeleteNenContract` all read it, no date-range pre-fetch is left. Readiness counting every member, Background included, is deliberate and written down (Gap 97 — completing must leave nothing to clean up). `IsRedemption`/`IsBonus`/`CountsTowardFloor` stay as one-line shortcuts onto `Membership` (tile tags, recap counts, attribution callers); their hours-query uses fold into `DayStatuses` (decision 6).

13. **One `PostSessionDto` builder; "how long did this session count for" in the Domain (2026-10-06, finding 8):** `Session.CountedTime(FocusBlock? originatingBlock)` — asked of an ended session only, measured at its own `EndedAt` (all three callers have one): the block's `CreditedSlot` length when planned (none for a missed block → 0), else elapsed; throws if the block isn't its own (step 4: the session owns the question, the block is passed in). `PostSessionDto.From(session, originatingBlock, presetName, attempts)` only maps and groups attempts; `EndSession`, `MarkFocusBlockMissed` and `GetUnacknowledgedPostSessions` all call it — retires `DtoBuilderRuleTests`' "PostSessionDto built in 3 files" entry. The rule inside the factory was rejected (a business rule in Application). A future counting rule that should reach the floor too (e.g. breaks don't count) goes in the block's credit rule — noted in Gap 78.

14. **Guards (2026-10-06):** *Compiler:* the deletions in decisions 1, 7, 8, 10, 11 leave nothing to call; and **`FocusBlock.RitualCompletedAt` / `SessionEndedAt` become Domain-only (`internal`)**, EF mapping them by name (as `TimeSlot.Start`/`End`, `NenFocusDbContext.cs:343`) — so late, credit and outcome can't be recomputed outside the block. Replaces the planned "list the credit callers" test. Forces three readers onto the block: the Planner tile's late flag and drawn span (`GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandler` — a fourth copy of the late/credit rule, new finding), `MarkFocusBlockMissedValidator`'s "Ritual not done", and `GetUnresolvedFocusBlocksAsync`'s SQL (→ a Domain-built filter, Gap 88 precedent). *Tests:* delete `DtoBuilderRuleTests`' "PostSessionDto built in 3 files"; pinning tests for every number decision 5 moves, for decision 11 (not ready / Complete and Break refused while a session runs), and badge-vs-banner same verdict for the same day. *Review:* whether `DayStatuses` stays deep.

15. **The block's spans and labels, each rule once (2026-10-06, decision 14's finding):**
    - **`FocusBlock.WorkSlot()`** — the whole span the block is worked over, from its stamps only, no "now": start = scheduled start if the Ritual completed within grace, else the Ritual moment; end = `SessionEndedAt` if before the scheduled end, else the scheduled end (still planned while running); null if the Ritual wasn't done (upcoming, missed). **`CreditedSlot(asOf)` = `WorkSlot()` cut at `asOf`, then rounded down to whole minutes (null if that leaves 0)** — rounding is credit's only own rule, as today (`CreditedWindow` floors; the tile deliberately stays exact); the grace and early-end rules live only in `WorkSlot`, so the tile and every hour count can't drift (refines decision 2's end side; verified against `ComputeRawWindow` in the read-through — same answers, including a session ended at/after the scheduled end → the scheduled end). The tile draws `WorkSlot() ?? Scheduled`. Named like its siblings (`Scheduled`, `ReservedSlot`, `CreditedSlot`): a `TimeSlot`, not a new type — no rule a plain span lacks.
    - **`StartedLate`** (Ritual past grace, before the block's end) and **`EndedEarly`** (session ended before the scheduled end) as block properties; **`Outcome(asOf)` is built from them** in today's priority (Missed → EndedEarly → StartedLate → CompletedOnTime). `PlannedBlockDto` carries both flags; `PlannerPageView.axaml.cs`'s "ended early and also started late → both labels" branch goes — it shows one label per true flag (and MISSED). The recap still counts each block once by `Outcome` (a late-and-early block counts as ended early — today's number).
    - **`PlannedBlockDto.IsCompleted` deleted** — nothing reads it (only a test); its fill line, the drafting line and the test go. `MarkFocusBlockMissedValidator` asks `IsUnresolved(asOf)` — the missed sweep's own rule — instead of the raw stamp. No "Ritual completed" property is added.
    - **Correction to decision 4:** `FocusBlock.IsCommitted` is *not* caller-less — the COMMITTED tile tag uses it (`GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandler`, pattern-match form missed by decision 4's check). It stays, with `IsRedemption`/`IsBonus`.
    - After this, StartedLate / EndedEarly / Missed / CompletedOnTime are decided only on the block; the tile, recap, deficit ledger and the Ritual's late notice (`IsLateAt(now)`) ask it; converters only word and colour it.

16. **The floor guard counts Committed blocks only; a redemption block's length is fixed (2026-10-06, found in the read-through — decision 7 vs `BlockChangeChecker`):** the guard summed every `CountsTowardFloor` block (Committed + Redemption, `BlockChangeChecker.cs:105`), so on a day with a missed-block redemption (Tuesday: 2h committed + 1h redemption) the committed block could shrink to 1h (the redemption paid for it), and the redemption itself could shrink away (its claim shrinks, the deficit quietly reopens). Architect's rule: that 3h can't drop to 2h. **(a)** `CheckFloor`'s plan side is Committed blocks only — compared against what was signed (decision 7); only a Committed block's change can lower it. **(b)** A Redemption block is **at most** the remaining deficit at creation (today's cap, kept — so a 2h deficit can be redeemed 1h today + 1h tomorrow) and **its length never changes afterwards**: it moves as far as the strictness allows and may be split (pieces sum to the same length), never resized. Home: `NenContract.CheckBlockMove` refuses a new slot of a different length for a Redemption block (typed `ChangeOutcome.RedemptionLengthFixed`, worded once) — `BlockChangeChecker` already asks it on every reschedule. The creation cap stays with attribution (Gap 106 moves it to the Domain). Behaviour changes, each pinned by a test: (i) shrinking a committed block refused when a redemption on its day is all that kept the guard passing; (ii) resizing a redemption refused. Simplifies Gap 106: attribution's carry-forward no longer rescales a claim after a resize. **Weighed and rejected:** "Committed only" alone (leaves the redemption shrinkable); one combined minimum of floor + that day's redemption claims (claims are rescaled by attribution, so the minimum would shrink with the block); length exactly the deficit (blocks partial redemption, and Lockdown can't split). **Built in slice 6 (2026-10-06), three details settled in the code:** (1) the length rule is `NenContract.CheckBlockLength(block, newSlot)`, asked only on a reschedule — not inside `CheckBlockMove`, because `CheckSplit` asks `CheckBlockMove` for each half and every half is shorter, which would have refused every Redemption split (Design Review Rule 16; it also keeps "how far may it move" and "may its length change" apart, Rule 2). (2) Editing a Redemption block no longer caps its slot: attribution recomputes the entries only, so the Update handler's re-check and `UpdatePlannedBlockResult.RedemptionCapped` are gone (the cap still applies at creation). (3) Not yet done: the Planner still offers the resize handle on a Redemption tile (`BlockChangeAllowances` has one flag for move and resize) — the resize is refused with `RedemptionLengthFixed` when dropped. Splitting that flag is for whoever next touches the allowances; noted for slice 8's app checks.

17. **Outcome stays an enum; no result value object now; freezing for Insights deferred to Gap 74 (2026-10-06):** `FocusBlockOutcome` is one verdict whose values each change behaviour (recap counts, colours, the deficit), and only the tile needs outcome + both flags together — a value object would be a wrapper used once (Design Review Rules 3, 5, 6). A frozen result record *will* earn its place when Insights needs history frozen at completion; because this gap routes every reader through the block, that freeze is a one-place change later. Recorded in Gap 74.

18. **One answer to "may this contract end now?" — `NenContract.CheckComplete` / `CheckBreak` (2026-10-06, during slice 3; replaces the signed-off `CheckEnd(bool sessionRunning)`):** walked "Decide in this order": a rule needing the contract's rules and status, its blocks and whether a session runs → the contract owns it with the rest passed in (step 4, like `CheckBlockMove`); a Domain service (anemic) and a value object (`ContractRules` holds `AllowCancellation` but not status, time or blocks) rejected. `CheckComplete(members, sessionRunning, completedFocusHours, asOf)` and `CheckBreak(sessionRunning)` return a `ContractEndVerdict` (`ContractEndOutcome`: `Allowed` / `SessionIsRunning` / `HoursNotDelivered` / `BlocksStillPending`, with the hours still owed and the blocks not done) — its own type, as placement and block changes each have theirs (Design Review Rules 2, 8). It also moves completion readiness's rule out of the Application query into the Domain. Worded once in `ContractEndVerdictDto.From`; readiness, both validators and both handlers ask it (the handlers too, so skipping the validator can't end a contract mid-session); Break's "force-end the running session" path is gone, now unreachable. `completedFocusHours` is passed in until slice 7's `DayStatuses`. **`AllowCancellation` stays unenforced** (interim since 2026-08-18): no `CancellationNotAllowed` value until Gap 83 decides whether it or Strict Mode is the gate — a value nothing returns changes no behaviour (Rule 5); `CheckBreak` is where it goes. **Not covered:** the heartbeat completing a contract at its natural end while a spontaneous session runs — closed by Gap 67 question 7.

**Still to interrogate:** nothing — read-through done 2026-10-06 (fixes folded into decisions 2, 6, 13, 15 and the closing paragraphs; one contradiction became decision 16).

**Plan (signed off 2026-10-06; the contract-end check reshaped by decision 18) — interfaces first, then thin slices, each red → green → refactor:**

*Interfaces (Domain unless noted):*
```csharp
// FocusBlock — the block answers for itself from its own stamps (decisions 1, 2, 10, 15)
public const int GracePeriodMinutes = 5;
public const double MinimumMeaningfulDeficitMinutes = 1;
public bool IsLateAt(Moment moment);              // past grace — asked with "now" by the Ritual notice
public bool StartedLate { get; }                  // Ritual past grace, before the block's end
public bool EndedEarly { get; }                   // session ended before the scheduled end
public FocusBlockOutcome? Outcome(Moment asOf);   // built from the two flags, today's priority
public TimeSlot? WorkSlot();                      // grace + early-end rules live here only
public TimeSlot? CreditedSlot(Moment asOf);       // WorkSlot cut at asOf, rounded down to whole minutes
public TimeSpan OwnDeficit(Moment asOf);          // 0 unless final and not a redemption
public override bool IsFinal(Moment asOf);        // session ended, or missed
internal Moment? RitualCompletedAt { get; }       // Domain-only (decision 14); SessionEndedAt likewise
// MarkSessionEnded: throws without a Ritual stamp, or with an end before it (decision 3)

// PlannedBlock
public virtual bool IsFinal(Moment asOf);         // Background → HasEnded (decision 11)

// Session (decision 13) — asked of an ended session, measured at its own EndedAt
public TimeSpan CountedTime(FocusBlock? originatingBlock);

// NenContract (decisions 6, 7, 8, 9, 11, 16)
public IReadOnlyList<ContractDayStatus> DayStatuses(
    IEnumerable<PlannedBlock> blocks, IEnumerable<Session> sessions, Moment asOf, UserTimeZone timeZone);
public ChangeVerdict CheckFloor(IReadOnlyDictionary<DateOnly, double> committedFocusHoursByDay); // Seal asks it too
// CheckBlockLength(block, newSlot): a Redemption block's new slot must keep its length → ChangeOutcome.RedemptionLengthFixed (decision 16, built slice 6)
// FateWhenBroken(block, brokenAt, keepBonusBlocks) — asks block.IsFinal itself, isResolved parameter gone
public ContractEndVerdict CheckComplete(IEnumerable<PlannedBlock> members, bool sessionRunning,
    double completedFocusHours, Moment asOf);       // decision 18; completedFocusHours goes in slice 7
public ContractEndVerdict CheckBreak(bool sessionRunning);  // decision 18; Gap 83 adds AllowCancellation here

// Value object
public sealed record ContractDayStatus(DateOnly Date, double TargetHours, double CompletedHours,
    double BonusHours, bool IsRest, bool IsMet);

// Application
GetNenContractDayStatusesQuery(Guid ContractId) → IReadOnlyList<ContractDayStatus>
PostSessionDto.From(Session session, FocusBlock? originatingBlock, string presetName,
    IReadOnlyList<BlockedAttempt> attempts)
ComputePlannedFocusHoursQuery(IReadOnlyList<TimeSlot> DraftFocusBlockRanges)   // drafts only
```
*Settled at sign-off, then reshaped in slice 3 (decision 18):* "no ending a contract while a session runs" lives in `NenContract.CheckComplete` / `CheckBreak`, returning a `ContractEndVerdict`; Gap 105 adds its Make/Edit reasons to `ContractEndOutcome`.

*Slices, in order (each compiles and passes before the next):*
1. **Block answers, side by side** — add the `FocusBlock` members; parity tests pin `WorkSlot`/`CreditedSlot`/`Outcome`/`StartedLate` to today's `FocusBlockOutcomeClassifier` across every case (on time, grace, late, early, at/after end, running, missed, cancelled). `MarkSessionEnded` guards (red first).
2. **Repoint credit and outcome callers** — the six handlers and the recap stop loading sessions for credit; the Ritual notice asks `IsLateAt(now)`; `OwnDeficit` replaces `CanContributeOwnDeficit` + `RawDeficitMinutes`. Delete the classifier and its interface; `MODULE_STANDARDS.md`'s example domain service → `PolicyEngine` (Gap 92 S2).
3. **One "done"** — test first: a contract with a running session is not ready (red today). Complete and Break refused while a session runs; `IsFinal` replaces `IsResolved`; delete `PlannedBlockResolutionExtensions` (Gap 92 S3); `FateWhenBroken` loses its parameter. Refusal through `CheckComplete` / `CheckBreak` (decision 18).
4. **Post-Session** — `Session.CountedTime`, `PostSessionDto.From`; the three handlers call it; delete the `DtoBuilderRuleTests` entry.
5. **Stamps internal** — `RitualCompletedAt`/`SessionEndedAt` → `internal`, EF by name; the tile reads `WorkSlot() ?? Scheduled`, `StartedLate`, `EndedEarly`, `Outcome`; `IsCompleted` deleted; code-behind shows one label per flag; `MarkFocusBlockMissedValidator` asks `IsUnresolved`; the missed-block SQL becomes a Domain-built filter. The build is the guard.
6. **Floors as minimums** — `CheckFloor` counts Committed only and Seal calls it (tests for decision 16 (i) and (ii) red first); redemption length fixed; delete `ContractFloor` + migration ("is period mode" read from the rules); `ComputePlannedFocusHoursQuery` loses `ExcludeBlockId`. **Built 2026-10-06.** Its `ContractId` branch moved to slice 7: the Planner badges still read saved blocks through it until `DayStatuses` replaces them.
7. **One day status** — `NenContract.DayStatuses` + `ContractDayStatus` (arithmetic in a Domain-internal helper); pinning tests for decision 5's moved numbers written first; the badge, banner, recap and readiness read it; delete `IsDayMet`, `PercentOfFloorMet`, `FloorHoursFor`, the completed/Bonus hours queries, the dedup adjustment; `ComputePlannedFocusHoursQuery` becomes drafts-only (its `ContractId` branch, moved from slice 6); badge-vs-banner same-verdict test. **Built 2026-10-06.** Three details settled in the code: (1) `FloorHoursFor` stays — its last caller is the contract edit form, which needs the stored planning minimum to reopen the wizard, not a day status (decision 7 assumed `DayStatuses` would replace every caller); `IsDayMet`, `PercentOfFloorMet`, `IsRestDay` and `DailyFloor.PercentComplete` are deleted. (2) A cancelled Committed block still counts toward its day's target, so breaking a contract can't clean up the break day (as `Break` already promises). (3) The Planner shows a future day as what it owes, met as planned — by the one rule the plan *is* the target. `CheckComplete` now works out completed hours from `DayStatuses` (its `completedFocusHours` parameter is gone).
8. **Close-out** — confirm no reader of a block's outcome, flags or credit goes around the block (Gap 74's freeze then changes one place — decision 17); `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md` / `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY_NEN_CONTRACT.md` mapping rows, one line in `TECHNICAL_SPEC_INFRA.md`, the history entry with the Step 5 answer (guards = decision 14), manual checks in `THINGS_TO_TEST_IN_APP.md` for what tests can't show (tile labels, live badge during a session, the refusal messages).

**Why before Gap 67 and not folded into it:** Gap 67 changes *what counts*; today that is decided in several places, so it would have to be changed in each. After this gap it is one place. The numbers this gap moves are exactly decision 5's list (one day-target rule — a deliberate product change made during the interrogation) and decision 11's readiness fix, each pinned by a test; nothing else moves. Gap 67 then changes what counts — kept apart, any number that moves traces to the gap that moved it.

**Last step — architecture tests (Gap 98):** decided in decision 14 (compiler first: the block's stamps go Domain-only, which replaces the caller-listing test first planned here).


## Gap 106 — One Source for Redemption Attribution *(closed, 2026-10-07)*

**What was wrong:** "how much of which missed blocks' deficit does this redemption block cover" was answered by a query (`ComputeRedemptionAttributionQuery`) whose answer Create, Update and Split then saved — a Domain rule in the Application layer, with three different attribution rules behind it (Create claimed, Update carried forward and rescaled, Split re-claimed from scratch). A split divided coverage by two rules (Split's proportional division; the Cover Deficit split's "first half claims, second gets the rest"), the Cover Deficit split was two non-atomic creates, Split could shorten a Redemption block (against Gap 95 decision 16), the move preview checked a capped slot while Update saved the uncapped one, and the ViewModel held its own copy of the redemption date rule.

**Built (2026-10-07, slices 1–8, uncommitted until close):**
1. `RedemptionReceipt` (value object) replaces the block's entry list, on `FocusBlock.Receipt`; owns the total and `MinutesFor`; migration `MoveRedemptionReceiptToFocusBlock`. The Domain gets `InternalsVisibleTo` for tests.
1b. The deficit ledger moves into `NenContract.Deficits` → `OutstandingDeficit` (arithmetic in the internal `ContractDeficitLedger`); the deficits query is a loader.
2. `NenContract.CheckRedemptionPlacement` / `PlaceRedemptionBlock` → `RedemptionVerdict` (`RedemptionPlacementOutcome`: `Placed`, `OutsideContract`, `WrongDay`, `NothingOwed`).
3. `CoverDeficitCommand` — the only path that makes a redemption block; one tile, no split in its form, End choices stop at the debt (decision 19); `DeclineRedemption` and `RedemptionCapped` deleted; Create no longer attributes.
4. A move keeps the receipt: attribution leaves Update and the verdict query; `AssignRedemptionEntries` deleted.
4b. Every member judged against its own contract; a drag never changes membership (`WouldJoinContract`); `PlaceUnder` deleted.
5. `RedemptionReceipt.SplitAt` and `FocusBlock.SplitInto` — a split divides the receipt, nothing recalculated or shortened; Split's dead Background branch deleted; the capping methods, `Join` and `OwnDeficit` internal; `MembershipForPlacedBlock()` Bonus only; `ExcludeCoverageFromBlockId` deleted. Slice 6 (deleting `ComputeRedemptionAttribution`, `RedemptionAttributionResult`, the `VerticalSliceRuleTests` entry, `ShellViewModel`'s `using`, plus the then-unused `RedemptionEntryDto`) landed here: making the capping methods internal left the attribution handler unable to compile, and Split was its last caller.
5b. `BackgroundBlock.Create` refuses a Redemption membership.
7. `CqrsRuleTests.CommandHandlersDoNotHoldQueryHandlers`.
8. Docs.

**Where the code differed from the signed-off plan:**
- `RedemptionVerdict` gained `Membership` (approved during slice 3): the validator passes it to `BlockPlacementChecker.Check`, and the Application can't make a Redemption membership (Design Review Rule 16).
- Decision 8's "`FocusBlock.Create`'s receipt parameter becomes internal" can't be written in C# (no internal parameter; two `Create` overloads with optional parameters are ambiguous): the public `Create` takes no receipt, an internal `CreateWithReceipt` takes a required one and holds the "only a redemption block covers a deficit" check.
- `PlannedBlock.Join` made internal (added after slice 4b): with `PlaceUnder` gone it had no production caller, and a public `Join` plus the verdict's public membership would have let the Application make a Redemption block with no receipt.
- `ExcludeCoverageFromBlockId` went in slice 5, not 4 — Split still passed the original's id to attribution until then.
- Considered and dropped the same day (slice 5b): moving the receipt into the Redemption membership (option C), over a `RedemptionBlock` subtype (B — the role stored twice) and a claims table (D — one fact in two aggregates). Re-checking the code showed the membership and the receipt are both the block's fields, set together once and never changed apart — a rule on one entity's data, so the entity is its home. Reopen only with new evidence (debts that are not a contract's, or claims to query → D).
- Docs drift fixed on the way: the Cover Deficit paragraph still described the pre-Gap 88 suggestion (shifting around Focus tiles, a confirmation for a partial tile).

**Guard (step 5):** yes.
- Compiler (rank 1): only the Domain writes a receipt (`CreateWithReceipt`, internal) or makes a Redemption membership (`PlaceRedemptionBlock`; `MembershipForPlacedBlock` hands out Bonus only; `Join` internal); the capping methods and `OwnDeficit` are internal, so the Application can't rebuild the cap or the ledger.
- New test: `CqrsRuleTests.CommandHandlersDoNotHoldQueryHandlers` — no command handler holds a query handler or the query dispatcher (validators exempt). Its first run listed exactly decision 7's two handlers; a temporary `IQueryDispatcher` on `CoverDeficitCommandHandler` turned it red. Shrink-only list: Break and Complete → the recap (handed back to the screen); Complete → readiness (`NenContract.CheckComplete` behind a loader).
- New rule tests on the way: `BlockChangeChecker` (`WouldJoinContract`, probed red with the parameter in place as a no-op), the Update validator passing contracts in effect (probed red with an empty list), `BlockPlacementChecker` holding every role to its own contract, `BackgroundBlock.Create` refusing the Redemption role.
- Exception entries retired: `VerticalSliceRuleTests` → `RedemptionAttributionResult`; `ViewModelRuleTests` → `PlannerPageViewModel` 33 → 31 (the deleted date message).

**Lessons (settled):**
- **Narrowing a member's visibility can pull a later deletion forward** — the compiler names the callers in the order they must go. *No rule: Design Review Rule 11 (list callers before removing) covers it.*
- **An approved signature that can't be written literally is a deviation, not an implementation detail** — say so and record the shape (decision 8 → `CreateWithReceipt`). *No test; a judgment call.*
- **A design chosen in conversation can fail against the code** — option C was picked on reasoning, dropped on reading the code (Design Review Rule 14). *No new rule.*
- **Scripted doc edits on Windows** silently skipped non-ASCII patterns and an `awk -v` path deleted lines without inserting. *`TESTING_GUIDE.md` note.*

**Manual checks (2026-10-07):** all five passed — the database upgraded on startup (it held no redemption blocks, so there was no coverage to compare); the Cover Deficit form has no Split and its End list stops at the debt, one REDEMPTION tile saved and the deficit dropped; a date outside the contract was refused in the form; a plain tile dragged into the contract was refused; a split redemption tile kept both pieces whole with the deficit unchanged. Run with the debug clock (Ctrl+Shift+T) to miss a block.

**Confirmed via:** the full suite (1705 passing).

**Principle demonstrated:** a fact decided once — what a redemption pays — is stored once, when it is decided, by the one thing that decides it, and never recomputed; a query whose answer a command saves is that decision living in the wrong layer.

*The write-up as it stood in `OPEN_GAPS.md` at close:*

**The question:** "how much of which missed blocks' deficit does this redemption block cover, and how does that coverage divide when a block is split?" Today it's asked by Create, Update and Split, and answered in the Application layer.

**Narrowed by Gap 95 decision 16 (2026-10-06):** a redemption block's length is fixed after creation (move and split only, never resized), so Update no longer rescales a claim after a resize — attribution's carry-forward only has moves and splits left to handle. The "at most the remaining deficit" cap at creation stays with attribution and moves to the Domain here.

**Findings (checked in code 2026-09-30):**
1. **The rule lives in a query.** `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQuery` returns entries and a capped slot that Create/Update/Split then save. `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` → Query slices names this exact case: a query whose answer commands save is a business rule in the wrong place; it belongs in the Domain. No gap owned that move until now.
2. **Two rules divide coverage across a split.** `SplitPlannedBlockCommandHandler.SplitEntriesAcrossHalves` asks attribution once for the whole span and divides it in proportion. The Cover Deficit split (`PlannerPageViewModel.ConfirmSplitAsync`, `_isCreatingRedemption` branch) sends two `CreatePlannedBlockCommand`s: the first half claims the deficit, the second gets what's left. They agree today only because a Cover Deficit tile is sized to exactly the deficit.
3. **The Cover Deficit split isn't atomic.** Each command is its own transaction. If the first half saves and the second fails, half a tile is left behind (surfaced as "First half created, but the second failed").

**Direction (for interrogation, not decided):** attribution and the dividing rule become Domain rules that Create, Update and Split all ask. The Cover Deficit split becomes one create with two slots in one transaction: either Create takes more than one slot, or a small sibling command does. The ViewModel's two-command branch goes. `SplitPlannedBlockCommand` stays separate: it edits an existing block and has its own permission rules (Gap 93), while the Cover Deficit case creates new blocks under Create's rules. They share the dividing rule, not the command. The new two-slot create must refuse halves that overlap each other explicitly, as `SplitPlannedBlockValidator` does; today the Cover Deficit path only catches it by accident, when the second create is refused as "time taken".
