**Why after Gap 97:** Gap 97 finding 3 (which contract a redemption attaches to) is part of the same attribution answer and should be settled first.

**Last step — architecture tests (Gap 98):** add the narrow rule this gap makes true: *no query handler's answer is saved by a command* (attribution in the Domain), and delete the `RedemptionAttributionResult` entry from `VerticalSliceRuleTests` if Gap 88 hasn't already. Exact test form decided when the gap is designed.

**More findings (read in code 2026-10-07, not yet tested):**
4. **Three attribution rules, not one.** Create makes new claims (day-gated); Update carries the receipt forward and rescales it; Split passes no existing entries (`SplitPlannedBlockCommandHandler.cs:45`), so it re-claims from scratch, oldest deficit first, day-gated on the first half's date — coverage can move to different missed blocks.
5. **Split can shorten a Redemption block.** Each half goes through `CapEndToRedemptionCoverage` (`:58-59`); with Rule 6 surplus, the pieces sum to less than the original — against Gap 95 decision 16.
6. **Move: the preview checks a different slot than the save.** `GetBlockPlacementVerdictQueryHandler:60` checks placement on attribution's capped slot; `UpdatePlannedBlockCommandHandler` saves `command.Slot` uncapped (Design Review Rule 12). Reachability not shown — removed by decision 3 either way.
7. **`CurrentDayOnly` copied into the ViewModel.** `PlannerPageViewModel.cs:613-623` limits a Cover Deficit tile's dates to the contract / today. A Cover Deficit tile can still become Bonus today (deficit covered meanwhile, or a tile landing in another contract's period): `MembershipForPlacedBlock(claimsDeficit: false)`.
- Verified resolved: Gap 97's suspicion that a moved Committed block could gain redemption entries — Update, the verdict query and Split only attribute for a Redemption-role block.

**Interrogated 2026-10-07 — plan written, awaiting the architect's review (supersedes "Direction" above):**

*Decisions*
1. **The deficit ledger and attribution both move into `NenContract`**, its member blocks passed in, as `DayStatuses` does ("Decide in this order" step 4; a separate service rejected as anemic). The two queries become thin loaders.
2. **A split never recalculates.** It divides the existing receipt across the halves in time order (first half fills first; a claim straddling the cut is split in two); no half is shortened; both halves stay Redemption. Safe because a receipt never over-claims later: its missed blocks are already final, and other blocks' coverage of them only shrinks.
3. **A move keeps the receipt.** Attribution runs only when a redemption block is created. The day badges (`DayStatuses`) still follow wherever the block sits. Rule 15 becomes: "a Redemption block's claims are set once, at creation; moves and splits keep them." The receipt is then a frozen history record for Insights (Gap 74).
4. **Cover Deficit always places one tile** — the split option leaves its dialog; splitting it afterwards is the ordinary Split. Two pieces are still possible via a partial tile plus a second Cover Deficit (and are the only way under Lockdown, where Split is refused). Removes finding 3 and the second dividing rule (finding 2).
5. **Cover Deficit refuses when nothing is owed** where the tile lands, instead of quietly making a Bonus block. A Cover Deficit tile is always Redemption.
6. **Cover Deficit gets its own slice**, `CoverDeficitCommand { ContractId, PresetId, Slot }` (+ handler, result, validator) — the only path that creates a redemption block. `DeclineRedemption` is deleted from `CreatePlannedBlockCommand`, its handler, validator and `GetBlockPlacementVerdictQuery`; the verdict query no longer runs attribution at all.
7. **Guard: command handlers may not hold query handlers** (validators exempt — asking "is this allowed" is their job). Shrink-only exception list, each with a one-line reason: `BreakNenContractCommandHandler` (recap, returned to the screen), `CompleteNenContractCommandHandler` (recap; completion readiness = `CheckComplete` behind a loader). Probed red. Rejected: following a query's value into a save (data flow — a custom Roslyn analyzer could, but loses the value through conversions like `RedemptionEntryDto` → `RedemptionEntry`, exactly attribution's shape); a "query-answer type" the Domain refuses (fields can be copied, so it shuts no door).
8. **Only the Domain writes a receipt** (Guard Hierarchy rank 1 for this rule): `NenContract.PlaceRedemptionBlock` on create, `FocusBlock.SplitInto` on split; `FocusBlock.Create`'s `redemptionEntries` parameter becomes `internal`; `AssignRedemptionEntries` is deleted. The Domain gets `InternalsVisibleTo("NenFocus.Tests")` so ~8 tests that need a block already holding a receipt (ledger, day badges, recap, day view, session snapshot, repository round-trip) keep building one directly. Tests of the deleted behaviour (`AssignRedemptionEntries`, "a Bonus/Committed block can't take entries", Update's three redemption-rescaling tests) are replaced by tests of `PlaceRedemptionBlock`, `SplitInto` and "a move keeps the receipt".
9. **The ViewModel's copy of the date rule is deleted** (finding 7): the user picks any date; the Domain's refusal explains a wrong one.
10. **A `RedemptionReceipt` value object replaces the block's bare `IReadOnlyList<RedemptionEntry>`.** It owns the total covered (written three times today: `FocusBlock.cs:291`, `ContractDayLedger.cs:39`, the ledger handler), the dividing rule (`SplitAt`, decision 2) and "how much for this missed block" (ledger, recap, snapshot), and refuses the same missed block listed twice. Why not on `FocusBlock`: the rules read only the entries, and a receipt exists before its block (`PlaceRedemptionBlock`, and a split's two halves). Why not a domain service: one thing's data — anemic. Deficit vs receipt: the deficit is the missed block's debt (worked out, changes as payments land or fail); the receipt is the redemption block's promise to pay (stored, fixed). Outcome, credit, Ritual, lateness and own deficit stay entity methods on `FocusBlock` — one block's own stamps; Gap 95 already deleted the outcome service and rejected a `Deficit` wrapper.
11. **Two more doors close (follows from 8):** `FocusBlock.CapEndToRedemptionCoverage` / `WasRedemptionCoverageMeaningfullyCapped` (how Split shortened halves, finding 5) and `FocusBlock.OwnDeficit` (how the Application rebuilt the ledger) become `internal` once only the Domain uses them. Stay public because they only read: `CheckRedemptionEligibility` (the Cover Deficit suggestion), the receipt getter (Planner, snapshot), `RedemptionEntry`'s constructor (EF loads rows). With `MembershipForPlacedBlock` handing out only Bonus, a Redemption role can come only from `PlaceRedemptionBlock`; `TestContracts.Redemption` moves to the internal `ContractMembership` constructor.
12. **A Cover Deficit tile may only be placed inside the contract whose deficit it covers** — the contract the Cover Deficit button belongs to, carried as `CoverDeficitCommand.ContractId`. Anywhere else is refused as "outside the contract", including a slot inside a *different* contract's period (which today would quietly pay that contract's debt). Consistent with every member block: a Committed or Redemption block is only ever placed inside its own contract (`BlockPlacementChecker`'s governing rule). **Changes a Gap 97 line on purpose:** Create's "which contract comes from where the slot sits, never from the client" — for Cover Deficit the client names *which debt it pays*, and the Domain still refuses any slot outside that contract, so the client never decides where a block may go.
13. **Check/Do pair** (as `CheckBreak`/`Break`, `CheckComplete`/`Complete`): the validator asks `CheckRedemptionPlacement` (changes nothing), the handler calls `PlaceRedemptionBlock`, which runs the same check itself and refuses to build a bad block. The handler's second run is not a duplicate check — it works out the receipt and capped slot it must build, since a validator can't hand values to the handler (putting the receipt in the command would let the client decide what is redeemed — what Gap 97 removed; skipping the validator would make this the one block command that refuses in its handler). The verdict holds values only — no entity.
14. **Four labels, worded once:** `Placed`; `OutsideContract` ("This time is outside the contract."); `WrongDay` (`CurrentDayOnly`: only today); `NothingOwed` (nothing of this preset owed). Only wording differs between the refusals (Design Review Rule 5), kept because each tells the user something different to do, they're checked in a fixed order so one situation never gets two, and `PlacementOutcome` set the precedent.
15. **`WasCapped`** = "shortened by enough to tell the user" (at least `MinimumMeaningfulDeficitMinutes`): Cover Deficit suggestions are rounded up to whole minutes on purpose (Gap 33), so almost every save trims seconds; the threshold keeps the notice from firing every time. In the Domain answer because comparing lengths against a threshold in the ViewModel is the Forbidden Zone.
16. **`SplitInto` is on `FocusBlock` only.** A Background block can never be split (`PlannedBlock.CheckOwnSplit` → `SplitNotOffered`, pinned by `SplitPlannedBlockValidatorTests.Validate_BackgroundBlock_IsInvalid`), so `SplitPlannedBlockCommandHandler`'s Background branch (`:69-77`) is dead code and is deleted.
17. **A block with a contract id is judged against its own contract — every role.** `BlockPlacementChecker.Check`: `membership is { IsBonus: false }` → `membership is not null`. Today a Bonus block is judged against whichever contract its slot sits in and kept in its own only by `NenContract.CheckBlockMove`'s period check — one rule written two ways, which agree only because contracts never share a day (Gap 103 will make them). `CheckBlockMove`'s own period check stays (the move-permission answer, worded differently; Gap 93's tests) — the overlap is noted, not removed here.
18. **Dragging never changes membership** (option A, chosen over "a drag in joins as Bonus" and over a join-confirmation pop-up). A plain block moved to a slot a contract governs is refused; `PlaceUnder` leaves the Update handler. Membership then changes only through contract actions: created inside (Bonus, or Redemption via Cover Deficit), Seal / re-draft adoption (Committed, Gap 68), Break / Delete (Bonus released). Makes the dictionary's "set once" true. Home: `BlockChangeChecker.CheckReschedule` (the move-permission answer — the placement checker can't tell a create from a move, both have no membership); exact parameter settled with the signatures. **Bridge until Gap 68:** a plain block already inside a contract's range (sealed around it, since drafting doesn't adopt yet) can be moved out or deleted, not moved within. **Possible later, not raised as a gap:** a deliberate "Join contract" action on a tile — one more contract action, not a drag side effect; A doesn't block it.
19. **A Cover Deficit tile can be shortened, never lengthened past the debt** (decided 2026-10-07, during slice 2). The form's End choices stop at what's owed, so the user can pay part now and the rest with a second Cover Deficit later (decision 4). Locking the end entirely was rejected: it would remove the partial tile, the only way to pay in two pieces under Lockdown. `CheckRedemptionPlacement` still caps the slot, as a safety net for the debt shrinking between opening the form and saving; so the "shortened to match" notice (`WasCapped`) is now rare. Because no tile is longer than the debt, "inside the contract" is checked on the requested slot before capping (the approved order, as built in slice 2) — checking the capped slot instead was considered and not needed.
- Out of scope, recorded as Gap 92 V10: minutes as fractional `double`s throughout the ledger.

*Signatures (Step 2 — approved by the architect 2026-10-07)*
- `NenContract.Deficits(blocks, asOf) -> IReadOnlyList<OutstandingDeficit>` — new value object, used by the deficits query, the banner (`GetNenContractsForDateRange`), the Cover Deficit suggestion and attribution; `FocusBlockDeficitDto` built from it in one place. `ExcludeCoverageFromBlockId` goes (only the move rescaling used it). The `1e-6` floating-point guard moves with the ledger.
  `OutstandingDeficit(MissedBlockId, PresetId, Scheduled, Outcome, OwedMinutes, RemainingMinutes)`; `FocusBlockDeficitDto.From(OutstandingDeficit)` (Planned = scheduled length, Completed = planned − owed). Same parameter shape as `DayStatuses`; `EnsureMine` refuses another contract's blocks.
- `NenContract.CheckRedemptionPlacement(presetId, slot, blocks, now, timeZone) -> RedemptionVerdict` — checks in order: inside this contract (`AllowsBlockPlacement`) → day rule (`CheckRedemptionEligibility`) → anything owed for the preset; on allowed, the receipt (oldest deficit first) and the slot capped to it.
- `NenContract.PlaceRedemptionBlock(blockId, presetId, slot, blocks, now, timeZone) -> FocusBlock` — runs the check; throws if refused; the block has a Redemption membership of this contract and the receipt. `blockId` passed in, as `ContractBlockBatchPreparer` does.
- `RedemptionVerdict` (value object, values only): `Outcome` (`RedemptionPlacementOutcome`), `Receipt` (`None` unless placed), `Slot?` (final capped slot when placed), `WasCapped`. **Added 2026-10-07 (slice 3):** `Membership?` (this contract's Redemption membership when placed, else null) — Rule 16: the validator needs it (it passes it to `BlockPlacementChecker.Check`, and Application can't build a Redemption membership); `PlaceRedemptionBlock` builds the block with it.
- `RedemptionPlacementOutcome` (enum): `Placed`, `OutsideContract`, `WrongDay`, `NothingOwed`; worded once in `RedemptionVerdictDto.From` (precedent `BlockPlacementVerdictDto.From`).
- `RedemptionReceipt` (value object): `None`, `Entries`, `TotalMinutes`, `MinutesFor(missedBlockId)`, `SplitAt(TimeSpan firstLength) -> (First, Second)` (today's `SplitEntriesAcrossHalves`); public constructor (EF loads rows) refuses a missed block listed twice. `FocusBlock.RedemptionEntries` → `FocusBlock.Receipt`. Stored as today's JSON column. The day badges' "once per missed block per day" grouping spans several blocks, so it stays in `ContractDayLedger`.
- `FocusBlock.SplitInto(firstId, first, secondId, second) -> (FocusBlock, FocusBlock)` — throws unless the pieces sum to the original's length (Gap 95 decision 16); both keep the membership; receipt divided by `SplitAt(first.Length)`.
- `CoverDeficitCommand { Guid ContractId; Guid PresetId; required TimeSlot Slot }`; `CoverDeficitResult.Ok(blockId, wasCapped)` / `Fail(message)`. Validator: load the contract → `CheckRedemptionPlacement` → refused: its wording; allowed: `BlockPlacementChecker.Check` on `verdict.Slot` with the Redemption membership (Rule 12). Handler: `PlaceRedemptionBlock`, save.
- `BlockPlacementChecker.Check`: `membership is { IsBonus: false }` → `membership is not null` (decision 17).
- `IBlockChangeChecker.CheckReschedule(block, newSlot, presetChanges, contract, contractBlocks, IEnumerable<NenContract> contractsInEffect, asOf, timeZone)` — the new list (decision 18): a plain block is refused if any contract `Governs(newSlot)`; a member block ignores it. A list, not one pre-chosen contract, so no caller can pass the wrong one (Gap 97's precedent for `BlockPlacementChecker.Check`). Member count unchanged. New `ChangeOutcome.WouldJoinContract` ("This block isn't part of the contract — create a new block inside it instead."), worded once in `BlockChangeVerdictDto.From`. Only caller: `UpdatePlannedBlockValidator` (loads `GetAllInEffectAsync`).
- Removed or narrowed: `FocusBlock.Create`'s entries parameter → `internal RedemptionReceipt? receipt`; `AssignRedemptionEntries` deleted; `PlannedBlock.PlaceUnder` deleted (Update was its only production caller); `SplitPlannedBlockCommandHandler`'s dead Background branch deleted; `CapEndToRedemptionCoverage`, `WasRedemptionCoverageMeaningfullyCapped`, `OwnDeficit` → `internal`; `MembershipForPlacedBlock(claimsDeficit)` → `MembershipForPlacedBlock()` (Bonus only); `DeclineRedemption` (Create command + verdict query), `CreatePlannedBlockResult.RedemptionCapped`, `GetNenContractDeficitsQuery.ExcludeCoverageFromBlockId`, `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQuery` + handler + `RedemptionAttributionResult` deleted; Domain `.csproj` gets `<InternalsVisibleTo Include="NenFocus.Tests" />`. No new Application interface.
- Test: `CqrsRuleTests.CommandHandlersDoNotHoldQueryHandlers` + `KnownExceptions` entries (Break: recap; Complete: recap, readiness).
- Hardest cases run through (Design Review Rule 16): Cover Deficit tile in another contract's period → `OutsideContract`; tile longer than the debt → capped, placement checked on the capped slot; split with surplus (2h covering 1h30, cut at 1h) → 1h | 30m covered, nothing shortened; plain block moved outside any contract → allowed; plain block left inside a range from before Seal → moving within refused, moving out or deleting allowed (bridge to Gap 68); Bonus dragged into another contract → refused by `CheckBlockMove` and by placement against its own contract; Bonus moved within its own contract → as today; new plain block created inside a contract → Create unchanged, joins as Bonus.

*Slices (each red first)*
1. `InternalsVisibleTo`; `RedemptionReceipt` replaces the entry list on `FocusBlock` (EF mapping updated, repository round-trip test green); the three sums move onto it.
1b. `OutstandingDeficit` + `NenContract.Deficits`, ledger tests ported to the Domain; `GetNenContractDeficitsQueryHandler` becomes a loader; `FocusBlock.OwnDeficit` made `internal`.
2. `RedemptionVerdict` + `NenContract.CheckRedemptionPlacement` / `PlaceRedemptionBlock`; new-claim tests ported from `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQueryHandlerTests`, plus outside-the-contract (incl. another contract's period), wrong day and nothing owed.
3. `CoverDeficit` slice (the validator checks placement on the same capped slot the handler saves — Rule 12); ViewModel save points at it; split and the date-limit copy leave the Cover Deficit form; its End choices stop at the debt (decision 19); `DeclineRedemption` deleted; Create and the verdict query simplified.
4. Move keeps the receipt: attribution leaves Update and the verdict query; `AssignRedemptionEntries` deleted.
4b. Membership by contract only (decisions 17, 18): the checker's Bonus exception goes; a plain block moved into a contract is refused (new `ChangeOutcome`, worded once in `BlockChangeVerdictDto.From`); `PlannedBlock.PlaceUnder` is deleted with its `PlannedBlockTests` (Update was its only production caller).
5. `FocusBlock.SplitInto`, used by Split with no capping; the handler's dead Background branch deleted; `redemptionEntries` on `FocusBlock.Create`, `CapEndToRedemptionCoverage` and `WasRedemptionCoverageMeaningfullyCapped` made `internal`; `MembershipForPlacedBlock` loses `claimsDeficit`. Also delete `GetNenContractDeficitsQuery.ExcludeCoverageFromBlockId` and its bridge filter in the loader — moved here from slice 4 because Split still passes the original's id to attribution until this slice. Also make `PlannedBlock.Join` `internal` (added 2026-10-07, after slice 4b): with `PlaceUnder` deleted it has no production caller, and `RedemptionVerdict.Membership` is public, so a public `Join` would let the Application make any plain block a Redemption block with no receipt — the door decision 11 closes. Its only callers are `PlannedBlockTests` and `ContractMembershipPersistenceTests` (both see internals).
5b. **Background guard only — the receipt stays on `FocusBlock` (decided 2026-10-07 after slice 5).** `BackgroundBlock.Create` throws if handed a Redemption membership: only Focus time redeems. Unreachable today (only `PlaceRedemptionBlock` makes the role, and it builds Focus blocks), but silently wrong if reached — the block would count toward the floor and be bound as a Redemption block. `Join` (internal, no production caller) is left alone.
   *Considered and dropped the same day:* moving the receipt into the Redemption membership (option C), over A (as built), a `RedemptionBlock` subtype (B — the role stored twice, class and membership) and a claims table (D — one fact in two aggregates). C was first chosen for cohesion ("only a Redemption member holds a receipt" in the membership's constructor). Dropped on re-checking the code after slice 5: the membership and the receipt are both fields of the block — a rule reading two fields of one entity belongs on that entity ("Decide in this order", step 3) — and they are only ever set together, once (`CreateWithReceipt` checks the role; a Redemption block's membership never changes: `Join` is internal, `Leave` is Bonus-only, `SplitInto` copies both). C's multi-contract benefit was grouping, not verification: in A and C alike, `PlaceRedemptionBlock` is what guarantees the entries are the block's own contract's. A's one remaining cost — non-redemption blocks hold `RedemptionReceipt.None` — is a null object, harmless. Decisions 8 and 10 stand; `RedemptionVerdict` keeps `Receipt`. Reopen only with new evidence (e.g. debts that are not a contract's, or claims that must be queried — which would point to D, not C).
6. Delete `ComputeRedemptionAttributionQuery` + `RedemptionAttributionResult`, the `VerticalSliceRuleTests` entry, and `ShellViewModel`'s stale `using`. **Done inside slice 5 (2026-10-07):** making the capping methods `internal` left the attribution handler unable to compile, and Split was its last caller; `RedemptionEntryDto` went with it (no other user). Slice 5 also put the receipt behind an internal `FocusBlock.CreateWithReceipt` (public `Create` takes none) — C# can't make one parameter `internal`, and two `Create` overloads with optional parameters would be ambiguous.
7. Test from decision 7, probed red.
8. Docs: `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY_NEN_CONTRACT.md` (Rules 6, 15, 16, Cover Deficit paragraph, mapping rows); `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md` (Redemption Block, RedemptionEntry rows, a RedemptionReceipt row); `TESTING_GUIDE.md`; a pointer under Gap 74 — Insights shows a redemption's *promised* coverage (the receipt) beside what it *delivered* (its outcome); `DOMAIN_DICTIONARY.md` Contract Membership / Bonus Block rows (membership changes only through contract actions); a dated "Changed 2026-10-07 by Gap 106" note inside Gap 97's `DESIGN_GAP_HISTORY.md` entry (Cover Deficit names its contract; every member judged against its own contract; a drag never joins); the `DESIGN_GAP_HISTORY.md` entry; the governance line removed.


## Gap 92 — Value Object and Service Review *(closed, 2026-10-07)*

**What was wrong:** the value objects and services had never been checked as a whole against `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` → Where a Rule Lives. The 2026-09-29 review found the shape problems (dead types, misplaced result types, oversized interfaces). Reading each open item's code on 2026-10-07 found bigger ones behind them: contract lifecycle rules copied into four validators with no Domain home; preset floor and Work Hours rules in validators that `ContractPreset` itself didn't hold; fractional minutes from deficits to a stored end time (Gap 33's bug class, held off by a `1e-6` guard); nothing outside the UI keeping "one session at a time"; the session monitor re-deriving the break rules `Session` already owned; and a 15-member `ISessionStateService` whose restart copy of the running session had drifted (Gap 89 F1).

**Built (2026-10-07, slices 1–9; slices 1–8 committed as `bae5c96`):**
1. V3 — `ChangeWindow` deleted (type, tests, `Seal`'s parameter, property, EF `Ignore`); the idea parked as Gap 109.
2. S4 — `UserTimeZone.HoursPerDay(slots)`; `PlannedFocusHoursCalculator`, its interface and DI line deleted, with the day ledger's hand-written copy.
3. R3, R1, R4 — `ChangeVerdict.RequiredHours`, DTO `TargetHours`, `TotalCommittedHours`; `ContractDayStatus` works out `IsRest`/`IsMet`; the two ledgers became `NenContract` partial files (new line in Where a Rule Lives).
4. D (+R2) — `CheckEdit`/`CheckDelete`/`CheckComplete`/`CheckBreak` → `ContractActionVerdict`; `Break` and `CompleteEarly` enforce their own verdict; `Complete(at)` is the sweep's.
5. V9 (A+) — fixed floor whole hours 1–24 in `ContractRules`; `DailyFloor.FitsIn`; `ContractPreset.CheckSettings` → typed verdict; commands carry `WorkHours?` and `int?`.
6. V10 — `WholeMinutes`; every planned block a whole number of minutes; credit's `RoundDown` the only rounding; epsilon deleted; migration `RoundRedemptionEntriesToWholeMinutes`.
7. A8 C+ / A8b — `GetActiveAsync` and `GetByOriginatingBlockIdAsync` the only session doors; `PolicyEngine`/`BlockPlacementChecker` take `Session? running`; StartSession refuses a second session; migration `SessionStorageBackstops` (two filtered unique indexes).
8. A4 D′ — `SessionTimeline` → `TimelineReading`; `Session` hands off to it; `SessionDto` carries it; the monitor 10 → 3 members.
9. A3 A′ — `ISessionStateService` split into `IActiveSessionState` (refreshes from `GetActiveSessionQuery`, the one `SessionDto` builder — Gap 89 F1, F3), `IRitualPrompt` (only "which block awaits its Ritual") and the interim `IRecapRelay` (→ Gap 107). D′: the Shell's Ritual follows the prompt; the heartbeat's sweep is the one home for "missed"; `TryMarkBlockMissed`, `HandleLiveRitualRelease`, `HandleRitualHandoff` and `RitualRequirementChange` deleted. Startup refreshes, then dispatches the sweep.
10. Docs.

**Where the code differed from the signed-off plan** (each recorded in its slice's "Done" note below): slice 5 narrowed to option B — the UI converts at its edges, and the wizard → drafting chain stays loose for Gaps 68/82. Slice 6's migration rounds receipt entries only; there was no dev database to check block lengths against, and an odd-length block now fails loudly on load. Slice 6's Gap 33 test was red only by not compiling. Slice 9: `HandlePendingRitual` was a third `TryMarkBlockMissed` caller the decision didn't name — it now asks the prompt (same D′ rule). `RitualRequirementChange` was deleted outright, not trimmed: only an id was left. `MainPageViewModel.IsRitualBannerVisible` was deleted, since no view binds it. Two small behaviour changes follow from D′: the prompt lags a session start by up to one tick, and a block falling due during a spontaneous Ritual is queued instead of dropped.

**Guard (step 5), per item** — the table below has each item's answer; in short:
- **Compiler (rank 1):** V3, S4, R1, R2, R3 (names), V10 (`WholeMinutes` can't hold a fraction; `PlannedBlock` refuses a non-whole length), A4 (one `SessionTimeline` answer), A3 (nothing pushed into `IActiveSessionState`; one `SessionDto` builder).
- **New tests:** `ContractLifecycleRuleTests.NoApplicationCodeDecidesFromAContractsStatus` (probed red on 9 files); Domain tests that `Break`/`CompleteEarly` throw when their verdict refuses; `ContractPreset` refuses an impossible preset and `ContractRules` a fractional/out-of-range floor (red at runtime first); Gap 33's regression case; StartSession refuses a second session and both indexes reject duplicates (all red on the old code); `SessionTimeline` boundary tests and the `LiftsBlockingAt` parity test; `RitualPromptTests` (change-only rule probed red) and headless `ShellRitualPromptTests` (D′ and the restart path; probed red). Added 2026-10-08 to replace manual checks, each probed red: `ShellHeartbeatIntegrationTests` (the real heartbeat tick on a real database; swapping the prompt before the sweep fails it), `MainPageBreakFlipTests` (the real monitor's timer), `PlannerContractButtonsViewTests`, `ContractPresetFormIntegrationTests` (the real validators). `ScheduledEnforcementService.TickAsync` extracted (internal) for them; Infrastructure gained `InternalsVisibleTo` for tests.
- **Exception entries retired:** `InterfaceSizeRuleTests` — `ISessionStateService has 15` (→ `IRecapRelay has 6`, pointing at Gap 107), `IActiveSessionMonitor has 10`, `ISessionRepository` 9 → 6; `DtoBuilderRuleTests` — `SessionDto built in 3 files`; `DispatcherRuleTests` — `SessionStateService.cs`; `SideEffectRuleTests.CommandHandlersDoNotUpdateTheSessionNoticeboard` (rule 19, a bridge) deleted with its last exception; `CqrsRuleTests` — the Complete handler's readiness query; `ValidatorWordingRuleTests` counts dropped for the contract and preset validators.
- **No test:** `HasStarted`/`HasEnded` stay public (a visibility judgment); R4 and the ledger's copied sum (one-offs).

**Lessons (settled):**
- **The first pass finds shapes; reading each item's code finds what they hide.** The review's table was right about every type it named, but the monitor's duplicate break rules, the unguarded "one session at a time" and an already-finished period target only showed up once each item's callers were read. *No new rule: Design Review Rule 14 (a standards check reads the code).*
- **Each "is there a more DDD option?" moved a rule from a validator or a reviewer's memory into a type** — `WholeMinutes`, `SessionTimeline`, the lifecycle verdicts, `ContractPreset.CheckSettings`. *No new rule: the Guard Hierarchy already prefers the compiler.*
- **Listing callers before deleting found one the decision missed** (`HandlePendingRitual`). *No new rule: Design Review Rule 11 is what caught it.*
- **A push hides drift that a read can't:** three `SessionDto` copies disagreed because each caller pushed its own. Reading the one query made the drift impossible and deleted a bridge test. *Guarded: one builder, `DtoBuilderRuleTests`.*
- **"Red" by not compiling isn't a probe.** Slice 6's Gap 33 test never ran red against the old code; slice 9 probed its new tests after they compiled. *No test; a habit (`TESTING_GUIDE.md` → probe red).*
- **A whole-file line-ending pass can make git see a file as binary.** *`TESTING_GUIDE.md` note.*

**Manual checks:** the real database upgraded on startup without error (2026-10-07, both migrations applied). The other planned checks were automated instead (2026-10-08, above); a restart mid-session was dropped as a manual check — the debugger start already ran `Program.Main`'s two startup lines, and what they do is tested.

**Confirmed via:** the full suite (1754 passing, 0 warnings).

**Principle demonstrated:** a rule has one home and everything else asks it. A copy — in a validator, a monitor, a pushed DTO, or a second "missed" path in the Shell — is a second answer waiting to disagree.

*The write-up as it stood in `OPEN_GAPS.md` at close:*

**Scope decided 2026-10-07 (the architect chose to finish this gap fully before Gaps 89 and 107):** every open item in the table below — V3, V9, V10, S4, A3, A4, A8 — plus the "From Gap 93" items (the contract lifecycle rules repeated in four validators; Seal's and Edit's shared floor rule; the period-target sum in the Seal and Edit handlers; whether `PlannedBlock.HasStarted`/`HasEnded` stay public), plus a quick re-check, by this gap's Method, of the value objects and services added since the 2026-09-29 review (e.g. `ContractRules`, `ContractMembership`, `PlacementVerdict`, `ChangeVerdict`, `BlockChangeAllowances`, `RedemptionReceipt`, `OutstandingDeficit`, `RedemptionVerdict`, `BlockPlacementChecker`, `BlockChangeChecker`, `ContractDayLedger`, `ContractDeficitLedger`). **Not in scope:** A6 and A7 stay with Gap 89 (preset-lock rule in SQL; the clock abstractions, with the one-heartbeat work). Next step: interrogation, one item at a time; A3 builds on Gap 89 area 4's agreed `RefreshAsync` direction.

**From Gap 93 (2026-10-02):** the contract's lifecycle rules are repeated in its validators — only a Scheduled contract edited or deleted (`EditNenContractValidator:39`, `DeleteNenContractValidator:28`), only an Active one completed (`CompleteNenContractValidator:27`), only one in effect broken (`BreakNenContractValidator:27`). Check whether each validator asks the entity or holds its own copy. Also: Seal's and Edit's validators both hold "floor whole hours, 1–24, not above the Work Hours window". **Also from Gap 93 (closed 2026-10-02):** "no daily floors → the whole-period target is the sum of what was drafted" is still two lines in both the Seal and Edit handlers; it could move into `NenContract.Seal`, which already has the planned hours — not done there because it would give every test-sealed Period contract a zero target. And `PlannedBlock.HasStarted` has no caller outside the Domain any more; decide with `HasEnded` whether it stays public.

**Decided 2026-10-07 — `HasStarted`/`HasEnded` stay public, nothing to change.** `HasEnded` has two callers outside the Domain (`StartSessionCommandHandler`, `GetPlannedBlocksForDayQueryHandler`); `HasStarted` none, but both are answers, not data: the raw `Start`/`End` are already `internal` inside `TimeSlot` (Gap 88), and the block's methods are one-line hand-offs that give the business question a name on the entity. Hiding an answer guards nothing (`block.Scheduled.HasStarted` stays public) and would only push a future caller to reach past the block. Guard: none — a visibility judgment, nothing that can drift.

**Decided 2026-10-07 — contract lifecycle rules (option D: every action enforces its own verdict).** Checked: "only Scheduled" for Edit is written in `EditNenContractValidator:39` and `EditNenContractCommandHandler:52`, for Delete only in `DeleteNenContractValidator:28` — no Domain home; "only Active" for Complete in `CompleteNenContractValidator:27`, `NenContract.Complete`'s throw and `PlannerPageViewModel:500`; Break's validator asks `IsInEffect`, but `CheckBreak` and `CheckComplete` leave status out and `Break` never calls `CheckBreak`. Decided: one verdict per action on the entity — `CheckComplete` refuses a non-Active contract first, `CheckBreak` one not in effect, new `CheckEdit()`/`CheckDelete()` anything not Scheduled; `ContractEndOutcome`/`ContractEndVerdict` renamed `ContractActionOutcome`/`ContractActionVerdict` (Gap 105's Edit reasons land in `CheckEdit`). Actions enforce their own verdict: `Break(brokenAt, timeZone, sessionRunning)` calls `CheckBreak` and throws; completing splits into `Complete(at)` (the end arrived — the sweep; guard: Active) and `CompleteEarly(at, members, sessions, sessionRunning, timeZone)` (the user's button; calls `CheckComplete`). Validators, the Edit handler and the Planner's Complete button only ask; status copies deleted; wording in the verdict DTO's one home. Out of scope: Edit as a real entity method instead of delete + re-seal. Guard: `ValidatorWordingRuleTests` counts drop; new architecture test — no Application code compares a contract's `Status` (probe red on today's validators); Domain tests that `Break`/`CompleteEarly` throw when their own verdict refuses.

**Checked 2026-10-07 — the period-target sum is already done.** Gap 95 (decision 8) moved it: `NenContract.Seal` sets `CommittedFocusHours = plannedFocusHoursByDay.Values.Sum()` and `HasPeriodTarget` is read off the floor rule; the Seal and Edit handlers keep only a comment. Nothing to change.

**Why it's a gap:** Gap 88's interrogation showed the existing catalogue has never been checked as a whole against `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` → Where a Rule Lives: e.g. `ContractBlockBatchPreparation` (a result type) sits in `Domain/Services/`, the Interface-First Rule's text disagrees with where every Domain service interface lives, and whether each Domain service name follows the new naming row was never checked.

**Method:** for every value object — does it carry a rule of its own (constructor invariant or behaviour), or is it a wrapper used once (the warning sign)? Does it hold values that really travel together? For every service (Domain `Services/`, Application `Services/`, Infrastructure `…Service`) — rule or coordination, and is it in the matching layer? Does it answer a question about a single entity (belongs on the entity)? Does it pass the Deep Module test and the ≤ 5-member checklist? Is it named per the Naming Conventions table? Output: a findings table (type, verdict, suggested change); nothing changed without sign-off.

**Plan (written 2026-10-07, awaiting sign-off).** Ten slices, in order; each lands verified (red test first, `dotnet build` with no new warnings, full `dotnet test` green) before the next starts. Order follows the dependencies: signature changes to `NenContract.Seal` and the ledgers come before the work that edits them; `SessionTimeline` before the `SessionDto` builder that carries it.

1. **V3 — delete `ChangeWindow`.** Type, its tests, `Seal`'s parameter, the property, the EF `Ignore`; ~50 test call sites lose `changeWindow: null`. Dictionary rows → "not built, Gap 109". **Done 2026-10-07:** 0 warnings; 1697 tests green (1705 − the 8 `ChangeWindowTests`).
2. **S4 — `UserTimeZone.HoursPerDay(slots)`.** Red: the 9 rule tests moved onto `UserTimeZone`. Then repoint `BlockChangeChecker`, `ContractBlockBatchPreparer`, `ContractDayLedger`, the query handler; delete the calculator, interface, DI line, the wiring-only tests and the Seal/Edit stubs. **Done 2026-10-07:** `ContractDayLedger`'s copy gone too; two Seal tests and one Edit test whose stubs didn't match their blocks now use real blocks; 0 warnings, 1694 green.
3. **R3, R1, R4 — small Domain tidy-ups.** Renames (`RequiredHours`, DTO `TargetHours`, check `TotalFloorHours`); `ContractDayStatus` computes `IsRest`/`IsMet`; the two ledgers become `NenContract` partial files; the new line in `MODULE_STANDARDS.md`. **Done 2026-10-07:** `NenContractRangeViewDto.TotalFloorHours` had the mix-up too → `TotalCommittedHours`; two test helpers had re-typed `IsMet`'s rule and now don't need to; 0 warnings, 1694 green.
4. **D (+R2) — contract lifecycle verdicts.** Red: Domain tests — `CheckEdit`/`CheckDelete` refuse non-Scheduled, `CheckComplete`/`CheckBreak` refuse wrong status, `Break`/`CompleteEarly` throw when their own verdict refuses; the architecture test "no Application code compares a contract's `Status`" probed red on today's validators. Then rename to `ContractActionVerdict`/`Outcome` with factories, split `Complete`/`CompleteEarly`, validators/Edit handler/Planner button ask, wording in the verdict DTO; `ValidatorWordingRuleTests` counts drop. **Done 2026-10-07:** the architecture test probed red on 9 files; 4 stay listed — two "active first" display orderings and the two "Completed or Broken" reads that Gap 103's "has finished" question owns. The readiness query's own status copy went too, and the Complete handler no longer holds a query (`CqrsRuleTests` exception deleted). Retired the interim test that pinned "Break succeeds with cancellation off"; five test setups that finish a contract by breaking it now seal it with cancellation on. Missed in slice 1, fixed here: `ChangeWindow` still named in three dictionary rows. 0 warnings, 1706 green.
5. **A+ — V9 floor and Work Hours rules.** Red: `ContractPreset` refuses an impossible preset; `ContractRules` refuses a fractional or out-of-range fixed floor; `DailyFloor.FitsIn` tests. Then `ContractDay`/`ContractCalendar`/`ContractPreset` ask `FitsIn`; `ContractPreset.CheckSettings` → typed verdict; commands carry `WorkHours?` (+ `WorkHours.TryCreate`) and `int?` floor; the four validators ask once; UI builds the new shapes. **Narrowed 2026-10-07 (option B):** the UI converts at its edges — the preset form builds `WorkHours.TryCreate` and parses a whole-hour floor; the Planner's Seal/Edit dispatch converts the draft values. The wizard → `NenContractDraftRules` → drafting chain stays loose, because Gaps 68 and 82 rebuild it (notes added there). **Done 2026-10-07:** red at runtime first (today's preset accepted a 4h floor in 9–12, rules accepted 0h and 1.5h). The conversion lives in one named bridge on `NenContractDraftRules` (`WorkHours`, `WholeFixedDailyFloorHours`). Seal/Edit handlers ask `CheckSettings` again, so a skipped validator gets the sentence, not `ContractRules`' exception. `DailyFloor.None` (a 0h floor, test-only) deleted. Tests: those of states commands can no longer express (start without end, end before start, fractional floor) deleted — the compiler guards them; about 20 setups used an impossible 0h fixed floor to mean "asks nothing" and now use no fixed floor, two that are about a fixed floor now use 1h with real blocks. Stored rules load through EF's private constructor, so no old row can fail to load. 0 warnings, 1715 green.
6. **V10 — `WholeMinutes`.** Red: Gap 33's regression case (ms-precise early end, covered, ledger exactly zero) with the epsilon removed; `WholeMinutes` rounding/`TryExact` tests; `PlannedBlock` refuses a 30 min 30 s slot. Then the type, `PlannedBlock`'s length rule, redemption/deficit/ledger/DTO types, `Covering`, exact `CapEndToRedemptionCoverage`, delete the epsilon. Migration: round stored fractional entries (and any odd block lengths) to whole minutes; persistence round trip. Check the dev DB for non-whole block lengths first (read-only). **Done 2026-10-07:** the dev DB check found no database at all (`%APPDATA%\NenFocus` holds only logs), so the migration (`RoundRedemptionEntriesToWholeMinutes`, pure SQL) rounds receipt entries up and skips block lengths — rounding stored end times in SQL is fiddly, and an odd-length block would now fail loudly on load. Also gone with the fractions: `WasRedemptionCoverageMeaningfullyCapped` and `MinimumMeaningfulDeficitMinutes` (they ignored the sub-minute margin of rounded-up suggestions; suggestions are now exact, so any cap is whole minutes and worth the notice — Gap 106 decision 15's intent kept as a test), and the deficit DTO's unread `PlannedMinutes`/`CompletedMinutes`. Credit in whole minutes named once (`FocusBlock.CreditAt`); `Session.GetElapsedTime` rounds through `WholeMinutes.RoundDown` too. The UI's two deficit bindings were runtime-only and would have broken silently — fixed, and `MinutesToStringConverter` takes `WholeMinutes`. Tests of states that can no longer be built (fractional coverage, the tolerance test, fractional `Covering`) deleted; three boundary tests that used a 59 min 59 s block now use exactly an hour. Not done: probing the Gap 33 test red against the old code without the epsilon — it was red only by not compiling. Gap 68 note added (its "split at the Seal moment" must cut on a whole minute). 0 warnings, 1727 green.
7. **A8 C+ and A8b — sessions.** Red: StartSession refuses a second session; the two partial unique indexes (one Active session; one session per originating block) reject duplicates. Then `GetActiveAsync`/`GetByOriginatingBlockIdAsync` as the doors (other three deleted); `PolicyEngine`/`BlockPlacementChecker` take `Session? running` — every caller's hardest case run through first (Rule 16); StartSession passes it to `CheckFree`; one migration for both indexes; interface counts drop. **Done 2026-10-07:** all three red tests failed on the old code (StartSession accepted a second session; storage took two Active rows and two sessions for one block). Rule 16 check: no caller passed more than one session, no test relied on two. StartSession words the case "A session is already running" when the slot in the way is the running session's. Migration `SessionStorageBackstops` (`IX_Sessions_OneActive`, `IX_Sessions_OnePerOriginatingBlock`, both filtered). Five history tests had stored several sessions all still running — now completed (`PastSession`); the tests of the three deleted methods dropped or turned into tests of the remaining door; `TestSessions` helper added. `ISessionRepository` 9 → 6 in `InterfaceSizeRuleTests`. 0 warnings, 1728 green.
8. **A4 D′ — `SessionTimeline`.** Red: `ReadingAt` tests at break boundaries; parity test with `Session.LiftsBlockingAt`. Then `Session` holds it and hands off; `SessionDto` carries it; the monitor pushes `TimelineReading` (3 members); `MainPageViewModel` copies one reading; the duplicate line goes. **Done 2026-10-07:** `SessionTimeline`, `TimelineReading`, `BreakMark` (one file each). `Session.Timeline` is worked out from its stored fields; `IsOnBreakAt`, `LiftsBlockingAt`, `CurrentBreakAt`, `BreakTransitionsBetween` and `BreaksTakenBy` hand off to it (parity test over every minute, with and without keep-blocking). The progress bar's break positions moved from Infrastructure into the timeline (`Marks`), so the monitor needs no markers member and `BreakMarkerDto` is gone. `SessionDto`'s `StartedAt`/`EndAt`/`Breaks`/`KeepBlockingDuringBreaks` (read only by the monitor) became one `Timeline` field — F7's `SessionDto` half; `SessionBreakDto` and `MapBreaks` deleted. Monitor 10 → 3 members, off the `InterfaceSizeRuleTests` list. 0 warnings, 1737 green.
9. **A3 A′ — split `ISessionStateService`.** `IActiveSessionState` (`RefreshAsync` via `GetActiveSessionQuery`, one `SessionDto` builder — F1, F3), `IRitualPrompt` (`RefreshAsync(at)`, `IsRitualRequired` deleted), interim relay interface (6, pointer to Gap 107); startup sweep dispatched from app startup. **Prompt release decided 2026-10-07 (D′):** checked — the heartbeat sweeps missed blocks (`FocusBlock.IsUnresolved`) and announces them via `PostSessionsReady` *before* it asks the Ritual query, in the same tick with one `now`; so the Shell's own `TryMarkBlockMissed` (in `HandleLiveRitualRelease`/`HandleRitualHandoff`) is a second home for "missed", always refused as a duplicate. Decided: `IRitualPrompt` only says which block awaits its Ritual (`FocusBlock.IsAwaitingRitual`) — `RitualRequirementChange` loses its release/handoff ids; the Shell opens a Ritual when the prompt names a block and closes it when the prompt stops naming that block, whatever the reason (window closed, or its session started — both mean "no longer awaiting"); missed screens come only from the sweep. Deleted: `TryMarkBlockMissed`, `HandleLiveRitualRelease`, `HandleRitualHandoff`. Back-to-back blocks keep their order (sweep's missed screen first, then the next Ritual). Gap 107's finding 5 is done by this (noted there). Manual check: a planned session's Start exits the Ritual cleanly; a Ritual left open past its block's end closes and the missed screen follows. Guards: size counts, `DtoBuilderRuleTests`' `SessionDto` entry and rule 19 deleted, off `DispatcherRuleTests`' list; headless check that the Main page and Ritual prompt still follow a restart. **Done 2026-10-07:** red first — `GetActiveSessionQuery` dropped `Origin`/`OriginatingBlockId`/`BlockPresetId` (F1, a runtime failure); it now carries them and is the one builder. Three singletons replace `SessionStateService`: `ActiveSessionState`, `RitualPrompt` (raises `Changed` only when the named block changes; locked, since the heartbeat writes it and the UI reads it) and `RecapRelay` (the interim relay, named `IRecapRelay`). `StartSession` pushes nothing and lost its preset lookup; the started/ended event handlers refresh first; the heartbeat refreshes the prompt last; `Program.Main` refreshes, then dispatches the startup sweep. Rule 11 found one caller the decision didn't name: `HandlePendingRitual` also called `TryMarkBlockMissed` (a queued Ritual whose window closed meanwhile) — it now asks the prompt instead ("still named? open it, else skip"), the same D′ rule; its clock check went with it. Also deleted: `RitualRequirementChange` entirely (with `Required` and both ids gone only an id was left, so `Changed` carries a `Guid?` — a one-field wrapper is the warning sign), and `MainPageViewModel.IsRitualBannerVisible`, the prompt's other subscriber, which no view binds. Two behaviour changes, both following D′: the prompt now lags a session start by up to one tick (the Ritual already closes itself on Start, and a stale queued Ritual is refused by the existing `AlreadyHasSession` check); a block falling due while a spontaneous Ritual is open is queued behind it instead of dropped. With F1 built, the Planner's running tile opens nothing after a restart, as before one (noted under Gap 89). Guards: rule 19 deleted; `InterfaceSizeRuleTests` lists `IRecapRelay has 6` with a pointer to Gap 107; `SessionDto` and `SessionStateService` entries deleted. New tests: `ActiveSessionStateTests`, `RitualPromptTests` (the change-only rule probed red), the started handler refreshes before syncing, and headless `ShellRitualPromptTests` — the restart path (Ritual reopened, Main page shows the running session) and the four D′ cases (open, close on Start, left open past its end → missed screen follows, back-to-back order), plus a stale queued Ritual not opened; the close and queued-Ritual checks probed red. Most of the two D′ manual checks are now automated; the manual ones stay for the real heartbeat timing. 0 warnings, 1742 green.
10. **Close-out.** Step 5 answers per item (recorded above); `DESIGN_GAP_HISTORY.md` entry with the lessons list; dictionary rows (`WholeMinutes`, `SessionTimeline`, lifecycle verdicts, Contract Rules, repositories' session doors); `TECHNICAL_SPEC_INFRA.md` one Done line; `THINGS_TO_TEST_IN_APP.md` — only what tests can't prove (Main page break flip at a boundary, contract buttons on each status, preset form with Work Hours, a restart mid-session). Ask Mikkel about committing.

**Lessons (settled at close-out):** the review's first pass found the shape problems; reading each item's code found the bigger ones it hid (the monitor's duplicate break rules, an unguarded "one session at a time", a half-built period target that was already done). Each "is there a more DDD option?" moved a rule from a validator or reviewer's memory to the type itself.


## Gap 111 — Domain Services Have No Interfaces; Interface First Becomes Contract First *(closed, 2026-10-08; built directly)*

**Raised by** Mikkel before starting Gap 110: four interfaces lived in the Domain (`IPolicyEngine`, `IBlockPlacementChecker`, `IBlockChangeChecker`, `IContractBlockBatchPreparer`) — should they move to Application? No: each sat next to its only implementation, and moving one would have made the Domain depend on Application. The better question was whether they were needed at all. Reading the tests answered it: three were never faked (every test news up the real class), and `IPolicyEngine` was faked in one file whose checks work as well against the real, pure engine. In DDD an interface marks a **port** — something the outside world supplies — and a Domain service is the rule itself; nothing swaps it.

**Changed:** the four interfaces deleted, their doc comments moved onto the sealed classes; DI registers the classes as themselves; `ComputeActivePolicySliceQueryHandlerTests` run against the real `PolicyEngine` and assert on the slice. `MODULE_STANDARDS.md` → Interface-First Rule became the Contract-First Rule: a port is an interface in `Application/Interfaces/`, a Domain service is a sealed class whose reviewed signatures are its contract. `ARCHITECT_GOVERNANCE.md` → Design Gap Protocol Step 2 and the Naming Conventions row follow it.

**Rejected:** moving the interfaces to Application (inverts the dependency); keeping only `IPolicyEngine` for its one fake (a rule "interface when faked" for one test file); dropping the rule outright (its point — design the signature before the body — still holds; only the `interface` keyword stopped fitting).

**Confirmed via** the full suite (1756 passing), including `CompositionRootTests`.

**Guard (step 5):** yes, two tests. `DomainRuleTests.DomainDeclaresNoInterfaces` — probed red by the four interfaces themselves before they were deleted. `InterfaceSizeRuleTests.DomainServicesStaySmall` — removing the interfaces would have silently removed their 5-member cap, since the size test only counted interfaces; it now counts a Domain service's declared public members too, probed red by two extra methods on `BlockChangeChecker` ("has 6").

**Principle demonstrated:** an abstraction earns its place by something that varies behind it. Ask "what would ever swap this?" — if nothing does, the class's public methods already are the contract, and the interface is a second file to keep in step with the first.
